in place of its upper bound, giving TAC16's sharper exact-data centre.

The four CPQ complements enter the allocation through FTR10 and
TAC17 with coefficient $1/4$; at TAC18's stated order $s=j$,
they enter the matching budget with coefficient $-1/4$.
They are therefore retained with both
coefficients before cancellation; they do not replace either packet
complement in FTR31. The independently computed relative proper-source
returns remain PRD21 and PRD28 at their degree-$k$ cutoffs. This
identifies the actual receiving maps for all three uses of the same
tail calculation, with their original domains, values and coefficients.
\endgroup

\label{FRBUILD:receiver:end}

\begingroup
\section{The original native tail as an attained relation Gram matrix}
\label{sec:NTG}

\subsection{Original data and the classical tools being used}
Fix the original simple quartet $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$,
its nonzero amplitude, its original period, its logarithm branch,
and its complete nonreduced conductor. The period is fixed before
any degree limit. Retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 k=4l+1\ge9,\quad j=k+4,\quad q=(j+1)^2,\quad q'=(j-7)^2,
 \quad c'=j/2-4,\\
 \beta_{a,u,w}=a/2+(2u-a)\delta+i(2w-a)\gamma,\quad
 \chi_a(S)=\prod_{u,w=0}^{a}(S-\beta_{a,u,w}),\quad
 \chi_-=\chi_{j-8},\\
 E_A(z)=\sum_{u,w=0}^{8}a_{uw}e^{\beta_{8,u,w}z},\quad
 \mu_d=E_A^{(d)}(0)=\sum_{u,w=0}^{8}a_{uw}\beta_{8,u,w}^{\,d},
 \quad v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A,\quad\mu_v\ne0,\\
 L=q-v,\quad m=L-q'=16j-48-v>0,\qquad s\in\{1,j\}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG1}
\]
The original bounds on $v$ and the original admissible period
domain remain in force; in particular no exceptional finite-period
fibre is replaced by $v=0$. Every $\mu_d$ is a derivative,
not a Taylor coefficient. The accepted original relation
calculation NIB1--9 supplies
\[
\begin{gathered}
 (\mathcal T_AP)(S')=\sum_{u,w=0}^{8}a_{uw}P(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}),
 \quad g_t=\mathcal T_A(\chi_j S^t),\\
 \ell_t=\mu_v\binom{q+t}{v},\quad u_t=g_t/\ell_t,\quad
 \deg u_t=L+t,\quad\operatorname{lc}u_t=1,\quad \chi_-\mid u_t .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG2}
\]
It also proves that $u_0,\ldots,u_t$ span the complete original
functional annihilator at degree $L+t$. We keep all original
analytic columns $F_b=N_b/E_A$ of NSO12, $1\le b\le m$, with
the original complex-linear functional convention
$\Lambda_F(S'^d)=F^{(d)}(0)$.

The scalar measure and polynomial recurrence are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s(y)=\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
              |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,\qquad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+s/2)p_{n-1,s},\\
 h_{n,s}=M_s n!(s/2)_n,\quad
 \phi_n(S')=p_{n,s}((S'-c')/i)/\sqrt{h_{n,s}},\\
 \langle P,Q\rangle_s=\int\overline{P(c'+iy)}Q(c'+iy)\,dm_s(y).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG3}
\]
Inner products are conjugate-linear in the first argument.
These Gamma-weight orthogonal polynomials belong to the classical
Meixner--Pollaczek theory, not to a new family introduced here.
One retained historical source record is Josef Meixner,
\emph{Orthogonale Polynomsysteme mit einer besonderen Gestalt der
erzeugenden Funktion}, J.\ London Math.\ Soc.\ 9 (1934), 6--13,
\href{https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms/s1-9.1.6}{doi:10.1112/jlms/s1-9.1.6}.
The precise recurrence, phase and mass used in this calculation
are the displayed original NTG3; its orthonormality is retained
from NIB2 and the cumulative source's full Gamma calculation.
The directly checked modern formula reference is NIST DLMF,
\href{https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.19.E8}{18.19.8},
\href{https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.19.E9}{18.19.9}, and
\href{https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.22.E8}{18.22.8}
\cite[eqs.~(18.19.8)--(18.19.9), (18.22.8)]{human:dlmf18}.
The exact parameter map is
$p_{n,s}(y)=n!P_n^{(s/4)}(y/2;\pi/2)$.
Substitution in the displayed DLMF recurrence gives NTG3.
Its norm under the original density is
\[
 (n!)^2\,\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{2\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
       \frac{2\pi\Gamma(n+s/2)}{2^{s/2}n!}
       =M_sn!(s/2)_n .
\]
The first factor of $2$ in the density conversion is the
Jacobian $dy=2\,dx$, so this dictionary also checks the
original mass. DLMF is used as a verified formula reference;
the Meixner record preserves a historical attribution trail whose
exact relation to these displayed formulas has not been verified here
from the complete article. It is not used as a checked theorem provider.

\begingroup\raggedright
The orthogonal projections and attained least-squares minima below
use the classical Gram--Schmidt construction. The retained historical
record includes Erhard Schmidt, \emph{Zur Theorie der linearen und
nichtlinearen Integralgleichungen. I}, Math.\ Ann.\ 63 (1907),
433--476,
\href{https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01449770}{doi:10.1007/BF01449770},
with the earlier J.\ P.\ Gram attribution retained for the historical
audit to trace in the original pages. The block determinant eliminations
are Schur-complement calculations; their retained historical trail includes
Issai Schur,
\emph{\"Uber Potenzreihen, die im Innern des Einheitskreises
beschr\"ankt sind}, J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.\ 147 (1917), 205--232,
\href{https://doi.org/10.1515/crll.1917.147.205}
{doi:10.1515/crll.1917.147.205}.
\par\endgroup
Every finite projection, determinant identity and inequality
needed below is proved explicitly. The contribution here is their
application to NTG1--3 with the full original conductor and
functional frames, including the precise native phase.
For the actual minimum and determinant identities we also cite the
directly read derivations in Boyd and Vandenberghe,
Appendix A.5.5, p.~650, and Appendix C.4.1, p.~673
\cite{human:boyd-vandenberghe}; the formulas below provide the
complete complex Hermitian specialization used in this programme.
The publisher records for Schmidt and Schur were checked for
their bibliographic metadata. Their complete historical proofs
were not read as external theorem providers in this calculation;
the finite proofs below are self-contained.

\subsection{Finite evaluation of every coefficient and every pairing}
Write $\chi_j(S)=\sum_{a=0}^{q}c_aS^a$, with $c_q=1$ and all
coefficients obtained from the original root product. Direct
binomial expansion gives, for $0\le h\le q+t$,
\[
 [S'^h]g_t
   =\sum_{\substack{0\le a\le q\\a+t\ge h}}
             c_a\binom{a+t}{h}\mu_{a+t-h}.
                                                               \tag{NTG4}
\]
Thus every coefficient above degree $L+t$ vanishes because
$\mu_d=0$ for $d<v$, and the coefficient at degree $L+t$ is
exactly $\ell_t$. This verifies the derivative factorials in
the original translation map. It does not replace that map
by a derivative of order $v$.

For each integer $d\ge0$ let $J^{(d)}_s$ be the matrix on indices
$0,\ldots,d$ with zero diagonal and off-diagonal entries
$J_s^{(d)}[n,n+1]=J_s^{(d)}[n+1,n]=\sqrt{(n+1)(n+s/2)}$.
Use the convention that the zeroth power is the identity.
The exact moments are
\[
 M_{d,s}:=\int y^d\,dm_s(y)=M_s((J_s^{(d)})^d)_{00}.
                                                               \tag{NTG5}
\]
Indeed multiplication by $y$ in the orthonormal basis of NTG3
has exactly those adjacent entries. A path of $d$ multiplication
steps starting at index zero never exceeds index $d$.
Taking its inner product with the constant unit polynomial
$1/\sqrt{M_s}$ proves NTG5. In particular it preserves the
entire original mass.

Put $\mathfrak H_s[h,b]=\langle S'^h,S'^b\rangle_s$. Expansion
on the original vertical line gives the completely finite formula
\[
 \mathfrak H_s[h,b]
 =\sum_{\alpha=0}^{h}\sum_{\beta=0}^{b}
 \binom h\alpha\binom b\beta
 (c')^{h+b-\alpha-\beta}(-i)^\alpha i^\beta
 M_{\alpha+\beta,s}.
                                                               \tag{NTG6}
\]
Consequently, for arbitrary polynomials $P,Q$ with coefficient
columns $p,q$, their inner product is $p^*\mathfrak H_s q$
at their finite degree cutoff. This formula evaluates every
cross pairing below without quadrature, unlisted constants, or
an asymptotic replacement of the Gamma density.

Set
\[
 B_s[a,b]=\langle\chi_-S'^a,\chi_-S'^b\rangle_s.
                                                               \tag{NTG7}
\]
NTG6 and the full lower root coefficients calculate its entries.
Every finite leading principal block is strictly positive:
a nonzero polynomial has positive squared integral against the
strictly positive Gamma density outside its finite zero set.
For $b\ge1$, let $B_{<b}=(B_s[a,d])_{a,d<b}$ and
$z_b=(B_s[a,b])_{a<b}$. Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \pi_b^-(S')=S'^b-\sum_{a<b}S'^a(B_{<b}^{-1}z_b)_a,\qquad
 \nu_{b,s}^-=B_s[b,b]-z_b^*B_{<b}^{-1}z_b,\\
 \pi_0^-=1,\quad\nu_{0,s}^-=B_s[0,0],\qquad
 e_b(S')=\chi_-(S')\pi_b^-(S')/\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG8}
\]
Completing the coefficient square proves that $\pi_b^-$ is
the unique monic minimizer of degree $b$ in the lower weighted
norm and $\nu_{b,s}^-$ is its strictly positive squared norm.
Its residual is orthogonal to every preceding monomial. It
follows by successive degrees that the $e_b$ are orthonormal,
and $e_0,\ldots,e_d$ span the complete ideal
$\chi_-\mathbb C[S']_{\le d}$. This is the actual Gram--Schmidt
operation just cited, with its entire finite coefficient map
given in NTG7--8.

For later use the analytic entries are also a finite calculation
from the same nonreduced conductor. Write
$n_{d,b}=N_b^{(d)}(0)=\sum_\lambda Z_{\lambda b}\beta_{j,\lambda}^d$
with the original numerator frame $Z$, and
$f_{d,b}=F_b^{(d)}(0)$. The original vanishing conditions
$n_{d,b}=0$ for $d<v$ make $N_b/E_A$ analytic at zero.
Leibniz's formula gives the recursion
\[
 f_{d,b}=
 \frac{n_{d+v,b}
       -\displaystyle\sum_{h=v+1}^{d+v}
              \binom{d+v}{h}\mu_h f_{d+v-h,b}}
      {\binom{d+v}{v}\mu_v}
       \quad(d\ge0).
                                                               \tag{NTG9}
\]
Each index in the sum is smaller than $d$, so the recursion
starts with $f_{0,b}=n_{v,b}/\mu_v$ and determines every required
derivative in finitely many operations. If $e_a=\sum_h e_{ha}S'^h$,
then $\Lambda_{F_b}(e_a)=\sum_h e_{ha}f_{h,b}$, with no
conjugation of this complex-linear functional value.

\subsection{The actual relation graph and its full attained Gram}
For $Q\ge0$ form the coefficient matrix of $u_0,\ldots,u_Q$
in the original orthonormal lower ideal basis:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 {\cal R}_Q[b,t]=\langle e_b,u_t\rangle_s,\quad
       0\le b\le m+Q,\ 0\le t\le Q,\\
 {\cal R}_Q=\begin{bmatrix}{\cal L}_Q\\ H_Q\end{bmatrix},
 \qquad {\cal L}_Q[b,t]={\cal R}_Q[b,t]\ (b<m),\quad
 H_Q[a,t]={\cal R}_Q[m+a,t].
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG10}
\]
NTG4,6,8 evaluate every entry. If $a>t$, $e_{m+a}$ is
orthogonal to $u_t$ by degree. For $a=t$, the difference
$u_t-\chi_-\pi_{m+t}^-$ has smaller degree in the same
lower ideal, so
\[
 H_Q[a,t]=0\ (a>t),\qquad
 H_Q[t,t]=\sqrt{\nu_{m+t,s}^-}>0.
                                                               \tag{NTG11}
\]
In particular $H_Q$ is invertible and is upper triangular
in the unchanged degree order. Define
\[
 X_Q={\cal L}_QH_Q^{-1}=[x_0,\ldots,x_Q],\qquad
 \Omega_Q=I_m+X_QX_Q^*,\qquad
 X_{-1}\text{ empty},\quad\Omega_{-1}=I_m.
                                                               \tag{NTG12}
\]
The first $Q+1$ columns of $X_{Q+1}$ equal $X_Q$. To prove
this, partition $H_{Q+1}$ as
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}H_Q&h\\0&d\end{smallmatrix}\right]$;
its first $Q+1$ inverse columns are
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}H_Q^{-1}\\0\end{smallmatrix}\right]$.
Multiplication by $[{\cal L}_Q,\ell]$ proves the assertion.
Thus the columns $x_t$ are actual compatible original-data
columns and are not selected anew at each cutoff.

In the coefficient frame $e_0,\ldots,e_{m+Q}$ the complete
functional relation space has the exact graph frame
\[
 {\cal R}_QH_Q^{-1}
     =\begin{bmatrix}X_Q\\I_{Q+1}\end{bmatrix}.
                                                               \tag{NTG13}
\]
Both sides are injective maps from $\mathbb C^{Q+1}$ into
the same original ideal. If $c$ is the original $u_t$
coefficient column and $z$ is its high-degree coordinate
column, the two mutually inverse coordinate maps are
$z=H_Qc$ and $c=H_Q^{-1}z$. The inverse of the graph
map itself is simply high-degree coordinate projection.
Thus this calculation preserves every original relation.

Let $G_Q=(\langle u_a,u_b\rangle_s)_{0\le a,b\le Q}$.
Orthonormality and NTG13 give
\[
\begin{gathered}
 G_Q=H_Q^*(I_{Q+1}+X_Q^*X_Q)H_Q,\\
 \frac{\det G_Q}{\prod_{t=0}^{Q}\nu_{m+t,s}^-}
       =\det(I_{Q+1}+X_Q^*X_Q)=\det\Omega_Q .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG14}
\]
For completeness the last determinant equality follows by
eliminating either diagonal identity block in
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}I_m&X_Q\\-X_Q^*&I_{Q+1}
\end{smallmatrix}\right]$. These two eliminations use block
unitriangular factors of determinant one. This proves the
identity without an assumption on singular values or ranks.
The scalar denominator in NTG14 is exactly
$|\det H_Q|^2$ by NTG11; none of the lower-ideal minima
has been replaced by a trial polynomial.

Successively completing the squares of $G_Q$ identifies its
last Schur complement with the original $\eta_{Q,s}$ of NIB5.
Induction gives $\det G_Q=\prod_{t=0}^Q\eta_{t,s}$, including
$Q=0$. NIB9 and NTG14 therefore imply
\[
 \log\frac{\det\Omega_Q}{\det\Omega_R}
   =\sum_{t=R+1}^{Q}\log\frac{\eta_{t,s}}{\nu_{m+t,s}^-}
   =\sum_{t=R+1}^{Q}\log(1+r_{L+t})
        \qquad(-1\le R<Q).
                                                               \tag{NTG15}
\]
This is a relation-Gram identity on the actual source, with
dimension $m=O(j)$ on its left. No asymptotic bound for this
left side is inferred merely from that dimension.

\subsection{The literal analytic quotient metric and the native phase}
Define the square matrix and its succeeding rows by
\[
 A[a,b]=\Lambda_{F_b}(e_a)\quad(0\le a<m,\ 1\le b\le m),
 \qquad F_{\rm hi}[t,b]=\Lambda_{F_b}(e_{m+t}).
                                                               \tag{NTG16}
\]
All entries are evaluated by NTG8--9. The matrix $A$ is
invertible. Indeed an ideal polynomial of degree at most
$q'+m-1=L-1$ annihilated by all $F_b$ also vanishes at all
lower nodes. The full original functional map at degree
$L-1$ is invertible by NIB4's Vandermonde argument.
Thus that polynomial is zero. In the $m$-dimensional basis
$e_0,\ldots,e_{m-1}$ its functional map is $A^{\mathsf T}$,
which is consequently injective and hence invertible.

Applying each complex-linear functional to NTG13 gives,
with ordinary transpose,
\[
          X_Q^{\mathsf T}A+F_{\rm hi}=0 .
                                                               \tag{NTG17}
\]
Here $F_{\rm hi}$ contains rows $0,\ldots,Q$. All signs,
conjugations and the complete lower ideal are fixed by this
actual polynomial equation.

Let $D_n$ be the original native quotient Gram after observing
rows $0,\ldots,n$:
\[
 D_n=T_n^*(I-V_n(V_n^*V_n)^{-1}V_n^*)T_n,\quad n\ge L-1.
                                                               \tag{NTG18}
\]
The columns of $V_n$ are all original lower evaluations and
those of $T_n$ are all original analytic NSO functionals
evaluated on $\phi_0,\ldots,\phi_n$. Their full ranks follow
from the same original Vandermonde argument. Let $C_n$ be
the matrix of coefficients of $e_0,\ldots,e_{n-q'}$ in
that orthonormal native basis. Then $C_n^*C_n=I$ and
$V_n^{\mathsf T}C_n=0$. Polynomial division by $\chi_-$
and the dimension count show that these are all vectors
in $\ker V_n^{\mathsf T}$. Thus
\[
 I-V_n(V_n^*V_n)^{-1}V_n^*
       =\overline{C_n}C_n^{\mathsf T}.
                                                               \tag{NTG19}
\]
Both sides are the orthogonal projector onto
$\ker V_n^*=\overline{\ker V_n^{\mathsf T}}$; the identity
also follows directly by applying them to their image and
its orthogonal complement. Since $C_n^{\mathsf T}T_n$
is the matrix of actual functional values on the $e_b$,
substitution of NTG17 into NTG18 proves the full matrix equality
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 D_{L+Q}
   =A^*(I_m+\overline{X_Q}X_Q^{\mathsf T})A
   =A^*\overline{\Omega_Q}A,\qquad D_{L-1}=A^*A .
 \quad}                                                        \tag{NTG20}
\]
This is equality in the unchanged analytic coefficient frame,
not only a determinant equality. It proves the exact map to
every original restriction and quotient: in four copies use
$\operatorname{diag}(A,4)$ on the original coefficient frame,
retain $\operatorname{diag}(\overline{\Omega_Q},4)$ as its
metric, and perform the original restriction or Schur
minimum on the original affine coefficient fibre. The
conjugation in NTG20 is forced by the complex-linear
functional pairing in NTG17 and NTG19.

There is also an exact row phase. Put $n=L+t$ and write
$v_n,t_n$ for the appended lower and analytic rows. At
cutoff $n-1$, retain
\[
 \zeta_n=v_n(V_{n-1}^*V_{n-1})^{-1}v_n^*,\quad
 \rho_n=t_n-v_n(V_{n-1}^*V_{n-1})^{-1}V_{n-1}^*T_{n-1}.
                                                               \tag{NTG21}
\]
The last native coefficient of $e_{m+t}$ is
$c_n=i^n\sqrt{h_{n,s}/\nu_{m+t,s}^-}
     =i^n/\sqrt{1+\zeta_n}$ by its leading coefficient
and NIB8. Its old coefficient column is the minimum-norm
solution of the lower evaluation equation, because it is
orthogonal to the old lower ideal. This solution is
$-c_n\overline{V_{n-1}}(V_{n-1}^{\mathsf T}
\overline{V_{n-1}})^{-1}v_n^{\mathsf T}$.
Multiplying its ordinary transpose by $T_{n-1}$ and adding
$c_nt_n$ gives
$\Lambda_F(e_{m+t})=c_n\rho_n$. Equation NTG17 therefore
proves
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \frac{\rho_n}{\sqrt{1+\zeta_n}}
        =-i^{-n}x_t^{\mathsf T}A,\qquad
 r_n=x_t^*\Omega_{t-1}^{-1}x_t .
 \quad}                                                        \tag{NTG22}
\]
For the last equality substitute NTG20 and the row equality
into $r_n=\rho_nD_{n-1}^{-1}\rho_n^*/(1+\zeta_n)$.
It yields $x_t^{\mathsf T}\overline{\Omega_{t-1}^{-1}}
\overline{x_t}$, the conjugate of the real nonnegative
number $x_t^*\Omega_{t-1}^{-1}x_t$. This supplies the
original phase-sensitive row to each of the eight fixed
flag compressions; zeros and all eigenvalues stay present.

\subsection{Joint minimization of the complete later relations}
Fix the accepted prefix endpoint
$R=R_j=\lfloor j^{3/2}/\log j\rfloor<q$ and any $R<Q\le q$.
Write $X_Q=[X_R,Y]$, with
$Y=[x_{R+1},\ldots,x_Q]$, and partition the upper triangular
matrix of NTG11 as
\[
 H_Q=\begin{bmatrix}H_R&H_{12}\\0&H_{22}\end{bmatrix}.
                                                               \tag{NTG23}
\]
Let $S_{R,Q}$ be the full attained Gram on the later relation
coefficients after minimizing over every earlier coefficient:
\[
 z^*S_{R,Q}z
   =\min_{a\in\mathbb C^{R+1}}
       \left\|\sum_{t=0}^Ra_tu_t+
                    \sum_{t=R+1}^Qz_{t-R-1}u_t\right\|_s^2 .
                                                               \tag{NTG24}
\]
The minimum is attained uniquely since the preceding relation
Gram is positive. In the graph coordinates NTG13, the
preceding columns are $[X_R;I_{R+1};0]$ and the new columns
are $[Y;0;I_{Q-R}]$. Their two diagonal Grams are
$I+X_R^*X_R$ and $I+Y^*Y$, and their cross Gram is
$X_R^*Y$. Completing the entire preceding square gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 S_{R,Q}
  =H_{22}^*\bigl(I_{Q-R}+Y^*\Omega_R^{-1}Y\bigr)H_{22},\\
 \frac{\det S_{R,Q}}
      {\displaystyle\prod_{t=R+1}^Q\nu_{m+t,s}^-}
  =\det(I_{Q-R}+Y^*\Omega_R^{-1}Y)
  =\frac{\det\Omega_Q}{\det\Omega_R}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG25}
\]
The $H_{12}$ component is in the preceding relation span
and is minimized over, so it disappears only through the
displayed full minimum. To verify its inverse factor,
direct multiplication proves
$I-X_R(I+X_R^*X_R)^{-1}X_R^*=(I+X_RX_R^*)^{-1}$.
Conjugating by $Y$ proves the first line. The determinant
identities follow by the same two identity-block
eliminations used in NTG14. This calculation jointly
minimizes every later linear combination and preserves
every off-diagonal pairing between those combinations.

\subsection{Finite analytic improvement from the retained cross pairings}
All the following quantities have already been evaluated by
the finite original-data operations NTG4--12:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 a_t=x_t^*\Omega_R^{-1}x_t,\quad
 c_{th}=x_t^*\Omega_R^{-1}x_h,\quad R<t,h\le Q,\\
 d=Q-R,\quad d_0=\min(m,d),\quad
 T_{R,Q}=\sum_{t=R+1}^Qa_t .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG26}
\]
The exact unweighted tail value $Z_{R,Q}:=
\log\det\Omega_Q-\log\det\Omega_R$ satisfies
\[
 0\le Z_{R,Q}\le
 d_0\log\left(1+\frac{T_{R,Q}}{d_0}\right).
                                                               \tag{NTG27}
\]
Indeed the nonzero eigenvalues of
$\Omega_R^{-1/2}YY^*\Omega_R^{-1/2}$ are nonnegative,
number at most $d_0$, and sum to $T_{R,Q}$.
The determinant equality NTG25 identifies $Z_{R,Q}$
with the sum of $\log(1+\lambda)$ over those eigenvalues.
Append zeros to reach $d_0$ terms. The elementary
concavity inequality for $\log$, proved by its negative
second derivative and finite weighted averaging, gives
NTG27. This bound charges the actual dimension $m$ rather
than the number of remaining rows when $Q-R>m$.
It does not bound $T_{R,Q}$ by an unproved small number.

There is an explicit second improvement that measures
the retained interaction of distinct later relations.
Partition the actual consecutive indices $R+1,\ldots,Q$
into adjacent pairs and at most one final singleton;
denote the pairs by $\mathscr P$. Define
\[
 \Delta_{R,Q}:=
 -\sum_{(t,h)\in\mathscr P}
       \log\left(1-
         \frac{|c_{th}|^2}{(1+a_t)(1+a_h)}\right)\ge0 .
                                                               \tag{NTG28}
\]
Every term is finite. Minimizing
$\|x_h-zx_t\|_{\Omega_R^{-1}}^2$ over complex $z$ gives
the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality in this exact positive metric
(if $x_t=0$ the mixed pairing is already zero). Thus
$|c_{th}|^2\le a_ta_h<(1+a_t)(1+a_h)$.
It follows that
\[
 Z_{R,Q}\le
       \sum_{t=R+1}^{Q}\log(1+a_t)-\Delta_{R,Q}.
                                                               \tag{NTG29}
\]
Here is the determinant proof, including the full interaction
discarded by a scalar bound. The positive matrix
$M=I+Y^*\Omega_R^{-1}Y$ has diagonal entries $1+a_t$
and off-diagonal entries $c_{th}$. For a two-block
partition, elimination gives
$\det M=\det M_{11}\det(M_{22}-M_{21}M_{11}^{-1}M_{12})
\le\det M_{11}\det M_{22}$.
The inequality follows since the second positive factor
is bounded above by $M_{22}$; conjugating by
$M_{22}^{-1/2}$ gives eigenvalues at most one and
therefore a determinant at most one. Applying this
successively to the prescribed pairs bounds $\det M$
by the product of the actual pair determinants and
the possible singleton entry. Each pair determinant
is exactly $(1+a_t)(1+a_h)-|c_{th}|^2$.
Taking logarithms proves NTG29.

This is the familiar determinant inequality associated with
Hadamard and its block refinements, applied to an explicitly
computed original Gram. The retained historical source record is
Jacques Hadamard, \emph{R\'esolution d'une question relative
aux d\'eterminants}, Bull.\ Sci.\ Math.\ (2) 17 (1893),
240--246; a scan of the original article is preserved at
\href{https://zenodo.org/records/18674602}{the primary-article archive}.
This archive's bibliographic metadata was consulted here;
its full original proof was not used as a verified theorem.
The proof above supplies the precise positive-block version
used here rather than importing an unstated variant.

For the first two remaining indices $a=R+1$, $b=R+2$,
the entire joint calculation is especially explicit:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log(1+r_{L+a})=\log(1+a_a),\\
 r_{L+b}=a_b-\frac{|c_{ab}|^2}{1+a_a},\\
 Z_{R,R+2}=\log\bigl((1+a_a)(1+a_b)-|c_{ab}|^2\bigr).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG30}
\]
The inverse identity
$(\Omega_R+x_ax_a^*)^{-1}
=\Omega_R^{-1}
-\Omega_R^{-1}x_ax_a^*\Omega_R^{-1}/(1+a_a)$
is verified by multiplication; substituting it in
NTG22 proves the second line and the other lines follow.
Thus the calculated correction for the second later row
is the exact nonnegative quantity $|c_{ab}|^2/(1+a_a)$.
It vanishes precisely when the displayed original
cross pairing vanishes. No strict positivity is asserted
without evaluating that original pairing, and none is
needed for NTG25--30.

The older scalar bound NIB14 remains valid for every row.
Combining it with the two joint bounds gives the
fully finite original-data estimate
\[
\begin{split}
 0\le Z_{R,Q}\le \min\bigg\{&
 \sum_{t=R+1}^{Q}E_{j,s,t},\
 d_0\log\left(1+\frac{T_{R,Q}}{d_0}\right),\\
 &\sum_{t=R+1}^{Q}\log(1+a_t)-\Delta_{R,Q}\bigg\}.
\end{split}                                                      \tag{NTG31}
\]
Every entry in the new two bounds is a finite expression
in the actual roots, all $a_{uw}$, the exact $\mu_d$,
the complete Gamma moments and the attained lower minima.
The sum of the old scalar estimates is retained as a
separate bound, so these new finite bounds cannot degrade
the previously proved one. The exact formula NTG25
is stronger still when its finite determinant is used.

\subsection{The surviving original tail and its precise receiving scope}
The original tail has weights two at $R+1,\ldots,q-1$
and weight one at $q$. Its scalar value is therefore
\[
\begin{gathered}
 {\cal T}_{j,s}(R)
   :=2Z_{R,q-1}+Z_{q-1,q}
    =Z_{R,q-1}+Z_{R,q},\\
 =\log\frac{\det\Omega_{q-1}\det\Omega_q}
                         {(\det\Omega_R)^2}\ge0 .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{NTG32}
\]
Here $Z_{R,R}=0$ when needed. For the accepted $j\ge13$
both $q-1$ and $q$ are larger than $R_j$.
Applying NTG31 separately at $Q=q-1,q$ gives an
explicit joint finite upper estimate for this entire
unchanged tail. By NIB16 the absolute signed native
loss tail, the absolute signed threshold tail, and
each of the four nonnegative CPQ complementary tails
are bounded by $4{\cal T}_{j,s}(R)$.
Their exact values retain their original eight fixed
flags and their respective quotient minima, all
supplied with the same matrix NTG20 and row NTG22.
The signs remain positive at $T_e,K_c,W_i,K_i$ and
negative at $W,S,J,I$; the two nested flags and
the full CPQ identity remain unchanged.

NIB20 already controls the prefix by $o(kq_k)$.
The unresolved asymptotic size here is the calculated
matrix expression NTG32, equivalently the correlated
original-data columns $x_t$ beyond $R_j$. A small rank
or the original scalar Christoffel bounds alone do
not assign its leading coefficient: its exact map
to those norms is NTG10--14, retaining the full block
$\mathcal L_Q$ and the complete triangular cross block $H_Q$.
Equations NTG24--31 calculate the simultaneous
minimization and its finite improvement; they do
not assume a missing asymptotic estimate. Both
orders $s=1,j$, the fixed period, every original
root and conductor coefficient, and all masses
and phases occur throughout.

\begingroup
\section{Szeg\H{o}'s confluent modification formula on the full original lower grid}
\label{sec:SLC}

\subsection{The primary source and its exact original-data dictionary}
The human source used here is G\'abor Szeg\H{o},
\emph{Orthogonal Polynomials}, fourth edition, AMS Colloquium
Publications 23 (1975), Theorem 2.5 and equations
(2.5.1)--(2.5.4), printed pages 29--30
\cite[Theorem 2.5, pp.~29--30]{human:szego1975}.
The publisher's book-level identifier is
\href{https://doi.org/10.1090/coll/023}{doi:10.1090/coll/023};
the fourth-edition date is printed on the controlling
local copy's edition-history page. The source explicitly
credits E.\ B.\ Christoffel's earlier special case.
Theorem 2.5 represents the orthogonal polynomial for a
nonnegative polynomial modification of a positive distribution
by a determinant of consecutive original orthogonal polynomials.
For a repeated multiplier root it specifies the value and
successive derivative rows. It does not assert that this
determinant is monic or orthonormal; its proof establishes
nonvanishing of the leading cofactor by positivity.
The calculation below retains that cofactor and proves the
complete specialization in the original coordinates.

Keep all NTG1--3 data, including the fixed original period,
the complete nonreduced conductor and actual $\mu_d=E_A^{(d)}(0)$,
the two source orders $s=1,j$, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 a=j-8,\quad c'=a/2,\quad d=q'=(a+1)^2,\quad D=2d,\quad
 q=(j+1)^2,\quad L=q-v,\quad m=L-d,\\
 z_{u,w}=(2w-a)\gamma-i(2u-a)\delta
       =(\beta_{a,u,w}-c')/i,\qquad 0\le u,w\le a,\\
 Q_-(y)=\prod_{u,w=0}^{a}(y-z_{u,w})
       =i^{-d}\chi_-(c'+iy),\quad
 \rho(y)=Q_-(y)^2 .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC1}
\]
The index order is the original lexicographic order of $(u,w)$.
The roots are distinct because real and imaginary parts
separately determine $w$ and $u$. The permutation
$(u,w)\mapsto(a-u,w)$ conjugates the roots; hence $Q_-$
has real coefficients. No root is real because $a$ is odd,
so $2u-a\ne0$ and $\delta>0$. In particular
\[
 \rho(y)>0\quad(y\in\mathbb R),\qquad
 |\chi_-(c'+iy)|^2=\rho(y).
                                                               \tag{SLC2}
\]
The source distribution is literally the original $dm_s$,
the old monic polynomials are the $p_{n,s}$ of NTG3,
and the multiplier is the degree-$D$ polynomial $\rho$.
It has each original $z_{u,w}$ as a root of multiplicity two.
The original Gamma density is strictly positive and has
all polynomial moments; the finite exponential upper bound
NIB11 also proves this directly. Thus all integrals used
here are finite. No finite-interval approximation is needed.

Szeg\H{o}'s displayed old polynomials are orthonormal.
In the present determinant we use the original monic
$p_{n,s}$ instead. This is the exact invertible column
map multiplying its $l$th old column by
$\sqrt{h_{b+l,s}}$, so the whole determinant is multiplied
by $\prod_{l=0}^{D}\sqrt{h_{b+l,s}}$. Every derivative
row is multiplied by the same column factor.
The denominator cofactor changes by its own corresponding
product, and we retain those factors through the original
$h_{n,s}=M_sn!(s/2)_n$ below. The direct proof that follows
also verifies this monic presentation without suppressing
any column or mass factor.

\subsection{The full confluent determinant and its leading cofactor}
For each $b\ge0$ form the $D$ by $D+1$ matrix
\[
\begin{gathered}
 {\cal M}_{b,s}[(\lambda,0),l]=p_{b+l,s}(z_\lambda),\qquad
 {\cal M}_{b,s}[(\lambda,1),l]=p'_{b+l,s}(z_\lambda),\\
 0\le l\le D.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC3}
\]
For each root in the stated order, its value row precedes
its first-derivative row. These are derivatives of order one,
without an inserted factorial or derivative in $S'$.
Define every signed cofactor and the complete polynomial
\[
\begin{gathered}
 C_{b,l}=(-1)^l\det{\cal M}_{b,s}^{\widehat l},\qquad
 \alpha_b=C_{b,D},\\
 \mathcal F_b(y)=
 \det\left[\begin{array}{c}
 (p_{b+l,s}(y))_{l=0}^{D}\\
 {\cal M}_{b,s}
 \end{array}\right]
       =\sum_{l=0}^{D}C_{b,l}p_{b+l,s}(y).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC4}
\]
The superscript $\widehat l$ removes exactly column $l$,
leaving all root and derivative rows in their original order.
The sign is the first-row Laplace sign $(-1)^{1+(l+1)}$.
The determinant block notation in SLC4 means its first row
followed by all $D$ rows of SLC3.

The leading cofactor $\alpha_b$ is nonzero. Since $D=2d$
is even, it is the determinant of columns $0,\ldots,D-1$
of $\mathcal M_{b,s}$. If it vanished, a nonzero coefficient
column would give a nonzero polynomial
$H=\sum_{l=0}^{D-1}v_lp_{b+l,s}$ vanishing with its first
derivative at every $z_\lambda$. Distinct-root division gives
$H=\rho G$ with $\deg G\le b-1$. For $b=0$ degree alone
makes $H=0$, a contradiction. For $b\ge1$, every
$p_{b+l,s}$ is orthogonal in $dm_s$ to every polynomial
of degree below $b$. Taking that polynomial to be $G$
in the conjugate-linear first argument yields
\[
 0=\int_{\mathbb R}\overline{G(y)}H(y)\,dm_s(y)
     =\int_{\mathbb R}\rho(y)|G(y)|^2\,dm_s(y).
                                                               \tag{SLC5}
\]
The strict positivity in SLC2 implies $G=0$: a nonzero
polynomial is nonzero on a nonempty real interval with
positive measure. This contradicts independence of the
distinct-degree monic polynomials in $H$.
This is the complex-coefficient version of the positivity
argument in Szeg\H{o}'s (2.5.4), preserving the repeated
roots and all derivative rows.

At $y=z_\lambda$, the first row of $\mathcal F_b$ repeats its
value row, so $\mathcal F_b(z_\lambda)=0$. Differentiating only
its first row shows that $\mathcal F_b'(z_\lambda)=0$ by the
corresponding derivative row. Thus $\rho\mid \mathcal F_b$.
Its degree is precisely $b+D$, with leading coefficient
$\alpha_b\ne0$, because only the last monic column in
SLC4 has this degree. Hence
\[
                 P_b^-(y)=\frac{\mathcal F_b(y)}{\alpha_b\rho(y)}
                                                               \tag{SLC6}
\]
is a monic polynomial of degree $b$. For every polynomial
$h$ of degree below $b$,
$\int\overline h\,\rho P_b^-\,dm_s
=\alpha_b^{-1}\int\overline h\,\mathcal F_b\,dm_s=0$
by SLC4 and the old orthogonality. Consequently
$P_b^-$ is the unique monic minimizer for the positive
measure $\rho\,dm_s$: adding any smaller-degree polynomial
adds its positive squared norm, by the just proved
orthogonality. Uniqueness and the real density imply that
$P_b^-$ has real coefficients, since coefficientwise
conjugation gives another monic polynomial with the
same orthogonality and norm.

The exact monic coordinate map and its inverse are
\[
 \pi_b^-(S')=i^bP_b^-((S'-c')/i),\qquad
 P_b^-(y)=i^{-b}\pi_b^-(c'+iy).
                                                               \tag{SLC7}
\]
The leading coefficient in $S'$ is one. These inverse
maps carry the same complete monic affine fibre to the
one in NTG8; they multiply values on the original line
by the phase $i^b$ of modulus one. Thus $\pi_b^-$ is
exactly the original NTG8 lower minimizer, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \nu_{b,s}^-=\int\rho(y)|P_b^-(y)|^2\,dm_s(y),\\
 e_b(c'+iy)=
        \frac{i^{d+b}Q_-(y)P_b^-(y)}{\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC8}
\]
Every displayed phase comes from SLC1 and SLC7.

\subsection{The norm, the cofactor sign and every polynomial coefficient}
Both endpoint cofactors in SLC4 are explicit. Since $D$
is even, deleting column zero gives
\[
 C_{b,0}=\det[p_{b+1,s},\ldots,p_{b+D,s}]_{\rm jets}
                    =\alpha_{b+1}.
                                                               \tag{SLC9}
\]
Using $P_b^-=p_{b,s}+$ a polynomial of degree below $b$,
old orthogonality gives
$\int\overline{P_b^-}p_{b,s}\,dm_s=h_{b,s}$.
All terms $p_{b+l,s}$ with $l>0$ are orthogonal to
$P_b^-$ of degree $b$. Multiplying SLC6 by
$\rho\,\overline{P_b^-}$ and integrating therefore yields
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \nu_{b,s}^-
       =h_{b,s}\frac{C_{b,0}}{\alpha_b}
       =M_sb!(s/2)_b\,\frac{\alpha_{b+1}}{\alpha_b}>0 .
 \quad}                                                        \tag{SLC10}
\]
In particular the actual cofactor ratio is positive real;
no absolute value is substituted for a determinant.

The initial cofactor is
\[
       \alpha_0=\prod_{\lambda<\mu}(z_\mu-z_\lambda)^4>0.
                                                               \tag{SLC11}
\]
Here is a direct verification of its sign and value.
The old columns $p_{0,s},\ldots,p_{D-1,s}$ are obtained
from $1,y,\ldots,y^{D-1}$ by a triangular coefficient
matrix with diagonal one, so this changes no determinant.
For each root use the two nearby evaluation rows at
$z_\lambda$ and $z_\lambda+\epsilon_\lambda$.
Subtract the first from the second and divide the latter
by $\epsilon_\lambda$. The ordinary Vandermonde determinant,
divided by $\prod_\lambda\epsilon_\lambda$, tends as the
nonzero $\epsilon_\lambda$ tend to zero to exactly the
value-and-first-derivative determinant in SLC3. Each
cross-root pair contributes four copies of
$z_\mu-z_\lambda$; each within-root factor divided by
$\epsilon_\lambda$ contributes one. This proves the product.
The root conjugation permutation sends the Vandermonde
$\Delta=\prod_{\lambda<\mu}(z_\mu-z_\lambda)$ to
$\overline\Delta=\pm\Delta$. Hence $\Delta$ is real or
purely imaginary, and $\Delta^4>0$ since $\Delta\ne0$.
This proves SLC11. SLC10 now proves $\alpha_b>0$ for
every $b$ by induction. Complex determinant rows and
their original order have been retained throughout.

For a complete coefficient evaluation write
$p_{n,s}(y)=\sum_h p_{n,h}y^h$ and
$\rho(y)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{D}\rho_\ell y^\ell$, with
$\rho_D=1$. The old coefficients follow directly from
the exact NTG3 recurrence, so SLC3 and SLC4 are finite
expressions in the original roots and $s$.
If $\mathcal F_b=\sum_h \mathfrak f_{b,h}y^h$ and
$P_b^-=\sum_{h=0}^{b}P_{b,h}y^h$, then
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathfrak f_{b,h}=\sum_{l=0}^{D}C_{b,l}p_{b+l,h},\quad P_{b,b}=1,\\
 P_{b,h}=\frac{\mathfrak f_{b,D+h}}{\alpha_b}
             -\sum_{r=h+1}^{b}P_{b,r}\rho_{D+h-r}
              \quad(h=b-1,b-2,\ldots,0),\\
 [S'^h]\pi_b^-=
       \sum_{r=h}^{b}i^{b-r}P_{b,r}\binom rh(-c')^{r-h}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC12}
\]
An index outside the degree range of a polynomial has
coefficient zero. The middle equation is downward
division of the already proved identity $\mathcal F_b/\alpha_b
=\rho P_b^-$ at degree $D+h$; the last is binomial
expansion of SLC7. Thus the whole modified polynomial,
including its cross-pairing coefficients, is evaluated.

\subsection{Evaluation of every original relation coefficient}
Keep the actual $g_t=\mathcal T_A(\chi_jS^t)$,
$\ell_t=\mu_v\binom{q+t}{v}$ and $u_t=g_t/\ell_t$,
without changing a single shift or conductor coefficient.
Exact root addition
$\beta_{a,u,w}+\beta_{8,r,h}=\beta_{j,u+r,w+h}$
shows separately in every translation summand that
$g_t$ vanishes at each lower root. The roots are distinct,
so define its exact polynomial quotient
\[
        G_t(S')=g_t(S')/\chi_-(S'),\qquad
        \deg G_t=m+t,\quad\operatorname{lc}G_t=\ell_t .
                                                               \tag{SLC13}
\]
This is polynomial division on the original object, not
division of its norm or deletion of any root.

Here are all coefficients needed for that division.
Let $g_{t,h}=[S'^h]g_t$ be the finite sum NTG4:
$g_{t,h}=\sum_{r+t\ge h}c_r\binom{r+t}{h}\mu_{r+t-h}$,
where $\chi_j=\sum c_rS^r$.
Write $\chi_-=\sum_{h=0}^{d}\chi_hS'^h$,
$G_t=\sum_{h=0}^{B_t}\gamma_{t,h}S'^h$, $B_t=m+t$.
Then
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \gamma_{t,B_t}=\ell_t,\qquad
 \gamma_{t,h}=g_{t,d+h}
       -\sum_{r=h+1}^{B_t}\gamma_{t,r}\chi_{d+h-r}
                   \quad(h=B_t-1,\ldots,0),\\
 G_t(c'+iy)=\sum_{h=0}^{B_t}d_{t,h}y^h,\qquad
 d_{t,h}=i^h\sum_{r=h}^{B_t}
                \gamma_{t,r}\binom rh(c')^{r-h}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC14}
\]
The zero coefficient convention in SLC12 applies here too.
The vanishing remainder follows from the proved divisibility,
and the formulas give its unique quotient and its exact
original-line phase.

Expand this complete quotient in the old Gamma polynomial
basis, with all finite coefficients retained:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 G_t(c'+iy)=\sum_{n=0}^{B_t}\kappa_{n,t}p_{n,s}(y),\\
 \kappa_{n,t}=\frac{1}{h_{n,s}}
       \sum_{h=0}^{B_t}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{n}
              d_{t,h}p_{n,\alpha}M_{h+\alpha,s},
 \qquad M_{r,s}=M_s((J_s^{(r)})^r)_{00}.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC15}
\]
The moments and $J_s^{(r)}$ are exactly NTG5.
The old polynomials have real coefficients, so
$\int p_{n,s}G_t(c'+iy)\,dm_s=h_{n,s}\kappa_{n,t}$.
Old orthogonality proves both this identity and the
finite expansion; it gives zero for $n>B_t$.
In particular $\kappa_{B_t,t}=i^{B_t}\ell_t$ by
the leading coefficient.

The actual NTG relation entry, in its original
conjugate-linear first argument, is now
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 {\cal R}_Q[b,t]=\langle e_b,u_t\rangle_s
   =\frac{i^{-b}}{\ell_t\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}\,\alpha_b}
       \sum_{l=0}^{\min(D,B_t-b)}
           C_{b,l}h_{b+l,s}\kappa_{b+l,t}.
 \quad}                                                        \tag{SLC16}
\]
For $b>B_t$ the sum is empty and the entry is zero.
To prove the formula, use SLC8 and
$g_t(c'+iy)=i^dQ_-(y)G_t(c'+iy)$. Since $Q_-$ and
$P_b^-$ are real on the integration line, this gives
\[
\begin{split}
 \langle e_b,u_t\rangle_s
 &=\frac{i^{-b}}{\ell_t\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}}
           \int \rho(y)P_b^-(y)G_t(c'+iy)\,dm_s(y)\\
 &=\frac{i^{-b}}{\ell_t\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}\alpha_b}
            \int \mathcal F_b(y)G_t(c'+iy)\,dm_s(y).
\end{split}
\]
The factor $i^{-b}$ is the exact product
$i^{-(d+b)}i^d$ of the two original-line phases.
Expanding $\mathcal F_b$ by SLC4 and using SLC15 proves SLC16.
No conjugation of $\ell_t$ or $\kappa_{n,t}$ is permitted:
they belong to the second, complex-linear argument.
All the $2d$ confluent rows and the original leading
cofactor remain in its evaluated numerator.

At $b=B_t=m+t$ only $l=0$ survives; substituting
$\kappa_{B_t,t}=i^{B_t}\ell_t$ and SLC9--10 gives
\[
 {\cal R}_Q[m+t,t]
       =\frac{C_{B_t,0}h_{B_t,s}}
                    {\sqrt{\nu_{B_t,s}^-}\alpha_{B_t}}
       =\sqrt{\nu_{m+t,s}^-}.
                                                               \tag{SLC17}
\]
Together with the empty-sum zero for $b>m+t$,
this recovers the exact positive triangular diagonal
of NTG11 directly from the confluent determinant.
Every entry with $b<m+t$ remains the complete sum
SLC16; scalar norm ratios alone would not give it.

\subsection{The full original NTG graph and analytic frame receive the formula}
For every $Q\ge0$ set, exactly as in NTG10--12,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 {\cal L}_Q[b,t]={\cal R}_Q[b,t]\quad(0\le b<m),\\
 H_Q[a,t]={\cal R}_Q[m+a,t]\quad(0\le a,t\le Q),\qquad
 X_Q={\cal L}_QH_Q^{-1}=[x_0,\ldots,x_Q].
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC18}
\]
Each entry in both blocks is now the actual determinant
and moment sum SLC16. For a direct finite column evaluation,
the same invertible triangular equation is
\[
 x_t=
 \frac{{\cal L}_Q[:,t]-\sum_{a=0}^{t-1}x_aH_Q[a,t]}
                    {\sqrt{\nu_{m+t,s}^-}},
                  \qquad t=0,\ldots,Q .
                                                               \tag{SLC19}
\]
Indeed $X_QH_Q={\cal L}_Q$ and SLC17 give this by
successive columns. The denominator is strictly positive.
This recurrence includes every preceding column and
every earlier/later cross pairing. It is the same
inverse map as NTG12, with an explicit evaluation,
so it proves compatibility of the resulting $X_Q$
as $Q$ increases without any new choice of frame.

The complete lower-basis coefficients used by the original
analytic frame can also be read off, rather than being
reconstructed from its determinant. From SLC12 and NTG8,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 e_{hb}:=[S'^h]e_b
 =\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{b,s}^-}}
   \sum_{r=0}^{d}\chi_r
      \sum_{\substack{0\le h-r\le\ell\le b}}
          i^{b-\ell}P_{b,\ell}
          \binom{\ell}{h-r}(-c')^{\ell-(h-r)},\\
 A[b,c]=\sum_{h=0}^{d+b}e_{hb} f_{h,c}\quad(0\le b<m).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC20}
\]
An empty inner sum is zero. Here the exact original
derivatives $f_{h,c}=F_c^{(h)}(0)$ are evaluated by NTG9,
with all $\mu_r$ and all numerator coefficients in their
original frame. Multiplying $\chi_-\pi_b^-$ and applying
the complex-linear functional proves SLC20 term by term.
Thus the fixed matrix $A$ and every column $x_t$ have
received the same complete Szeg\H{o} polynomial, not only
its squared norm.

It follows by the already proved NTG20,22 that these
explicit entries give exactly
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \Omega_Q=I_m+X_QX_Q^*,\qquad
 D_{L+Q}=A^*\overline{\Omega_Q}A,\\
 \frac{\rho_{L+t}}{\sqrt{1+\zeta_{L+t}}}
           =-i^{-(L+t)}x_t^{\mathsf T}A .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{SLC21}
\]
The common lower-ideal denominator, source order,
fixed period, $\mu_v\binom{q+t}{v}$, every root,
derivative row and native phase have all survived
the map SLC3--21. It consequently evaluates the
input matrices for the complete original eight
flags and CPQ quotient minima in their unchanged
coefficient frames. It changes no endpoint or
weight and assigns no asymptotic coefficient
to the growing determinant.

\section{The simultaneous original tail and its eight coefficient flags}
\label{sec:STF}

The Schur minimum and determinant factorization used below are classical:
see Boyd and Vandenberghe, Appendix A.5.5, p.~650, and Appendix C.4.1,
p.~673 \cite{human:boyd-vandenberghe}. We prove the required complex
Hermitian formulas explicitly. The application is to the unchanged
NIB/IPR rows and the original CPQ flags; no generic subspace of the same
dimension replaces a programme map.

Fix the original simple quartet, its period and logarithm branch, and its
complete conductor. Retain
\[
 j=k+4,\quad q=(j+1)^2,\quad q'=(j-7)^2,\quad
 L=q-v,\quad m=L-q',\quad s\in\{1,j\},\quad c'=j/2-4 .       \tag{STF1}
\]
Here $k=4l+1\ge9$, $v=\operatorname{ord}_0 E_A$ and the full
nonzero derivative $\mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)$ remain those of NIB1.
In particular $m>0$ on its original domain. The complete native basis is
$\phi_n(S')=p_{n,s}((S'-c')/i)/\sqrt{M_sn!(s/2)_n}$, with
$M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}$ and the original Meixner--Pollaczek dictionary
HPS1--4 \cite[eqs.~(18.19.8)--(18.19.9), (18.22.8)]{human:dlmf18}.
For all original lower roots $\beta_{j-8,u,w}$, put
$V_d[n,(u,w)]=\phi_n(\beta_{j-8,u,w})$; for each original analytic
numerator functional $\Lambda_{F_b}$ put
$T_d[n,b]=\Lambda_{F_b}(\phi_n)$, $0\le n\le d$, $1\le b\le m$.
The functional pairing is complex-linear, as in NIB; inner products
are conjugate-linear in their first entry. The original target form is
\[
 D_N=T_{N-v}^*\bigl(I-V_{N-v}(V_{N-v}^*V_{N-v})^{-1}
                         V_{N-v}^*\bigr)T_{N-v},
 \qquad \mathcal D_N=\operatorname{diag}(D_N,4).             \tag{STF2}
\]
All inverses exist on $q-1\le N\le2q$ by the full original rank
proof NIB4. In particular the original masses and phases occur in
every entry of these matrices.

At $N=q+r$, $n=L+r$, let $V_0=V_{n-1}$, $T_0=T_{n-1}$, and
write $v_n,t_n$ for their original next rows. Retain
\[
 \zeta_n=v_n(V_0^*V_0)^{-1}v_n^*,\qquad
 \rho_n=t_n-v_n(V_0^*V_0)^{-1}V_0^*T_0,
 \qquad U_r=\operatorname{diag}
              (\rho_n/\sqrt{1+\zeta_n},4).                \tag{STF3}
\]
IPR1 proves the exact update
$\mathcal D_{q+r}=\mathcal D_{q+r-1}+U_r^*U_r$.
The old lower Gram, conductor and analytic functionals in STF3 are
retained at their actual changing cutoff.

\subsection{Two matrices for the entire original tail}
Choose any original integer $0\le R<q$ and let
$N_0=q+R$, $H=\mathcal D_{N_0}$.
For $t\in\{q-1,q\}$, define the actual stacked rows and relative forms
\[
 A_t=\begin{bmatrix}U_{R+1}\\ \vdots\\ U_t\end{bmatrix},
 \quad C_t=A_tH^{-1/2},\quad M_t=C_t^*C_t,
 \quad \mathcal D_{q+t}=H^{1/2}(I_{4m}+M_t)H^{1/2}.          \tag{STF4}
\]
An empty stack gives $C_t$ with zero rows and $M_t=0$.
Summing STF3 proves STF4, including $t=R=q-1$.
The map $z\mapsto H^{1/2}z$ is an isometry from the original
coefficient space with metric $H$ to Euclidean $\mathbb C^{4m}$,
with inverse $H^{-1/2}$. Formula STF4 records both directions and
the entire original $H$; it does not divide a source measure by its mass.
The matrices $M_t$ have size $4m$, independent of the number of tail rows.

For any fixed original subspace $X$ with independent column frame $X_0$,
set
\[
 Z_X=H^{1/2}X_0(X_0^*HX_0)^{-1/2},\quad
 P_X=Z_XZ_X^*,\quad K_{X,t}=C_tP_XC_t^*,\quad
 F_t(X)=\log\det(I+K_{X,t}).                              \tag{STF5}
\]
For $X=0$ use empty $Z_X$, $P_X=0$ and $F_t(X)=0$.
Since $Z_X^*Z_X=I$, $P_X$ is exactly the orthogonal projector onto
$H^{1/2}X$. Direct multiplication of STF4 gives
\[
 \frac{\det(X_0^*\mathcal D_{q+t}X_0)}{\det(X_0^*HX_0)}
 =\det(I+Z_X^*M_tZ_X)=\det(I+K_{X,t}).                    \tag{STF6}
\]
For the last equality, elimination of either diagonal identity block in
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}I&B\\-A&I\end{smallmatrix}\right]$
proves $\det(I+AB)=\det(I+BA)$ for rectangular $A,B$.
Apply it with $A=C_tZ_X$ and $B=Z_X^*C_t^*$.
Thus every original fixed-frame determinant cancels only in its own
same-frame ratio. No cutoff-dependent frame is substituted.

The nonzero spectrum of $K_{X,t}$ can be calculated in $4m$ dimensions:
\[
             \widetilde K_{X,t}=M_t^{1/2}P_XM_t^{1/2}.     \tag{STF7}
\]
Here is the exact map, including singular $M_t$. On
$\operatorname{im}M_t^{1/2}$ define
$W_t(M_t^{1/2}z)=C_tz$. It is well defined since
$\|C_tz\|^2=z^*M_tz=\|M_t^{1/2}z\|^2$, and is an isometry onto
$\operatorname{im}C_t$. Extend it by zero on $\ker M_t$.
Then $C_t=W_tM_t^{1/2}$, and
$K_{X,t}=W_t\widetilde K_{X,t}W_t^*$.
Both operators vanish on the corresponding complementary kernels;
on their ranges this is a unitary equivalence with inverse $W_t^*$.
It preserves every nonzero eigenvalue with multiplicity. The zero
eigenvalues contribute zero to both functions $\log(1+x)$ and
$\log\max(1,x)$ used below.

\subsection{Each original quotient, with its cross terms}
Let $K\subset Y$ be fixed original coefficient spaces. Choose independent
$X_K$ for $K$ and fixed lifts $X_Q$ of the specified quotient values, so
$[X_K,X_Q]$ frames $Y$. Let $Q_0$ be its exact quotient metric in $H$,
and retain the attained base representative
\[
 Z=X_Q-X_K(X_K^*HX_K)^{-1}X_K^*HX_Q,\quad
 Q_0=Z^*HZ,\quad E=H^{1/2}ZQ_0^{-1/2}.                   \tag{STF8}
\]
For $K=0$ omit its entire term. For $Y=K$ the quotient value space
is zero-dimensional: its determinant is $1$, its log determinant is $0$,
and its inverse and square root are the unique operators on that space.
These conventions also apply to empty frames throughout.
The columns of $E$ are orthonormal,
orthogonal to $H^{1/2}K$, and span its orthogonal complement in
$H^{1/2}Y$. Thus $EE^*=P_Y-P_K$. Let $Z_K$ be an orthonormal frame
for $H^{1/2}K$. Every vector representing quotient value $x$ has
transformed coordinate $EQ_0^{1/2}x+Z_Ka$. Its new quadratic form is
\[
 \|Q_0^{1/2}x\|^2+\|a\|^2+
       \|C_tEQ_0^{1/2}x+C_tZ_Ka\|^2 .                   \tag{STF9}
\]
For any matrix $B$ and vector $y$, differentiation or square completion
gives the unique minimum of $\|a\|^2+\|y+Ba\|^2$ at
$a=-(I+B^*B)^{-1}B^*y$. Its value is
$y^*(I+BB^*)^{-1}y$: multiplication verifies
$I-B(I+B^*B)^{-1}B^*=(I+BB^*)^{-1}$.
Applying this complete minimum to STF9 proves the literal original
quotient form at the later endpoint,
\[
 Q_t=Q_0^{1/2}\left[I+E^*C_t^*(I+C_tP_KC_t^*)^{-1}C_tE\right]Q_0^{1/2}.
                                                               \tag{STF10}
\]
In particular $Q_t\succeq Q_0$ and no kernel direction or cross pairing
has been removed without minimizing over it. The expression inside
STF10 also has the finite coefficient-space realization
\[
 C_t^*(I+C_tP_KC_t^*)^{-1}C_t
 =M_t^{1/2}(I+M_t^{1/2}P_KM_t^{1/2})^{-1}M_t^{1/2}.       \tag{STF11}
\]
To prove it, use the isometry $W_t$ just constructed. On its range the
two inverses are intertwined; on the complementary range both surrounding
$C_t$ factors vanish. This proves STF11 even at rank loss.

Block elimination in the fixed frame $[X_K,X_Q]$ gives
$\det\operatorname{Gram}(Y)=\det\operatorname{Gram}(K)\det Q$.
Taking its ratio at $q+t$ and $N_0$, all frame constants cancel, yielding
\[
 \log\frac{\det Q_t}{\det Q_0}=F_t(Y)-F_t(K)\ge0.        \tag{STF12}
\]
Consequently STF10 evaluates every complementary tail at the same two
matrices $M_{q-1},M_q$, preserving the dependence between the quotients.

\subsection{The eight flags and the exact allocation identity}
Use precisely the CPQ coefficient maps on $\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m}$:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 B=\operatorname{im}B_{\rm cof},\quad
 I=\operatorname{im}(B_{\rm cof}J_0),\quad
 S=\operatorname{im}[B_{\rm cof},F_e],\quad
 J=\ker\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4),\\
 K_i=I\cap J,\quad W_i=I+J,\quad W=B+J,\quad
 K_c=S\cap W,\quad T=S+J,
 \qquad e\in\{0,1\}.                                    \tag{STF13}
 \end{gathered}
\]
Since $B\subset S$, this $T$ is the same $S+W$ as CPQ.
The original inclusions give
$J\subset W_i\subset W\subset T$ and
$K_i\subset I\subset K_c\subset S$.
The actual pairs for the four CPQ complementary forms are
\[
 \begin{array}{c|cccc}
 \text{form}&Q_b^E&Q_{{\rm rem},e}&G_{s,e}&A_{\rm inv}\\ \hline
 (Y,K)&(W,W_i)&(\mathcal U,T)&(S,K_c)&(I,K_i).
 \end{array}                                                   \tag{STF14}
\]
These identifications are the exact same-value CPQ maps, not merely
dimension equalities: $W/J$ identifies with the original image of
$\Phi$, $W_i/J$ with its invariant image; $\mathcal U/T$ is CPQ8's
remaining target quotient; $S/K_c$ is CPQ7's connecting source quotient;
and $I/K_i$ is the source pullback quotient in CPQ9. The corresponding
fixed lift changes are independent of cutoff and therefore cancel in
STF12. All their original value metrics are recovered by STF8--10.

For the logarithm of a restriction determinant in a fixed frame,
$f_X(N)=\log\det(X_0^*\mathcal D_NX_0)$, IPR3 gives exactly
\[
 \mathcal R_{>R}f_X=F_{q-1}(X)+F_q(X).                    \tag{STF15}
\]
Indeed both terms on the right subtract the same $f_X(q+R)$.
It follows that the original signed tail is
\[
\begin{gathered}
\mathcal L_e^{>R}=\sum_{t=q-1,q}\sum_{a=1}^8\sigma_a F_t(X_a),\\
(X_1,\ldots,X_8)=(T,K_c,W_i,K_i,W,S,J,I),\qquad
(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_8)=(1,1,1,1,-1,-1,-1,-1).
\end{gathered}
                                                               \tag{STF16}
\]
The tuple consists of labelled occurrences: coincident subspaces are
retained in every position with its indicated sign. All flag sums below
use this tuple, including when a quotient is zero-dimensional.
Every complementary tail is $\sum_{t=q-1,q}[F_t(Y)-F_t(K)]$
using its exact pair in STF14. The four-copy kernel restriction and
target quotient allocations are
\[
 4S_R^{>R}=\sum_{t=q-1,q}F_t(J),\qquad
 4S_K^{>R}=\sum_{t=q-1,q}[F_t(\mathcal U)-F_t(J)].         \tag{STF17}
\]
The latter is the minimum over the actual full kernel of
$\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4)$. Substitution of STF14 into STF16 cancels
the same terms algebraically, proving
\[
 4S_K^{>R}=\mathcal L_e^{>R}+V_b^{>R}+V_{\rm rem}^{>R}
                        +V_s^{>R}+V_A^{>R},\qquad
 S_K^{>R}+S_R^{>R}=\mathcal T^{>R}.                       \tag{STF18}
\]
Thus the joint calculation supplies every term of IPR7 through common
finite matrices and its exact quotient operations.

\subsection{Finite comparison with the original row-threshold profile}
Define the simultaneous endpoint threshold value
\[
\mathcal B_e^{>R}=\sum_{t=q-1,q}\sum_{a=1}^8\sigma_a
             \sum_b\log\max(1,\lambda_b(K_{X_a,t})),
 \qquad d_t=\operatorname{rank}C_t\le4m.                 \tag{STF19}
\]
All matrices $K_{X,t}$ are supported on the same $d_t$-dimensional
space $\operatorname{im}C_t$. Compress to it by the exact isometry in
STF7. Nested $X$ give ordered projectors and ordered $K_{X,t}$, hence
ordered eigenvalues: for each test subspace every Rayleigh quotient of
the larger Hermitian matrix dominates the smaller one, and successive
minima and maxima preserve that inequality. This proves the required
eigenvalue ordering without assuming a common eigenbasis.

For $x\ge0$, put $h(x)=\log(1+x)-\log\max(1,x)=u(x)-v(x)$,
where
$u(x)=\log(1+\min(x,1))$ and $v(x)=0$ for $x\le1$,
$v(x)=\log(2x/(1+x))$ for $x\ge1$.
Both $u,v$ are increasing from zero and at most $\log2$.
Along any four-element ordered eigenvalue chain, the sum of the first
and last disjoint increments of either function lies in $[0,\log2]$:
adding its omitted middle nonnegative increment proves the upper bound.
The corresponding sum of $h$ increments lies in $[-\log2,\log2]$.
The two chains in STF13 therefore give a difference bounded by
$2\log2$ per eigenvalue. Summing over the common range proves
\[
 |\mathcal L_e^{>R}-\mathcal B_e^{>R}|
             \le2(d_{q-1}+d_q)\log2\le16m\log2.         \tag{STF20}
\]
This is a bound for the simultaneous endpoint representation, whose
eigenvalues differ from the original successive four-row compressions.
The exact map to the latter scalar profile is as follows. At each
original row the same two-chain argument uses four eigenvalues, hence
its signed logarithmic and threshold increments differ by at most
$8\log2$. The tail weights sum to $2(q-R)-1$, so the original
row-threshold tail $\mathcal P_e^{>R}$ satisfies
\[
 |\mathcal P_e^{>R}-\mathcal B_e^{>R}|
 \le\bigl[8\{2(q-R)-1\}+2(d_{q-1}+d_q)\bigr]\log2.      \tag{STF21}
\]
This proves the relation between the two threshold constructions with
their precise finite discrepancy; it asserts no equality of individual
row and endpoint eigenvalues.

For a positive semidefinite finite matrix let
$n_X(t,a)=\#\{\lambda_b(K_{X,t})>a\}$, counting multiplicity.
The elementary identity
$\log\max(1,\lambda)=\int_1^\infty\mathbf1_{a<\lambda}\,da/a$
and the finite eigenvalue sum give the exact remaining integral
\[
 \mathcal B_e^{>R}
   =\sum_{t=q-1,q}\int_1^\infty
                    \sum_{b=1}^8\sigma_b n_{X_b}(t,a)\,\frac{da}{a}. \tag{STF22}
\]
No limiting interchange is needed; each integrand vanishes beyond
the largest eigenvalue of its finite matrices. It is the signed
spectral count of the original correlated flag compressions.

Finally choose only the already-proved original prefix
$R=R_j=\lfloor j^{3/2}/\log j\rfloor<q$.
NIB19--20 and IPR8 bound the removed signed and threshold prefixes
by $4\mathfrak E_{s,R_j}=o(kq_k)$. Since $m=16j-48-v$ and
$q=(j+1)^2$, STF20--21 are respectively $O(j)$ and $O(j^2)$.
Thus the full signed and original threshold returns each differ
from the exact finite expression STF22 by $o(kq_k)$, with explicit
finite errors given by the prefix budget and STF20--21. This evaluates
the complete source-to-expression map and its error; the growing-degree
value of STF22 itself is still to be calculated. Both source orders,
both primitive indices, the independent proper source and the full
action retain their existing domains and receiving identities.
\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:SIMULTANEOUS_TAIL_FLAGS:end}
% END ADDITIVE MIXED TAIL INTEGRATION SIMULTANEOUS_TAIL_FLAGS.tex

\section{The original relation graph supplies the simultaneous mixed flags}
\label{sec:NFI}

This calculation joins the original-data relation formulas NTG4--22 to
the simultaneous restriction and quotient formulas STF4--22. The finite
Hermitian operations are proved below; the Schur quotient operations
being transported are those of Boyd and Vandenberghe,
Appendix A.5.5, p.~650, and C.4.1, p.~673
\cite{human:boyd-vandenberghe}, with their complex specialization
proved in STF8--12. Every scalar polynomial and norm retains the
Meixner--Pollaczek parameter and mass dictionary of NTG3
\cite[eqs.~(18.19.8)--(18.19.9), (18.22.8)]{human:dlmf18}.

Use NTG's original $A$, $x_r$ and $\Omega_R$, and choose an integer
$0\le R<q$. The symbol $D^{\rm nat}_n$ in this section denotes the
matrix called $D_n$ in NTG18. STF2's target-cutoff convention is
$D_N=D^{\rm nat}_{N-v}$; therefore $N=q+r$ corresponds to $n=L+r$.
Define, with the unique positive Hermitian square roots,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 G=\overline{\Omega_R},\qquad H_1=A^*GA=D_{q+R},\qquad
 H=\operatorname{diag}(H_1,4),\\
 U=G^{1/2}AH_1^{-1/2},\quad U_4=\operatorname{diag}(U,4).
\end{gathered}
                                                               \tag{NFI1}
\]
All matrices in the inverses are positive definite by NTG12,16,20.
Direct multiplication gives $U^*U=I_m$. It is square and injective,
so its inverse is $U^*$ and $UU^*=I_m$ as well. In particular
$U_4$ is a unitary map between the two stated Euclidean coordinate
spaces, not an alteration of the original source measure.

For $R\le t\le q$ set $d=t-R$ and retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 Y_t=[x_{R+1},\ldots,x_t],\qquad
 Z_t=G^{-1/2}\overline{Y_t},\\
 B_t=Z_t^*U=Y_t^{\mathsf T}AH_1^{-1/2},\qquad
 \Theta_t=\operatorname{diag}_{r=R+1}^{t}(-i^{-(L+r)}).
\end{gathered}
                                                               \tag{NFI2}
\]
Here $Y_t,Z_t$ are $m$ by $d$, $B_t$ is $d$ by $m$, and
$\Theta_t$ is unitary of size $d$. For $d=0$ each displayed
rectangular matrix is the corresponding empty map. The equality
for $B_t$ follows from $(G^{-1/2})^*=G^{-1/2}$ and the formula
for $U$; it keeps the ordinary transpose required by the original
complex-linear functionals.

Extend STF4's endpoint notation to every $R\le t\le q$ by
$C_t=[U_{R+1}^{\mathsf T},\ldots,U_t^{\mathsf T}]^{\mathsf T}H^{-1/2}$,
where the bracket means the vertical stack of the original row-block
matrices $U_r$, and use the empty stack for $t=R$.
It agrees with STF4 at its two stated endpoints.
These four-copy original row updates are stacked by degree. Write their
row coordinates as $(r,b)$, with $R+1\le r\le t$ and $1\le b\le4$,
and let $\Pi_t$ be the permutation unitary sending coordinate
$(r,b)$ to $(b,r)$. Thus it changes only the stated row ordering.
Set
\[
 \mathscr R_t=\operatorname{diag}(\Theta_t^*,4)\Pi_t,\qquad
 \mathscr Z_t=\operatorname{diag}(Z_t,4).
                                                               \tag{NFI3}
\]
NTG22 gives the full original row
$\rho_{L+r}/\sqrt{1+\zeta_{L+r}}=-i^{-(L+r)}x_r^{\mathsf T}A$.
Multiplying on the right by $H_1^{-1/2}$, stacking and using the
defined permutation proves, entry by entry,
\[
 \Pi_t C_t=\operatorname{diag}(\Theta_tB_t,4),\qquad
 \boxed{\mathscr R_t C_t=\mathscr Z_t^*U_4.}
                                                               \tag{NFI4}
\]
The inverse row map is $\mathscr R_t^*$. Thus the original native
phase has been recorded in an explicit invertible map before taking
any Gram matrix. No phase is inferred merely from equality of norms.

Taking the Gram of NFI4 gives the exact coefficient-space relation
\[
 \mathscr N_t=\mathscr Z_t\mathscr Z_t^*
       =\operatorname{diag}(G^{-1/2}\overline{Y_t}Y_t^{\mathsf T}
                                      G^{-1/2},4),\qquad
 M_t=U_4^*\mathscr N_tU_4.
                                                               \tag{NFI5}
\]
This is an equality in the original STF coordinates after the
specified unitary map. For example,
\[
 \det(I_{4m}+M_t)
       =\left(\frac{\det\Omega_t}{\det\Omega_R}\right)^4.
                                                               \tag{NFI6}
\]
To prove NFI6, $\Omega_t=\Omega_R+Y_tY_t^*$, so conjugating
by $\Omega_R^{-1/2}$ gives
$\det(I+\Omega_R^{-1/2}Y_tY_t^*\Omega_R^{-1/2})
=\det\Omega_t/\det\Omega_R$.
Complex conjugation gives the determinant of the single block of
$I+\mathscr N_t$; both determinants are positive real numbers.
There are four blocks. This also proves the empty-tail case, with
ratio $1$. It is consistent with both allocations in STF17--18,
without selecting either allocation.

For every original coefficient subspace $X$, including those in the
labelled eight-flag tuple STF16, retain its fixed independent frame $X_0$
and put
\[
 \widehat Z_X=
  \operatorname{diag}(G^{1/2}A,4)X_0(X_0^*HX_0)^{-1/2},
 \qquad \widehat P_X=\widehat Z_X\widehat Z_X^*.
                                                               \tag{NFI7}
\]
If $X=0$ use the empty frame. NFI1 and STF5 give
$\widehat Z_X=U_4Z_X$, hence
$\widehat Z_X^*\widehat Z_X=I$ and
$\widehat P_X=U_4P_XU_4^*$. This supplies the exact transported
flag with its full original conductor-dependent coefficient matrix
$A$. Its inverse is $U_4^*$ on the transformed Euclidean space,
followed by $H^{-1/2}$ when recovering original coefficient vectors.
Consequently NFI4 proves
\[
 \mathscr R_tK_{X,t}\mathscr R_t^*
       =\mathscr Z_t^*\widehat P_X\mathscr Z_t,
 \qquad
 F_t(X)=\log\det(I+\mathscr Z_t^*\widehat P_X\mathscr Z_t).
                                                               \tag{NFI8}
\]
The determinant equality uses a unitary conjugation of the entire
row space, including its zero eigenvalues. Its version on the fixed
$4m$-dimensional coefficient space is
\[
 \widehat K_{X,t}=\mathscr N_t^{1/2}\widehat P_X
                                  \mathscr N_t^{1/2}.
                                                               \tag{NFI9}
\]
For completeness define the map
$\mathcal W_t(\mathscr N_t^{1/2}z)=\mathscr Z_t^*z$ on
$\operatorname{im}\mathscr N_t^{1/2}$, extending it by zero on
the kernel. It is well defined and isometric because
$\|\mathscr Z_t^*z\|^2=z^*\mathscr N_tz
=\|\mathscr N_t^{1/2}z\|^2$.
Then the right side of NFI8 equals
$\mathcal W_t\widehat K_{X,t}\mathcal W_t^*$.
The inverse on the image is $\mathcal W_t^*$; its complementary
kernel contributes only zero eigenvalues. This proves the precise
spectral correspondence used by both logarithmic functions in STF,
including rank loss and coincident flags.

There is a corresponding formula for every original quotient
$K\subset Y$. Retain $Q_0$ and $E$ from STF8 and set
$\widehat E=U_4E$; then
$\widehat E\widehat E^*=\widehat P_Y-\widehat P_K$.
Substituting NFI4 into the complete attained minimum STF10 gives
\[
 Q_t=Q_0^{1/2}\left[I+
 \widehat E^*\mathscr Z_t
 (I+\mathscr Z_t^*\widehat P_K\mathscr Z_t)^{-1}
 \mathscr Z_t^*\widehat E\right]Q_0^{1/2}.
                                                               \tag{NFI10}
\]
Indeed the middle inverse is carried into STF10's inverse by the
unitary $\mathscr R_t$, and its two adjacent factors carry
$E$ into $\widehat E$. This is an equality of matrices on the
original quotient value space, with its original $Q_0$ on both
sides. All cross terms survive the attained minimum. It applies
to the four actual pairs in STF14 and to the target quotient
$(\mathcal U,J)$; the empty quotient conventions remain those
of STF8. Thus it transfers every complement and both allocations,
rather than just their sum of determinants.

Finally use the eight labelled occurrences $(X_a,\sigma_a)$ of
STF16, retaining repeated subspaces separately. The remaining
signed count in STF22 is now the fully original-data expression
\[
 \mathcal B_e^{>R}
  =\sum_{t=q-1,q}\int_1^\infty
       \sum_{a=1}^8\sigma_a
       \#\{\lambda_b(\widehat K_{X_a,t})>u\}
       \,\frac{du}{u}.
                                                               \tag{NFI11}
\]
All eigenvalues count multiplicities. The matrices in NFI11 are
obtained from the finite coefficients NTG4--12, the original
analytic recursion NTG9, and the actual CPQ frames in NFI7.
The equality follows from the proved unitary and partial-isometry
maps, not from a comparison of dimensions. With the accepted prefix
$R_j$, the finite discrepancy bounds STF20--21 and its prefix
budget apply without change. NFI11 gives the full coefficient
formula for the growing-degree quantity; it does not assert a
large-degree estimate for that quantity.
\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:NATIVE_GRAPH_FLAG_INTERTWINER:end}
% END ADDITIVE MIXED TAIL INTEGRATION NATIVE_GRAPH_FLAG_INTERTWINER.tex

\section{Propagation through the original receiving maps}

% EXACT INPUT SHA256 cb9b3c53968a5125775646fe4017fc07edcc122b113ff975d404485745d53507
\label{MTBUILD:CPQ_SIMULTANEOUS_RECEIVER:start}
\paragraph{The full original quotient metrics at the common tail base.}
The new simultaneous formula in STF8--12 evaluates these same CPQ
affine value fibres after all preceding native rows have been retained.
The exact pairs are $(W,W_i)$, $(\mathcal U,T)$, $(S,K_c)$ and
$(I,K_i)$, with the original value coordinates and lift factors
preserved as in STF14. NFI10 transports each attained matrix through
the full original analytic frame $A$ and relation matrix $X_t$,
including every cross term. The Schur minimum is the classical
operation of Boyd and Vandenberghe, Appendix A.5.5, p.~650
\cite{human:boyd-vandenberghe}; STF9--12 proves its exact complex
Hermitian specialization on these fibres. The eight signed terms
are a labelled tuple, so opposite labels are retained even when
their subspaces coincide. Substitution in the original CPQ identity
gives STF18 with both allocations and all four complements. A fixed
lift determinant cancels only in its own same-frame endpoint ratio;
no absolute frame factor or proper-source comparison is set to one.

\label{MTBUILD:CPQ_SIMULTANEOUS_RECEIVER:end}
% END ADDITIVE MIXED TAIL INTEGRATION CPQ_SIMULTANEOUS_RECEIVER.tex

\label{MTBUILD:NIB_TAIL_RECEIVER:start}
\paragraph{Continuation of the original prefix and its full relation tail.}
The row estimate NIB14 and the proved prefix NIB18--20 now receive the
complete original relation calculation in Sections~\ref{sec:NTG},
\ref{sec:SLC} and \ref{sec:NFI}. In the native-cutoff convention
$n=L+t$, the literal analytic Gram is
$D_n^{\rm nat}=A^*\overline{\Omega_t}A$ and its new row is
$\rho_n/\sqrt{1+\zeta_n}=-i^{-n}x_t^{\mathsf T}A$.
NTG16--22 proves these equalities in the original functional frame;
SLC3--20 calculates every entry from the full original root and
conductor data using Szeg\H{o}'s confluent modification formula
\cite[Theorem~2.5, pp.~29--30]{human:szego1975}.
For the retained prefix $R=R_j$, the earlier scalar tail budget can
use the minimum of all three
proved bounds in NTG31, with its retained cross-pair correction, and
the full weighted tail is exactly
$\log\det\Omega_{q-1}+\log\det\Omega_q-2\log\det\Omega_R$.
The proof is NTG14--15 followed by the unchanged endpoint weights,
as displayed in NTG32. The earlier prefix estimate remains valid;
this new formula supplies the complete surviving correlated tail,
whose growing-degree value remains the next calculation.

\label{MTBUILD:NIB_TAIL_RECEIVER:end}

\label{MTBUILD:IPR_SIMULTANEOUS_RECEIVER:start}
\paragraph{Simultaneous evaluation at the two original tail endpoints.}
Section~\ref{sec:STF} now evaluates every occurrence in IPR3--7 using
the same base $N_0=q+R$ and the two actual matrices $M_{q-1},M_q$
of size $4m$. For a fixed original space $X$, its exact restriction
return is $F_{q-1}(X)+F_q(X)$ by STF6,15. For any one of the four
CPQ quotient pairs, the attained value matrix is STF10 and its return
is the difference of those same restriction returns by STF12.
This proves the formulas for both allocations and all complementary
returns in STF17--18 without changing IPR's weights or source orders.
The original signed tail differs from the simultaneous signed spectral
count by at most $2(d_{q-1}+d_q)\log2\le16m\log2$; its original
successive-row threshold version has the additional explicit error
$8\{2(q-R)-1\}\log2$, as proved in STF20--22.
NFI1--11 gives the exact phase, permutation and unitary maps from
the original relation coefficients to these flag matrices. At the
already proved $R_j$, both discrepancies and the removed prefix are
$o(kq_k)$. This does not assign a value to the remaining signed
count, and it leaves the independent proper source and complete
action at their original receiving identities.

% BEGIN PRIMITIVE BAND COMPLETE PROVIDER SGT SHA256 3635ec5d81cfad8cd3538505b3c33a8ddfe31cfee77be37d30a430b80d12ceb5
