\begingroup
\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}
\providecommand{\rank}{\operatorname{rank}}
% Complete original polynomial translation, finite Toeplitz poles, and small-rank volume.
\section{The original finite Gamma translation on every small-rank plane}
\label{sec:SGT}

Fix the original simple quartet, its actual five-orbit period and logarithm
branch. Keep every coefficient of the original nonreduced conductor:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0<\delta<\tfrac12,\quad\gamma>2,\qquad
 \beta_*=\tfrac12-\delta-i\gamma,\qquad
 \beta_{n,a,b}=n\beta_*+2a\delta+2bi\gamma,\\
 E_A(z)=\sum_{a,b=0}^8a_{ab}e^{\beta_{8,a,b}z},\qquad
 \mu_m=E_A^{(m)}(0),\qquad
 v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A,\quad0\le v\le80,\quad\mu_v\ne0,\\
 k=4l+1\ge9,\qquad s\in\{1,k\},\qquad
 c_0=k/2,\quad c_1=k/2-4 .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT1}
\]
The coefficients, symbol, order and first nonzero moment are fixed as
$k$ varies. No particular coefficient is assumed nonzero except through
the stated exact order. Every result below is for this original operator
and these two original source orders. An arbitrary integer $N\ge0$ is
allowed in the finite statements.

\subsection{Full Gamma measure, recurrence and coefficient isometry}

Write $b=s/2>0$ and retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\qquad
 dm_s(y)=\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
               |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,\\
 \|P\|_{s,c}^2=\int_{\mathbb R}|P(c+iy)|^2dm_s(y),\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+b)p_{n-1,s}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT2}
\]
These are exactly the measure and recurrence in PDS6 and DNE7.
For completeness their generating and norm identities are derived here.
The recurrence, multiplied by $t^n/n!$ and summed, gives
$(1+t^2)\partial_t F=(y-bt)F$, $F(y,0)=1$. The unique formal solution,
also holomorphic for $|t|<1$ at fixed $y$, is
\[
 F(y,t)=\sum_{n\ge0}p_{n,s}(y)\frac{t^n}{n!}
       =(1+t^2)^{-b/2}\exp(y\arctan t).
 \tag{SGT3}
\]
Both branches take their value zero or one at $t=0$ as appropriate.

Here is a direct check of the full mass and norms. With $a=s/4$,
the Euler gamma integrals and the change of variables
$r=u+v_0$, $x=\log(u/v_0)$ in their product give
\[
 |\Gamma(a+i\xi)|^2
 =\Gamma(2a)\int_{\mathbb R}e^{i\xi x}
                    (2\cosh(x/2))^{-2a}\,dx .
\]
The integrand as a function of $x$ is analytic for $|\Im x|<\pi$.
Shifting the integration line by any fixed amount of modulus less than
$\pi$ is justified by its exponential decay at both real ends.
It bounds this Fourier transform by a constant times
$e^{-h|\xi|}$ for each $0<h<\pi$. Fourier inversion is therefore
absolutely convergent; it may also be obtained by inserting a Gaussian
factor and passing to the limit by dominated convergence. Substituting
$y=2\xi$ and the exact multiplier in SGT2 gives
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{iuy}dm_s(y)=M_s(\cosh u)^{-b},
 \qquad
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{uy}dm_s(y)=M_s(\cos u)^{-b}
 \quad(|u|<\pi/2).
 \tag{SGT4}
\]
For the second equality, the preceding exponential bounds give analytic
continuation to the indicated strip; equality on the real Fourier axis
then proves it there. In particular the mass is exactly $M_s$.
For sufficiently small real $t,z$, SGT3--4 and
$\cos(\arctan t+\arctan z)
 =(1-tz)/\sqrt{(1+t^2)(1+z^2)}$ imply
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}F(y,t)F(y,z)dm_s(y)=M_s(1-tz)^{-b}.
\]
The exponential moment bound justifies differentiating under the
integral in a neighborhood of $(0,0)$. Comparing coefficients proves
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \int_{\mathbb R}p_{n,s}(y)p_{m,s}(y)dm_s(y)
       =\mathbf1_{n=m}h_{n,s},\qquad
 h_{n,s}=M_sn!(b)_n,\\
 \phi_{n,s,c}(S)=
       \frac{p_{n,s}((S-c)/i)}{\sqrt{h_{n,s}}},\qquad
 \rho_n=\sqrt{\frac{n!}{(b)_n}}>0 .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT5}
\]
The $p_{n,s}$ are real monic polynomials by their recurrence. Thus these
$\phi_n$ are orthonormal for the complex Hilbert norm in SGT2 and span
each indicated polynomial degree range. Their leading coefficient in
$S$ is $i^{-n}/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$, not $1/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$.

\subsection{The original quotient map and its exact Toeplitz matrix}

Let $\mathcal P_m=\mathbb C[S]_{\le m}$, with
$\mathcal P_{-1}=0$. The original translation is
\[
 (\mathcal T_AP)(S')=\sum_{a,b=0}^8
                 a_{ab}P(S'+\beta_{8,a,b}),\qquad
 \mathcal T_AS^m=
       \sum_{j=0}^m\binom mj\mu_jS'^{\,m-j}.
 \tag{SGT6}
\]
The second equality is the binomial theorem followed by the exact
definition of every moment. If $m\ge v$ and $P$ has leading coefficient
$p_m\ne0$, then $\mathcal T_AP$ has degree $m-v$ and leading coefficient
$p_m\binom mv\mu_v\ne0$. If $m<v$ its image is zero.
Consequently the following map is an isomorphism, with an onto
representative map and the displayed full kernel:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \ker(\mathcal T_A:\mathcal P_{N+v}\longrightarrow\mathcal P_N)
       =\mathcal P_{v-1},\\
 A_{N,s}:\bigl(\mathcal P_{N+v}/\mathcal P_{v-1},
                       \|\cdot\|_{s,c_0,\mathrm{quot}}\bigr)
       \xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }
                (\mathbb C[S']_{\le N},\|\cdot\|_{s,c_1}),\\
 \|[P]\|_{s,c_0,\mathrm{quot}}^2
       =\min_{L\in\mathcal P_{v-1}}\|P+L\|_{s,c_0}^2 .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT7}
\]
Surjectivity follows successively by degree from the nonzero diagonal
coefficients in SGT6. The orthogonal representative of this quotient
is precisely the span of $\phi_{v,s,c_0},\ldots,\phi_{N+v,s,c_0}$.
It is not a quotient by an omitted arithmetic relation.

On the entire open unit disk put
\[
 w(t)=-i\arctan t,\qquad
 g(t)=t^{-v}e^{-4w(t)}E_A(w(t)),\qquad
 g(0)=(-i)^v\mu_v/v!\ne0 .
 \tag{SGT8}
\]
The value at zero is removable by the exact order $v$.
For $|t|<1$, $|w(t)|\le\operatorname{arctanh}|t|$ by the
arctangent series. This proves holomorphy of the specified $g$.
Applying the finite sum SGT6 to SGT3, with its polynomial argument,
gives exactly
\[
\begin{split}
 \sum_{m\ge0}(\mathcal T_Ap_{m,s}((S-c_0)/i))(S')
                   \frac{t^m}{m!}
 &= (1+t^2)^{-b/2}e^{(S'-c_0)w(t)}E_A(w(t))\\
 &=t^vg(t)(1+t^2)^{-b/2}e^{(S'-c_1)w(t)} .
\end{split}\tag{SGT9}
\]
The center factor is $e^{-4w}$ because $c_0-c_1=4$.
No inverse of $\mathcal T_A$, or scalar $\alpha_A$, has been inserted
in this identity.

For a holomorphic germ $u(t)=\sum_{j\ge0}u_jt^j$ define
$T_N(u)_{r,n}=u_{n-r}$ for $0\le r\le n\le N$, and zero for $r>n$.
Coefficients of SGT9 and $n!/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}=\rho_n/\sqrt{M_s}$
give the complete matrix of SGT7 in its two orthonormal frames:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_{N,s}=D_{\mathrm{out}}^{-1}T_N(g)D_{\mathrm{in}},\\
 D_{\mathrm{out}}=\operatorname{diag}(\rho_0,\ldots,\rho_N),
 \qquad
 D_{\mathrm{in}}=\operatorname{diag}(\rho_v,\ldots,\rho_{N+v}),\\
 (A_{N,s})_{r,n}=
 \begin{cases}
 g_{n-r}\rho_{n+v}/\rho_r,&r\le n,\\0,&r>n .
 \end{cases}
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT10}
\]
Each source and target frame contains $1/\sqrt{M_s}$. Those identical
factors cancel only in this coefficient matrix, since $s$ is unchanged.
All actual squared norms still use the full SGT2 measure.
In particular, for $N=0$ the matrix is the literal scalar
$(-i)^v\mu_v\rho_v/v!$.

\subsection{A proved original-data zero-free disk}

The following constants use every original coefficient and shift:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_{\mathrm{abs}}=\sum_{a,b}|a_{ab}|,\qquad
 B=\max\{1,\max_{a,b}|\beta_{8,a,b}|\},\qquad
 C_v=\frac{A_{\mathrm{abs}}B^v}{|\mu_v|}\ge1,\\
 R_0=\frac1B\min\{1,(v+1)/(2\exp(1)C_v)\},\qquad
 a_0=\min\{1/2,\tanh(R_0/2)\},\\
 d_0=1-\frac{a_0^2}{3(1-a_0^2)}\ge8/9,\qquad
 M_0=\frac{2v!}{|\mu_v|}\,d_0^{-v}e^{2R_0}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT11}
\]
All are finite, $R_0,a_0,d_0,M_0$ are positive, and $a_0<1$.
Indeed $|\mu_v|\le A_{\mathrm{abs}}B^v$ proves the stated bound
on $C_v$. Define the entire removable quotient
$B_\infty(z)=v!E_A(z)/(\mu_vz^v)$, with value one at zero.
Its full Taylor remainder is bounded by
\[
 |B_\infty(z)-1|
 \le\frac{C_vB|z|}{v+1}e^{B|z|}
 \le\tfrac12\quad(|z|\le R_0).
 \tag{SGT12}
\]
For the first inequality expand each exponential, use
$|\mu_m|\le A_{\mathrm{abs}}B^m$ and
$v!/(v+1+j)!\le1/((v+1)j!)$, and sum the exponential series.
The two inequalities $BR_0\le1$ and
$C_vBR_0/(v+1)\le1/(2\exp(1))$ prove the second.
This is the complete RDS11 disk proof for the unchanged symbol.

On $|t|\le a_0$ we have $|w(t)|\le R_0/2$ and, also at zero,
\[
 \left|\frac{\arctan t}{t}-1\right|
 \le\sum_{j\ge1}\frac{a_0^{2j}}{2j+1}
 \le\frac{a_0^2}{3(1-a_0^2)}.
\]
Thus $|w(t)/t|\ge d_0$. Factoring SGT8 as
$g(t)=(\mu_v/v!)(w(t)/t)^ve^{-4w(t)}B_\infty(w(t))$
shows that it has no zero on that entire closed disk and proves
\[
 |g(t)|\ge \frac{|\mu_v|}{2v!}d_0^ve^{-2R_0},
 \qquad |1/g(t)|\le M_0 \quad(|t|\le a_0).
 \tag{SGT13}
\]
This supplies the zero-free disk and the inverse bound from the
actual finite symbol rather than assuming them.

\subsection{Every actual pole and its finite-rank Toeplitz contribution}

Call $0<a<1$ admissible when $g$ has no zero on $|t|=a$.
Zeros of a nonzero holomorphic function are isolated; therefore
there are only finitely many in each compact subdisk. There are
admissible radii arbitrarily close to one. For a fixed such $a$,
let $\mathcal Z_a$ be the exact zeros $\zeta$ of $g$ with $|\zeta|<a$,
and let $m_\zeta$ be their orders. Zero is not among them.
Retain every multiplicity and define the actual Laurent coefficients
\[
\begin{gathered}
 H_a=\sum_{\zeta\in\mathcal Z_a}m_\zeta,\qquad
 A_{\zeta,h}
 =\frac1{(m_\zeta-h)!}
  \left.\frac{d^{m_\zeta-h}}{dt^{m_\zeta-h}}
           \left(\frac{(t-\zeta)^{m_\zeta}}{g(t)}\right)
       \right|_{t=\zeta},\quad1\le h\le m_\zeta,\\
 f=1/g,\qquad
 f_a(t)=f(t)-\sum_{\zeta\in\mathcal Z_a}
                      \sum_{h=1}^{m_\zeta}\frac{A_{\zeta,h}}{(t-\zeta)^h}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT14}
\]
The factors $t^{-v}$ and $e^{-4w}$ in $g$ create no nonzero zero.
Moreover $w'(t)=-i/(1+t^2)\ne0$ and
$e^{-2w(t)}=(1+it)/(1-it)$ proves injectivity of $w$ on the disk.
Thus these poles are precisely the symbol zeros already identified in
PEC3--5, under this exact change of variable, with their same orders.
The extra center factor is fully retained in every $A_{\zeta,h}$.
The function $f_a$ is holomorphic on a neighborhood of the closed
$a$ disk, since all its interior principal parts have been subtracted
and no boundary zero exists.

Here are completely specified finite constants on that circle:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 W_a=\operatorname{arctanh}a,\quad
 m_{E,a}=\min_{|t|=a}|E_A(w(t))|>0,\quad
 G_a=A_{\mathrm{abs}}a^{-v}e^{(B+4)W_a},\\
 M_a=\frac{a^ve^{4W_a}}{m_{E,a}}
       +\sum_{\zeta,h}\frac{|A_{\zeta,h}|}{(a-|\zeta|)^h},
 \qquad
 P_a=\sum_{\zeta,h}\frac{|A_{\zeta,h}|}{(1-|\zeta|)^h},\\
 S_N(a)=\sum_{j=0}^Na^{-j}
       =a^{-N}\frac{1-a^{N+1}}{1-a}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT15}
\]
Empty pole sums are zero. The minimum is the same actual finite-circle
quantity used in PEC4, and is positive by continuity and admissibility.
It is not a new uniform zero-free assertion. The triangle inequality on
that circle gives $|g|\le G_a$ and $|f_a|\le M_a$.
Every constant in SGT15 is defined by the original fixed function and
its exact finitely many interior poles; none depends on $k,N$ or $s$.

For one term $A/(t-\zeta)^h$, its Taylor coefficients at zero are
\[
 c_n=A(-1)^h\zeta^{-(n+h)}
             \binom{n+h-1}{h-1}\quad(n\ge0).
 \tag{SGT16}
\]
Extend the binomial as the polynomial
$\binom{x+h-1}{h-1}=\prod_{\ell=1}^{h-1}(x+\ell)/(h-1)!$
to every integer $x$, also for $h=1$ by the empty product.
The full matrix $C_{i,j}=c_{j-i}$, $0\le i,j\le N$, has rank at most
$h$: after multiplying rows by $\zeta^{-i}$ and columns by $\zeta^j$,
its entries are a polynomial of total degree at most $h-1$ in $j-i$.
Expanding that polynomial in powers of $i$ gives at most $h$ column
vectors, independently of $N$. Equivalently its column space is
contained in $\operatorname{span}\{(\zeta^ii^d)_i:0\le d<h\}$.
For all terms belonging to one pole, these nested spaces give combined
rank at most $m_\zeta$, rather than the sum of the integers $h$.

For a lower-diagonal gap $\ell=i-j\ge1$, direct substitution in
the polynomial binomial gives
\[
 c_{-\ell}=
 \begin{cases}
 0,&1\le\ell<h,\\
 -A\zeta^{\ell-h}\binom{\ell-1}{h-1},&\ell\ge h .
 \end{cases}\tag{SGT17}
\]
For $\ell\ge h$ each factor in the binomial is negative, and their
$(-1)^{h-1}$ sign combines with $(-1)^h$ to give the displayed
minus sign. For $1\le\ell<h$ one factor is zero.
Consequently the upper triangular Taylor matrix of this pole is
$C+R$, where $R$ is strictly lower triangular and its gap-$\ell$
coefficient is $A\zeta^{\ell-h}\binom{\ell-1}{h-1}$ for $\ell\ge h$.
If $J$ is the upper shift with $J_{i,i+1}=1$, then
$(J^*)^\ell$ has norm at most one. The absolutely convergent binomial
series therefore proves
\[
 \|R\|\le |A|\sum_{\ell=h}^{\infty}
                 |\zeta|^{\ell-h}\binom{\ell-1}{h-1}
          =\frac{|A|}{(1-|\zeta|)^h}.
 \tag{SGT18}
\]
The series identity follows by differentiating the geometric series
$h-1$ times, or multiplying $h$ copies of it and collecting coefficients.
All powers and signs are those of the original pole; no principal
part has been deleted from the actual Toeplitz inverse.

Since $T_N(u)=\sum_{j=0}^Nu_jJ^j$, multiplication of these finite
matrices is truncated power-series multiplication. In particular
$T_N(g)^{-1}=T_N(f)$, because $g(0)\ne0$ and $gf=1$ as germs.
Cauchy's coefficient estimate, SGT13 and SGT15--18 prove
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \|T_N(g)\|\le F_{a,N}:=G_aS_N(a),\\
 \|T_N(f)\|\le G_{0,N}:=M_0S_N(a_0),\\
 T_N(f)=C_{a,N}+R_{a,N},\quad
 \operatorname{rank}C_{a,N}\le H_a,\quad
 \|R_{a,N}\|\le V_{a,N}:=M_aS_N(a)+P_a.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT19}
\]
Indeed sum the coefficient estimates times $\|J^j\|\le1$ for the
analytic terms, and sum the exact pole ranks and lower-triangular
remainders just proved. The total inverse matrix is unchanged.

\subsection{Finite exterior bounds, including the expensive directions}

Write $d=N+1$ and arrange singular values in decreasing order.
If a matrix $C$ has rank at most $H$, then $\ker C$ has codimension
at most $H$. For $x\in\ker C$, $\|(C+R)x\|\le\|R\|\|x\|$.
The variational description of singular values therefore gives
$\sigma_j(C+R)\le\|R\|$ for $j>H$. To verify this description
directly, diagonalize $(C+R)^*(C+R)$: the complement of its first
$j-1$ eigenvectors attains the upper bound, and every subspace of
codimension $j-1$ intersects the first $j$ eigenvectors for the
reverse inequality. Applying this argument to SGT19 and retaining
the separate global bound gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 h=\min\{r,H_a\},\quad 0\le r\le d,\qquad
 V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}}=\min\{G_{0,N},V_{a,N}\},\\
 \|\bigwedge^rT_N(g)\|\le F_{a,N}^{\,r},\qquad
 \|\bigwedge^rT_N(f)\|
       \le G_{0,N}^{\,h}(V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}})^{\,r-h}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT20}
\]
The norm of the $r$th exterior power is the product of the $r$
largest singular values: this follows by applying a singular-value
decomposition to the orthonormal wedge bases. Only at most $H_a$
directions use the zero-free-radius bound. If $H_a>d$, then
$h=r$ and there is no tail factor. Every constant is positive;
the empty exterior power has norm one.

Retain the exact diagonal constants from the original norms:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \alpha_+=
  \left(\max_{0\le n\le N}\rho_{n+v}\right)
  \left(\max_{0\le n\le N}\rho_n^{-1}\right),\qquad
 \alpha_-=
  \left(\max_{0\le n\le N}\rho_{n+v}^{-1}\right)
  \left(\max_{0\le n\le N}\rho_n\right),\\
 L_{s,N,v}=\tfrac12\max\{1,s/2-1\}\,\mathfrak H_{N+v},
 \qquad \mathfrak H_m=\sum_{j=1}^m1/j,\quad\mathfrak H_0=0.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT21}
\]
For $s=k\ge9$, the product defining $\rho_n$ and
$\log(1+x)\le x$ imply
$|\log\rho_n|\le(b-1)\mathfrak H_n/2$.
For $s=1$, its logarithm is positive and
$\log((j+1)/(j+1/2))\le1/(2j+1)\le1/(j+1)$ gives
$|\log\rho_n|\le\mathfrak H_n/2$.
Thus $|\log\rho_n|\le L_{s,N,v}$ for all $0\le n\le N+v$,
including $n=0$. In particular
$|\log\alpha_\pm|\le2L_{s,N,v}$.
Applying the two diagonal maps in SGT10 on exterior powers gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \|\bigwedge^r A_{N,s}\|
       \le\alpha_+^r F_{a,N}^r,\\
 \|\bigwedge^r A_{N,s}^{-1}\|
       \le\alpha_-^rG_{0,N}^{\,h}
                    (V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}})^{\,r-h}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT22}
\]
This retains the rectangular degree shift $n\mapsto n+v$ exactly.

Let $[P_1],\ldots,[P_r]$ be any independent original classes in
SGT7 and use that same ordered frame in both Grams:
\[
 H_{\mathrm{in}}=
    (\langle[P_i],[P_j]\rangle_{s,c_0,\mathrm{quot}})_{i,j},
 \quad
 H_{\mathrm{out}}=
    (\langle\mathcal T_AP_i,\mathcal T_AP_j\rangle_{s,c_1})_{i,j},
 \quad
 \Delta_{N,s}(\mathbf P)=
          \log\frac{\det H_{\mathrm{out}}}{\det H_{\mathrm{in}}}.
 \tag{SGT23}
\]
The inner products are conjugate-linear in their first variable.
Both Grams are positive, by independence and SGT7. Exterior
volumes and their inverse give the finite, potentially asymmetric bounds
\[
\begin{split}
 -2r\log\alpha_- -2h\log G_{0,N}
            -2(r-h)\log V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}}
 &\le\Delta_{N,s}(\mathbf P)\\
 &\le2r\log\alpha_+ +2r\log F_{a,N}.
\end{split}\tag{SGT24}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M12_SGT_EXTERIOR
The classical Gram/exterior identity used here is recorded in
Lyons \cite[\S4, (4.1), p.~11]{human:lyons2003}; the source and
image frames below specify its exact application to SGT24.
% END HUMAN CITATION M12_SGT_EXTERIOR

For proof, express the classes in their SGT7 orthonormal quotient
frame as columns $X$. Their squared wedge norm is $\det(X^*X)$,
and the squared norm of their image wedge is
$\det(X^*A_{N,s}^*A_{N,s}X)$. Apply SGT22 to that wedge and then
to its image with $A_{N,s}^{-1}$. A change of the original ordered
frame by a matrix $U$ multiplies both determinants by $|\det U|^2$;
the ratio therefore retains the original frame and is frame invariant.
No specially conditioned plane has been selected.

For a nonnegative enclosure write $\log^+x=\max\{0,\log x\}$ and set
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal U_{a,N,s,r}
    =4rL_{s,N,v}+2r\log^+F_{a,N},\\
 \mathcal L_{a,N,s,r}
    =4rL_{s,N,v}+2h\log^+G_{0,N}
                    +2(r-h)\log^+V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}} .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT25}
\]
Then $-\mathcal L\le\Delta_{N,s}(\mathbf P)\le\mathcal U$.
These are finite explicit constants even for $r=0$, with value zero
for that empty Gram ratio. All maxima, minima, poles and residues
in the constants refer to the fixed original symbol SGT1.

\subsection{Uniform small-rank volume distortion and the actual PDS frame}

Fix finite constants $C_N,C_r>0$. Uniformly over
$0\le N\le C_Nk^2$, $0\le r\le\min\{C_rk,N+1\}$,
$s=1$ or $k$, and every original rank-$r$ plane in SGT7,
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \sup\frac{|\Delta_{N,s}(\mathbf P)|}{k^3}
                   \longrightarrow0
 \quad(k=4l+1\longrightarrow\infty).
 \qquad}\tag{SGT26}
\]
Here the supremum is over precisely those finite-degree and rank
domains, including all coefficient choices for the planes.
We give both limiting estimates. For each fixed admissible $a$,
$S_N(a)\le(N+1)a^{-N}$ and $a^{-N}\ge1$, so
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log^+F_{a,N}\le N\log(1/a)+\log(N+1)+\log^+G_a,\\
 \log^+V_{a,N}^{\mathrm{cap}}
 \le\log^+V_{a,N}
 \le N\log(1/a)+\log(N+1)+\log^+(M_a+P_a),\\
 \log^+G_{0,N}
 \le N\log(1/a_0)+\log(N+1)+\log^+M_0 .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT27}
\]
Also $\mathfrak H_m\le1+\log(m+1)$, by integral comparison.
The common diagonal error $4rL$ is therefore
$O_{C_N,C_r,v}(k^2\log(k+1))$. The global inverse term has
$h\le H_a$, which is fixed before the $k$ limit, so it is
$O_{\mathrm{actual},a,C_N}(k^2+\log(k+1))$.
It follows from SGT25 and SGT27 that
\[
 \limsup_{k\to\infty}
 \sup\frac{|\Delta_{N,s}(\mathbf P)|}{k^3}
       \le 2C_rC_N\log(1/a).
 \tag{SGT28}
\]
The constants in this step may depend on this fixed $a$.
Only after this limit do we take admissible radii arbitrarily close
to one, as proved before SGT14. The right side then tends to zero.
This proves SGT26 without assuming a zero-free unit disk or a
uniform pole count as $a$ tends to one.

For the original PDS quotient set
\[
\begin{gathered}
 q_k=(k+1)^2,\quad Q=(k+5)^2,\quad e\in\{0,1\},\\
 N_e=Q-v-1+e,\quad
 \ell_e=8k+23+e,\quad d_e=\ell_e+1=8k+24+e,\\
 P_a(S)=\chi_k(S)S^a,\qquad
 r_a(S')=\mathcal T_AP_a(S'),\qquad 0\le a\le\ell_e,\\
 \chi_k(S)=\prod_{u,w=0}^k(S-\beta_{k,u,w}) .
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT29}
\]
Then $q_k+\ell_e=N_e+v=Q-1+e$. A nonzero linear combination
of the $P_a$ has degree at least $q_k>v-1$ because it is
$\chi_k$ times a nonzero polynomial. Thus their classes modulo
$\mathcal P_{v-1}$ are independent, of exactly the original rank
$d_e$, without a minor or genericity assumption.

Let $X_e$ be their full column matrix in the original
$c_0$ orthonormal frame of degrees $0,\ldots,N_e+v$, and write
$P_{<v}$ and $P_{\ge v}$ for the row selections in that frame.
The exact source and image Grams are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 Y_e=P_{\ge v}X_e,\qquad
 H_{\mathrm{src},e}=Y_e^*Y_e
       =X_e^*X_e-(P_{<v}X_e)^*(P_{<v}X_e)>0,\\
 C_e=A_{N_e,s}Y_e,\qquad
 H_e=C_e^*C_e,\qquad
 -\mathcal L_{a,N_e,s,d_e}
       \le\log\frac{\det H_e}{\det H_{\mathrm{src},e}}
       \le\mathcal U_{a,N_e,s,d_e}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SGT30}
\]
The first equality is the attained full quotient minimum over the
entire original $\mathcal P_{v-1}$. The next equality is the
coefficient map SGT10, since those removed low columns are killed
by $\mathcal T_A$. The resulting $C_e,H_e$ are exactly PDS7,
including the target center $c_1$ and both original orders.
For $k\ge9$, $Q\le(14k/9)^2<3k^2$, so $N_e\le3k^2$ and
$d_e\le11k$. Hence SGT26 applies directly:
\[
 \log\frac{\det H_e}{\det H_{\mathrm{src},e}}
          =o(k^3)=o(kq_k)
 \quad(e=0,1,\ s=1,k),
 \tag{SGT31}
\]
uniformly over those four original source/endpoint choices.
The full original mass has not been removed from either Gram.

The precise dual statement also retains all PDS conjugations:
for the complex-linear functional $x^{\mathsf T}u$ on a Gram $H$,
its squared dual norm is $x^*\overline{H^{-1}}x$.
Thus the same coefficient coordinates carry
\[
 G_e=\overline{H_e^{-1}},\qquad
 G_{\mathrm{src},e}=\overline{H_{\mathrm{src},e}^{-1}},\qquad
 \log\frac{\det G_e}{\det G_{\mathrm{src},e}}
       =-\log\frac{\det H_e}{\det H_{\mathrm{src},e}} .
 \tag{SGT32}
\]
This is an equality of the original full quotient-source dual
metrics, not a statement about a later connecting-target Gram.

For the endpoint $e=1$ and the original four PDS8 rows, retain the same full rank-four
matrix $W$ and choose any basis $Z$ of $\ker W^{\mathsf T}$.
The polynomial quotient by those four columns, in the dual coordinates
$a=Z^{\mathsf T}f$, has metric
$(Z^{\mathsf T}H^{-1}\overline Z)^{-1}$ by minimizing $f^*Hf$.
Consequently it is the conjugate inverse of the restriction
$Z^*\overline{H^{-1}}Z$, exactly as in PDS9. For the paired
forms $H_e,H_{\mathrm{src},e}$, fixed complementary frames or the
same attained Schur decomposition therefore give
\[
\begin{split}
 \log\frac{\det(Z^*G_eZ)}
              {\det(Z^*G_{\mathrm{src},e}Z)}
 &=-\Delta_{N_e,s}(\mathbf P)
   +\log\frac{\det(W^*H_eW)}
                   {\det(W^*H_{\mathrm{src},e}W)},\\
 -\mathcal U_{a,N_e,s,d_e}-\mathcal L_{a,N_e,s,4}
 &\le \log\frac{\det(Z^*G_eZ)}
                   {\det(Z^*G_{\mathrm{src},e}Z)}
 \le\mathcal L_{a,N_e,s,d_e}+\mathcal U_{a,N_e,s,4}.
\end{split}\tag{SGT33}
\]
Indeed $\ker Z^{\mathsf T}=\operatorname{im}W$. If $J$ is any
fixed right inverse of $Z^{\mathsf T}$, then $[W,J]$ is invertible,
and block Schur factorization gives
$\det((Z^{\mathsf T}H^{-1}\bar Z)^{-1})
 =|\det[W,J]|^2\det H/\det(W^*HW)$.
Taking the ratio cancels this exact frame determinant and proves
the first equality. The $W$ columns correspond to the four independent
original polynomials $\chi_kR_i$ modulo $\mathcal P_{v-1}$;
SGT24--25 applied to that actual rank-four plane prove the interval.
At $e=1$ this is the original primitive source, and its determinant
distortion is again $o(kq_k)$. At $e=0$ the primitive condition is
empty and SGT32 already gives its full statement.

The earlier PEC1--18 theorem uses actual numerator-jet cancellation
at symbol zeros to control a positive dual spectrum. Here SGT7--10
first proves the polynomial source/target morphism, and SGT16--22
retains all principal parts in an exact finite-rank Toeplitz summand,
giving both directions on every small-rank plane. Neither statement
replaces the original lower quotient, the four primitive rows, or the
connecting-target metric. The original-data zero-free disk is the
RDS11 disk with its full proof repeated in SGT11--13.

\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:CPQ_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:start}

% STATUS ACCEPTED_PROVIDER_PER_ROOT

\clearpage
\begingroup
\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}
\providecommand{\rank}{\operatorname{rank}}
% Original connecting image, source kernel and remaining target quotient.
\section{The connecting image inside the full remaining target quotient}
\label{sec:CPQ}

This calculation uses the complete original CIT, RQB and NSR objects.
The fixed hypothetical simple quartet, five-orbit period, logarithm
branch, conductor $E_A$, order $v=\operatorname{ord}_0 E_A$, nonzero
moment $\mu_v$ and scalar $\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v$ remain those of the
original construction. Put $k=4l+1\ge9$, $j=k+4$,
$q_a=(a+1)^2$, $q'_a=(a-7)^2$, $m_a=16a-48-v$,
$t_a=8a-16-t_{0,a}$ and $j_{0,a}=8a-32-v+t_{0,a}$.
Use the original rank-valid domain, including $|u|\ge R_*$.
The original source pairs are $p=0:(s_0,s_1)=(1,1)$ and
$p=1:(s_0,s_1)=(k,j)$. Every form below has its actual pair $p$;
we suppress this index only in identities valid separately for both.

At each original target cutoff $q_j-1\le N\le2q_j$, write $D=N-v$.
The native coefficient form is
\[
 \mathcal D_N=\operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D}^{(s_1)},4),\qquad
 \mathcal Rf=-f(q_j-1)-f(q_j)+f(2q_j-1)+f(2q_j).
 \tag{CPQ1}
\]
Here $D_{j,D}$ is the full analytic-numerator form after minimizing
over all original lower evaluations, as in DNE and CIT19. Its rows
use $M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}$ and $h_{n,s}=M_s n!(s/2)_n$, the original
centres, division moments and phases. Nothing in this calculation
changes those rows or replaces their lower Gram. Fixed low-cycle
coefficient flags are used at all these later cutoffs.

\subsection{All coefficient maps and their kernels}
Retain
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m_j},\quad \mathcal V=\mathbb C^{m_k},
 \quad B=B_{\rm cof}:\mathcal V\longrightarrow\mathcal U,\\
 \mathbb B=\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4):\mathcal U
                   \longrightarrow\mathbb C^{4t_j},\quad
 \mathbb J=\ker\mathbb B,\quad\Phi=\mathbb BB,\\
 \mathscr B=\operatorname{im}B,\quad
 \mathscr S_e=\operatorname{im}[B,F_e],\quad
 W=\mathbb J+\mathscr B,\quad
 K_e=\mathscr S_e\cap W,\quad T_e=\mathscr S_e+W.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ2}
\]
$B$ is the original injective coefficient multiplier and $B_j$ is
onto with its full original invariant kernel. The columns $F_e$,
for $e=0,1$, are the actual CIT7 connecting columns, determined at
$N=q_j-1+e$ by the complete Taylor equation with independent target
lower corrections. They give an isomorphism
$\mathbb C^{r_e}\to\mathscr S_e/\mathscr B$,
$x\mapsto[F_ex]$, with $r_e=8k+24-3e$. Their independence and
equality in the two source orders are proved in CIT9 and NSR5.

Use the original full target frame
\[
 C_T=[U_R,U_b,U_o],\quad U_R=\Phi J_0Z_R,\quad U_b=\Phi E_b,
 \quad \operatorname{im}[U_R,U_b]=\operatorname{im}\Phi.
 \tag{CPQ3}
\]
It is the same invertible RQB27 frame. Its block dimensions are
$r_I=j_{0,k}-\epsilon$, $r_b=t_k-\ell+\epsilon$ and
$r_o=4t_j-m_k+\ell$, where $\ell=\dim\ker\Phi$ and
$\epsilon=\dim\ker(\Phi J_0)$. Define the actual quotient-coordinate
map and its connecting matrix by
\[
 \pi_o=[0\ \ 0\ \ I_{r_o}]C_T^{-1}\mathbb B,
 \qquad E_e=\pi_oF_e:\mathbb C^{r_e}\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{r_o}.
 \tag{CPQ4}
\]
The map $\pi_o$ is onto and $\ker\pi_o=W$. Indeed its value is zero
exactly when $\mathbb Bz\in\operatorname{im}\Phi$, equivalently
$\mathbb B(z-Bx)=0$ for some $x\in\mathcal V$. Its onto property
uses the onto map $\mathbb B$ and the full last block of $C_T$.
Consequently the maps
\[
 \mathcal U/W\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{r_o},\quad[z]\mapsto\pi_oz,
 \qquad
 \mathscr S_e/K_e\longrightarrow\operatorname{im}E_e,\quad
 [z]\mapsto\pi_oz
 \tag{CPQ5}
\]
are isomorphisms; the latter identifies $T_e/W$ with the same image.
The exact sequence in the original connecting coordinates is
\[
 0\longrightarrow\ker E_e\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{r_e}
                 \xrightarrow{E_e}\operatorname{im}E_e\longrightarrow0,
 \quad \ker E_e\simeq K_e/\mathscr B,\quad
 c_e=\operatorname{rank}E_e,
 \quad h_e=r_e-c_e.
 \tag{CPQ6}
\]
For its asserted kernel isomorphism, $F_ex\in W$ means that
$F_ex-Bb\in\mathbb J$ for some $b$. Its class lies in
$K_e/\mathscr B$, and every such class has a unique original
connecting coordinate $x$. This proves both injectivity and onto
without removing any conductor or invariant kernel. In particular
$c_e=\operatorname{rank}[\Phi,\mathbb BF_e]-\operatorname{rank}\Phi$,
and $0\le c_e\le\min(r_e,r_o)$.

Choose fixed independent columns $Z_h$ spanning $\ker E_e$ and
fixed complement columns $Z_c$ such that
$C_P=[Z_h,Z_c]$ is invertible. Set $A_e=E_eZ_c$ and choose $R_e^o$
so that $C_o=[A_e,R_e^o]$ is an invertible $r_o$-square matrix.
These choices concern only the actual fixed coefficient maps.
They are made once and used at every $N$ and both orders. Their
determinants are retained below. Empty blocks have their ordinary
zero-dimensional meanings and determinant one.

\subsection{The two original source and target minima}
Let $H_e(N)=H_{{\rm conn},e}(N)$ be CIT18's full connecting Gram
on $\mathscr S_e/\mathscr B$ in $F_e$ coordinates. It is the minimum
of $(F_ex+Bb)^*\mathcal D_N(F_ex+Bb)$ over all $b$.
The source kernel restriction and source quotient are exactly
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 G_{h,e}=Z_h^*H_eZ_h,\\
 G_{s,e}=Z_c^*H_eZ_c-Z_c^*H_eZ_h
                    (Z_h^*H_eZ_h)^{-1}Z_h^*H_eZ_c,\\
 x^*G_{s,e}x=\min_{z,b}
 (F_eZ_cx+F_eZ_hz+Bb)^*\mathcal D_N
                         (F_eZ_cx+F_eZ_hz+Bb),\\
 |\det C_P|^2\det H_e=\det G_{h,e}\det G_{s,e}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ7}
\]
The kernel fibre in the displayed minimum is exactly $K_e$ by
CPQ6. Thus $G_{s,e}$ is the original metric on $\mathscr S_e/K_e$
in the values $[F_eZ_cx]$. Block elimination in $C_P^*H_eC_P$
proves its formula and the full determinant identity. Positive
definiteness follows from independence in these quotient values.

Write $Q_o(N)$ for the original RQB remaining quotient on
$\mathbb C^{4t_j}/\operatorname{im}\Phi$, in the lifts $U_o$.
The map CPQ5 is an isometry from $\mathcal U/W$ with its attained
$\mathcal D_N$ metric to this form. To prove it, first minimize
over $\mathbb J$, giving exactly
$\operatorname{diag}(H_{B,j,D},4)$ on $\mathbb Bz$, where
$H_{B,j,D}=(B_jD_{j,D}^{-1}B_j^*)^{-1}$. Then minimize over
$\operatorname{im}\Phi$. The combined fibre is $W$; this is
precisely the definition of $Q_o$ in the frame $C_T$.

It follows that the original connecting target metric and its
remaining target quotient are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 G_{t,e}=A_e^*Q_oA_e,\\
 Q_{{\rm rem},e}=(R_e^o)^*Q_oR_e^o-(R_e^o)^*Q_oA_e
                 (A_e^*Q_oA_e)^{-1}A_e^*Q_oR_e^o,\\
 x^*G_{t,e}x=\min_{w\in W}
       (F_eZ_cx+w)^*\mathcal D_N(F_eZ_cx+w),\\
 |\det C_o|^2\det Q_o=\det G_{t,e}\det Q_{{\rm rem},e},
 \qquad \omega_{{\rm conn},e}=\det G_{t,e}/\det G_{s,e}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ8}
\]
For the target restriction, every representative of a value in
$T_e/W$ lies in $T_e$ because $W\subset T_e$. Thus the ambient
minimum over $W$ is its intrinsic quotient minimum. Substitution
of $\pi_oF_eZ_c=A_e$ proves the first and third lines. The second
line is the complete minimum over the connecting image inside
$Q_o$. Block elimination in $C_o^*Q_oC_o$ proves the determinant
identity. The last equality uses the identical value coordinates
on both sides of the original CIT21 contraction. Its inverse and
frame factors have therefore been established, rather than
deduced from equality of dimensions. The new quotient has rank
$r_o-c_e$ and represents exactly $\mathcal U/T_e$.

\subsection{Positive returns and the formerly signed remainder}
For the forms in CPQ7--8 set
\[
 V_h=\mathcal R\log\det G_{h,e},\quad
 V_s=\mathcal R\log\det G_{s,e},\quad
 V_t=\mathcal R\log\det G_{t,e},\quad
 V_{\rm rem}=\mathcal R\log\det Q_{{\rm rem},e}.
 \tag{CPQ9}
\]
Keep the original $V_I=\mathcal R\log\det H_{\rm inv}$,
$V_b=\mathcal R\log\det Q_b^E$,
$V_o=\mathcal R\log\det Q_o$ and
$V_A=\mathcal R\log\det A_{\rm inv}$, with the full invariant
source fibre $FZ_Rx+FE$, $F=BJ_0$ and
$E=\ker(\Phi J_0)$. In particular $A_{\rm inv}$ uses the target
order $s_1$ and is not replaced by the independent source form
$Q_{\rm inv}$ of NSR25.

All eight returns $V_h,V_s,V_t,V_{\rm rem},V_I,V_b,V_o,V_A$
are nonnegative. Indeed the original row extension increases
$\mathcal D_N$. Restriction preserves this order. Minimizing two
ordered forms over the same full affine fibre preserves it too.
Every displayed form is such a restriction or quotient on a fixed
flag. Its successive log-determinant increments are nonnegative,
and $\mathcal R$ is their sum with weights one at both ends and
two at each interior row. This proves the assertion for these
particular forms, including their complete gauges.

Write $V_{\rm conn}=\mathcal R\log\det H_e$. CPQ7--8 and the
original RQB target identity give
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 V_{\rm conn}=V_h+V_s,\qquad V_o=V_t+V_{\rm rem},\\
 4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}=V_I+V_b+V_t+V_{\rm rem},\\
 \mathcal R\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}=V_t-V_s,\qquad
 \mathcal L_e:=\mathcal R\log(\omega_{{\rm conn},e}
                          \omega_{\rm inv})=V_I+V_t-V_s-V_A.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ10}
\]
All fixed frame determinants in CPQ7--8 cancel only here, since
$\mathcal R1=0$. No varying minimum representative is treated as
a fixed frame. Substitution into CIT30's original identity
$\mathcal L_e=\mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}-V_b$ now evaluates its
entire signed Gram combination as
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}
     =4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-V_{\rm rem}-V_s-V_A,
 \qquad
 \mathcal L_e
     =4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-V_b-V_{\rm rem}-V_s-V_A.
 \quad}\tag{CPQ11}
\]
This equality retains both new proper quotients. In particular
neither $Q_o$ nor the source quotient has been discarded. It
expresses the original signed combination by the complete target
return and three specified nonnegative returns.

For completeness the fixed factors before taking the return can
also be made explicit. Use the original CIT constants
$c_{{\rm conn},e}$ and $C_W$, and the fixed change
$Q_b^{\rm CIT}=C_b^*Q_b^EC_b$. The original expression
$P(N)=\log g_{T_e}+\log g_{K_e}-\log g_{\mathscr S_e}
-\log g_{\mathbb J}-\log\det A_{\rm inv}$ satisfies
\[
 \begin{split}
 P(N)={}&4\log\det H_{B,j,D}
  -\log\det Q_{{\rm rem},e}-\log\det G_{s,e}
  -\log\det A_{\rm inv}+\log\mathfrak c_e,\\
 \mathfrak c_e={}&
 \frac{|\det C_T|^2|\det C_o|^2|\det C_b|^2}
      {c_{{\rm conn},e}|\det C_W|^2}.
 \end{split}\tag{CPQ12}
\]
To verify every factor, CIT26 gives
$P=\log\omega_{\rm conn}+\log\omega_{\rm inv}
+\log\det Q_b^{\rm CIT}-\log c_{{\rm conn},e}
-2\log|\det C_W|$. Substitute CPQ8 and
$|\det C_T|^2(\det H_{B,j,D})^4
=\det H_{\rm inv}\det Q_b^E\det Q_o$.
Then substitute $\det G_{t,e}=|\det C_o|^2\det Q_o/
\det Q_{{\rm rem},e}$. These substitutions give CPQ12
with the displayed positive constant. Its return is zero,
which proves CPQ11 in the original absolute frames as well.

The following directed intervals follow from these exact
nonnegative quantities:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 V_I-V_s-V_A\ \le\ \mathcal L_e
     \ \le\ 4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-V_b-V_s-V_A,\\
 V_I+V_b-V_s-V_A\ \le\ \mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}
     \ \le\ 4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-V_s-V_A,\\
 V_I-V_{\rm conn}-V_A\ \le\ \mathcal L_e
     \ \le\ 4U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native}-V_b-V_s-V_A .
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ13}
\]
The width of each of the first two intervals is exactly $V_o$;
the location of its actual value is fixed by the retained
$V_{\rm rem}$. The first follows from
$0\le V_t\le V_o=4\widehat F_j-V_I-V_b$ in CPQ10.
The second adds $V_b$. For the third use $V_s\le V_{\rm conn}$
on the lower side and the original RQB35 cap, on its proved
domain, on the upper. These are finite bounds on the original
scalar. They do not assign an uncomputed asymptotic value to a
positive return.

\subsection{Actual row formulas for each new factor}
The new factors can be evaluated by the original arithmetic rows.
Here is a full common formula specifying their minima. Let $Y$
be any of the fixed coefficient subspaces and $K\subset Y$ the
kernel in one of the following pairs:
\[
 (Y,K)=(K_e,\mathscr B),\ (\mathscr S_e,K_e),\ (T_e,W),\
 (\mathcal U,T_e),\ (\operatorname{im}F,FE).
 \tag{CPQ14}
\]
They represent respectively $G_h,G_s,G_t,Q_{\rm rem},A_{\rm inv}$.
Choose independent coefficient columns $X_K$ for $K$ and lifts
$X_Q$ for the specified quotient values. The lifts are respectively
$F_eZ_h,F_eZ_c,F_eZ_c$, any fixed lifts of $U_oR_e^o$ under
$\mathbb B$, and $FZ_R$. In the fourth pair the coordinate map
to $\mathcal U/T_e$ is the last quotient in CPQ8.

Let $\mathcal V_N$ be the full original four-copy lower-evaluation
matrix through $D$, and $\mathcal T_N$ the four-copy original
analytic-numerator matrix through $D$. Form the raw matrices
\[
 Z_{K,N}=[\mathcal V_N,\mathcal T_NX_K],\qquad
 Z_{Y,N}=[Z_{K,N},\mathcal T_NX_Q],\qquad
 G_K^{\rm raw}=Z_{K,N}^*Z_{K,N}.
 \tag{CPQ15}
\]
Their columns are independent: the full lower frame is
independent, and the DNE coefficient Gram is positive after its
minimum; the remaining fixed coefficient columns are independent.
Thus all the following inverses exist, including at the first
cutoff, with the empty-block convention. Sequential elimination
gives exactly the quotient form $Q=\operatorname{Gram}_{\mathcal
D_N}(Y/K)$ in these lifts and
$\det Q=\det(Z_{Y,N}^*Z_{Y,N})/\det G_K^{\rm raw}$.

At the next original degree $n=D+1$, append the four rows
\[
 a=[\operatorname{diag}(v_n,4),\operatorname{diag}(t_n,4)X_K],
 \quad b=\operatorname{diag}(t_n,4)X_Q,\quad
 C=(G_K^{\rm raw})^{-1}Z_{K,N}^*\mathcal T_NX_Q,\quad R=b-aC.
 \tag{CPQ16}
\]
These are literally CIT19's $v_n,t_n$, with the complete
arithmetic recursion
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 f_{a,m}=\frac{\nu_{a,m+v}-\sum_{d=v+1}^{m+v}
                  \binom{m+v}{d}\mu_df_{a,m+v-d}}
                    {\binom{m+v}{v}\mu_v},\qquad
 \nu_{a,n}=\sum_{u,w}(Z_{\exp,j})_{uw,a}\beta_{j,u,w}^{\,n},\\
 b_{0,m}=\mathbf1_{m=0},\ b_{-1,m}=0,\quad
 b_{n+1,m}=i^{-1}(b_{n,m-1}-c'_jb_{n,m})
                    -n(n-1+s_1/2)b_{n-1,m},\\
 (t_n)_a=h_{n,s_1}^{-1/2}\sum_m b_{n,m}f_{a,m},\qquad
 (v_n)_{u,w}=h_{n,s_1}^{-1/2}\sum_m b_{n,m}\beta_{j-8,u,w}^{\,m}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ17}
\]
The symbol $b_{n,m}$ in CPQ17 is the original polynomial
coefficient, distinct from the new-row block $b$ in CPQ16.
Arrays outside their original polynomial range are zero.
The divisor $\mu_v$ and the full old lower and coefficient
Gram occur explicitly. The row formula remains valid for every
allowed zero row and for both prescribed source orders.

Put $L=a(G_K^{\rm raw})^{-1}a^*$. The exact next quotient is
\[
 Q^+=Q+R^*(I_4+L)^{-1}R,\qquad
 \gamma_{Y/K,n}=\log\det(I_4+L+RQ^{-1}R^*)-\log\det(I_4+L)\ge0.
 \tag{CPQ18}
\]
To prove the first equality, subtract the old attained minimum
and change the old kernel coefficient by $\eta$. The remaining
minimum is $\eta^*G_K^{\rm raw}\eta+\|Rx-a\eta\|^2$.
Solving its positive normal equations gives the residual
$x^*R^*(I_4+L)^{-1}Rx$. The determinant lemma gives the second
equality. For its sign, conjugate by $(I_4+L)^{-1/2}$; the added
form is positive. Thus each of the five returns in CPQ14 is the
sum of these particular $\gamma$'s, with endpoint weights one
and all interior weights two. This supplies the original row
calculation for all terms of CPQ11, including the previously
unspecified remainder, without a new row model.

\subsection{Back substitution into both source orders and the allocation}
The ranks of $(G_h,G_s,G_t,Q_{\rm rem},A_{\rm inv})$ are
respectively $(h_e,c_e,c_e,r_o-c_e,r_I)$. NSO's full native
same-fibre comparison therefore gives, for each of their returns
$V_X$, the finite interval
\[
 -\operatorname{rank}(X)\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le
    V_X^{p=1}-V_X^{p=0}\le
       \operatorname{rank}(X)\Omega_j^{\rm lo}.
 \tag{CPQ19}
\]
The cutoffs inside $\Omega_j$ are exactly those in CPQ1 and
NSR2. The same coefficient frames occur in both orders by CPQ4
and NSR5. Their determinants therefore cancel in these ratios.
Adding the two correlated decompositions gives precisely
$\Delta V_o=\Delta V_t+\Delta V_{\rm rem}$ and
$\Delta V_{\rm conn}=\Delta V_h+\Delta V_s$, preserving the
full earlier NSR intervals. All ranks are $O(k)$ on the fixed
actual programme, so the new individual order differences are
$O_{\rm actual}(k^2\log q_j)=o(kq_k)$.

Finally the original degree-$j$ allocation obeys
$S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}=\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}$ and
$S_{R,j}^{(s_1)}=\mathcal T_{j,s_1}-\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}$
by the exact HJR/NSR31 inverse-metric calculation. Hence CPQ10
gives the complete positive refinement of that kernel allocation:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 4S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}=V_I+V_b+V_t+V_{\rm rem},\qquad
 4S_{R,j}^{(s_1)}=4\mathcal T_{j,s_1}-V_I-V_b-V_t-V_{\rm rem},\\
 4S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}
      =\mathcal L_e+V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CPQ20}
\]
The target degree is $j=k+4$ in this identity. The independent
degree-$k$ source quotient $Q_{\rm inv}$ and the proper-source
correction in NFR retain their own centre, displaced cutoffs
and source order. In particular replacing $V_A$ by
$\mathcal R\log\det Q_{\rm inv}$ is not part of CPQ20.
The exact relation is
$V_A=\mathcal R\log\det Q_{\rm inv}
+\mathcal R\log(\det A_{\rm inv}/\det Q_{\rm inv})$;
both forms use the same lifts $Z_R$, so this ratio is the
determinant of their actual relative positive operator. ITL/NFR
retains its full proper-source comparison. CPQ20 thus carries
the new quotient refinement into the original allocation while
preserving that remaining source comparison and every positive
term which must enter a quantitative estimate.

\operatorname{rank}(X)\Phi_j^-\le

% Complete full-form proof; all received equations (2)--(13) reconstructed.
% Immutable original source SHA256:
% 9926c5b5670777548f15f3fd626a4237d2c10a26834f51ba2b466a3b3a349656
\begingroup
\newtheorem{grftheorem}{Theorem}
\newtheorem{grflemma}[grftheorem]{Lemma}
\section{The complete root-form comparison at growing degree ratios}
\label{GRF:section}
\paragraph{Scope and audited conclusion.}
This section proves equations (2)--(13) of the received submission,
including all their finite constants,
on the original stipulated complete-order packet. The verdict on that scope is
\emph{proved as written}, with the displayed guards retained. The accepted
EIQ equilibrium and ECL evaluations are stated below in their exact original
domain and normalization; the new full-form argument is proved in full here.
No assertion about arithmetic monic minima, allowance asymptotics, shifted
windows, an intrinsic mixed allocation, or a period evaluation is made here.
In particular no original mass, period, root, or multiplicity is altered.

\subsection{Original objects and exact dependency statements}

Fix $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$, and the positive integer complete order
$m_{\rm pkt}$. Retain the original integer subsequence $k=4l+1\ge5$ and put
\[
 e_k=1+k(m_{\rm pkt}-1),\qquad q=e_k(k+1)^2,\qquad c=k/2,
 \qquad R=R_k=k\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}.
\]
The root polynomial and its exact real-coordinate image are
\begin{align*}
 \chi(S)&=\prod_{a,b=0}^k
 [S-c-(2a-k)\delta-i(2b-k)\gamma]^{e_k},\\
 Q(y)&=i^{-q}\chi(c+iy)
      =\prod_{a,b=0}^k(y-\zeta_{ab})^{e_k},
 \qquad \zeta_{ab}=(2b-k)\gamma-i(2a-k)\delta.
\end{align*}
The symbol $m_{\rm pkt}$ here denotes exactly the received packet order $m$;
the subscript merely distinguishes it from the degree of a parity block.
All $q$ roots, with their complete multiplicities, occur in opposite and
conjugate pairs. They satisfy $|\zeta_{ab}|\le R$.

The measure remains
\[
 d\sigma(y)=s(y)\,d y,
 \qquad s(y)=\frac{|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2}{2\pi}.
\]
Its accepted original density bounds are, on all of $\mathbb R$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF1}\label{GRF:density}
 c_\Gamma e^{-\pi|y|/2}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}
 \le s(y)\le
 C_\Gamma e^{-\pi|y|/2}(1+|y|)^{-1/2},
 \quad
 c_\Gamma=\sqrt2e^{-7/6},\quad
 C_\Gamma=\sqrt{2\sqrt5}e^{2/3}.
\end{equation*}
These are AMT5/TW4, restated in RGC2; no new constants are substituted.
For completeness the mass follows directly from the Gamma integral.
Writing $f_a(x)=e^{ax-e^x}$ gives
$\Gamma(a+it)=\int_\mathbb R f_a(x)e^{itx}\,d x$ for $a>0$.
Plancherel and $z=e^x$ therefore give
\[
 \int_\mathbb R |\Gamma(a+it)|^2\frac{dt}{2\pi}
 =\int_\mathbb R e^{2ax-2e^x}\,d x
 =\frac{\Gamma(2a)}{2^{2a}}.
\]
Setting $a=1/4$ and $y=2t$ proves
$\sigma(\mathbb R)=2\Gamma(1/2)/\sqrt2=\sqrt{2\pi}$.

Here is the precise accepted equilibrium theorem used. For every
$\alpha>0$, $\beta>0$, let
$V(x)=\beta\sqrt x-\alpha\log x$ on $(0,\infty)$.
The EIQ probability measure has support $[u^2,v^2]$, $0<u<v$, where
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF2}\label{GRF:endpoints}
 uK(\kappa)=\frac{\alpha\pi}{\beta},\qquad
 vE(\kappa)=\frac{(\alpha+2)\pi}{\beta},\qquad
 \kappa^2=1-u^2/v^2,
\end{equation*}
and
\[
 K(\kappa)=\int_0^{\pi/2}\frac{d\theta}
                 {\sqrt{1-\kappa^2\sin^2\theta}},\qquad
 E(\kappa)=\int_0^{\pi/2}
                 \sqrt{1-\kappa^2\sin^2\theta}\,d\theta.
\]
The density, extended by zero off this interval, is
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF3}\label{GRF:eiqdensity}
 \rho(x)=\frac{\sqrt{(v^2-x)(x-u^2)}}{2\pi}h(x),\quad
 h(x)=\frac{\beta}{2\pi x}
 \int_0^\infty
 \frac{\sqrt s}{(x+s)\sqrt{(u^2+s)(v^2+s)}}\,d s>0.
\end{equation*}
It is bounded and continuous, has total mass one, and its logarithmic
potential
$P^{\rm eq}(z)=\int_{u^2}^{v^2}\log|z-x|\rho(x)\,d x$
is continuous on $\mathbb C$. With the original Robin constant $\lambda$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF4}\label{GRF:euler}
 V(x)-2P^{\rm eq}(x)=\lambda\quad(u^2\le x\le v^2),
 \qquad V(x)-2P^{\rm eq}(x)>\lambda
 \quad(0<x<u^2\ \text{or}\ x>v^2).
\end{equation*}
These statements are EIQ1--19 on the entire positive parameter quadrant.
Writing $w=(u+v)/2$, $z_0=uv$, and
$c_{\rm cap}=(v^2-u^2)/4$, the accepted ECL6 and ECL14 give exactly
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF5}\label{GRF:ecl}
 \lambda=2(\alpha+2)-2\alpha\log w-2\log c_{\rm cap},
 \qquad
 P^{\rm eq}(0)=2(\alpha+1)\log w-\alpha\log z_0-2.
\end{equation*}
The use of these already proved dependencies does not assume compactness
of a moving family of parameters. All estimates below use the actual
$\alpha,\beta,u,v$.

\subsection{The complete root polynomial}

\begin{grflemma}\label{GRF:roots}
Put
\[
 U_2=\frac{k(k+2)}3,\qquad
 U_4=\frac{k(k+2)(3k^2+6k-4)}{15},
\]
\[
 D_2=qU_2(\gamma^2-\delta^2),\qquad
 M_4=q\bigl[(\delta^4+\gamma^4)U_4
                  +2\delta^2\gamma^2U_2^2\bigr].
\]
For real $|y|>R$ one has
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF6}\label{GRF:rootexpansion}
 \log\frac{|Q(y)|^2}{y^{2q}}=-\frac{D_2}{y^2}+E_Q(y),
 \qquad
 |E_Q(y)|\le\frac{M_4}{2y^4(1-R^2/y^2)}.
\end{equation*}
For real $|y|\ge2R$ one has
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF7}\label{GRF:directed}
 \log\frac{|Q(y)|^2}{y^{2q}}
 \le-\frac{13D_2}{30y^2}<0.
\end{equation*}
For every real $y$ one has
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF8}\label{GRF:envelope}
 |Q(y)|^2\le(y^2+R^2)^q.
\end{equation*}
\end{grflemma}
\begin{proof}
The original odd values of $k$ give the literal positive real factorization
\[
 Q(y)=\prod_{a,b\in\{1,3,\ldots,k\}}
 \left[((y-b\gamma)^2+a^2\delta^2)
       ((y+b\gamma)^2+a^2\delta^2)\right]^{e_k}>0.
\]
No root is real, and this also shows that $Q$ is even. Finite differentiation
of
\[
 \sum_{a=0}^k e^{(2a-k)t}=
 \frac{\sinh((k+1)t)}{\sinh t}
\]
at $t=0$, using the removable value there, gives zero odd moments and
\[
 \frac1{k+1}\sum_{a=0}^k(2a-k)^2=U_2,
 \qquad
 \frac1{k+1}\sum_{a=0}^k(2a-k)^4=U_4.
\]
For example division of the Taylor series gives the coefficients
$U_2/2$ and $U_4/24$ in the series of the displayed ratio divided by $k+1$.
Expanding $\zeta_{ab}^2$ and
$|\zeta_{ab}|^4=[(2b-k)^2\gamma^2+(2a-k)^2\delta^2]^2$
therefore proves
\[
 \sum_{a,b}e_k\zeta_{ab}^2=D_2,
 \qquad \sum_{a,b}e_k|\zeta_{ab}|^4=M_4\le qR^4.
\]
For $|y|>R$ the series for $\log(1-\zeta_{ab}/y)$ converges absolutely.
Taking twice the real part, opposite-root pairing cancels its odd terms,
and conjugate pairing makes its even sums real. Hence
\[
 \log\frac{|Q(y)|^2}{y^{2q}}
 =-\sum_{j\ge1}\frac{\sum_{a,b}e_k\zeta_{ab}^{2j}}{j y^{2j}}.
\]
This is the real logarithm of a positive number. Since
$\sum e_k|\zeta_{ab}|^{2j}\le M_4R^{2j-4}$ for $j\ge2$ and
$1/j\le1/2$, summing the geometric majorant proves
\eqref{GRF:rootexpansion}.

The packet restrictions give $\delta^2/\gamma^2<1/16$, so
\[
 \frac{D_2}{qR^2}
 =\frac{k+2}{3k}\frac{\gamma^2-\delta^2}{\gamma^2+\delta^2}
 \ge\frac{5}{17}>0.
\]
At $|y|\ge2R$, the error divided by $D_2/y^2$ is at most
\[
 \frac{qR^2}{D_2}\frac{R^2/y^2}{2(1-R^2/y^2)}
 \le\frac{17}{5}\frac{1/4}{2(3/4)}=\frac{17}{30}.
\]
Subtracting this from the negative main term proves \eqref{GRF:directed}.
Finally pair each root with its opposite, with multiplicities. Every factor
satisfies $|(y-\zeta)(y+\zeta)|=|y^2-\zeta^2|\le y^2+R^2$.
There are exactly $q/2$ such factors in $Q$, and squaring their product
proves \eqref{GRF:envelope}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The exact parity coordinate and moving evaluation bound}

Let $r$ be an integer, set $m_\varepsilon=\lfloor(r-\varepsilon)/2\rfloor$
for $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}$, and impose $m_0,m_1\ge1$.
This guard is equivalent to $r\ge3$ and is retained throughout the finite
theorem. Every complex polynomial $p$ of degree at most $r$ has the unique
decomposition
\[
 p(y)=P_0(y^2/q^2)+(y/q)P_1(y^2/q^2),\qquad
 \deg P_\varepsilon\le m_\varepsilon.
\]
Indeed, if $p(y)=\sum_{j=0}^r a_jy^j$, then
$P_\varepsilon(x)=\sum_{j\ge0}a_{2j+\varepsilon}
q^{2j+\varepsilon}x^j$, with only indices $2j+\varepsilon\le r$ included.
This gives both the map and its inverse on coefficient spaces.
Define, for $y>0$,
\[
 \mathfrak r(y)=\frac{s(y)e^{\pi y/2}\sqrt y}{\sqrt2}.
\]
For each block let
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF9}\label{GRF:blockparameters}
 \alpha_\varepsilon=\frac{q+\varepsilon-3/4}{m_\varepsilon}>0,
 \quad \beta_\varepsilon=\frac{\pi q}{2m_\varepsilon}>0,
 \quad V_\varepsilon(x)=\beta_\varepsilon\sqrt x-
                                      \alpha_\varepsilon\log x,
\end{equation*}
and
\[
 \|P\|_\varepsilon^2=
 \int_0^\infty |P(x)|^2e^{-m_\varepsilon V_\varepsilon(x)}
                         \mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\,d x.
\]
Denote its actual equilibrium data by
$u_\varepsilon,v_\varepsilon,P^{\rm eq}_\varepsilon,\lambda_\varepsilon$.
Since $s$ and $|Q|^2$ are even, the two half-lines cancel the cross term
$2\operatorname{Re}(P_0\overline{P_1})y/q$ for both norms. Using
$y=q\sqrt x$, $dy=q\,dx/(2\sqrt x)$ on the positive half-line gives
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF10}\label{GRF:paritynorm}
 \mathcal N_0[p]:=\int_\mathbb R y^{2q}|p(y)|^2\,d\sigma(y)
 =\sqrt2\,q^{2q+1/2}\sum_{\varepsilon=0}^1\|P_\varepsilon\|_\varepsilon^2.
\end{equation*}
In particular the power of $x$ in the integrand is
$q+\varepsilon-3/4$, exactly as in received equation (5).
With
$H_Q(x)=q^{-2q}|Q(q\sqrt x)|^2$, the other norm is exactly
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF11}\label{GRF:parityroot}
 \mathcal N_\chi[p]:=\int_\mathbb R |Q(y)p(y)|^2\,d\sigma(y)
 =\sqrt2\,q^{2q+1/2}
 \sum_{\varepsilon=0}^1
 \int_0^\infty |P_\varepsilon(x)|^2H_Q(x)x^{\varepsilon-3/4}
 e^{-\pi q\sqrt x/2}\mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\,d x.
\end{equation*}
All integrals converge by \eqref{GRF:density}.

For each block put
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF12}\label{GRF:parameters}
 \begin{gathered}
 \Sigma_\varepsilon=m_\varepsilon
                  (2P^{\rm eq}_\varepsilon(0)+\lambda_\varepsilon),\qquad
 b_\varepsilon=\min\left\{\frac{u_\varepsilon}{2},
             \frac14e^{-\Sigma_\varepsilon/(2q)}\right\}>0,\\
 \Lambda_\varepsilon=\frac{\alpha_\varepsilon}{u_\varepsilon^2}
                     +\frac{\beta_\varepsilon}{2u_\varepsilon},\qquad
 r_+=\frac{C_\Gamma}{\sqrt2},\qquad
 r_{-,\varepsilon}=\frac{c_\Gamma}{\sqrt2}
       \left(\frac{qu_\varepsilon}{1+qu_\varepsilon}\right)^{1/2}>0.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation*}
The original density bounds imply
$\mathfrak r(y)\le r_+$ for all $y>0$ and
$\mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\ge r_{-,\varepsilon}$ on the actual support.
Indeed the extra factor is $\sqrt{y/(1+y)}$, which increases in $y$.
Also $|V_\varepsilon'(x)|\le\Lambda_\varepsilon$ on that support.

Adding the two exact expressions in \eqref{GRF:ecl} yields, for each parameter
pair and its original $w,z_0$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF13}\label{GRF:sigmaformula}
 2P^{\rm eq}(0)+\lambda
 =2\alpha\left(1+\log\frac{w}{z_0}\right)
       +2\log\frac{v+u}{v-u}.
\end{equation*}
Here $4\log w-2\log c_{\rm cap}
=2\log((v+u)/(v-u))$ and the constants give
$-4+2(\alpha+2)=2\alpha$. Thus the $-2$ in each occurrence of
$P^{\rm eq}(0)$ is retained.

\begin{grflemma}\label{GRF:evaluation}
Assume $m=m_\varepsilon\ge1$ and
$m(v_\varepsilon^2-u_\varepsilon^2)\ge2$. For every complex polynomial
$P$ of degree at most $m$ and every $0\le x\le b_\varepsilon^2$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF14}\label{GRF:evalbound}
 |P(x)|^2\le
 \frac{32e^{3\Lambda_\varepsilon/2}m^4}{r_{-,\varepsilon}}
 e^{\Sigma_\varepsilon}\|P\|_\varepsilon^2.
\end{equation*}
\end{grflemma}
\begin{proof}
Suppress the block subscripts only within this proof, retaining their
actual values. If $P=0$ the result holds. Otherwise let
$M=\max_{[u^2,v^2]}|P(x)|e^{-mV(x)/2}>0$, attained at $x_0$.
At least one side of $x_0$ in this interval has length at least $1/m$,
because its full width is at least $2/m$. Take the closed one-sided
interval $J$ of length $1/m$ from $x_0$. For $x\in J$,
\[
 |P(x)|\le M e^{mV(x)/2}
 \le e^{\Lambda/2}|P(x_0)|.
\]
For any interval $J$ and every complex polynomial of degree at most $m$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF15}\label{GRF:derivative}
 \|P'\|_{\infty,J}\le\frac{4m^3}{|J|}\|P\|_{\infty,J}.
\end{equation*}
To prove this, map $J$ affinely to $[-1,1]$ and expand
$P=\sum_{j=0}^m c_jT_j$, where $T_j(\cos\theta)=\cos(j\theta)$.
Cosine orthogonality gives $|c_j|\le2\|P\|_\infty$ for $j\ge1$,
also for complex $c_j$. The finite geometric-sum identity for
$\sin(j\theta)/\sin\theta$ gives its modulus at most $j$.
Thus $|T'_j|\le j^2$, including the endpoints by continuity.
Since $\sum_{j=1}^m j^2\le m^3$ for every integer $m\ge1$,
the derivative bound on $[-1,1]$ is $2m^3\|P\|_\infty$;
the affine derivative factor $2/|J|$ proves \eqref{GRF:derivative}.

On $J$, this gives
$|P'|\le4m^4 e^{\Lambda/2}|P(x_0)|$.
Consequently on the segment from $x_0$ of length
$d=(8e^{\Lambda/2}m^4)^{-1}\le1/m$, the fundamental theorem of calculus
and the triangle inequality imply $|P|\ge|P(x_0)|/2$.
On the same segment $e^{-mV(x)}\ge e^{-\Lambda}e^{-mV(x_0)}$.
Using the actual lower density $r_-$ on it proves
\[
 \|P\|_\varepsilon^2\ge
 r_-d\,\frac{|P(x_0)|^2}{4}e^{-\Lambda}e^{-mV(x_0)}
 =\frac{r_-M^2}{32e^{3\Lambda/2}m^4}.
\]

For the passage off the support, continuity on the whole complex plane can
be checked directly from the accepted bounded density. If its bound is
$K_\rho$, then for $0<a<1$ and every complex $z$,
\[
 \int_{|z-t|<a}-\log|z-t|\rho(t)\,d t
 \le2K_\rho a(1-\log a).
\]
Indeed the real length of $\{t:|z-t|<s\}$ is at most $2s$; integrating
the level sets of the negative logarithm gives the stated bound.
It tends uniformly to zero as $a\downarrow0$. Truncation of this
singularity gives continuous potentials converging locally uniformly,
while the positive logarithmic part is continuous on compact sets.
Thus $P^{\rm eq}$ is continuous on $\mathbb C$. Outside the support its
integrands are harmonic and uniformly differentiable on compact subsets,
so $P^{\rm eq}$ is harmonic there.
Now consider
$f(z)=\log|P(z)|-mP^{\rm eq}(z)$ on
$\mathbb C\setminus[u^2,v^2]$. The second term is harmonic there and the first
is subharmonic, including its value $-\infty$ at zeros. Continuity of
$P^{\rm eq}$ and \eqref{GRF:euler} give boundary upper values
$f\le\log M+m\lambda/2$. This inequality also bounds the entire exterior,
including infinity: if $n=\deg P\le m$ and $w=1/z$, then near $w=0$
\[
 f(1/w)=(m-n)\log|w|
 +\log|w^nP(1/w)|
 -m\int\log|1-tw|\rho(t)\,d t.
\]
The last two terms are harmonic near zero because their arguments are
nonzero there. For $n=m$ the function extends harmonically at infinity;
for $n<m$ it extends subharmonically with value $-\infty$. Thus it is
subharmonic on the complement of the interval in the Riemann sphere.
Its boundary values have the stated upper bound. The maximum principle
applies on this compact closure: an upper value greater than the boundary
bound would attain an interior maximum, forcing constancy on the connected
exterior, contrary to the boundary bound. Hence
\[
 |P(z)|^2\le M^2 e^{m(2P^{\rm eq}(z)+\lambda)}.
\]
For $0\le x<u^2$, every support point $t$ satisfies
$0<t-x\le t$, so
$\log|x-t|\le\log t$ and
$P^{\rm eq}(x)\le P^{\rm eq}(0)$.
Since $b\le u/2$, this applies throughout $[0,b^2]$.
Substituting the proved estimate for $M^2$ yields \eqref{GRF:evalbound}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The entire inner region and the finite full-form theorem}

Let $\varrho=R/q$. Impose, for both blocks,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF16}\label{GRF:finiteguards}
 m_\varepsilon\ge1,\qquad
 m_\varepsilon(v_\varepsilon^2-u_\varepsilon^2)\ge2,
 \qquad \varrho\le b_\varepsilon/2.
\end{equation*}
Set
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF17}\label{GRF:eta}
 \eta_\varepsilon=
 \frac{32r_+e^{3\Lambda_\varepsilon/2}m_\varepsilon^4
                b_\varepsilon^{2\varepsilon+1/2}}
 {r_{-,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon+1/4)}8^{-q},
 \qquad \eta=\max(\eta_0,\eta_1).
\end{equation*}
On $[0,b_\varepsilon^2]$, \eqref{GRF:envelope} gives
$H_Q(x)\le(x+\varrho^2)^q$. The cutoff definition and the third guard give
\[
 e^{\Sigma_\varepsilon}(b_\varepsilon^2+\varrho^2)^q
 \le e^{\Sigma_\varepsilon}(5b_\varepsilon^2/4)^q
 \le (5/64)^q\le8^{-q}.
\]
The same estimate holds with $x^q$ instead of $H_Q(x)$.
Multiplying \eqref{GRF:evalbound} by the remaining factors in
\eqref{GRF:parityroot}, and using $e^{-\pi q\sqrt x/2}\le1$ and
$\mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\le r_+$, bounds each entire inner block by
\[
 \frac{32r_+e^{3\Lambda_\varepsilon/2}m_\varepsilon^4}
      {r_{-,\varepsilon}}8^{-q}\|P_\varepsilon\|_\varepsilon^2
 \int_0^{b_\varepsilon^2}x^{\varepsilon-3/4}\,d x
 =\eta_\varepsilon\|P_\varepsilon\|_\varepsilon^2.
\]
The integral is finite for both $\varepsilon=0,1$, and its exact value is
$b_\varepsilon^{2\varepsilon+1/2}/(\varepsilon+1/4)$.
Thus this estimate includes the endpoint zero and every neighborhood of it.

Define the finite quantity
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF18}\label{GRF:Cchi}
 C_\chi=\max_{\varepsilon=0,1}
 \left\{\frac{D_2}{q^2b_\varepsilon^2}
 +\frac{M_4}{2q^4b_\varepsilon^4
             (1-R^2/(q^2b_\varepsilon^2))}\right\}.
\end{equation*}
Its denominators are positive, at least $3/4$ in the parenthesis,
by \eqref{GRF:finiteguards}.

\begin{grftheorem}\label{GRF:finite}
Under \eqref{GRF:finiteguards} and $\eta<1$, for every complex polynomial
$p$ of degree at most $r$,
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF19}\label{GRF:fullform}
 e^{-C_\chi}(1-\eta)\mathcal N_0[p]
 \le \mathcal N_\chi[p]\le(1+\eta)\mathcal N_0[p].
\end{equation*}
\end{grftheorem}
\begin{proof}
In block $\varepsilon$, the outer interval $x\ge b_\varepsilon^2$
corresponds to $|y|\ge qb_\varepsilon\ge2R$.
By \eqref{GRF:rootexpansion} and monotonicity of its displayed majorants there,
the density ratio $H_Q(x)/x^q$ is at least $e^{-C_\chi}$.
By \eqref{GRF:directed} the same ratio is at most $1$.
Write $N_\varepsilon=\|P_\varepsilon\|_\varepsilon^2$.
The proved homogeneous inner bound implies its homogeneous outer
contribution is at least $(1-\eta_\varepsilon)N_\varepsilon$.
Positivity of the root inner contribution then gives the lower bound
$e^{-C_\chi}(1-\eta_\varepsilon)N_\varepsilon$ for the root block.
For the upper bound, its outer contribution is at most $N_\varepsilon$
and its entire inner contribution is at most
$\eta_\varepsilon N_\varepsilon$.
Sum the two block inequalities with the common, unchanged factor
$\sqrt2q^{2q+1/2}$ of \eqref{GRF:paritynorm}--\eqref{GRF:parityroot}.
The result is \eqref{GRF:fullform}. No assumption on the location of polynomial
mass and no monic minimization has been used.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proof of the broad growing-ratio range}

\begin{grftheorem}\label{GRF:growing}
Keep the fixed original packet and let $k=4l+1\to\infty$.
Let $r=r_k$ be an integer, $t=r/q$, and suppose
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF20}\label{GRF:broad}
 t\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad \log t=o(q/R).
\end{equation*}
Then both blocks eventually satisfy every guard in Theorem \ref{GRF:finite}.
More precisely,
\begin{gather*}
 u_\varepsilon\sim\frac2{\log t},\quad
 v_\varepsilon\sim2t,\quad
 b_\varepsilon\sim\frac{u_\varepsilon}{2e}
                   \sim\frac1{e\log t},\tag{GRF21}\label{GRF:cutoffasymptotic}\\
 C_\chi=O_h\left(\frac{R^2}{q}(1+\log t)^2\right)=o(q),\qquad
 \eta=\exp[-q\log8+o(q)].\tag{GRF22}\label{GRF:rates}
\end{gather*}
The corresponding original reference endpoint $u_t$ at
$(\alpha,\beta)=(2/t,\pi/t)$ has $u_t\sim2/\log t$, so
$b_\varepsilon\sim u_t/(2e)$ and $qb_\varepsilon\sim q/(e\log t)$.
\end{grftheorem}
\begin{proof}
All limits below hold for each of the two possible block indices, so are
uniform over that finite set. Put
\[
 c_\varepsilon=\varepsilon-3/4,\qquad
 d_\varepsilon=r-2m_\varepsilon\in\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon+1\},
 \qquad A_\varepsilon=1+c_\varepsilon/q,
 \qquad B_\varepsilon=A_\varepsilon+2m_\varepsilon/q.
\]
These are exact identities; in particular
$B_\varepsilon=1+t+(c_\varepsilon-d_\varepsilon)/q$.
The endpoint equations \eqref{GRF:endpoints} at the actual parameters become
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF23}\label{GRF:shiftedendpoints}
 u_\varepsilon K(\kappa_\varepsilon)=2A_\varepsilon,
 \qquad v_\varepsilon E(\kappa_\varepsilon)=2B_\varepsilon.
\end{equation*}
Here $A_\varepsilon\to1$, $B_\varepsilon/t\to1$, and
$m_\varepsilon\sim qt/2$. Thus $m_\varepsilon\ge1$ eventually.

We prove the endpoint asymptotics by the defining elliptic integrals,
without importing a uniform remainder estimate. Write $s=u/v\in(0,1)$,
$L=\log(1/s)$, and suppress the block index for this argument.
The substitution $z=\tan\theta$ gives exactly
\[
 K(\sqrt{1-s^2})=
 \int_0^\infty\frac{dz}{\sqrt{(1+z^2)(1+s^2z^2)}}.
\]
The integral on $[0,1]$ is at most $1$, and that on $[1/s,\infty)$
is at most $\int_{1/s}^\infty dz/(sz^2)=1$.
On $[1,1/s]$ the integrand is
$z^{-1}(1+z^{-2})^{-1/2}(1+s^2z^2)^{-1/2}$.
For nonnegative $a,b$,
$0\le1-(1+a)^{-1/2}(1+b)^{-1/2}\le(a+b)/2$;
this follows by applying $(1+x)^{-1/2}\ge1-x/2$ separately and
using $1-AB=(1-A)+A(1-B)$ with $0<A\le1$.
The difference of the middle integral from $\int_1^{1/s}dz/z=L$
has magnitude at most
\[
 \frac12\int_1^{1/s}(z^{-3}+s^2z)\,d z
 =\frac{1-s^2}{2}\le\frac12.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF24}\label{GRF:Kbound}
 L-\tfrac12\le K(\sqrt{1-s^2})\le L+2.
\end{equation*}
The defining $E$ integral has $1\le E\le\pi/2$ and $E\to1$ as
$s\to0$, by dominated convergence to the integral of $\cos\theta$.
By \eqref{GRF:shiftedendpoints},
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF25}\label{GRF:ratioendpoint}
 \frac{sK}{E}=\frac{A}{B}\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation*}
Since $K\ge\pi/2$ and $E\le\pi/2$, this implies $s\to0$ and $L\to\infty$.
Taking logarithms in \eqref{GRF:ratioendpoint} gives
\[
 L=\log(B/A)+\log K-\log E
   =\log t+\log L+O(1).
\]
Here \eqref{GRF:Kbound} gives $\log K=\log L+o(1)$ and
$\log(B/A)=\log t+o(1)$.
As $L\to\infty$, division of
$\log t=L-\log L+O(1)$ by $L$ proves
$L\sim\log t$ and then $K\sim\log t$.
Now \eqref{GRF:shiftedendpoints} yields
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF26}\label{GRF:endpointasymptotic}
 u\sim2/\log t,\qquad v\sim2t,
 \qquad s=O(1/(t\log t)).
\end{equation*}
The same proof with $A=1$, $B=1+t$ is exactly the claimed statement for
$u_t$ at $(2/t,\pi/t)$.

The exact expression \eqref{GRF:sigmaformula} gives
\begin{equation*}\tag{GRF27}\label{GRF:sigmashifted}
 \frac{\Sigma_\varepsilon}{2q}
 =A_\varepsilon\left[1+
          \log\frac{1+s_\varepsilon}{2u_\varepsilon}\right]
 +\frac{m_\varepsilon}{q}
          \log\frac{1+s_\varepsilon}{1-s_\varepsilon}.
\end{equation*}
For $0<s\le1/2$,
$0\le\log((1+s)/(1-s))\le4s$, by integration of $2/(1-s^2)$.
Thus the last term is $O(1/\log t)=o(1)$ by
\eqref{GRF:endpointasymptotic}. Also $\log(1+s)=o(1)$.
Since $A_\varepsilon-1=O(1/q)$ and
$1+|\log u_\varepsilon|=O(1+\log\log t)$, the error from replacing
$A_\varepsilon$ by $1$ is $O((1+\log\log t)/q)=o(1)$.
The last conclusion follows from \eqref{GRF:broad}: for large $k$,
$\log t\le q/R\le q$, so $\log\log t=O(\log q)=o(q)$.
Therefore
\[
 \frac{\Sigma_\varepsilon}{2q}
 =1+\log\frac1{2u_\varepsilon}+o(1),\qquad
 \frac14e^{-\Sigma_\varepsilon/(2q)}
 =\frac{u_\varepsilon}{2e}(1+o(1)).
\]
Since $1/e<1$, this second quantity is eventually the smaller member
in \eqref{GRF:parameters}, proving \eqref{GRF:cutoffasymptotic}.

We now verify every remaining finite guard and error. The support width
satisfies $v_\varepsilon^2-u_\varepsilon^2\sim4t^2$, so
\[
 m_\varepsilon(v_\varepsilon^2-u_\varepsilon^2)
 \sim2qt^3\longrightarrow\infty.
\]
Furthermore
\[
 \frac{R}{qb_\varepsilon}\sim\frac{eR\log t}{q}
 \longrightarrow0,
\]
which proves the cutoff guard. The exact parameters satisfy
$\alpha_\varepsilon=O(1/t)$ and $\beta_\varepsilon=O(1/t)$, whence
\[
 \Lambda_\varepsilon=O((\log t)^2/t+\log t/t)
 =O((\log t)^2/t)=o(1).
\]
Since $q/\log t\to\infty$, one has
$qu_\varepsilon\to\infty$ and
$r_{-,\varepsilon}\to c_\Gamma/\sqrt2>0$.
Taking logarithms in the exact \eqref{GRF:eta} gives
\begin{align*}
 \log\eta_\varepsilon
 ={}&-q\log8+4\log m_\varepsilon
 +(2\varepsilon+1/2)\log b_\varepsilon
 +\frac32\Lambda_\varepsilon\\
 &+\log\frac{32r_+}{r_{-,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon+1/4)}.
\end{align*}
Here $\log m_\varepsilon=\log q+\log t+O(1)=o(q)$:
$\log q=o(q)$ and \eqref{GRF:broad}, with $R\to\infty$, gives
$\log t=o(q)$. Also
$\log b_\varepsilon=-\log\log t+O(1)=o(q)$, and all other displayed
terms are $o(q)$. Both block errors are therefore
$\exp[-q\log8+o(q)]$; their maximum has the same form and is eventually
less than $1$.

Finally $D_2\le qR^2$ by its root-sum representation and
$M_4\le qR^4$. The two terms in \eqref{GRF:Cchi} are bounded by
\[
 \frac{R^2}{qb_\varepsilon^2}
 \left(1+
 \frac{R^2/(q^2b_\varepsilon^2)}
      {2(1-R^2/(q^2b_\varepsilon^2))}\right).
\]
The parenthesis tends to $1$ and
$b_\varepsilon^{-2}=O((1+\log t)^2)$.
This proves the first estimate in \eqref{GRF:rates}. Dividing it by $q$
and using $R\log t/q\to0$ proves $C_\chi=o(q)$.
\end{proof}
