\section{The reference norm at its actual moving parameters}
\label{GRE:section}

The coordinate throughout is the literal coordinate $y=q\sqrt x$ on
the positive half-line. The underlying measure is
\[
 d\sigma(y)=\frac{|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2}{2\pi}\,dy.
 \tag{GRE1}
\]
It is not divided by its mass. Fix $q>0$, an integer $m\ge1$, and
$\alpha,\beta>0$. Write $\mu=\mu_{\alpha,\beta}$ for the probability
measure constructed in the complete EIQ proof included in the accompanying complete source, with
support $[a,b]=[u^2,v^2]$, $0<u<v$, logarithmic potential
$P(x)=\int\log|x-s|\,d\mu(s)$, and Robin constant $\lambda$ in the
convention
\[
 V(x)=\beta\sqrt x-\alpha\log x,
 \qquad D(x)=V(x)-2P(x)-\lambda\ge0\quad(x>0).
 \tag{GRE2}
\]
Equality holds on $[a,b]$ and is strict outside it. The complete EIQ
argument, included in the accompanying complete source, constructs the endpoints
for every point of $(0,\infty)^2$, proves positivity and mass one,
computes the Cauchy transform, and proves this inequality on both
components of the exterior. In particular the inequality is available
near zero as well as at infinity. Put
\[
 W=v^2-u^2,\qquad c=W/4,\qquad w=(u+v)/2,\qquad z=uv.
 \tag{GRE3}
\]
Here $c$ is logarithmic capacity, and $z$ is a positive scalar, not
a complex integration variable.

\subsection{Exact Robin and logarithmic-moment identities}

The complete ECL1--14 proof is included in the accompanying complete source. Its moving-support
derivative has the following precise test-function meaning:
\[
 \frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}\int f(x)\,d\mu_{\alpha,\beta}(x)
 =\frac12\int_a^b f(x)\left(1-\frac z x\right)
       \frac{dx}{\pi\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}}.
 \tag{GRE4}
\]
It holds at fixed $\beta$ for every continuously differentiable $f$
on a neighbourhood of the support. To track the endpoint terms,
write $p(x)=h(x)/(2\pi)$ and $\rho(x)=p(x)\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}$.
Differentiation at the actual moving endpoints gives the signed density
\[
 \left[\dot p(x)\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}+
 \frac{p(x)\{\dot b(x-a)-\dot a(b-x)\}}
 {2\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}}\right]\mathbf1_{(a,b)}(x).
 \tag{GRE5}
\]
The proof changes variables to $x=a+(b-a)s$, $0\le s\le1$,
differentiates there, and integrates the term containing $f'$ by
parts. The boundary term is proportional to $\sqrt{s(1-s)}$ and
vanishes. The remaining derivative is integrable, bounded by a
constant times $[s(1-s)]^{-1/2}$. Thus no endpoint atom is present
and no moving-support term was dropped. The complete transform and
zero-mass logarithmic-energy calculation in ECL7--10 identifies
(GRE5) exactly with (GRE4).

In particular, setting $\mathcal L=P(0)=\int\log x\,d\mu(x)$ gives
\[
 \begin{split}
 \lambda&=2(\alpha+2)-2\alpha\log w-2\log c,\\
 \mathcal L&=2(\alpha+1)\log w-\alpha\log z-2,\\
 \lambda_\alpha&=-2\log w,\qquad
 \mathcal L_\alpha=2\log w-\log z.
 \end{split}\tag{GRE6}
\]
The constant $-2$ in the logarithmic moment remains. Direct addition,
using $w^2/c=(u+v)/(v-u)$, yields
\[
 \boxed{2P(0)+\lambda
 =2\alpha\left(1+\log\frac w z\right)
      +2\log\frac{v+u}{v-u}.}
 \tag{GRE7}
\]
This is the received equation (6), with the original potential and
Robin sign convention.

For clarity, the second Robin derivative also retains all support
movement. For $\beta_0>0$ put $c_0=\beta_0/\beta$. The dilation
$x\mapsto c_0^2x$ pushes $\mu_{\alpha,\beta_0}$ to
$\mu_{\alpha,\beta}$: on every probability measure its energy changes
by the constant $-2(\alpha+1)\log c_0$, so the proved unique
minimizer is carried to the proved unique minimizer. At corresponding
points its potential is $2\log c_0+P_{\alpha,\beta_0}(x)$, while
its external field is $V_{\alpha,\beta_0}(x)-2\alpha\log c_0$.
Consequently
\[
 \lambda(\alpha,\beta)
 =\lambda(\alpha,\beta_0)
       +2(\alpha+2)\log(\beta/\beta_0),\qquad
 \boxed{\lambda_\beta=\frac{2(\alpha+2)}\beta.}
 \tag{GRE8}
\]
The distinct identity
$\int\sqrt x\,d\mu=2(\alpha+1)/\beta$ concerns the moment, not
the Robin derivative. It follows by differentiating the energy of
the competitor $x\mapsto c_0^2x$ at $c_0=1$ as in EIQ31.

\subsection{Original Gamma constants, mass, and monic factorials}

Retain exactly
\[
 c_\Gamma=\sqrt2e^{-7/6},\quad
 C_\Gamma=\sqrt{2\sqrt5}\,e^{2/3},\quad
 \mathfrak r(y)=\frac{\sigma(y)e^{\pi y/2}\sqrt y}{\sqrt2}
 \quad(y>0).
 \tag{GRE9}
\]
The identity rederived here is Binet's integral formula in the
human-authored Gamma chapter of Askey and Roy
\cite[eq.~(5.9.10\_2)]{human:dlmf5}, valid for $\Re z>0$.
Its integration variable $t$ is the present $s$, and its kernel is
exactly
\[
 \frac1s\left(\frac1{e^s-1}-\frac1s+\frac12\right)
 =\frac{\coth(s/2)-2/s}{2s}=K(s).
\]
The holomorphic logarithm of $\Gamma$ is the branch real on the
positive axis; $\log z$ is principal on this half-plane. At
$z=\zeta=1/4+iy/2$ the local BI17--19 bound gives
$|R(\zeta)|\le(12\Re\zeta)^{-1}=1/3$.
With the retained density
$\sigma(y)=|\Gamma(\zeta)|^2/(2\pi)$, twice the real part
gives GRE10. The explicit constants in GRE9--11 are then obtained
by the displayed local inequalities.

The complete derivation of Binet's identity from the defining Gamma
integral is reproduced in the accompanying complete source as BI1--26. Its remainder kernel is
$K(s)=(\coth(s/2)-2/s)/(2s)$ with $0<K(s)<1/12$. Therefore at
$\zeta=1/4+iy/2$ its remainder satisfies $|R(\zeta)|\le1/3$,
and twice the real part of that exact identity gives, with $Y=|y|$,
\[
 \sigma(y)=|\zeta|^{-1/2}
 \exp\{-Y\arctan(2Y)-1/2+2\Re R(\zeta)\}.
 \tag{GRE10}
\]
For $Y>0$, $Y\arctan(1/(2Y))\le1/2$, whereas
$\arctan(2Y)\le\pi/2$; the same resulting inequalities hold at
$Y=0$. Thus the exponent in (GRE10) lies between
$-\pi Y/2-7/6$ and $-\pi Y/2+2/3$. Also
\[
 \frac{(1+Y)^2}{4}-|\zeta|^2=\frac{3+8Y}{16},\qquad
 |\zeta|^2-\frac{(1+Y)^2}{20}=\frac{(4Y-1)^2}{80}.
\]
Taking the appropriate positive fourth roots proves
\[
 c_\Gamma e^{-\pi|y|/2}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}
 \le\sigma(y)\le
 C_\Gamma e^{-\pi|y|/2}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}.
 \tag{GRE11}
\]
In particular all polynomial moments are finite and
\[
 r_+=C_\Gamma/\sqrt2,\qquad
 r_-=(c_\Gamma/\sqrt2)\sqrt{\frac{qu}{1+qu}},\qquad
 \mathfrak r(y)\le r_+,\quad
 \mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\ge r_-\ (a\le x\le b).
 \tag{GRE12}
\]
The lower estimate follows from the increasing function $y/(1+y)$.

For completeness, the mass and factorial monic norms can be recovered
without selecting another measure. The beta identity, proved in
BI13, with $a>0$ and the substitution $x=\log(t/(1-t))$, gives
\[
 \frac{\Gamma(a+i\eta)\Gamma(a-i\eta)}{\Gamma(2a)}
 =\int_{\mathbb R}e^{i\eta x}
        (2\cosh(x/2))^{-2a}\,dx.
 \tag{GRE13}
\]
The beta integrand after substitution is absolutely integrable.
At $a=1/4$, Fourier inversion therefore gives
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{i\xi y}\,d\sigma(y)
 =\sqrt{2\pi}(\cosh\xi)^{-1/2}.
 \tag{GRE14}
\]
Here Fourier inversion can be justified directly by inserting a
Gaussian factor $e^{-\delta\eta^2}$ in the inversion integral,
using Fubini, and evaluating the Gaussian Fourier integral. The result
is convolution with the probability density
$(4\pi\delta)^{-1/2}e^{-x^2/(4\delta)}$, which tends to the continuous
beta density at each point: split at $|x|=\varepsilon$, use uniform
continuity on the first piece and the Gaussian tail on the second.
On the Fourier side dominated convergence is valid by (GRE11), with
$y=2\eta$. This proves the inversion used in (GRE14).
Both sides of (GRE14) extend holomorphically to $|\Im\xi|<\pi/2$:
for the left side (GRE11) gives locally uniform exponential domination;
for the right side the branch is positive at zero and has no zero
of $\cosh\xi$ in the strip. The identity theorem consequently gives
\[
 \int e^{\theta y}\,d\sigma(y)
 =\sqrt{2\pi}(\cos\theta)^{-1/2}\quad(|\theta|<\pi/2).
 \tag{GRE15}
\]
This retains mass $\sqrt{2\pi}$ at $\theta=0$.

Define monic polynomials $p_n$ by the convergent generating series
\[
 F(y,z)=(1+z^2)^{-1/4}e^{y\arctan z}
       =\sum_{n\ge0}p_n(y)\frac{z^n}{n!}.
\]
The coefficient of $y^n$ in $p_n$ is one, because
$\arctan z=z+O(z^3)$ and the other prefactor has value one at zero.
For sufficiently small real $z,w$, (GRE15) and
$\cos(\arctan z+\arctan w)=(1-zw)/\sqrt{(1+z^2)(1+w^2)}$
give the exact generating identity
\[
 \int F(y,z)F(y,w)\,d\sigma(y)
 =\sqrt{2\pi}(1-zw)^{-1/2}.
\]
Termwise differentiation at zero is justified by (GRE11) and
exponential domination in a neighbourhood of $(z,w)=(0,0)$. Hence
\[
 \int p_n p_j\,d\sigma=\mathbf1_{n=j}\,
 \sqrt{2\pi}\,n!(1/2)_n,
 \qquad
 \gamma_n=\sqrt{2\pi}\,n!(1/2)_n
          =\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{(2n)!}{4^n}.
 \tag{GRE16}
\]
For a complex monic degree-$n$ polynomial, subtraction of $p_n$
leaves degree at most $n-1$, orthogonal to $p_n$. Pythagoras proves
that $\gamma_n$ is precisely the monic minimum; the product equality
in (GRE16) follows by separating the even and odd factors of $(2n)!$.

\subsection{A global density bound and a full tail integral}

The actual positive density in EIQ10--11 obeys
\[
 0<h(x)\le\frac\alpha{xuv},\qquad
 \rho(x)=\frac{\sqrt{(v^2-x)(x-u^2)}}{2\pi}h(x).
\]
For $u^2\le x\le v^2$,
$(v^2-x)(x-u^2)\le v^2x$. It follows, including the zero endpoint
values, that
\[
 \boxed{\|\rho\|_\infty\le
 \mathcal M:=\frac\alpha{2\pi u^2}.}
 \tag{GRE17}
\]
This is the received equation (16).

Set, at these very parameters,
\[
 \begin{split}
 C_\lambda&=2\log2+\max(\lambda,0),\\
 T_*&=\max\left\{1,v,
       \left(\frac{8(\alpha+2)}\beta\right)^2,
       \frac{4C_\lambda}\beta\right\},\qquad
 J_*=T_*^2+\frac8{\beta^2}.
 \end{split}\tag{GRE18}
\]
For $s\ge\max\{1,v\}$ and every support point $y$,
$|s^2-y|\le s^2+v^2\le2s^2$. The logarithm is integrable even
if $s=v$, so integration gives $P(s^2)\le2\log s+\log2$. Therefore
\[
 D(s^2)\ge\beta s-2(\alpha+2)\log s-C_\lambda.
\]
For $s\ge1$, $\log s\le\sqrt s$: the maximum of
$\log s/\sqrt s$ is $2/e<1$, obtained by direct differentiation.
If $s\ge T_*$, then
$2(\alpha+2)\log s\le2(\alpha+2)\sqrt s\le\beta s/4$
and $C_\lambda\le\beta s/4$. Consequently
\[
 D(s^2)\ge\beta s/2\quad(s\ge T_*),\qquad
 \int_0^\infty e^{-mD(x)}\,dx
 \le T_*^2+\int_0^\infty2s e^{-\beta s/2}\,ds=J_*.
 \tag{GRE19}
\]
The first interval uses $D\ge0$, and the tail uses $m\ge1$.
The integral starts at zero. All constants are finite at each point
of the full positive parameter quadrant.

\subsection{Arcsine lower bound and explicit quantile upper bound}

Define the actual monic minimum
\[
 h_{m,q}(\alpha,\beta)=
 \min_{p\in\mathbb C[x],\ p\ \text{monic},\ \deg p=m}
 \int_0^\infty |p(x)|^2e^{-mV(x)}
                   \mathfrak r(q\sqrt x)\,dx.
 \tag{GRE20}
\]
The integrals are finite: the weight near zero is at most
$r_+x^{m\alpha}$ and at infinity at most
$r_+x^{m\alpha}e^{-m\beta\sqrt x}$. Its density is positive on
$(0,\infty)$, so its finite coefficient Gram matrix is positive
definite. Completing the square in that finite matrix proves existence
and uniqueness of the monic minimizer.

Let $d\nu=dx/(\pi\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)})$ be arcsine probability
on $[a,b]$. Its potential satisfies
\[
 \int\log|x-\zeta|\,d\nu(x)\ge\log c
   \quad(\zeta\in\mathbb C),\qquad
 \int\log|x-y|\,d\nu(x)=\log c\quad(y\in[a,b]).
 \tag{GRE21}
\]
To prove both statements rootwise, put $C=(a+b)/2$, $d=W/2$ and
$x=C+d\cos\theta$. The polynomial
$Z^2-2(\zeta-C)Z/d+1$ has roots $z_1,z_2$ with product one.
On the unit circle,
$|x-\zeta|=(d/2)|Z-z_1||Z-z_2|$.
The circle mean of $\log|Z-z_j|$ equals
$\log\max\{1,|z_j|\}$. For $|z_j|<1$ this follows by integrating
the absolutely convergent logarithmic series, for $|z_j|>1$ by
factoring $|z_j|$, and for $|z_j|=1$ by a limit dominated near the
single singular angle by a constant plus $|\log|\theta||$.
The dependence on $\cos\theta$ identifies the whole-circle mean
with the arcsine integral. Both roots have modulus one precisely
when $\zeta\in[a,b]$, proving the equality; their two log-max
terms are nonnegative, proving the inequality. All logarithmic
integrals just used are absolutely integrable.

For a monic degree-$m$ polynomial $p$, factorization and (GRE21)
give $\int\log|p|\,d\nu\ge m\log c$. Integrate the Euler equality
against $\nu$. Fubini is justified by the bounded equilibrium
density and the integrable arcsine logarithmic singularity, and gives
\[
 \int V\,d\nu=2\log c+\lambda.
 \tag{GRE22}
\]
The arcsine entropy is exactly
\[
 \int\log\frac{d\nu}{dx}\,d\nu=-\log(\pi c).
 \tag{GRE23}
\]
Indeed $\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}=(W/2)\sin\theta$, and
$\pi^{-1}\int_0^\pi\log\sin\theta\,d\theta=-\log2$.
For the latter identity, put $I=\int_0^{\pi/2}\log\sin\theta\,d\theta$.
Symmetry gives the same cosine integral, while doubling the argument
in $\log\sin\theta+\log\cos\theta=\log\sin(2\theta)-\log2$
gives $2I=I-(\pi/2)\log2$. Endpoint logarithms are integrable.

Probability Jensen applied to
$|p|^2e^{-mV}/(d\nu/dx)$, whose logarithm is integrable, now proves
\[
 \int_a^b |p|^2e^{-mV}\,dx
 \ge\exp\left\{2\int\log|p|\,d\nu-m\int V\,d\nu
                    -\int\log(d\nu/dx)\,d\nu\right\}
 \ge\pi c\,e^{-m\lambda}.
 \tag{GRE24}
\]
Multiplication by the lower remainder bound in (GRE12) gives the
claimed lower monic bound.

For the upper bound take the unique midpoint quantiles $x_i\in(a,b)$
with $\mu((0,x_i])=(i-1/2)/m$, $1\le i\le m$, and define
$p_m(x)=\prod_{i=1}^m(x-x_i)$. The distribution function of its
empirical probability measure differs from that of $\mu$ by at most
$1/(2m)$, and hence by at most $1/m$. For fixed $x>0$ put
\[
 \delta=1/m,\qquad f_x(y)=\log\max\{|x-y|,\delta\}.
\]
This is continuous and of bounded variation on $[a,b]$. If $x$ lies
inside $[a,b]$, its decreasing and increasing pieces each have
variation at most $\log(1+W/\delta)$. If $x$ lies outside the
interval it is monotone and its endpoint ratio after clipping is
at most $1+W/\delta$. Thus, in all cases,
$\operatorname{Var}_{[a,b]} f_x\le2\log(1+mW)$.
Stieltjes integration by parts against the zero-mass empirical error
has zero endpoint term, and gives
\[
 \sum_{i=1}^m f_x(x_i)
 \le m\int f_x\,d\mu+2\log(1+mW).
 \tag{GRE25}
\]
By (GRE17),
\[
 \int(f_x(y)-\log|x-y|)\,d\mu(y)
 \le\mathcal M\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}
                      \log(\delta/|s|)\,ds
 =2\mathcal M\delta.
 \tag{GRE26}
\]
Each nodal logarithm is at most its clipped value; at a root its
value is $-\infty$, where the resulting inequality is automatic.
Combining (GRE25)--(GRE26), then multiplying by $e^{-mV}$, yields
the global positive-half-line estimate
\[
 |p_m(x)|^2e^{-mV(x)}
 \le e^{4\mathcal M}(1+mW)^4
                         e^{-m\lambda}e^{-mD(x)}\quad(x>0).
 \tag{GRE27}
\]
Integrating it with (GRE12) and (GRE19) proves the complete finite
norm theorem
\[
 \boxed{c_-e^{-m\lambda}\le h_{m,q}(\alpha,\beta)
                    \le c_+e^{-m\lambda},\qquad
 c_-=r_-\pi W/4,\quad
 c_+=r_+J_*e^{4\mathcal M}(1+mW)^4.}
 \tag{GRE28}
\]
This is the received (17). Neither its proof nor its constants require
a compact parameter rectangle, an endpoint lower density estimate,
or a lower bound on quantile-cell lengths.

\subsection{Exact parity map, shifts, and factorial cancellation}

Let $q\ge1$ be an integer and $r=2m+\varepsilon$ an integer with
$m\ge1$, $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}$. Define the full homogeneous
reference monic minimum
\[
 \nu_r^0=
 \min_{p\ \text{monic},\ \deg p=r}
         \int_{\mathbb R}y^{2q}|p(y)|^2\,d\sigma(y).
 \tag{GRE29}
\]
An even positive weight makes the even and odd coefficient spaces
orthogonal. For any monic degree-$r$ polynomial, the component with
parity opposite to $r$ contributes a nonnegative separate squared norm and has no leading
term, so the unique monic minimizer has parity $r$. The exact bijection
between its parity fibre and the monic degree-$m$ fibre is
\[
 p(y)=q^r(y/q)^\varepsilon P(y^2/q^2).
 \tag{GRE30}
\]
The leading coefficient is $q^{r-\varepsilon-2m}=1$, and expansion
in powers proves surjectivity and injectivity. Both half-lines
contribute equally. Substitution $y=q\sqrt x$ has Jacobian
$q/(2\sqrt x)$, and (GRE9) gives exactly
\[
 \boxed{\nu_r^0=\sqrt2\,q^{2(q+r)+1/2}
      h_{m,q}(\alpha,\beta),\qquad
 \alpha=\frac{q+\varepsilon-3/4}{m},\quad
 \beta=\frac{\pi q}{2m}.}
 \tag{GRE31}
\]
Every mass and power, including the quarter shift, is retained.

Put $t=r/q$, $\alpha_0=2/t$, $\beta_0=\pi/t$, and
$\lambda_0=\lambda(\alpha_0,\beta_0)$. The differences are exactly
\[
 A:=m(\alpha-\alpha_0)=\varepsilon-\tfrac34+
                              \frac{q\varepsilon}{r},\qquad
 B:=m(\beta-\beta_0)=\frac{\pi q\varepsilon}{2r}.
 \tag{GRE32}
\]
Let $(\alpha_\theta,\beta_\theta)
=(\alpha_0,\beta_0)+\theta(\alpha-\alpha_0,\beta-\beta_0)$,
$0\le\theta\le1$, and let $w_\theta$ be its actual midpoint
$(u_\theta+v_\theta)/2$. All these parameter pairs are positive.
The full support derivatives in (GRE6),(GRE8) and the fundamental
theorem of calculus give
\[
 \begin{split}
 \Delta&:=m\lambda(\alpha,\beta)-\frac r2\lambda_0\\
 &=\int_0^1\left[-2A\log w_\theta+
              B\frac{2(\alpha_\theta+2)}{\beta_\theta}\right]d\theta
             -\frac\varepsilon2\lambda_0.
 \end{split}\tag{GRE33}
\]
Thus the finite, explicitly evaluable parameter bound is
\[
 |\Delta|\le B_{\rm par}:=
 2|A|\int_0^1|\log w_\theta|\,d\theta+
 2|B|\int_0^1\frac{\alpha_\theta+2}{\beta_\theta}\,d\theta
 +\frac\varepsilon2|\lambda_0|.
 \tag{GRE34}
\]
No fixed-endpoint differentiation is used in this formula.

Define the original profile in its exact Robin form
\[
 \psi(t)=(1+t)(1-\log(1+t))
                  -\frac t4\lambda(2/t,\pi/t).
 \tag{GRE35}
\]
To identify it explicitly, let $\kappa\in(0,1)$ be the EIQ modulus
and $s=\sqrt{1-\kappa^2}$. The endpoint equations at these actual
parameters give
\[
 u_tK=2,\qquad v_tE=2(1+t),\qquad
 1+t=\frac E{sK},\quad
 w_t=\frac{(1+t)(1+s)}E,\quad
 c_t=\frac{(1+t)^2\kappa^2}{E^2}.
\]
Inserting these formulas into (GRE6),(GRE35), with no endpoint change,
gives
\[
 \psi(t)=\log\frac{1+s}E+t\log\frac\kappa E.
 \tag{GRE36}
\]
This is precisely the original UCT26 profile.

Write $n=q+r$. BI26 evaluated at $2n$ and the recurrence
$\Gamma(2n+1)=2n\Gamma(2n)$ give
\[
 \log(2n)!=(2n+\tfrac12)\log(2n)-2n+
               \tfrac12\log(2\pi)+R_{2n},
 \qquad0<R_{2n}<\frac1{24n}.
\]
Combine this with (GRE16),(GRE31). Exact cancellation gives
\[
 \log\frac{\nu_r^0}{\gamma_{q+r}}
 =2(q+r)(1-\log(1+t))+\log h_{m,q}
        -\tfrac12\log(1+t)-\log(2\pi)-R_{2(q+r)}.
 \tag{GRE37}
\]
Equivalently, for $X=\log h_{m,q}+m\lambda$,
\[
 \boxed{\log\frac{\nu_r^0}{\gamma_{q+r}}
 =2q\psi(t)+X-\Delta-
         \tfrac12\log(1+t)-\log(2\pi)-R_{2(q+r)},
 \quad\log c_-\le X\le\log c_+.}
 \tag{GRE38}
\]
The useful finite endpoints, before any asymptotic replacement, are
also exactly
\[
 \log\frac{\sqrt2q^{2(q+r)+1/2}}{\gamma_{q+r}}
 -m\lambda+\log c_-
 \ \le\ \log\frac{\nu_r^0}{\gamma_{q+r}}
 \ \le\
 \log\frac{\sqrt2q^{2(q+r)+1/2}}{\gamma_{q+r}}
 -m\lambda+\log c_+.
 \tag{GRE39}
\]

\subsection{Uniform size of the retained errors}

The elementary elliptic proof included in the accompanying complete source
establishes, uniformly also along the segment in (GRE33),
\[
 u_\theta\sim\frac2{\log t},\quad v_\theta\sim2t,\quad
 W\sim4t^2,\quad w_\theta\sim t,
 \quad |\lambda_0|+|\lambda|=O(1+\log t)
 \tag{GRE40}
\]
as $q\to\infty$, $t\to\infty$. The segment has
$\alpha_\theta=O(1/t)$ and $\beta_\theta\asymp1/t$. Equations
(GRE32)--(GRE34) consequently give
$|\Delta|=O(1+\log t)$, since $A=O(1)$ and $B=O(1/t)$.
The same support estimates in the full finite constants give
\[
 \mathcal M=O((\log t)^2/t),\qquad
 T_*=O(t^2),\qquad J_*=O(t^4).
 \tag{GRE41}
\]
For $T_*$, its third entry is $O(t^2)$, its fourth is
$O(t(1+\log t))=O(t^2)$, and its other entries are $O(t)$.
These statements concern the actual endpoints, rather than compact
rectangle extrema.

For the packet range of the received source, let
$q=[1+k(M-1)](k+1)^2$, with the fixed original multiplicity $M\ge1$,
$R_k=k\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}$, and
$\log t=o(q/R_k)$. Then $q/\log t\to\infty$, hence $qu\to\infty$
and $r_-$ is bounded above and below by fixed positive constants.
Thus $c_-\asymp t^2$, and
\[
 |\log c_-|+|\log c_+|=O(\log(q+r)).
 \tag{GRE42}
\]
Indeed $\mathcal M=O(1)$, $\log J_*=O(1+\log t)$ and
$mW=O(rt^2)$; since $q\ge1$, $t=r/q\le r$, so
$\log(1+rt^2)=O(\log(q+r))$. Lower bounds for $J_*$ and
$1+mW$ are one, so this also bounds the absolute logarithm of
$c_+$, not only its positive part. The positive lower bound of $r_+$
is fixed. Similarly (GRE40) and $r_-\asymp1$ bound both signs of
$\log c_-$.

Finally (GRE38), (GRE34), (GRE42) and
$0<R_{2(q+r)}<1/(24(q+r))$ prove
\[
 \boxed{\log\frac{\nu_r^0}{\gamma_{q+r}}
       =2q\psi(r/q)+O(\log(q+r)).}
 \tag{GRE43}
\]
The constants are uniform along every such growing-ratio sequence
and for either parity. This is the received equation (18). All finite
content is already specified by (GRE18), (GRE28), (GRE33), and
(GRE38)--(GRE39); no unspecified equilibrium norm theorem occurs.

\label{GRBUILD:REFERENCE_NORM_PROOF:end}

\section{Original arithmetic transfer and the complete shifted action}
\label{gra:section}

This section proves the arithmetic receiving calculation for the moving
equilibrium comparison.  It retains the stipulated complete zero quartet,
all source masses and divided jets, the original remainder quotient, and
both adjacent contraction factors.  The moving-cutoff full-form theorem
and the moving-parameter reference-norm theorem are the analytic results
proved in the companion sections.  Their exact conclusions are displayed
where used; none of their constants is replaced by a compact-parameter
constant.  The new calculation here is the exact arithmetic transfer,
its degree-uniform asymptotic consequences, and its original complete-action
receiver.

\subsection{Objects, coefficient maps, and all-degree arithmetic constants}

Fix the original complete quartet data
\[
 0<\delta<\tfrac12,\quad \gamma>2,\quad m\in\mathbb N_{>0},\quad
 h(z)=\prod_{\zeta\in\{1/2\pm\delta\pm i\gamma\}}(z-\zeta)^m,
 \qquad v_h=2\xi/h.
\]
The zero order at each centre is exactly the stated $m$, so the entire
quotient $v_h$ is nonzero at every centre.  Along the original tensor
subsequence put
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 k=4\ell+1\ge5,\quad c=k/2,\quad e_k=1+k(m-1),\quad
 q=e_k(k+1)^2,\quad R_k=k\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2},\\
 \lambda_{ab}=c+(2a-k)\delta+i(2b-k)\gamma,\qquad
 \chi(S)=\prod_{a,b=0}^k(S-\lambda_{ab})^{e_k},\\
 Q(y)=i^{-q}\chi(c+iy),\qquad E=\mathbb C[S]/(\chi),\qquad
 w_h(y)=|v_h(1/2+iy)|^2/(2\pi),\\
 d\mu_k(y)=w_h^{*k}(y)\,dy,\quad
 d\sigma(y)=\frac{|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2}{2\pi}\,dy.
 \end{gathered}\tag{GRA1}\label{gra:objects}
\end{equation}
The masses are $(\int w_h)^k$ and $\sqrt{2\pi}$, respectively.  For
$P\in\mathcal P_j(S)=\mathbb C[S]_{\le j}$ the arithmetic squared norm is
$\|P\|_{\rm ar}^2=\int|P(c+iy)|^2d\mu_k(y)$; the corresponding source
Gamma norm uses $d\sigma$ in the same coordinate.  Inner products are
conjugate-linear in their first argument.  Every polynomial coefficient
below may be complex.

The precise coefficient maps are
\begin{equation}
 U_jP(y)=i^{-j}P(c+iy),\qquad
 U_j^{-1}p(S)=i^jp((S-c)/i),\qquad
 U_{q+j}M_\chi=M_QU_j.
 \tag{GRA2}\label{gra:coordinates}
\end{equation}
Direct substitution proves both inverse identities and the multiplication
identity.  The leading coefficient of a polynomial of degree $j$ is
unchanged.  The leading coefficient of a polynomial of degree $a<j$ is
multiplied by $i^{a-j}$, which is retained.  Thus $U_j$ bijects the two
degree-$j$ monic affine spaces, and its scalar phase has modulus one in
each pulled-back integral norm.  No Euclidean norm of a coefficient vector
is asserted to be invariant under translation.

Write $\alpha_* =\pi/2$ to distinguish this fixed tail constant from
the moving equilibrium parameters.  The original AMT/TW constants are
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 p_h=8m-\tfrac92,\quad B_h=42+8m,\quad M_h=21+4m,\\
 \vartheta_h=\int_{-1}^1w_h(y)\,dy,\quad
 M_{\alpha_*}=\int_{\mathbb R}e^{\alpha_*y}w_h(y)\,dy,\\
 c_\Gamma=\sqrt2e^{-7/6},\qquad
 C_\Gamma=\sqrt{2\sqrt5}\,e^{2/3},\\
 a_k=\frac{c_h\vartheta_h^{k-3}e^{-\alpha_*(k-3)}(k-2)^{-B_h}}
 {C_\Gamma2^{B_h/2}},\qquad
 A_k=\frac{C_h^{\rm tilt}}{c_\Gamma}k^{p_h+1}M_{\alpha_*}^{k-1},\\
 D_d=[1+4(d+M_h)^2]^{M_h},\qquad
 L_{k,d}=\log(A_kD_d/a_k).
 \end{gathered}\tag{GRA3}\label{gra:constants}
\end{equation}
Here $c_h$ and $C_h^{\rm tilt}$ are exactly the positive original TW7
convolution lower-envelope and BT12 compact/tail constants.  Their
already proved defining estimates, with the full numerator $2\xi/h$, are
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 w_h^{*k}(y)&\ge c_h\vartheta_h^{k-3}
 e^{-\alpha_*(|y|+k-3)}(k-2+|y|)^{-B_h},\\
 w_h^{*k}(y)&\le kC_h^{\rm tilt}M_{\alpha_*}^{k-1}
 e^{-\alpha_*|y|}(1+|y|/k)^{-p_h},\\
 c_\Gamma e^{-\alpha_*|y|}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}
 &\le d\sigma/dy\le
 C_\Gamma e^{-\alpha_*|y|}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{GRA4}\label{gra:densitybounds}
\end{equation}
These bounds hold for every convolution order $k\ge3$.  In particular
there is no upper polynomial-degree restriction in the following argument.

The first and third estimates, $k-2+|y|\le(k-2)(1+|y|)$, and
$(1+|y|)^{B_h}\le2^{B_h/2}(1+y^2)^{M_h}$ give
$w_h^{*k}\ge a_k(1+y^2)^{-M_h}d\sigma/dy$.  The second and third,
together with $1+|y|/k\ge(1+|y|)/k$ and $p_h>1/2$, give
$w_h^{*k}\le A_kd\sigma/dy$.
For the original Gamma orthonormal polynomials $e_n$ one has
\[
 ye_n=\sqrt{(n+1)(n+1/2)}e_{n+1}
       +\sqrt{n(n-1/2)}e_{n-1}.
\]
The two shifts on polynomials of degree at most $d$ have norms at most
$d+1$ and $d$, respectively.  Hence
$\|yf\|_\sigma\le2(d+1)\|f\|_\sigma$.  Applying this inequality
successively gives, for $0\le j\le M_h$,
$\|y^jf\|_\sigma^2\le[4(d+M_h)^2]^j\|f\|_\sigma^2$.
Expansion of $(1+y^2)^{M_h}$ therefore bounds its weighted squared norm
by $D_d\|f\|_\sigma^2$.  Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\[
 \|f\|_\sigma^4\le
 \left(\int|f|^2(1+y^2)^{-M_h}d\sigma\right)
 \left(\int|f|^2(1+y^2)^{M_h}d\sigma\right).
\]
Combining these inequalities and using the bijective substitution
$S=c+iy$ proves, on the complete coefficient space and at every $d\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{a_k}{D_d}H_d^\sigma\preceq H_d^{\rm ar}
 \preceq A_kH_d^\sigma.
 \tag{GRA5}\label{gra:AMT}
\end{equation}
Both forms are positive definite by their positive densities, and all
moments are finite by their exponential upper bounds.  Applying
\eqref{gra:AMT} to a nonzero vector proves $L_{k,d}\ge0$.
Its dependence on degree is explicit.  Subtracting the logarithms in
\eqref{gra:constants} gives
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \log(A_k/a_k)=k\mathfrak s_h+(p_h+1)\log k
             +B_h\log(k-2)+\mathfrak c_h,\\
 \mathfrak s_h=\alpha_*+\log(M_{\alpha_*}/\vartheta_h),\\
 \mathfrak c_h=-\log M_{\alpha_*}+3\log\vartheta_h-3\alpha_*
 +\log\frac{C_h^{\rm tilt}C_\Gamma2^{B_h/2}}{c_hc_\Gamma},\\
 L_{k,d}=O_h(k+\log(d+1)).
 \end{gathered}\tag{GRA6}\label{gra:Lgrowth}
\end{equation}
The constant implicit here is independent of $d$ and $k$.

\subsection{Full forms, fixed affine fibres, and arithmetic monic minima}

For $p\in\mathcal P_r(y)$ retain the two full forms
\[
 \mathcal N_0[p]=\int y^{2q}|p(y)|^2d\sigma(y),\qquad
 \mathcal N_\chi[p]=\int|Q(y)p(y)|^2d\sigma(y).
\]
The companion moving-cutoff theorem gives, with its actual finite
constants $C_\chi(k,r)$ and $\eta(k,r)$ and all its displayed guards,
\begin{equation}
 e^{-C_\chi}(1-\eta)\mathcal N_0[p]
 \le\mathcal N_\chi[p]\le(1+\eta)\mathcal N_0[p],
 \qquad 0\le\eta<1.
 \tag{GRA7}\label{gra:analyticform}
\end{equation}
It is the original $Q$ on the entire real line.  In particular this
theorem does not remove the inner integration interval.  Applying
\eqref{gra:AMT} at $d=q+r$ to $\chi U_r^{-1}p$ and using
\eqref{gra:coordinates} proves the exactly typed form of the arithmetic
receiver:
\begin{equation}
 \frac{a_k}{D_{q+r}}e^{-C_\chi}(1-\eta)\mathcal N_0[p]
 \le\|\chi U_r^{-1}p\|_{\rm ar}^2
 \le A_k(1+\eta)\mathcal N_0[p].
 \tag{GRA8}\label{gra:arform}
\end{equation}
Indeed the middle integrand after substitution is exactly $|Q(y)p(y)|^2$.
This specifies the coordinate implicit in the received notation
$\|\chi p\|_{\rm ar}$.

For completeness the same constants act on every unchanged affine
coefficient fibre.  Let $C:\mathcal P_r(y)\to F$ be a specified complex
linear map and $z\in\operatorname{im}C$.  Each positive form has a unique
minimum on $Cp=z$: after choosing one lift, minimization is a strictly
positive quadratic problem on $\ker C$.  Apply each inequality of
\eqref{gra:arform} to every vector of this same nonempty fibre and take
the infima.  This proves the two inequalities for the resulting minimum
forms, also at $z=0$.  In original coordinates the observation is
$C U_r$, and $U_r$ is the exact fibre bijection.  Restriction follows by
applying the form inequality to every vector in the specified subspace.
No mixed matrix entry is set equal to zero by either operation.

Let $\omega_d^{\rm ar}$ be the degree-$d$ monic arithmetic squared norm,
and retain the original Gamma monic squared norm
\[
 \gamma_d=\sqrt{2\pi}\,d!(1/2)_d
          =\sqrt{2\pi}\,(2d)!/4^d.
\]
The mass is its degree-zero value; the Gamma recurrence gives
$\gamma_d/\gamma_{d-1}=d(d-1/2)$.  The monic bijection $U_d$ and
\eqref{gra:AMT} give
\begin{equation}
 \frac{a_k}{D_d}\gamma_d\le\omega_d^{\rm ar}\le A_k\gamma_d.
 \tag{GRA9}\label{gra:denominator}
\end{equation}
Define the original relation minima and ratios by
\[
 \nu_r^{\rm ar}=\min_{P(S)\text{ monic},\,\deg P=r}
                 \|\chi P\|_{\rm ar}^2,\quad
 \nu_r^0=\min_{p(y)\text{ monic},\,\deg p=r}\mathcal N_0[p],\quad
 T_r=\nu_r^{\rm ar}/\omega_{q+r}^{\rm ar}.
\]
Taking these identical monic affine minima in \eqref{gra:arform}, then
dividing by the opposite endpoints of \eqref{gra:denominator}, proves
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \log T_r&\ge\log(\nu_r^0/\gamma_{q+r})-L_{k,q+r}
                 -C_\chi+\log(1-\eta),\\
 \log T_r&\le\log(\nu_r^0/\gamma_{q+r})+L_{k,q+r}
                 +\log(1+\eta).
 \end{aligned}\tag{GRA10}\label{gra:monictransfer}
\end{equation}
The denominator is the actual arithmetic monic minimum throughout.

Here are the complete finite scalar endpoints obtained from the companion
reference theorem.  Write $r=2n_r+\varepsilon_r$ with
$\varepsilon_r\in\{0,1\}$, and retain its actual moving parameters,
support, Robin constant $\lambda_r$, and positive constants $c_{-,r}$,
$c_{+,r}$.  That theorem proves
\[
 c_{-,r}e^{-n_r\lambda_r}
 \le h_{n_r,q}\le c_{+,r}e^{-n_r\lambda_r},\qquad
 \nu_r^0=\sqrt2q^{2(q+r)+1/2}h_{n_r,q}.
\]
Consequently, with every constant evaluated at this $r$,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 Z_{q,r}&=\log\frac{\sqrt2q^{2(q+r)+1/2}}{\gamma_{q+r}}
                  -n_r\lambda_r,\\
 \underline E_r&=Z_{q,r}+\log c_{-,r}-L_{k,q+r}
                  -C_\chi(k,r)+\log(1-\eta(k,r)),\\
 \overline E_r&=Z_{q,r}+\log c_{+,r}+L_{k,q+r}
                  +\log(1+\eta(k,r)),\\
 \underline E_r&\le\log T_r\le\overline E_r.
 \end{aligned}\tag{GRA11}\label{gra:finiteE}
\end{equation}
These are usable finite endpoints wherever the analytic theorem's finite
guards hold.  They are obtained by inequalities, not by replacing an
arithmetic norm with an equilibrium norm.

\subsection{Original quotient, full-jet support, and adjacent allowance}

For $N\ge q-1$ the literal remainder map $J_N:\mathcal P_N(S)\to E$
is onto and has kernel $\chi\mathcal P_{N-q}(S)$; at $N=q-1$ that kernel
is zero.  In the coefficient frame $1,S,\ldots,S^N$ define its quotient
minimum Gram and determinant by
\[
 G_N=(J_N(H_N^{\rm ar})^{-1}J_N^*)^{-1},\qquad V_N=\det G_N.
\]
To verify the precise ratio, use the frame
\[
 (\chi,\chi S,\ldots,\chi S^{N-q};\ 1,S,\ldots,S^{q-1}).
\]
Reordering it by degree makes a unit-triangular coefficient matrix,
because $\chi$ is monic; its determinant therefore has modulus one.
Positive block elimination gives the first equality below.
Gram--Schmidt on the full source and on the relation coefficient space
with norm $\|\chi P\|_{\rm ar}$ gives the next two:
\[
 \det H_N^{\rm ar}=\det H_{\rm rel,N}\det G_N,\qquad
 \det H_N^{\rm ar}=\prod_{d=0}^N\omega_d^{\rm ar},\qquad
 \det H_{\rm rel,N}=\prod_{j=0}^{N-q}\nu_j^{\rm ar}.
\]
Empty products are one.  Dividing consecutive equalities proves
\begin{equation}
 d_{q+r}:=\frac{V_{q+r}}{V_{q+r-1}}=T_r^{-1},\qquad r\ge0.
 \tag{GRA12}\label{gra:quotientratio}
\end{equation}
This is a source/relation determinant identity for the original quotient
$\mathbb C[S]/(\chi)$.

The actual support map remains as follows.  Set $E_h=\mathbb C[z]/(h)$,
$\upsilon_h=j_hv_h\in E_h^\times$, and
$A_\Sigma=\sum_{i=1}^kM_{z_i}$ on $E_h^{\otimes k}$.  Then
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{h,k}:E\longrightarrow E_h^{\otimes k},\qquad
 \eta_{h,k}[P]=\upsilon_h^{\otimes k}P(A_\Sigma)1,\qquad
 \eta_{h,k}M_S=A_\Sigma\eta_{h,k}.
 \tag{GRA13}\label{gra:jets}
\end{equation}
In each local factor the nilpotent has exact order $m$.  In each local
tensor block their sum has exact order $1+k(m-1)$: its power $k(m-1)$
has nonzero coefficient $[k(m-1)]!/[(m-1)!]^k$ on the product of the
top nilpotent powers, and its next power vanishes because at least one
exponent is $m$.  The real and imaginary signs at the four original
centres can be chosen independently, so every pair $(a,b)$ occurs as a
sum centre $\lambda_{ab}$.  Distinct pairs give distinct centres because
$\delta,\gamma>0$.  The annihilator of $1$ under $A_\Sigma$ is therefore
exactly $\chi$.  Multiplication by the full unit preserves this
annihilator, proving injectivity of \eqref{gra:jets}; direct multiplication
proves its intertwining identity.  The original source realization and
jets consequently retain their exact commuting formula
\[
 \mathcal V(P)=P\Bigl(\sum_iD_i\Bigr)F_h^{\otimes k},\qquad
 J^{(k)}\mathcal V(P)=\eta_{h,k}J_NP.
\]
In particular the relation norm above is measured before its quotient
image is zero.  No quotient by $y^q$ enters this map.

Here is the complete adjacent calculation in these same norms.  Let
$p_n(S)=i^nQ_n((S-c)/i)$ be the original monic arithmetic orthogonal
polynomial, let $b_n=[p_n]_\chi$, and put
\[
 R_N=G_N^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^N b_nb_n^*/\omega_n^{\rm ar},\qquad
 \beta_n=\omega_n^{\rm ar}/\omega_{n-1}^{\rm ar}.
\]
Evenness of $w_h$, hence of its convolution, gives $Q_n(-y)=(-1)^nQ_n(y)$.
Multiplication by $S$ has the original recurrence
$Sp_n=p_{n+1}+cp_n-\beta_np_{n-1}$, with the last term absent at zero.
Inserting it into $R_N$ cancels all interior adjacent pairs and gives
\[
 M_SR_N+R_NM_S^*-kR_N
 =\frac{b_{N+1}b_N^*+b_Nb_{N+1}^*}{\omega_N^{\rm ar}}.
\]
Reflection $P(S)\mapsto P(k-S)$ permutes the roots of the even-degree
polynomial $\chi$ and induces an involution $\Sigma$ on $E$.
Reflection is an isometry on every original remainder fibre, so the
unique minimum implies $\Sigma^*G_N\Sigma=G_N$.  Since
$\Sigma b_n=(-1)^nb_n$, it follows that $b_N^*G_Nb_{N+1}=0$.
For $x=G_N^{1/2}b_N$ and $y=G_N^{1/2}b_{N+1}$ the relative Hermitian
control has nonzero eigenvalues $\epsilon_N,-\epsilon_N$, with
\[
 \epsilon_N^2=\|x\|^2\|y\|^2/(\omega_N^{\rm ar})^2.
\]
The determinant lemma for adding and removing the last covariance column
proves
\[
 \frac{b_N^*G_Nb_N}{\omega_N^{\rm ar}}=1-d_N\quad(N\ge q),
 \qquad
 \frac{b_{N+1}^*G_Nb_{N+1}}{\omega_{N+1}^{\rm ar}}=d_{N+1}^{-1}-1.
\]
At $N=q-1$ the first $q$ monic remainder columns form a basis and the first
fraction is exactly one.  Thus no inverse at $q-2$ is used.  Together
with \eqref{gra:quotientratio} these identities prove
\begin{equation}
 \epsilon_{q+r-1}^2=\beta_{q+r}(T_r-1)
 \begin{cases}1,&r=0,\\1-T_{r-1}^{-1},&r\ge1.\end{cases}
 \tag{GRA14}\label{gra:adjacent}
\end{equation}
Only the total pairing vanished.  In the original orthogonal
kernel--boundary split, $x=x_K\oplus x_B$, $y=y_K\oplus y_B$, one has
$x_B^*y_B=-x_K^*y_K$.  The mixed exterior term therefore remains
\begin{equation}
 \|x_K\otimes y_B-y_K\otimes x_B\|^2
 =\|x_K\|^2\|y_B\|^2+\|x_B\|^2\|y_K\|^2+2|x_K^*y_K|^2.
 \tag{GRA15}\label{gra:mixed}
\end{equation}



% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M20_GRA_EXTREMAL
The classical eigenvalue-sum lineage is Ky Fan
\cite{human:fan1949}, as credited by Overton and Womersley
\cite{human:overton1992}; the original rank-two exterior case
used in GRA16 is calculated explicitly below.
% END HUMAN CITATION M20_GRA_EXTREMAL

The unchanged exterior benchmark is also explicit.  Let $E_+$ be the
sum of the complete primary blocks with $a>k/2$.  Since $k$ is odd,
$\dim E_+=q/2$.  The nonzero top wedge of a full primary basis has
additive $M_S$ eigenvalue $\operatorname{Tr}(M_S|_{E_+})$; all nilpotent
parts have trace zero.  The relative Hermitian control's additive
operator on exterior degree $q/2$ has largest eigenvalue $\epsilon_N$,
since it sums distinct members of
$\{\epsilon_N,-\epsilon_N,0,\ldots,0\}$.  Its expectation on the stated
wedge is $2\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}(M_S|_{E_+})-kq/2$.
The wedge stays nonzero under the injective full-jet map
\eqref{gra:jets}.  Summing the positive odd integers $2a-k$ gives
$(k+1)^2/4$.  Therefore
\begin{equation}
 0<\mathcal L_{h,k}:=
 2\delta e_k(k+1)\frac{(k+1)^2}{4}
 =\frac{\delta q(k+1)}2\le\epsilon_N\quad(N\ge q-1).
 \tag{GRA16}\label{gra:benchmark}
\end{equation}
Positive covariance gives $0<d_n\le1$.  Equation
\eqref{gra:adjacent} and the strict positive benchmark first force
$d_q<1$ and then $d_n<1$ at every subsequent degree.  Thus all
logarithms below are defined for the actual objects.

Finally \eqref{gra:denominator} gives the sharpened finite recurrence
enclosure
\begin{equation}
 -L_{k,d}\le
 \log\frac{\beta_d}{d(d-1/2)}
 \le L_{k,d-1},\qquad d\ge1.
 \tag{GRA17}\label{gra:recurrence}
\end{equation}
For its lower bound divide the lower bound for $\omega_d^{\rm ar}$
by the upper bound for $\omega_{d-1}^{\rm ar}$; reverse the choices for
the upper bound.  This is an adjacent norm comparison, not a derivative
of a determinant asymptotic.

\subsection{Growing ratios and uniformity on every shifted index}

The companion moving-parameter reference theorem proves
\[
 \log(\nu_r^0/\gamma_{q+r})=2q\psi(r/q)+O_h(\log(q+r)),
 \qquad \psi(t)\sim\frac1{2t\log t}.
\]
The companion full-form theorem proves, throughout
$t=r/q\to\infty$, $\log t=o(q/R_k)$, that its finite guards eventually
hold and that
$C_\chi=O_h(R_k^2(1+\log t)^2/q)$ and
$\eta=\exp[-q\log8+o(q)]$.  The constants in the reference estimate
are those evaluated at the actual moving support.  Insert these proved
estimates into \eqref{gra:monictransfer}; for $0\le\eta\le1/2$,
$|\log(1\pm\eta)|\le2\eta$.  This proves
\begin{equation}
 \log T_r=2q\psi(t)+
 O_h\left(k+\log(q+r)+\frac{R_k^2}{q}(1+\log t)^2\right).
 \tag{GRA18}\label{gra:scalar}
\end{equation}
In particular the reference exponent has not been presumed larger than
the arithmetic error on the whole of the full-form range.

Now take integers $s=s_k\ge1$ and suppose
\begin{equation}
 t=s/q\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad
 t\log t=o(q/k),\qquad H_k=\frac{q}{t\log t}.
 \tag{GRA19}\label{gra:narrow}
\end{equation}
We prove every needed uniformity directly.  The exact formula for $q$
gives $q\ge k^2$, $q\le C_mk^3$, and
$R_k^2/q\le\delta^2+\gamma^2$ for $k\ge1$.  Eventually $t\ge e$ and
$t\log t\le q/k$, hence $t\le q/k\le C_mk^2$.  It follows that
\[
 \log t=O_m(\log k),\qquad
 \log(q+s+q)=O_m(\log k),\qquad
 (R_k^2/q)(1+\log t)^2=O_h((\log k)^2)=o_h(k).
\]
Thus the error in \eqref{gra:scalar} is $O_h(k)$, while
$H_k/k=q/(kt\log t)\to\infty$.  Also
\[
 \frac{R_k\log t}{q}
 =\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}\,
   \frac{kt\log t}{q}\,\frac1t\longrightarrow0,
\]
so the narrower range lies inside the proved full-form range.

For every integer $j\in\{-1,0,\ldots,q\}$ put
$\tau_j=(s+j)/q$.  Uniformly in this entire set,
\[
 |\tau_j-t|\le1,\quad \tau_j/t=1+O(1/t),\quad
 \log\tau_j=\log t+O(1/t),\quad
 \frac{\tau_j\log\tau_j}{t\log t}=1+O(1/t).
\]
For example the logarithm estimate follows by the mean value theorem
on $[t-1,t+1]$ once $t\ge2$.  These bounds prove the two range
conditions and the $O_h(k)$ error uniformly for all $\tau_j$.
The definition of the scalar limit
$2v\log v\,\psi(v)\to1$ implies uniformity on all $v\ge t-1$:
the supremum of its absolute error on that tail tends to zero.
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 \max_{-1\le j\le q}
 \left|\frac{\log T_{s+j}}{H_k}-1\right|\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{GRA20}\label{gra:uniformT}
\end{equation}
The same argument applied to the finite bounds in
\eqref{gra:finiteE} shows that their lower and upper endpoints are
$H_k(1+o_h(1))$ uniformly.  In particular all the finite lower endpoints
are eventually positive; positivity has been proved in this range,
not inserted as a new asymptotic premise.

For $r\ge1$ take logarithms of the exact adjacent identity:
\begin{equation}
 2\log\epsilon_{q+r-1}
 =\log\beta_{q+r}+\log T_r
 +\log(1-T_r^{-1})+\log(1-T_{r-1}^{-1}).
 \tag{GRA21}\label{gra:rowlog}
\end{equation}
For $r=s,\ldots,s+q$, \eqref{gra:recurrence} and the preceding degree
bounds give $\log\beta_{q+r}=O_h(k)$.  If
$M_k=\min_{s-1\le r\le s+q}\log T_r$, then $M_k/H_k\to1$ and
\[
 0\le-\log(1-T_r^{-1})
 \le\frac{e^{-M_k}}{1-e^{-M_k}}\longrightarrow0
\]
uniformly for both adjacent factors.  Divide \eqref{gra:rowlog} by
$H_k$ and use $k/H_k\to0$ to obtain
\begin{equation}
 \max_{s\le r\le s+q}
 \left|\frac{\log\epsilon_{q+r-1}}{H_k/2}-1\right|\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{GRA22}\label{gra:uniformeps}
\end{equation}
For a single sequence $r$ this proves
$\log\epsilon_{q+r-1}\sim q/(2(r/q)\log(r/q))$ under the same narrower
range.  Since
$\log\mathcal L_{h,k}=\log(\delta/2)+\log q+\log(k+1)=O_h(\log k)$,
it also proves the original benchmark comparison
\begin{equation}
 \log\frac{\epsilon_{q+r-1}}{\delta q(k+1)/2}
 \sim\frac{q}{2(r/q)\log(r/q)}\longrightarrow+\infty.
 \tag{GRA23}\label{gra:epsbenchmark}
\end{equation}
These statements do not extend the allowance asymptotic to degrees for
which the arithmetic error in \eqref{gra:scalar} is comparable to its
decreasing reference exponent.

\subsection{Exact shifted two-window identities and finite enclosures}

Put $a=q+s-1$ and $b=a+q$, and give the rows $N=a+i$, $0\le i\le q$,
the weights $w_0=w_q=1$, $w_i=2$ otherwise.  Their sum is $2q$.
Since $s\ge1$, $a\ge q$, so every row has both adjacent factors.
Define
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal B_{k,s}^{\rm ar}=\log\frac{V_aV_{a+1}}{V_bV_{b+1}},\qquad
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}=\log\frac{\omega_b^{\rm ar}\omega_{b+1}^{\rm ar}}
                                      {\omega_a^{\rm ar}\omega_{a+1}^{\rm ar}},\\
 \mathcal P_{k,s}=-\sum_{i=0}^qw_i
       [\log(1-d_{a+i})+\log(1-d_{a+i+1})],\qquad
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}=2\sum_{i=0}^qw_i\log\epsilon_{a+i}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{GRA24}\label{gra:shifteddefs}
\end{equation}
For any positive sequence $z_n$, expansion of the coefficients gives
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^qw_i(\log z_{a+i+1}-\log z_{a+i})
 =\log z_b+\log z_{b+1}-\log z_a-\log z_{a+1}.
\]
Applying this identity to $\omega_n^{\rm ar}$ and $V_n$ in
\eqref{gra:rowlog}, with $r=s+i$ and
$\log T_{s+i}=-\log d_{a+i+1}$, proves
\begin{equation}
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}
 =\mathcal B_{k,s}^{\rm ar}+\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_{k,s},
 \qquad
 \mathcal B_{k,s}^{\rm ar}=\sum_{i=0}^qw_i\log T_{s+i}.
 \tag{GRA25}\label{gra:action}
\end{equation}
The four endpoints are thus exactly $a,a+1,a+q,a+q+1$.

To derive the penalty weights, let $f(x)=-\log(1-x)$ for $0<x<1$.
The coefficient of $f(d_{a+j})$ is $w_j+w_{j-1}$, taking a missing
$w$ as zero.  Since $q\ge2$ for the original packet, the result is
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal P_{k,s}=\sum_{j=0}^{q+1}c_jf(T_{s-1+j}^{-1}),\qquad
 (c_0,\ldots,c_{q+1})=(1,3,\underbrace{4,\ldots,4}_{q-2},3,1),
 \quad\sum_jc_j=4q.
 \tag{GRA26}\label{gra:penaltyweights}
\end{equation}
The first coefficient is present because the first row is shifted beyond
the exceptional row $q-1$.

For finite bounds suppose all the lower endpoints in
\eqref{gra:finiteE} on $s-1\le r\le s+q$ are positive, and define
$\ell=\min_r\underline E_r>0$ and $u=\max_r\overline E_r$.
The derivative of $f$ is positive, while $e^{-x}$ decreases, so
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{j=0}^{q+1}c_jf(e^{-\overline E_{s-1+j}})
 \le\mathcal P_{k,s}\le
 \sum_{j=0}^{q+1}c_jf(e^{-\underline E_{s-1+j}}).
 \tag{GRA27}\label{gra:penaltyfinite}
\end{equation}
The elementary inequalities $x\le f(x)\le x/(1-x)$, proved by
integrating $1/(1-x)$ between zero and $x$, give the useful common bounds
\begin{equation}
 4qe^{-u}\le\mathcal P_{k,s}
 \le\frac{4qe^{-\ell}}{1-e^{-\ell}}.
 \tag{GRA28}\label{gra:penaltycommon}
\end{equation}
Each individual final term also gives
$\mathcal P_{k,s}\ge e^{-\overline E_{s+q}}$.

Every row has a corresponding explicit finite interval.  Put $d=q+r$ and
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A_r^-&=\tfrac12\bigl[\log(d(d-1/2))-L_{k,d}
 +\underline E_r+\log(1-e^{-\underline E_r})
 +\log(1-e^{-\underline E_{r-1}})\bigr],\\
 A_r^+&=\tfrac12\bigl[\log(d(d-1/2))+L_{k,d-1}
 +\overline E_r+\log(1-e^{-\overline E_r})
 +\log(1-e^{-\overline E_{r-1}})\bigr].
 \end{aligned}
\]
Each displayed scalar function of $E$ is increasing on $E>0$.
Equations \eqref{gra:recurrence} and \eqref{gra:rowlog} therefore prove
\begin{equation}
 A_r^-\le\log\epsilon_{q+r-1}\le A_r^+,\qquad
 \min_{s\le r\le s+q}A_r^-
 \le\log\min_{a\le N\le b}\epsilon_N
 \le\min_{s\le r\le s+q}A_r^+.
 \tag{GRA29}\label{gra:minfinite}
\end{equation}
The two positive contraction factors are retained in both endpoints.

For the monic part define the literal Gamma window
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}
 =\log\frac{\gamma_b\gamma_{b+1}}{\gamma_a\gamma_{a+1}}
 =\log\frac{(2b)!(2b+2)!}{4^{2q}(2a)!(2a+2)!}.
 \tag{GRA30}\label{gra:Wgamma}
\end{equation}
Write $v_n=\log(\omega_n^{\rm ar}/\gamma_n)$.
Equation \eqref{gra:denominator} places $v_n$ in
$[\log A_k-L_{k,n},\log A_k]$.  Applying these four intervals with
their actual signs, so the four $\log A_k$ terms cancel, proves
\begin{equation}
 -L_{k,b}-L_{k,b+1}\le
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}-\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}
 \le L_{k,a}+L_{k,a+1}.
 \tag{GRA31}\label{gra:Wfour}
\end{equation}
Thus the recurrence contribution has four endpoint errors, rather than
one independent arithmetic error for every row.  In particular, writing
$P_-$ and $P_+$ for the lower and upper quantities in
\eqref{gra:penaltyfinite}, all finite data combine to give
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{i=0}^qw_i\underline E_{s+i}+\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}
       -L_{k,b}-L_{k,b+1}-P_+
 &\le J_{k,s}^{\rm action},\\
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}&\le
 \sum_{i=0}^qw_i\overline E_{s+i}+\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}
       +L_{k,a}+L_{k,a+1}-P_-.
 \end{aligned}\tag{GRA32}\label{gra:Jfinite}
\end{equation}

\subsection{Complete asymptotics and the unchanged mixed receiver}

Under \eqref{gra:narrow}, \eqref{gra:uniformeps} directly implies
\begin{equation}
 \log\min_{a\le N\le a+q}\epsilon_N\sim\frac{H_k}{2}
 =\frac{q}{2t\log t}.
 \tag{GRA33}\label{gra:minasymp}
\end{equation}
Indeed every row logarithm divided by $H_k/2$ lies between $1-o(1)$
and $1+o(1)$, so its minimum does too.  This argument does not identify
the minimizing row with an endpoint or equate a minimum with a geometric
mean.

For the penalty, \eqref{gra:uniformT} implies that its minimum and maximum
$\log T_r$ are both $H_k(1+o(1))$.  Apply the proof of
\eqref{gra:penaltycommon} using these exact minima and maxima to obtain
\[
 \log(4q)-\max_r\log T_r\le\log\mathcal P_{k,s}
 \le\log(4q)-\min_r\log T_r
     -\log(1-e^{-\min_r\log T_r}).
\]
Here $\log q=O_m(\log k)=o(H_k)$, and the final logarithm tends to zero.
Dividing by $H_k$ proves the full logarithmic rate
\begin{equation}
 \log\mathcal P_{k,s}\sim-H_k=-\frac{q}{t\log t}.
 \tag{GRA34}\label{gra:Pasymp}
\end{equation}
All $4q$ coefficients have been retained in the upper estimate.

The separate terms of the action can also be evaluated.  From
\eqref{gra:uniformT} and $\sum_iw_i=2q$,
$\mathcal B_{k,s}^{\rm ar}\sim2qH_k$.  The exact Gamma recurrence gives
\[
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}
 =\sum_{i=0}^qw_i\log[(s+q+i)(s+q+i-1/2)].
\]
Since $s=qt$, every summand equals $2\log s+O(1/t)$ uniformly, giving
$\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}=4q\log(qt)+O(q/t)$.
More explicitly, $\sum_iw_ii=q^2$, and $0\le\log(1+x)\le x$ for
$x\ge0$ gives
\[
 0\le\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\sigma}-4q\log s
 \le (6q^2-q)/s.
\]
The four-endpoint estimate \eqref{gra:Wfour} now yields
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal B_{k,s}^{\rm ar}\sim2qH_k,\qquad
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}=4q\log(qt)+O_h(q/t+k),\\
 \mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}=o(qH_k),\qquad
 \mathcal P_{k,s}=\exp[-H_k(1+o(1))]=o(qH_k),\\
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}\sim2qH_k=\frac{2q^2}{t\log t}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{GRA35}\label{gra:actionasymp}
\end{equation}
For the smallness of $\mathcal W$, divide its bound by $qH_k$ and use
$\log(qt)=O_m(\log k)$, $H_k/k\to\infty$, and $q\to\infty$.
Equation \eqref{gra:action} then proves the last assertion independently
of summing the row asymptotics.

It remains to specify exactly how this action reaches the existing mixed
calculation.  Keep its original surjection $\Lambda:E\twoheadrightarrow B$
and kernel inclusion $I:K\hookrightarrow E$, with the complete divided
Taylor unit and original period branches.  Explicitly, with the original
period map $\Pi$ and tensor invariant projector $P_k$, its image inclusion
$\jmath$ satisfies
$\jmath\Lambda=P_k\Pi^{\otimes k}M_{\upsilon_h}^{\otimes k}\iota$,
where $\iota[P]=[P(z_1+\cdots+z_k)]$.  For each original source metric
$G_N^v$, put
\[
 H_{K,N}^v=I^*G_N^vI,\quad
 Q_{B,N}^v=(\Lambda(G_N^v)^{-1}\Lambda^*)^{-1},\quad
 S_N^v=(G_N^v)^{-1}\Lambda^*Q_{B,N}^v.
\]
Direct multiplication gives $\Lambda S_N^v=1$ and
$I^*G_N^vS_N^v=0$.  For a fixed section $S_*$, the difference
$S_N^v-S_*$ has image in $K$, so the frame change from $[I,S_*]$ to
$[I,S_N^v]$ is triangular with identity diagonal.  Hence
\[
 \det H_{K,N}^v\det Q_{B,N}^v=|\det[I,S_*]|^2\det G_N^v.
\]
Taking the return at $a,a+1,b,b+1$ cancels all four frame constants.
If $F_K^v,F_B^v,\mathcal B^v$ denote these same four-endpoint returns,
the result is exactly $F_K^v+F_B^v=\mathcal B^v$.  Empty determinant
factors are one.  For the original sources $v={\rm ar},\sigma,(k)$ set
\[
 \mathcal H=\mathcal B^\sigma-\mathcal B^{(k)},\qquad
 \Delta^\sigma=\mathcal B^{\rm ar}-\mathcal B^\sigma.
\]
The complete shifted allocation and matching budget therefore obey
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \Psi&=F_K^{\rm ar}+F_B^{\rm ar}-F_B^{(k)}
       =F_K^{(k)}+\mathcal H+\Delta^\sigma,\\
 C^{\rm budget}&=F_B^{(k)}+\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_{k,s}
       =\mathcal B^{(k)}-F_K^{(k)}
          +\mathcal W_{k,s}^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_{k,s},\\
 \Psi+C^{\rm budget}&=J_{k,s}^{\rm action}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{GRA36}\label{gra:mixedreceiver}
\end{equation}
This proves the exact map of the new window into the earlier complete
action.  The common allocation cancels with its matching budget.  The
new scalar profile consequently assigns no separate leading coefficient
to the intrinsic kernel and boundary mixed terms or to their
complementary CPQ returns.  Those original quantities remain in
\eqref{gra:mixed} and \eqref{gra:mixedreceiver}, with their maps unchanged.

\section{The original three-equation transfer of every connecting return}
\label{sec:PRT}

Fix the simple quartet, its actual amplitude, period and logarithm branch
on the original five-orbit locus. Keep $k=4l+1\ge9$, $j=k+4$,
$q=(k+1)^2$, $Q=(j+1)^2$, $d=Q-q=8k+24$, and the full
nonreduced conductor
\[
 E_A(z)=\sum_{a,b=0}^8a_{ab}e^{\beta_{8,a,b}z},\qquad
 v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A,\quad\mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)\ne0,
 \quad\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v.
 \tag{PRT1}
\]
No conductor factor is removed. Write $B=B_{\rm cof}$,
$\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m_j}$, $\mathbb J=\ker\mathbb B$,
$\mathscr B=\operatorname{im}B$ and $W=\mathscr B+\mathbb J$,
as in CPQ2. Define, in this same coefficient space,
\[
 S_e=\operatorname{im}[B,F_e],\qquad K_e=S_e\cap W,
 \qquad T_e=S_e+W\qquad(e=0,1).
 \tag{PRT2}
\]
These are the CPQ spaces, with $S_e$ abbreviated only to make the
two endpoint subscripts legible. The later norm cutoff $N$ is independent
of the defining endpoint $e$. At every $Q-1\le N\le2Q$ use exactly
\[
 D=N-v,\qquad \mathcal D_N^{(s)}=
 \operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D}^{(s)},4),\qquad
 c'_j=j/2-4,\quad h_{n,s}=(2\pi)^{s/2}n!(s/2)_n,
 \quad s=1\text{ or }j.
 \tag{PRT3}
\]
Thus both actual source pairs $(1,1)$ and $(k,j)$ are covered in
their target order. Their degree-$k$ proper source retains its separate
form; none of the identities below substitutes that form for $A_{\rm inv}$.

\subsection{The actual three rows in the original period coefficients}

Retain the original polynomials $L_i=(\mathsf H(ZW,Z,W,1)^{\mathsf T})_i$,
$b_{i,a,b}=[Z^aW^b]L_i^4$, the exponential $B_i(z)$ and the
translation maps $\mathcal T_i,\mathcal T_A$ of CPR2. In particular
\[
 R_i(S)=\frac{\sum_{a,b=0}^4b_{i,a,b}
                         \chi_j(S+\beta_{4,a,b})}{\chi_k(S)},
 \qquad b_i=B_i(0),\qquad
 \chi_n(S)=\prod_{a,b=0}^n(S-\beta_{n,a,b}).
 \tag{PRT4}
\]
The quotient is the complete polynomial quotient: root addition makes
every numerator vanish at every distinct root of $\chi_k$.
Choose the first original index $i_*$ with $b_{i_*}\ne0$, and put
\[
 C_i=R_i-(b_i/b_{i_*})R_{i_*}\quad(i\ne i_*),\qquad
 V=([S^a]C_i)_{0\le a<d,\ i\ne i_*}.
 \tag{PRT5}
\]
Existence of $i_*$ and independence of these three columns are the
complete PDS10--11/CPR17 calculation: the original Bezout identity at
zero gives a nonzero $b_i$; the four $R_i$ are independent; their leading
coefficients are $b_i$. Subtracting their exact leading multiples
therefore gives three independent columns of degree at most $d-1$.
No period coefficient is replaced in this operation.

Let $h_b$ be the original primitive germs used to define the $d$ columns
of $F_0$ in CIT7. They are specified modulo the entire raw analytic
source. Define their relation-value matrix by
\[
 (M_0)_{a,b}=\Lambda_{h_b}
                  \bigl(\mathcal T_A(\chi_k S^a)\bigr),
             \qquad0\le a<d,
 \qquad L=V^{\mathsf T}M_0.
 \tag{PRT6}
\]
Every entry uses the ordinary, complex-linear transpose. The complete
annihilator identity CPR9--10 proves that the displayed $d$ independent
relation polynomials are the full dual of the raw source quotient.
The $h_b$ are a basis of that quotient, so $M_0$ is invertible. Thus
$L$ has rank exactly three. The matrix is computable from the original
period arrays by finite polynomial division and pairing; it contains
neither a new numerical model nor a selected nonzero period minor.

Choose once a full column basis $Z$ of $\ker L$ and a three-column
complement $Y$ so that $[Z,Y]$ is invertible. Then the literal coefficient
spaces obey
\[
 \boxed{\quad S_1=\mathscr B+F_0\ker L\subset S_0,
       \qquad S_0/S_1\text{ has the three lifts }F_0Y.\quad}
 \tag{PRT7}
\]
Here equality is of subspaces of the original $\mathcal U$, not only
of abstract quotient dimensions. To prove it, use PDS11: truncation of
the endpoint-one primitive quotient identifies it with exactly
$\ker V^{\mathsf T}$ in endpoint-zero relation-value coordinates.
The missing top value is uniquely
$-u_*^{\mathsf T}x_0/b_{i_*}$ with the complete $u_*$ from PDS10.
For a representative $h$, set $f=E_Ah$. The original target numerator
in component $i$ is
\[
 N_i(f)=\mathbb V_j^{-1}
        \bigl(r![z^r](B_i f)\bigr)_{0\le r<Q},
 \qquad(\mathbb V_j)_{r,(a,b)}=\beta_{j,a,b}^{r}.
 \tag{PRT8}
\]
Those values use only the truncation through degree $Q-1$.
At endpoint one, the additional four equations assert exactly that
the same interpolants also have the required next jet; they do not
alter a coefficient in PRT8. Division by the complete $E_A$ gives
the same target analytic functions, modulo precisely the original lower
exponentials. An analytic source change has numerator $d$ and changes
the four target numerators by $(L_i^4d)_i$, hence changes their DNE
coordinates by $Bx$ with the full original scalar $\alpha_A$.
This is the coefficient square RRC5--9/CIT8. It proves both inclusions
in PRT7, including every analytic-source and lower-gauge change.
Finally $F_0$ induces an injection modulo $\mathscr B$ by CIT9;
the invertible matrix $[Z,Y]$ therefore proves the asserted dimension
and the stated three lifts.

The coefficient change can itself be written explicitly. For the original
endpoint-one primitive columns $h_{1,b}$ put
$ (M_1)_{a,b}=\Lambda_{h_{1,b}}(\mathcal T_A(\chi_kS^a))$
for $0\le a\le d$, and let $P_{<d}$ select the first $d$ rows.
Then
\[
 \Gamma=M_0^{-1}P_{<d}M_1,\qquad
 \operatorname{im}\Gamma=\ker L,\qquad
 F_1=F_0\Gamma+BH
 \tag{PRT7a}
\]
for the unique matrix $H$ with the displayed equality.
Indeed PDS11 proves the first two assertions, including that $\Gamma$
has rank $d-3$. The preceding numerator interpolation calculation
shows that each difference column lies in $\mathscr B$.
The original injectivity of $B$ gives its unique coordinate in $H$.
Equivalently $\Gamma=ZT$ for an invertible $(d-3)$-square matrix $T$,
so every original fixed frame change in PRT7 is specified by a literal
coefficient equation. No conjugation occurs in $M_0,M_1,L,\Gamma$.
Hermitian adjoints enter only when evaluating the actual metric below.

\subsection{An exact sequence of these three directions}

PRT7 gives $K_1\subset K_0$ and $T_1\subset T_0$. The identity on
coefficient representatives gives the exact sequence
\[
 0\longrightarrow K_0/K_1\xrightarrow{[x]\mapsto[x]}
 S_0/S_1\xrightarrow{[x]\mapsto[x]}T_0/T_1\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{PRT9}
\]
The first map is injective because $K_0\cap S_1=K_1$.
If $x\in S_0$ maps to zero on the right, write $x=s+w$ with
$s\in S_1$, $w\in W$; then $w=x-s\in K_0$, so its class is the
image of $K_0/K_1$. Conversely every such class maps to zero. Every
element of $T_0=S_0+W$ has the class of its $S_0$ summand modulo
$T_1=S_1+W$, proving surjectivity.
Let
\[
 a=\dim(T_0/T_1)=\dim(\mathcal G_k\cap\mathcal C_k)
                    \quad\text{from CPR17--18}.
 \quad
 \dim(K_0/K_1)=3-a,\qquad0\le a\le3.
 \tag{PRT10}
\]
The equality with CPR's actual polynomial intersection follows because
$\dim(T_e/W)=c_e$ and CPR18 proves $c_0-c_1=a$. It can also be
calculated directly in the three columns $F_0Y$: take their images
modulo $T_1$, retaining the full $\mathbb B$, $B$ and $L$ of
PRT2/PRT6. Their exact rank is $a$ and their kernel has dimension
$3-a$. This proves a complete original-data rank computation without
assuming a generic minor is nonzero. Rank strata, including $a=0$,
are retained.

Give the three terms of PRT9 their original attained metrics
\[
 A_N=\operatorname{Gram}_{\mathcal D_N}(S_0/S_1),\quad
 B_N=\operatorname{Gram}_{\mathcal D_N}(K_0/K_1),\quad
 C_N=\operatorname{Gram}_{\mathcal D_N}(T_0/T_1).
 \tag{PRT11}
\]
Every value frame is fixed in $N$ and in the source-order comparison.
For example if $X_1$ is any fixed independent frame of $S_1$, the first
one, in the exact three lifts $X_A=F_0Y$, is
\[
 A_N=X_A^*\mathcal D_NX_A-X_A^*\mathcal D_NX_1
 (X_1^*\mathcal D_NX_1)^{-1}X_1^*\mathcal D_NX_A.
 \tag{PRT12}
\]
Select the first independent images of $F_0Y$ modulo $T_1$ to obtain
the $a$ lifts $X_C$. A full basis of $\ker(F_0Y\bmod T_1)$, followed
by subtracting an $S_1$ representative as in PRT9, gives $3-a$
lifts $X_B$ in $K_0$. Applying PRT12 with $(X_1,X_A)$ replaced by
the frames of $(K_1,X_B)$ and $(T_1,X_C)$ gives $B_N$ and $C_N$.
All three are positive definite on their stated quotient values;
the determinant of an empty quotient is one.

For completeness every fixed frame factor can be retained before
taking a return. For a fixed inclusion $K\subset Y_0$, choose frames
$P_K,P_{Y_0}$ and quotient lifts $X$. Put
\[
 [P_K,X]=P_{Y_0}R_{Y_0/K},\qquad
 d_{Y_0/K}=|\det R_{Y_0/K}|^2,\qquad
 g_{Y_0}(N)=\det(P_{Y_0}^*\mathcal D_NP_{Y_0}).
 \tag{PRT13}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M05_PRT_QUOTIENT
This is the standard positive-block Schur calculation
\cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994}; the determinant
factorization follows from the same block elimination
\cite[p.~222, (6a)--(6b)]{human:hager1989}.
Here the block frame is exactly $[P_K,X]$, with its retained
factor $d_{Y_0/K}$; neither reference supplies the programme's
connecting sequence or its endpoint comparison.
% END HUMAN CITATION M05_PRT_QUOTIENT

The full block Schur determinant identity is
\[
 \det\operatorname{Gram}_{\mathcal D_N}(Y_0/K)
       =d_{Y_0/K}\,g_{Y_0}(N)/g_K(N).
 \tag{PRT14}
\]
It follows by expressing the restricted form in $[P_K,X]$,
taking its determinant, and eliminating its upper-left block.
This defines and evaluates every factor from the stated original
frames. It does not identify a varying minimizing representative
with a fixed lift. CPQ's frames for each quotient are used in
PRT13 when that quotient appears below.

\subsection{Evaluation of the difference of the actual CPQ returns}

Use the unchanged target return
$\mathcal Rf=-f(Q-1)-f(Q)+f(2Q-1)+f(2Q)$ and write
\[
 U_A=\mathcal R\log\det A_N,\qquad
 U_B=\mathcal R\log\det B_N,\qquad
 U_C=\mathcal R\log\det C_N.
 \tag{PRT15}
\]
These symbols are returns of precisely the three evaluated quotient
matrices PRT11--12. They are not freely assignable parameters.
The exact CPQ differences are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 V_{{\rm conn},0}-V_{{\rm conn},1}&=U_A,&
 V_{h,0}-V_{h,1}&=U_B,\\
 V_{s,0}-V_{s,1}&=U_A-U_B,&
 V_{t,0}-V_{t,1}&=U_C,\\
 V_{{\rm rem},1}-V_{{\rm rem},0}&=U_C,&
 \mathcal L_0-\mathcal L_1&=U_C-U_A+U_B.
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRT16}
\]
Here $V_b,V_I,V_A$ are independent of the defining primitive endpoint
$e$: their spaces, coefficient maps and frames are the same CPQ3/9
objects. To prove every line, apply PRT14 to
$S_e/\mathscr B$, $K_e/\mathscr B$, $S_e/K_e$, $T_e/W$ and
$\mathcal U/T_e$, respectively. For example the logarithmic
determinant difference of the latter two endpoint quotients is
$\log g_{T_0}-\log g_{T_1}$ plus the explicitly defined fixed
frame-factor difference; PRT14 for $T_0/T_1$ identifies its
nonconstant term with $\log\det C_N$.
Each frame factor is independent of $N$, and
$\mathcal R1=-1-1+1+1=0$, so it cancels only after this full return.
The other four differences follow by the same stated determinant
identities. Finally substitute them into CPQ10's
$\mathcal L_e=V_I+V_{t,e}-V_{s,e}-V_A$.

This also gives the exact propagation, without deleting a complement,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Z_e&=V_b+V_{{\rm rem},e}+V_{s,e}+V_A,
 &Z_1-Z_0&=U_C-U_A+U_B,\\
 \mathcal P^{\rm rest}_0-\mathcal P^{\rm rest}_1
    &=U_C-U_A+U_B,
 &4S_{K,j}&=\mathcal L_0+Z_0=\mathcal L_1+Z_1.
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRT17}
\]
In particular a change of primitive endpoint changes the common loss
and complementary sum by exactly opposite amounts in their allocation
identity. The original total and the complete-action allocation
cancellation are preserved.

\subsection{Directed finite bounds with the original masses}

Use the proved original reconstruction/source constant of FRE1,
evaluated at degree $j$ and order $s$:
\[
 A_{j,s}^{\rm end}=
 \max\{1,Q\mathcal B_{2Q-v,s,j,A}^{2}\}
                    (M_{j,s}^{\rm rec})^2.
 \tag{PRT18}
\]
The two factors retain DNE13's full mass $(2\pi)^{s/2}$,
the full conductor scalar and division coefficients, and LJR16's
original fixed-disk reconstruction, phases and Vandermonde.
FRE2--4 proves on this exact metric
\[
 \mathcal D_{Q-1}^{(s)}\preceq\mathcal D_N^{(s)}
   \preceq A_{j,s}^{\rm end}\mathcal D_{Q-1}^{(s)},
 \qquad
 \log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}\le2Q\log j+O_{\rm actual}(Q).
 \tag{PRT19}
\]
Substitution in each full affine fibre defining PRT11 and subsequent
minimization preserve both inequalities. Original row extension also
proves that each of $A_N,B_N,C_N$ increases with $N$.
If $G_N$ is one of these forms of rank $r$, set
$f(N)=\log\det G_N-\log\det G_{Q-1}$.
The generalized eigenvalues in PRT19 show $0\le f(N)\le
r\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}$ and $f$ is increasing. Hence
$\mathcal R\log\det G=f(2Q-1)+f(2Q)-f(Q)$ is nonnegative
and at most $2r\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}$.
This proves the actual directed intervals
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 0\le U_A\le6\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end},\qquad
 0\le U_B\le2(3-a)\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end},\qquad
 0\le U_C\le2a\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end},\\
 \boxed{\quad0\le V_{{\rm rem},1}-V_{{\rm rem},0}
                   \le2a\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}.\quad}\\
 -2(3-a)\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}
 \le V_{s,0}-V_{s,1}\le6\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end},\\
 |\mathcal L_0-\mathcal L_1|\le6\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end},
 \qquad |Z_1-Z_0|\le6\log A_{j,s}^{\rm end}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{PRT20}
\]
The last bound uses $\dim B+\dim C=3$, keeping the exact
sequence rather than separately charging two unrelated rank-three
errors. These are finite bounds on actual CPQ expressions.
For each fixed original period they are
$O_{\rm actual}(Q\log j)=o(kq)$, for both source pairs.
At the actual rank stratum $a=0$, the remaining-target forms are
the same quotient in fixed value frames and their returns are exactly
equal. At $a=3$, the kernel spaces coincide and $U_B=0$ exactly.
All intermediate strata remain governed by their actual original
three-column matrix. No claim is made that either endpoint's common
leading return has now been evaluated; PRT16 evaluates their difference
and reduces its calculation to the stated three original directions.

The proof requires the five-orbit coefficient construction, its original
distinct quartet roots and $\mu_v\ne0$. It does not use the additional
pole-coordinate gate minors of RPI/RPK. Consequently a period excluded
by a pole-coordinate pivot remains covered when it belongs to the
original five-orbit locus: PRT6/8/12 construct all maps without that
pivot. Rank changes of the three-row image are handled by PRT10.
The value $v$ is retained throughout; neither $v\le32$ nor a generic
value of $v$ has been substituted for an actual coefficient.
