\label{MPBUILD:FIRST_ROW_PROOF:start}
% Complete independent first-original-row derivation, 2026-09-17.
% Source: CURRENT/14_CURRENT_KERNEL_AND_MULTIPLICITY_PROOFS.tex,
% NSO1--13, CPQ17--18, SFB11--15, PSG4--6.
\section{The first original row: exact lower cancellation and its finite bound}
\label{sec:FRG}

Fix the actual simple quartet, original conductor coefficients, period,
and branch. Write
\[
\begin{gathered}
j\equiv1\pmod4,\quad j\ge13,\quad q=(j+1)^2,\quad q'=(j-7)^2,
\quad c'=j/2-4,\\
E_A(z)=\sum_{a,b=0}^8a_{ab}e^{\beta_{8,a,b}z},\qquad
\mu_d=E_A^{(d)}(0),\quad v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A\le80,
\quad\mu_v\ne0,\\
\beta_{r,a,b}=r/2+(2a-r)\delta+i(2b-r)\gamma,\quad
\sigma=(\delta^2+\gamma^2)^{1/2},\\
\chi_j(S)=\prod_{a,b=0}^j(S-\beta_{j,a,b}),\qquad
\chi'(S')=\chi_{j-8}(S')=\prod_{a,b=0}^{j-8}(S'-\beta_{j-8,a,b}),\\
n=q-v,\quad m=n-q'=16j-48-v\ge0,
\quad s\in\{1,j\},\quad \ell=(s-1)/4.
\end{gathered}\tag{FRG1}
\]
The prime on $\chi'$ names the complete lower root polynomial; it is
not differentiation. The original norm is
\[
\begin{gathered}
\|P\|_{s,c'}^2=\int_{\mathbb R}|P(c'+iy)|^2dm_s(y),\qquad
dm_s=\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
 |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy,\quad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
p_{r+1,s}=yp_{r,s}-r(r-1+s/2)p_{r-1,s},\\
h_{r,s}=M_sr!(s/2)_r,\qquad
\phi_r(S')=p_{r,s}((S'-c')/i)/\sqrt{h_{r,s}}.
\end{gathered}\tag{FRG2}
\]
These are exactly NSO2,7's native orthonormal polynomials, with the
full generating-function and orthogonality proof in SGT2--5; its
source mass and recurrence coefficients are unchanged here.
% BEGIN FRG2 POINT-OF-USE HUMAN CREDIT 20260925
The classical family underlying FRG2 is the Meixner--Pollaczek family:
$p_{r,s}(y)=r!P_r^{(s/4)}(y/2;\pi/2)$.
See Koornwinder, Wong, Koekoek, Swarttouw and Reinhardt,
\cite[eqs.~(18.19.6)--(18.19.9), (18.22.8), (18.23.7)]{human:dlmf18};
the generating function is also directly available at
\href{https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.23#E7}{DLMF (18.23.7)}.
The exact dictionary and qualified source ancestry are retained in HPS1--4:
$dy=2\,dx$, $M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}$ and
$h_{r,s}=M_sr!(s/2)_r$ are unchanged.
This credits the classical polynomial input, not the programme-specific
conductor and first-row bounds that follow.
% END FRG2 POINT-OF-USE HUMAN CREDIT 20260925

Thus the leading coefficient of $\phi_r$ is
$i^{-r}/\sqrt{h_{r,s}}$. All inner products are conjugate-linear
in the first variable, whereas every functional below is complex-linear.

\subsection{The actual annihilator and the full first-row leverage}
Let $F_b=N_b/E_A$ be the original NSO12 numerator germs, with
$N_b(z)=\sum_{a,b'}(Z_{\exp,j})_{(a,b'),b}e^{\beta_{j,a,b'}z}$.
Keep their original fixed NSO12 frame. Let $V_0,T_0$ be NSO13's lower
and numerator matrices on rows $0,\ldots,n-1$, and let $v_n,t_n$ be
their next rows. There are $q'$ lower columns and $m$ numerator columns;
therefore
\[
F_0=[V_0,T_0]\in\mathbb C^{n\times n},\qquad w_n=[v_n,t_n].
\tag{FRG3}
\]
The NSO12--13 Vandermonde proof applies at cutoff $n-1=q-v-1$:
if such a combined functional has its first $n$ derivatives zero,
multiplication of its germ by $E_A$ makes an upper-grid numerator
have its first $q$ derivatives zero. The upper Vandermonde kills this
numerator, its class in the fixed conductor quotient vanishes, and the
lower Vandermonde kills the remaining lower coefficients. Since the
triangular change from derivatives to the $\phi_r$ values has nonzero
diagonal, $F_0$ is invertible.

Define the original translation and polynomial
\[
(\mathcal T_AP)(S')=\sum_{a,b=0}^8a_{ab}P(S'+\beta_{8,a,b}),
\qquad g=\mathcal T_A\chi_j,\qquad
\lambda=\mu_v\binom qv\ne0.
\tag{FRG4}
\]
Expanding every shift shows that all coefficients above degree $q-v$
vanish and the coefficient at degree $q-v$ is $\lambda$: for a monomial
$S^d$, its coefficient at $S'^{d-r}$ is $\binom dr\mu_r$, and
$\mu_0=\cdots=\mu_{v-1}=0$. Hence $\deg g=n$.
For any polynomial $P$ the finite Leibniz identity gives the exact pairing
\[
\Lambda_{F_b}(\mathcal T_AP)=\Lambda_{E_AF_b}(P)=\Lambda_{N_b}(P).
\tag{FRG5}
\]
The right side for $P=\chi_j$ is a linear combination of evaluations
of $\chi_j$ at all its upper roots, so is zero. Also
$\beta_{j-8,u,w}+\beta_{8,a,b}=\beta_{j,u+a,w+b}$ lies in the
original upper grid. Every summand in $g(\beta_{j-8,u,w})$ is zero.
Distinctness of all the lower roots proves $\chi'\mid g$.
Consequently all columns in $[V,T]$ annihilate $g$.

Expand $g=\sum_{r=0}^nc_r\phi_r$. The exact top coefficient and row
relation are
\[
c_n=i^n\lambda\sqrt{h_{n,s}},\qquad
c_{<n}^{\mathsf T}F_0+c_nw_n=0.
\tag{FRG6}
\]
Here the transpose is ordinary because of complex linearity.
Since $F_0$ is square and invertible,
$F_0(F_0^*F_0)^{-1}F_0^*=I_n$. Thus
\[
\begin{gathered}
\zeta_{\rm tot}=w_n(F_0^*F_0)^{-1}w_n^*
 =\frac{\sum_{r<n}|c_r|^2}{|c_n|^2},\\
1+\zeta_{\rm tot}
 =\frac{\|\mathcal T_A\chi_j\|_{s,c'}^2}{|\lambda|^2h_{n,s}}.
\end{gathered}\tag{FRG7}
\]
No factorial, phase, conductor coefficient, or source mass was removed.

\subsection{The exact lower ideal norm}
Let
\[
\nu_{m,s}=\min_{P\text{ monic},\ \deg P=m}
                     \|\chi'P\|_{s,c'}^2.
\tag{FRG8}
\]
This is the attained positive norm on the original monic affine fibre;
it exists because its finite positive Gram has a unique quadratic
minimum. A monic degree-$n$ polynomial $u=\sum_{r=0}^nd_r\phi_r$
has $d_n=i^n\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$. Divisibility by $\chi'$ is exactly its
vanishing at all lower roots, hence exactly
\[
V_0^{\mathsf T}d_{<n}=-d_nv_n^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{FRG9}
\]
The matrix $V_0$ has full column rank. In these coefficient coordinates
the attained minimum is
\[
d_{<n,\min}=-d_n\overline{V_0}
             (V_0^{\mathsf T}\overline{V_0})^{-1}v_n^{\mathsf T}.
\tag{FRG10}
\]
Multiplication proves the constraint. Its ordinary Hermitian pairing
with every vector in $\ker V_0^{\mathsf T}$ vanishes, proving the
minimum and its Pythagorean decomposition. Its squared norm is
$|d_n|^2\overline{v_n}(V_0^{\mathsf T}\overline{V_0})^{-1}
v_n^{\mathsf T}=|d_n|^2\zeta_{\rm low}$, where
$\zeta_{\rm low}=v_n(V_0^*V_0)^{-1}v_n^*$ is real and nonnegative.
Thus
\[
\boxed{\quad \nu_{m,s}=h_{n,s}(1+\zeta_{\rm low}).\quad}
\tag{FRG11}
\]
This is a typed monic-fibre map, not a change of the original measure.
In $y$ coordinates it retains the phase explicitly: write
$\chi'(c'+iy)=i^{q'}Q'(y)$, with monic $Q'$, and
$P(c'+iy)=i^m\widetilde P(y)$, with monic $\widetilde P$.
The forward and inverse maps on monic fibres are
$\widetilde P(y)=i^{-m}P(c'+iy)$ and
$P(S')=i^m\widetilde P((S'-c')/i)$. Their total phase is $i^n$
and has modulus one; their squared norm is precisely FRG8.

Put $A=V_0^*V_0$, $B=V_0^*T_0$,
$D_0=T_0^*T_0-B^*A^{-1}B$, and
$\rho=t_n-v_nA^{-1}B$. Block factorization of $F_0^*F_0$ gives
\[
\zeta_{\rm tot}=\zeta_{\rm low}+\rho D_0^{-1}\rho^*,\qquad
r_n=\frac{\rho D_0^{-1}\rho^*}{1+\zeta_{\rm low}}.
\tag{FRG12}
\]
For instance factor the Gram as
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}I&0\\B^*A^{-1}&I\end{smallmatrix}\right]
\operatorname{diag}(A,D_0)
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}I&A^{-1}B\\0&I\end{smallmatrix}\right]$
and multiply its inverse against $[v_n,t_n]$ and its adjoint.
Equations FRG7 and FRG11 now prove
\[
\boxed{\quad
1+r_n=\frac{1+\zeta_{\rm tot}}{1+\zeta_{\rm low}}
 =\frac{\|\mathcal T_A\chi_j\|_{s,c'}^2}{|\mu_v\binom qv|^2\nu_{m,s}}.
\quad}\tag{FRG13}
\]
The scalar $\lambda$ maps $g$ to the unique monic polynomial $g/\lambda$
in its original one-dimensional coefficient fibre; it is kept in FRG13.
In particular, if $P_{m,s}^{\min}$ is the unique monic minimizer in
FRG8, then
\[
1+r_n=1+
\frac{\|g/\lambda-\chi'P_{m,s}^{\min}\|_{s,c'}^2}{\nu_{m,s}}\ge1.
\tag{FRG14}
\]
Indeed the difference belongs to $\chi'\mathbb C[S']_{<m}$, to which
the minimizer is orthogonal. This also characterizes the exact zero-row
case: $r_n=0$ precisely when $g/\lambda=\chi'P_{m,s}^{\min}$.

\subsection{An explicit finite bound from the original root products}
Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
\theta=\pi/2,\quad h=1/q,\quad\kappa=\theta-h>0,
\quad\beta_s=\frac{M_s}{\sqrt2\Gamma(s/2)},\\
L_h=\frac{\pi}{\Gamma(3/4)^2}(\sin h)^{3/2},\qquad
U_h=\frac{\Gamma(1/4)^2}{2\pi}(\sin h)^{-1/2},\\
Q_-=q'+\ell,\quad Q_+=q+\ell,\quad
R'=(j-8)\sigma,\quad M_0=2Q_-/\theta,\quad M=\max\{M_0,2R'\},\\
B_+=\max\{1,(j+8)\sigma,2\ell\},\qquad
A_{\rm abs}=\sum_{a,b=0}^8|a_{ab}|,\\
f_\theta(x)=2x\log(2x/\theta)-2x,\quad F_M=2Q_-\log M-\theta M,\\
\epsilon_-=\left(\theta-\frac{2Q_-}M\right)
 +\frac{Q_-}{M^2}+h(M+1)+\frac{4q'R'}M,\qquad
C_m=(2m+1)\binom{2m}{m}^{\!2}.
\end{gathered}\tag{FRG15}
\]
The two inequalities $M\ge2R'$ and $M\ge M_0$ hold by definition,
so the finite bounds below apply at every $j\ge13$.
For each fixed quartet, $M=M_0$ eventually, because $M_0$ grows
quadratically and $R'$ linearly in $j$. On that eventual range,
$F_M=f_\theta(Q_-)$ and
$\epsilon_-=\theta^2/(4Q_-)+h(M+1)+4q'R'/M$.

For completeness, the Gamma input used in the bound is proved as
follows. Gamma recurrence gives
\[
dm_s(y)=\beta_s\prod_{r=0}^{\ell-1}(y^2+(2r+1/2)^2)d\sigma(y),
\qquad d\sigma(y)=\frac{|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2}{2\pi}dy.
\tag{FRG16}
\]
The factor $4^{-\ell}$ from the recurrence is cancelled exactly by the
$2^{2\ell}$ in the original density. Rotate Euler's integral for
$\Gamma(a+iy/2)$ through $\operatorname{sgn}(y)(\pi/2-h)$:
absolute integration along that ray proves
$|\Gamma(a+iy/2)|\le\Gamma(a)(\sin h)^{-a}
e^{-(\pi/2-h)|y|/2}$. The small closing arc tends to zero as its radius
to the power $a$, and the large closing arc tends to zero since its
cosine is at least $\sin h$. For the lower bound use the exact
reflection product
$|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)\Gamma(3/4-iy/2)|^2=2\pi^2/\cosh(\pi y)$,
the same upper bound on the second Gamma factor, and
$\cosh(\pi y)\le e^{\pi|y|}$. Therefore, also at $y=0$,
\[
L_he^{-(\theta+h)|y|}\le\frac{d\sigma}{dy}(y)
                        \le U_he^{-\kappa|y|}.
\tag{FRG17}
\]

For $y\in[M,M+1]$, every original lower root in the $y$ coordinate
has modulus at most $R'$. Since $R'/y\le1/2$, integration of
$(1-t)^{-1}\le2$ gives
$|\chi'(c'+iy)|^2\ge y^{2q'}e^{-4q'R'/M}$.
Every Gamma recurrence factor is at least $y^2$. Writing $y=M+t$,
$0\le t\le1$, the inequality $\log(1+x)\ge x-x^2/2$ gives
\[
2Q_-\log y-\theta y\ge F_M-
             \left(\theta-\frac{2Q_-}M\right)-\frac{Q_-}{M^2}.
\tag{FRG18}
\]
Thus the complete density $|\chi'(c'+iy)|^2dm_s/dy$ on this one
original-coordinate interval is at least
$\beta_sL_h\exp(F_M-\epsilon_-)$.

Here is the exact monic minimum on an interval of length one. Set
$L_m(x)=\frac1{m!}\frac{d^m}{dx^m}[x^m(x-1)^m]$.
Its leading coefficient is $\binom{2m}{m}$. Integrating by parts $m$
times, all boundary terms vanish, proving its orthogonality to degrees
less than $m$. The same integrations give
\[
\int_0^1L_m(x)^2dx
=\binom{2m}{m}\int_0^1x^m(1-x)^m dx=\frac1{2m+1}.
\tag{FRG19}
\]
The beta integral here is $m!^2/(2m+1)!$, obtained by repeated
integration by parts. Subtracting $L_m/\binom{2m}{m}$ from any complex
monic polynomial makes an orthogonal lower-degree difference. The
least squared norm is exactly $C_m^{-1}$. Translation of the interval
preserves its leading coefficient. The polynomial $P(c'+iy)$ for monic
$P$ has leading coefficient $i^m$; multiply the displayed minimizing
polynomial by that retained phase. Applying this minimum to FRG18 yields
\[
\boxed{\quad
\nu_{m,s}\ge\frac{\beta_sL_h}{C_m}
                \exp(F_M-\epsilon_-).\quad}
\tag{FRG20}
\]

On the entire real line, for every original shift and upper root,
\[
|c'+iy+\beta_{8,a,b}-\beta_{j,u,w}|
\le |y|+(j+8)\sigma\le |y|+B_+.
\tag{FRG21}
\]
Indeed its constant part is
$[(2a-8)-(2u-j)]\delta+i[(2b-8)-(2w-j)]\gamma$.
Every original summand in $g$ therefore has modulus at most
$(|y|+B_+)^q$, and the complete sum has modulus at most
$A_{\rm abs}(|y|+B_+)^q$.
Also $2r+1/2\le2\ell\le B_+$ for every recurrence factor when
$\ell>0$, and the empty product for $\ell=0$ is one. FRG16--17,
the substitution $t=y+B_+$ on $y\ge0$, and enlargement of its
integral to $[0,\infty)$ prove
\[
\boxed{\quad
\|g\|_{s,c'}^2\le
2\beta_sU_hA_{\rm abs}^{2}e^{\kappa B_+}
                \frac{(2Q_+)!}{\kappa^{2Q_++1}}.\quad}
\tag{FRG22}
\]
The cancellation of coefficients above degree $n$ is still exact in
$g$; the bound estimated the complete original finite sum before those
cancellations and changed no polynomial.

Combining FRG13, FRG20 and FRG22 gives the explicit finite interval
\[
\begin{gathered}
0\le\log(1+r_n)\le E_{j,s},\\
E_{j,s}=2\log\frac{A_{\rm abs}}{|\mu_v\binom qv|}
 +\log\frac{2U_h}{L_h}+\kappa B_++\log((2Q_+)!)
 -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa\\
\hfill{}-F_M+\epsilon_-+\log C_m.
\end{gathered}\tag{FRG23}
\]
The mass factor $\beta_s$ cancels between these two displayed bounds
on the same original norm. In particular FRG23 has not assumed unit
mass. Positivity of the exact ratio proves that this computed upper
bound is nonnegative throughout its stated domain.

\subsection{Evaluation of the first-row scale and the cancelled leading term}
All the following limits fix the actual quartet and conductor, including
$\mu_v$, and let $j\equiv1\pmod4$ tend to infinity. They hold for both
$s=1$ and $s=j$. First, $Q_\pm\asymp j^2$, $Q_+-Q_-=16j-48$,
$B_+=O(j)$, $R'=O(j)$, and $m=O(j)$. Eventually $M=M_0$,
so $F_M=f_\theta(Q_-)$. Directly from FRG15,
$\epsilon_-=O(j)$; also
$\log C_m\le\log(2m+1)+4m\log2=O(j+\log j)$.
Since $2h/\pi\le\sin h\le h$, $\log(U_h/L_h)=O(\log q)$.
The fixed-$v$ product for $\binom qv$ makes
$|\log|\lambda||=O(\log q)$.

For every integer $N\ge1$, integration bounds on the increasing
function $\log x$ give
\[
N\log N-N+1\le\log(N!)\le N\log N-N+1+\log N.
\tag{FRG24}
\]
Consequently
\[
\begin{split}
\log((2Q_+)!) -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa
&\le f_\theta(Q_+)+1+\log(2Q_+)-\log\theta\\
&\qquad +(2Q_++1)\log(\theta/\kappa).
\end{split}\tag{FRG25}
\]
Here $\log(\theta/\kappa)\le h/(\theta-h)$, so the last term is
$O(1)$. Since $f_\theta'(x)=2\log(2x/\theta)$, its integral between
$Q_-$ and $Q_+$ is $O(j\log j)$. Every term of the fully finite
FRG23 is now controlled, proving
\[
\boxed{\quad 0\le\log(1+r_n)=O_{\rm actual}(j\log j).\quad}
\tag{FRG26}
\]

The leading terms of the two leverages themselves can also be
evaluated without taking a difference of uncontrolled remainders.
For the upper bound on $\nu_{m,s}$ choose the actual monic trial
$P(S')=(S'-c')^m$. Put
$B_-=\max\{1,R',2\ell\}$ and $Q_0=n+\ell$. The same whole-line
integral used in FRG22 now gives
\[
\nu_{m,s}\le
2\beta_sU_he^{\kappa B_-}
                  \frac{(2Q_0)!}{\kappa^{2Q_0+1}}.
\tag{FRG27}
\]
The factor $|y|^{2m}$ is bounded by $(|y|+B_-)^{2m}$ and
$q'+m=n$, so the exponent is exactly $2Q_0$.
Both $Q_--q$ and $Q_0-q$ are $O(j)$. Equations FRG20, FRG24--25,
FRG27 and the preceding explicit estimates imply
\[
\log\nu_{m,s}=\log\beta_s+f_\theta(q)+O_{\rm actual}(j\log j).
\tag{FRG28}
\]
To retain the full original factorial normalization, write
$b=s/2=2\ell+1/2$ and $u=n+2\ell$. Then
\[
h_{n,s}=\sqrt2\beta_s\,n!\,\Gamma(u+1/2),\qquad
\Gamma(u+1/2)=\frac{\sqrt\pi(2u)!}{4^u u!}.
\tag{FRG29}
\]
FRG24 in these three factorials shows
$\log\Gamma(u+1/2)=u\log u-u+O(\log u)$.
Both $n-q$ and $u-q$ are $O(j)$; integration of $\log x$ between
these arguments and $q$ therefore gives
\[
\log h_{n,s}=\log\beta_s+2(q\log q-q)+O_{\rm actual}(j\log j).
\tag{FRG30}
\]
Subtracting the two controlled expressions in FRG28 and FRG30, and
then using the exact identity FRG13 together with FRG26, proves
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
\log(1+\zeta_{\rm low})
 &=2q\log(4/\pi)+O_{\rm actual}(j\log j),\\
\log(1+\zeta_{\rm tot})
 &=2q\log(4/\pi)+O_{\rm actual}(j\log j).
\end{aligned}}\tag{FRG31}
\]
Thus the same positive order-$q$ term belongs to both original
leverages and cancels in their exact corrected ratio. This conclusion
uses no limiting theorem for a growing-rank determinant and no derivative
of an asymptotic error.

Finally the original SFB13--15 four-copy update has budget
$\beta_n=4\log(1+r_n)$. Its common signed increment satisfies
$|\delta L_n|\le\beta_n$, either by SFB8 integrated along this
positive update, or by the same original projection determinants.
For this first original row, FRG26 therefore yields
\[
0\le\beta_n=O_{\rm actual}(j\log j),\qquad
\frac{|\delta L_n|}{j q}\longrightarrow0.
\tag{FRG32}
\]
Each original attained quotient in CPQ14 also has its first-row
logarithmic determinant increment between $0$ and $\beta_n$:
along the same positive path, its derivative is the trace of the
positive relative derivative against the orthogonal projector on its
minimum-lift quotient space, which lies between zero and the full
trace. Integrating proves the claim with the original quotient kept.
Hence each of the five complementary first-row increments is
$O_{\rm actual}(j\log j)$ as well. This evaluates one actual row;
FRG32 makes no assertion about the remaining $q$ consecutive rows.

\label{MPBUILD:FIRST_ROW_PROOF:end}

% EXACT INPUT SHA256 b08a6dc51f59d1e17ab5fa7fded854e68377feb2f99ea41c86c19ac1ae7f634e
\clearpage
\label{MPBUILD:FIRST_ROW_MIXED_GAMMA:start}
% Full proof body. Base source SHA256:
% 6b76e69e05a657c6d6675df2e5ad15345c0d0d5263da3bc0d40dc49b88319dc1
\section{The first original mixed row in the full Gamma polynomial ideal}
\label{sec:FMG}

Keep the original hypothetical simple quartet, fixed five-orbit period,
logarithm branch, complete nonreduced conductor, and both source pairs
of NSO and CPQ. This calculation concerns their actual first appended
target row, rather than a freely chosen matrix update. Put
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 k=4l+1\ge9,\qquad j=k+4,\qquad q=(j+1)^2,\qquad\\
 q'=(j-7)^2,\qquad c'=j/2-4,
 \qquad n=q-v,\quad m=n-q'=16j-48-v .
 \end{gathered}\tag{FMG1}
\]
Here $v=\operatorname{ord}_0 E_A$ is the actual conductor order.
The retained BTK estimate is $v\le32$ on its stated original domain;
the earlier $v\le80$ already makes $m>0$ in this calculation.
Let $s=1$ or $s=j$, according to the prescribed source pair. Throughout,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \beta_{a,u,w}&=a(\tfrac12-\delta-i\gamma)+2u\delta+2wi\gamma,\\
 \chi_a(S)&=\prod_{u,w=0}^a(S-\beta_{a,u,w}),\\
 E_A(z)&=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}e^{\beta_{8,u,w}z},\\
 \mu_r&=E_A^{(r)}(0),\\
 (\mathcal T_Ap)(S')&=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}p(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}),\\
 \ell&=\mu_v\binom qv\ne0 .
\end{aligned}                                                     \tag{FMG2}
\]
The symbol $\ell$ in FMG2 is a leading coefficient, separate from the
integer $l$ in $k=4l+1$. No root, period coefficient or conductor
factor has been discarded. Define the actual polynomials
\[
        \chi_-:=\chi_{j-8},\qquad g:=\mathcal T_A\chi_j .
                                                               \tag{FMG3}
\]
For every lower root $\beta_{j-8,a,b}$, each summand defining
$g(\beta_{j-8,a,b})$ evaluates $\chi_j$ at the upper root
$\beta_{j,a+u,b+w}$. Thus $\chi_-$ divides $g$ exactly.
The first nonzero moment in FMG2 gives
$\deg g=n$ and $\operatorname{lc}_{S'}g=\ell$ by the binomial
formula. This is a divisibility assertion for the full translated
polynomial, including every coefficient.

The original measure and orthonormal polynomials are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
       |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,\qquad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{a+1,s}=yp_{a,s}-a(a-1+s/2)p_{a-1,s},\\
 h_{a,s}=M_sa!(s/2)_a,\qquad
 \phi_a(S')=p_{a,s}((S'-c')/i)/\sqrt{h_{a,s}},\\
 \langle f_1,f_2\rangle_s=
  \int_{\mathbb R}\overline{f_1(c'+iy)}f_2(c'+iy)\,dm_s(y).
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG4}
\]
This inner product is conjugate-linear in its first argument.
In particular the leading coefficient of $\phi_a$ in $S'$ is
$i^{-a}/\sqrt{h_{a,s}}$.

\subsection{The exact original row and its two coupled leverages}
Use exactly the NSO12 quotient columns $F_b=N_b/E_A$, $1\le b\le m$.
Their numerators lie on the original degree-$j$ grid and vanish to
order $v$ at zero. The original lower evaluation and numerator
matrices at the preceding cutoff are
\[
 V_0[a,(u,w)]=\phi_a(\beta_{j-8,u,w}),\qquad
 T_0[a,b]=\Lambda_{F_b}(\phi_a),\qquad 0\le a<n,
                                                               \tag{FMG5}
\]
where $\Lambda_F(S'^a)=F^{(a)}(0)$ is complex-linear. There are
$q'+m=n$ columns in $[V_0,T_0]$. To prove their independence,
a zero combination of their first $n$ native values has a generating
germ $F$ with $F^{(a)}(0)=0$ for $0\le a<n$, since the polynomial
change of basis in FMG4 is invertible. Its numerator $E_AF$ is
a degree-$j$ grid exponential polynomial and vanishes through
order $n+v-1=q-1$. The original upper Vandermonde is invertible,
so that numerator is zero. The NSO12 quotient frame then kills
the numerator coefficient, and the lower Vandermonde kills the
remaining lower coefficient. This proves that $[V_0,T_0]$ is
square and invertible, with its complete original frame.

Write the actual next rows as $v_n,t_n$. Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_L=V_0^*V_0,\qquad P_V=V_0A_L^{-1}V_0^*,\qquad
 D_0=T_0^*(I-P_V)T_0,\\
 \zeta=v_nA_L^{-1}v_n^*,\qquad
 \rho=t_n-v_nA_L^{-1}V_0^*T_0,\qquad
 r=\frac{\rho D_0^{-1}\rho^*}{1+\zeta}.
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG6}
\]
These are precisely the first SFB12 and NTE1 quantities. Inverses
exist by the independence just proved. Set
$C_0=[V_0,T_0]$ and $w_n=[v_n,t_n]$. The total leverage is
$\zeta_{\rm all}=w_n(C_0^*C_0)^{-1}w_n^*$.

Expand $g=\sum_{a=0}^n c_a\phi_a$ with column
$c_<=(c_0,\ldots,c_{n-1})^{\mathsf T}$. The leading coefficient is
$c_n=\ell i^n\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$. Every column functional in FMG5
annihilates $g$. For the numerator columns, the full Leibniz identity
gives
\[
 \Lambda_{F_b}(\mathcal T_A\chi_j)
   =\Lambda_{E_AF_b}(\chi_j)
   =\sum_{u,w}(N_b)_{uw}\chi_j(\beta_{j,u,w})=0.
\]
For the lower columns, FMG3 gives the same zero. These are
complex-linear equations, so their coefficient row is an ordinary
transpose. Therefore
\[
 w_n=-c_n^{-1}c_<^{\mathsf T}C_0,\qquad
 1+\zeta_{\rm all}=\frac{\|g\|_s^2}{|\ell|^2h_{n,s}},\qquad
 1+r=\frac{1+\zeta_{\rm all}}{1+\zeta}.
                                                               \tag{FMG7}
\]
The middle identity uses the invertibility of $C_0$, not a
replacement of its Gram by the identity. To prove the last
identity with every cross term retained, replace the numerator
columns in $C_0$ by $(I-P_V)T_0$ using the invertible triangular
column operation which subtracts $V_0A_L^{-1}V_0^*T_0$.
The preceding Gram then has diagonal blocks $A_L,D_0$ and the new
row is $[v_n,\rho]$. Consequently
$\zeta_{\rm all}=\zeta+\rho D_0^{-1}\rho^*$, which gives FMG7.

\subsection{A polynomial minimum for the complete lower denominator}
For each integer $a\ge0$ let
\[
 \nu_a=\min_{P\text{ monic},\,\deg P=a}
                  \|\chi_-P\|_s^2>0.
                                                               \tag{FMG8}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M02_FMG_MINIMUM
The positive quadratic minimum used in FMG6--10 is a Schur-complement
calculation \cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994}.
Here the eliminated coordinates are the coefficients of the complete
lower-root ideal. The following calculation retains the ordinary
transpose in its evaluation constraint and the Hermitian adjoint
only in its norm; the cited matrix method does not supply the
Gamma-polynomial estimates.
% END HUMAN CITATION M02_FMG_MINIMUM

The coefficients here are in the unchanged variable $S'$.
This is an attained finite-dimensional minimum in the original
measure; strict positivity follows because a nonzero polynomial
cannot vanish on the entire integration line. The transformation
$P\mapsto\ell\chi_-P$ is an explicitly invertible map from the
monic degree-$a$ affine fibre to the fibre with leading coefficient
$\ell$ and factor $\chi_-$. The norm under that map is multiplied
by exactly $|\ell|^2$; no scalar is suppressed.

At degree $n=q'+m$, a monic polynomial with factor $\chi_-$ has
orthonormal coefficient $a_n=i^n\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$. Its old coefficient
column $a_<$ must satisfy
$V_0^{\mathsf T}a_<=-a_nv_n^{\mathsf T}$. This is the exact
evaluation constraint, including the ordinary transpose.
Its unique coefficient minimum is
\[
 a_<=-a_n\overline{V_0}
        (V_0^{\mathsf T}\overline{V_0})^{-1}v_n^{\mathsf T}.
                                                               \tag{FMG9}
\]
Indeed the displayed column satisfies the constraint, and it is
orthogonal to the constraint kernel. Its squared norm is
$h_{n,s}v_nA_L^{-1}v_n^*=h_{n,s}\zeta$; the equality follows
by conjugating the middle Gram and using that the resulting scalar
is real. Adding $|a_n|^2$ proves
\[
 \boxed{\quad \nu_m=h_{n,s}(1+\zeta),\qquad
       1+r=\frac{\|g\|_s^2}{|\ell|^2\nu_m}.\quad}       \tag{FMG10}
\]
Thus the lower denominator in the corrected original row is
calculated by an actual lower-root ideal minimum. Replacing it by
one would remove the explicitly present factor $\nu_m/h_{n,s}$.

\subsection{All eight compressed matrices in exact polynomial coordinates}
Put $f_a(S')=\chi_-(S')S'^a$ for $0\le a<m$, and write
$f_a=\sum_{b<n}C_{ba}\phi_b$. Define
\[
 G=C^*C=(\langle f_a,f_b\rangle_s)_{a,b<m},\qquad
 H=C^*c_<=(\langle f_a,g\rangle_s)_{a<m},\qquad
 \mathsf A=C^{\mathsf T}T_0
       =(\Lambda_{F_b}(f_a))_{a<m,\,b\le m}.
                                                               \tag{FMG11}
\]
The column span of $C$ is exactly $\ker V_0^{\mathsf T}$:
every $f_a$ vanishes on the lower roots; their degrees are distinct;
and both spaces have dimension $m$. Thus
\[
 I-P_V=\overline C\,\overline G^{-1}C^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 D_0=\mathsf A^*\overline G^{-1}\mathsf A .                \tag{FMG12}
\]
The first displayed matrix is self-adjoint, idempotent, and has
range $\ker V_0^*$, proving it is exactly the required orthogonal
projector. It also proves the second identity. The matrix
$\mathsf A$ is invertible: if its product with $x$ is zero,
FMG12 gives $x^*D_0x=0$, and the positivity already proved forces
$x=0$. This is the original functional pairing on
$\chi_-\mathbb C[S']_{<m}$, with its row and column orientations
specified in FMG11.

The projection of $g$ onto that polynomial ideal is $CG^{-1}H$.
Its perpendicular component has leading coefficient $\ell$ and
is exactly the monic-fibre minimizer multiplied by $\ell$.
Orthogonality and FMG8 therefore give
\[
 |\ell|^2\nu_m=\|g\|_s^2-H^*G^{-1}H,\qquad
 r=\frac{H^*G^{-1}H}{|\ell|^2\nu_m},\qquad
 \frac{\rho}{\sqrt{1+\zeta}}=\mathsf b\mathsf A,
 \quad \mathsf b=-\frac{H^{\mathsf T}\overline G^{-1}}
                            {\ell i^n\sqrt{\nu_m}}.
                                                               \tag{FMG13}
\]
For the row identity, substitute FMG7 in the definition of $\rho$,
then apply FMG12 and $c_<^{\mathsf T}\overline C=H^{\mathsf T}$.
Its denominator is
$c_n\sqrt{1+\zeta}=\ell i^n\sqrt{\nu_m}$ by FMG10.
This calculation proves both the phase and the single occurrence
of the original leading coefficient.

Retain every fixed original CPQ coefficient subspace of
$\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m}$:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 I=\operatorname{im}(B_{\rm cof}J_0),\quad
 B=\operatorname{im}B_{\rm cof},\quad
 S=\operatorname{im}[B_{\rm cof},F_e],\quad
 J=\ker\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4),\\
 K_i=I\cap J,\quad W_i=I+J,\quad W=B+J,\quad
 K_c=S\cap W,\quad T_e=S+J,\qquad e=0,1.
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG14}
\]
Their matrices are the original period-dependent maps; none is
replaced by a plane having merely the same dimension. Let $X$ denote
any fixed independent column frame for one of these eight subspaces.
Set
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal A=\operatorname{diag}(\mathsf A,4),\qquad
 \mathcal G^\vee=\operatorname{diag}(\overline G^{-1},4),\qquad
 \mathcal b=\operatorname{diag}(\mathsf b,4),\qquad Y_X=\mathcal A X,\\
 \boxed{\quad
 K_{X,n}=\mathcal bY_X(Y_X^*\mathcal G^\vee Y_X)^{-1}
                           Y_X^*\mathcal b^* .\quad}
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG15}
\]
Here $\operatorname{diag}(\mathsf b,4)$ has four one-row blocks,
so FMG15 is exactly a $4\times4$ matrix. If $X$ is empty the result
is the zero matrix. To verify FMG15 in the original metric, FMG12
gives $\mathcal D_0=\mathcal A^*\mathcal G^\vee\mathcal A$,
and FMG13 gives $\mathcal U_n=\mathcal b\mathcal A$. Substituting
these identities in NTE1 proves FMG15 as literal equality, with
no spectral approximation. The map $x\mapsto\mathcal A x$ is
an isometry from $(\mathcal U,\mathcal D_0)$ to
$(\mathbb C^{4m},\mathcal G^\vee)$, with inverse
$\mathcal A^{-1}$. It carries sums and intersections bijectively;
in particular it preserves both full chains
\[
 J\subset W_i\subset W\subset T_e,
       \qquad K_i\subset I\subset K_c\subset S.             \tag{FMG16}
\]
All original invariant, conductor and connecting coefficients are
retained in $Y_X$ through their exact pairing $\mathcal A$.

Writing $b_X=\log\det(I_4+K_{X,n})$, the first actual signed row is
\[
 \delta\mathcal L_{e,n}=
 b_{T_e}+b_{K_c}+b_{W_i}+b_{K_i}-b_W-b_S-b_J-b_I .         \tag{FMG17}
\]
Equations FMG2--15 evaluate every entry using original polynomial
inner products and the actual period coefficient maps. They retain
all four eigenvalues of each matrix, including zero eigenvalues.
The same equality supplies its threshold contribution by replacing
$b_X$ by $\sum_{a=1}^4\log\max(1,\lambda_{X,a})$ with
$\lambda_{X,a}$ the four eigenvalues of FMG15. No simultaneous
diagonalization is assumed. Every matrix is positive and bounded
above by $rI_4$, since FMG15 is the unchanged NTE1 compression
and FMG13 evaluates its full relative row norm.

\subsection{Finite evaluation from the unchanged Gamma moments}
The matrices in FMG11 involve no infinite series. For explicit finite
evaluation, expand each polynomial on the original line,
$f(c'+iy)=\sum_a d_a(f)y^a$. Define
$M_r^{(s)}=\int y^r\,dm_s(y)$. Then
\[
 \langle f_1,f_2\rangle_s
    =\sum_{a,b}\overline{d_a(f_1)}d_b(f_2)M_{a+b}^{(s)}.
                                                               \tag{FMG18}
\]
These moments can be evaluated directly by the retained recurrence:
for a requested degree $r$, form the $(r+1)$-square real symmetric
Jacobi matrix with off-diagonal entries
$J_{a,a+1}=J_{a+1,a}=\sqrt{(a+1)(a+s/2)}$, $0\le a<r$,
and zero diagonal. Then
\[
        M_r^{(s)}=M_s(J^r)_{00}.                         \tag{FMG19}
\]
Indeed multiplication by $y$ in the orthonormal frame
$p_{a,s}/\sqrt{h_{a,s}}$ has exactly these entries by FMG4.
Starting in degree zero, $r$ multiplications never visit a degree
larger than $r$, so the finite truncation changes no path contributing
to that matrix entry. Its constant component has the displayed
integral, including $M_s$. Odd moments vanish, while all even
moments are finite positive sums of these exact recurrence paths.

The entries of $\mathsf A$ have the finite original conductor
calculation
\[
\begin{gathered}
 f_{b,a}=\frac{\nu_{b,a+v}-\sum_{d=v+1}^{a+v}
          \binom{a+v}{d}\mu_df_{b,a+v-d}}
          {\binom{a+v}{v}\mu_v},\qquad
 \nu_{b,r}=\sum_{u,w}(Z_{\exp,j})_{uw,b}\beta_{j,u,w}^{r},\\
 \mathsf A_{ab}=
       \sum_r[S'^r](\chi_-(S')S'^a)f_{b,r}.
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG20}
\]
The triangular equation is exactly the coefficient of $z^{a+v}$
in $E_AF_b=N_b$, retaining every factorial and $\mu_v$.
Thus all entries in FMG15 are finite expressions in the original
roots, full conductor, actual chosen quotient frame, actual flag
frames and Gamma moments. This gives an executable polynomial
evaluation of the original first row, with no new choice of period.

\subsection{A conductor-moment witness inside the actual residual}
The lower root set is symmetric about $c'$ under negation and
conjugation. More explicitly
\[
 i^{-q'}\chi_-(c'+iy)=
 \prod_{a,b=0}^{j-8}\bigl(y-(2b-j+8)\gamma+i(2a-j+8)\delta\bigr)
                                                               \tag{FMG21}
\]
is real and even as a polynomial in real $y$. Therefore
$|\chi_-(c'+iy)|^2dm_s(y)$ is an even positive measure.
Its monic degree-$a$ orthogonal polynomial in $y$ has parity
$(-1)^a$: reflecting it and multiplying by $(-1)^a$ gives another
monic minimizer with the same norm, and uniqueness identifies them.
Let $P_a$ be its monic polynomial in $S'$ under the exact map
$p(y)\mapsto i^ap((S'-c')/i)$. It has coefficient
$[S'^{a-1}]P_a=-ac'$ for $a\ge1$, while $P_0=1$; in every
case $\|\chi_-P_a\|_s^2=\nu_a$.

Define the actual centered conductor moment
\[
 \eta_A=\frac{\mu_{v+1}}{(v+1)\mu_v}-4,
          \qquad \beta_m^-=\frac{\nu_m}{\nu_{m-1}}>0 .   \tag{FMG22}
\]
The coefficient of $S^{q-1}$ in $\chi_j$ is $-qj/2$,
because its roots have exact mean $j/2$. Using FMG2 gives
\[
 [S'^{n-1}](g/\ell)
    =n\left(\frac{\mu_{v+1}}{(v+1)\mu_v}-\frac j2\right)
    =-nc'+n\eta_A .                                    \tag{FMG23}
\]
The displayed division by $\ell$ is the explicit affine-fibre map
in FMG8; it does not alter the original polynomial $g$.
The coefficient of $S'^{n-1}$ in $\chi_-P_m$ is $-nc'$.
Their difference lies in $\chi_-\mathbb C[S']_{<m}$ and its
coefficient at $S'^{n-1}$ is $n\eta_A$. Its component along the orthogonal
column $\chi_-P_{m-1}$ is consequently $n\eta_A$, because every
other column has smaller degree. Pythagoras therefore proves
\[
 \boxed{\quad
     r\ge \frac{n^2|\eta_A|^2\nu_{m-1}}{\nu_m}
          =\frac{n^2|\eta_A|^2}{\beta_m^-}.\quad}         \tag{FMG24}
\]
This is an original-data lower witness, which may vanish for the
actual period; no nonvanishing is assumed. Even when it vanishes,
FMG13 and FMG15 retain all other orthogonal components and the
entire signed mixed row. In particular the exact criterion for
$r=0$ is $H=0$, equivalently $g=\ell\chi_-P_m$ in the original
Gamma inner product. Equality of a single conductor moment alone
does not assert this full polynomial identity.

\subsection{Position in the full return}
The old target cutoff for this row is $N=q_j-1$, hence native
cutoff $n-1=q_j-v-1$. Its new target cutoff is $q_j$, so its
weight in the original four-endpoint return is one. FMG15--17
apply separately to $e=0$ and $e=1$ and both prescribed source
orders. They have no effect on the coefficients of the other
rows. Bounds for this row must therefore be subtracted from the
full signed profile by its exact weight; the remaining interior
rows still require their original arithmetic calculation. The
complete CPQ identity remains
\[
 4S_{K,j}=\mathcal L_e+V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A,
                                                               \tag{FMG25}
\]
and all four complementary returns use the same original rows.
The calculation above does not replace any of those returns by
a dimension or an assigned constant.

\subsection{Receiving the proved finite endpoint estimate in every signed term}
The complete companion calculation FRG15--31, using the same actual
$g,\ell,\nu_m$, proves the following finite input. Put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 L_s=(s-1)/4,\quad \theta=\pi/2,\quad h=1/q,\quad
 \kappa=\theta-h,\quad Q_-=q'+L_s,\quad Q_+=q+L_s,\\
 \sigma=(\delta^2+\gamma^2)^{1/2},\quad R'=(j-8)\sigma,
 \quad M_0=2Q_-/\theta,\quad M=\max\{M_0,2R'\},\quad\\
 B_+=\max\{1,(j+8)\sigma,2L_s\},\quad
 A_{\rm abs}=\sum_{u,w}|a_{uw}|,\\
 L_h=\frac{\pi}{\Gamma(3/4)^2}(\sin h)^{3/2},\quad
 U_h=\frac{\Gamma(1/4)^2}{2\pi}(\sin h)^{-1/2},\quad\\
 f_\theta(x)=2x\log(2x/\theta)-2x,\quad
 F_M=2Q_-\log M-\theta M,\\
 C_m=(2m+1)\binom{2m}{m}^{2},\quad
 \epsilon_-=\left(\theta-\frac{2Q_-}M\right)
       +\frac{Q_-}{M^2}+h(M+1)+\frac{4q'R'}M,\\
 \begin{aligned}
 E_{j,s}&=2\log\frac{A_{\rm abs}}{|\ell|}
 +\log\frac{2U_h}{L_h}+\kappa B_++\log((2Q_+)!)\\
 &\quad -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa-F_M+\epsilon_-+\log C_m.
 \end{aligned}
\end{gathered}                                                   \tag{FMG26}
\]
With these retained finite constants, that proof gives
$0\le\log(1+r)\le E_{j,s}$. In particular $E_{j,s}$ is nonnegative
at every original $j\ge13$, as follows also from the two proved bounds on the
positive ratio FMG10. For each fixed original quartet and conductor,
the actual choice eventually satisfies $M=M_0$ and
$E_{j,s}=O_{\rm actual}(j\log j)$,
for both $s=1,j$. The companion proof includes the complete Gamma
density estimate at $h=1/q$, the monic minimum on an original
unit interval, the whole-line upper integral, and all factorials;
the estimate is not an assumed analytic input.

Here is its exact effect on the entire signed row. The two chains
in FMG16 imply
\[
\begin{split}
 0&\le (b_{T_e}-b_W)+(b_{W_i}-b_J)
          \le b_{T_e}-b_J\le4\log(1+r),\\
 0&\le (b_S-b_{K_c})+(b_I-b_{K_i})
          \le b_S-b_{K_i}\le4\log(1+r).
\end{split}                                                     \tag{FMG27}
\]
Indeed for nested subspaces the orthogonal projectors in their
common original metric are ordered; compressing by the same row
preserves this order. Monotonicity of $\log\det(I+K)$ follows
by differentiating along their positive connecting segment, or by
the ordered eigenvalues. The missing middle increment in each
chain is nonnegative, proving the middle bound. The last uses
$0\preceq K_X\preceq rI_4$. Subtraction of these two nonnegative
quantities gives the exact signed expression FMG17. It follows that
\[
 |\delta\mathcal L_{e,n}|\le4E_{j,s},\qquad
 |\delta\mathcal P_{e,n}|\le4E_{j,s},\qquad
 0\le\delta V_{X,n}\le4E_{j,s}
       \quad(X=b,{\rm rem},s,A).                         \tag{FMG28}
\]
For the threshold quantity $\delta\mathcal P$, apply the same
argument to $\sum_{a=1}^4\log\max(1,\lambda_a(K_X))$.
The min--max characterization orders every eigenvalue when two
Hermitian matrices are ordered: restrict the larger Rayleigh
quotient to each test subspace, take its extremum there, and then
the same extremum over subspaces. The increasing scalar function
$\log\max(1,x)$ preserves those four inequalities. Thus the
same two chain estimates hold with upper bound
$4\log\max(1,r)\le4\log(1+r)$. This also retains every zero
and every eigenvalue below one.

For the four CPQ returns, their first-row increments are the paired
determinant increments on the actual quotients
$W/W_i$, $\mathcal U/T_e$, $S/K_c$, and $I/K_i$, respectively.
For any fixed $Y\supset K$ in this list, the determinant of its
attained quotient Gram is $\det G_Y/\det G_K$ times the fixed
frame factor, by full block elimination. The factor cancels in a
successive increment, leaving $b_Y-b_K$. The same order argument
places it between zero and $4\log(1+r)$, proving the final part
of FMG28. For $Y=\mathcal U$ its compression is exactly $rI_4$.
All four quotient fibres and all their cross terms therefore
remain in the row-wise CPQ identity.

Since $j=k+4$ and $q_k=(k+1)^2$, each quantity in FMG28 divided
by $kq_k$ tends to zero. The original endpoint weight is one.
Consequently the first original row can be removed from the
leading $kq_k$ calculation with the explicit finite error in
FMG28, simultaneously in the signed spectral profile and in
all four complementary CPQ returns. This statement concerns this
one specified row; it leaves all remaining rows in their original
correlated expressions.

\label{MPBUILD:NATIVE_CONDUCTOR_SUBQUOTIENT_TRANSFER:start}
% New receiver of the complete SGT conductor theorem on DNE/CIT/CPQ flags.
% Base source14 SHA256 6b76e69e05a657c6d6675df2e5ad15345c0d0d5263da3bc0d40dc49b88319dc1.
% All original coefficient frames and all lower-state fibres are retained.
\begingroup
\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}
\providecommand{\rank}{\operatorname{rank}}
\section{The original conductor on every native mixed subquotient}
\label{sec:NCT}

This section receives the proved SGT7--28 estimates on the actual
DNE6--9 and NSO12--15 spaces, and then on the fixed CIT/CPQ flags.
The exterior quotient argument is already present in CEP37--38;
we give its complete proof in the form needed here.  The new receiver
is the full finite numerator-observation metric, with the conductor
multiples retained as its entire quotient denominator, and the
rank-dependent SGT error on every original native subquotient.

Fix the original actual hypothetical simple quartet, its fixed original
five-orbit period and logarithm branch.  Keep
\(0<\delta<1/2\), \(\gamma>2\), every coefficient \(a_{uw}\)
of the nonzero original conductor, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \beta_{a,u,w}=a/2+(2u-a)\delta+i(2w-a)\gamma,
 \quad 0\le u,w\le a,\quad a=4\ell+1\ge9,\\
 E_A(z)=\sum_{u,w=0}^{8}a_{uw}e^{\beta_{8,u,w}z},\quad
 \mu_n=E_A^{(n)}(0),\quad v=\operatorname{ord}_0 E_A\le80,
 \quad \mu_v\ne0,\quad \alpha_A=v!/\mu_v,\\
 q_a=(a+1)^2,\quad q'_a=(a-7)^2,\quad m_a=q_a-q'_a-v,
 \quad c_0=a/2,\quad c_1=a/2-4,\\
 N\ge q_a-1,\quad D=N-v,\quad s\in\{1,a\}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT1}
\]
The original domain of the fixed period, including its original rank
conditions, is unchanged.  The roots in each displayed grid are
distinct: equality of two roots gives separately equality of the
indices from their real and imaginary parts.  Every polynomial
space below has complex coefficients.  Its Hermitian inner product
is conjugate-linear in the first variable.  The complex-linear dual
consists of linear, rather than conjugate-linear, functionals.

The full original measures, masses, recurrence and frames are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
        |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,
 \quad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+s/2)p_{n-1,s},\\
 h_{n,s}=M_sn!(s/2)_n,\quad
 \phi_{n,s,c}(S)=p_{n,s}((S-c)/i)/\sqrt{h_{n,s}},\\
 \|P\|_{s,c}^2=\int_{\mathbb R}|P(c+iy)|^2dm_s(y).
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT2}
\]
SGT3--5 proves that these are orthonormal frames on every indicated
finite polynomial space.  Their leading coefficients are
\(i^{-n}/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}\); neither this phase nor the mass is removed.

\subsection{The exact dual conductor square}

Write \(\mathcal P_d=\mathbb C[S]_{\le d}\), with
\(\mathcal P_{-1}=0\), and distinguish the target variable \(S'\).
The original, unscaled conductor map is
\[
\begin{gathered}
 (\mathcal T_AP)(S')=\sum_{u,w=0}^{8}a_{uw}
                       P(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}),\\
 A_{D,s}: (\mathcal P_N/\mathcal P_{v-1},\|\cdot\|_{s,c_0,\rm quot})
          \longrightarrow(\mathbb C[S']_{\le D},\|\cdot\|_{s,c_1}),
 \qquad [P]\longmapsto\mathcal T_AP .
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT3}
\]
It is the SGT7 isomorphism.  For completeness,
\(\mathcal T_AS^n=\sum_{j=0}^n\binom nj\mu_jS'^{n-j}\).
If \(n\ge v\), its leading coefficient is
\(\binom nv\mu_v\ne0\), and its degree is \(n-v\).
Successive elimination in degree gives surjectivity, and the full
kernel is \(\mathcal P_{v-1}\).  The quotient is represented
isometrically by the original orthogonal complement spanned by
\(\phi_{v,s,c_0},\ldots,\phi_{N,s,c_0}\).

For an analytic germ \(F\) define
\(\Lambda_F(S'^n)=F^{(n)}(0)\).  An upper-variable germ is paired
with \(S^n\) by the same derivative convention.  The contravariant
dual map in the direction relevant here is
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_{D,s}^{\vee}:(\mathbb C[S']_{\le D})^{\vee}
       \longrightarrow(\mathcal P_N/\mathcal P_{v-1})^{\vee},
 \qquad \Lambda\longmapsto\Lambda\circ A_{D,s},\\
 A_{D,s}^{\vee}\Lambda_F=\Lambda_{E_AF}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT4}
\]
To prove the identity, on every \(0\le n\le N\) its left side is
\(\sum_{j=v}^n\binom nj\mu_jF^{(n-j)}(0)\), precisely
\((E_AF)^{(n)}(0)\) by the Leibniz rule.  For \(n<v\) both
sides vanish.  The highest derivative of \(F\) that occurs is
\(n-v\le D\), so the map is well defined on these finite jets;
it does not depend on any higher derivative.  Conversely the
nonzero coefficient \(\binom{v+r}{v}\mu_v\) recursively recovers
the derivative of order \(r\) of \(F\) from these products.
This also proves the isomorphism on finite jets directly.

Let \(w=(\Lambda_F(\phi_{n,s,c_1}))_{n=0}^D\), and let
\(\widetilde w=(\Lambda_{E_AF}(\phi_{v+n,s,c_0}))_{n=0}^D\).
If \(A_{D,s}\) denotes its original orthonormal matrix as in SGT10,
then
\[
 \widetilde w=A_{D,s}^{\mathsf T}w,
 \qquad
 \Lambda_{E_AF}(\phi_{n,s,c_0})=0\quad(0\le n<v).
 \tag{NCT5}
\]
Indeed the \(j\)-th source basis vector has image
\(\sum_i(A_{D,s})_{ij}\phi_{i,s,c_1}\), and applying the
complex-linear functional gives the ordinary transpose in NCT5.
There is no complex adjoint in this algebraic square.  Squared
functional norms are \(w^*w\) and \(\widetilde w^*\widetilde w\).
If \(A=U\Sigma V^*\) is a singular-value decomposition, then
\(A^{\mathsf T}=\overline V\Sigma U^{\mathsf T}\) is one as well.
Thus \(A\) and \(A^{\mathsf T}\), and their inverses, have exactly
the same singular values and every corresponding exterior norm.

No scalar \(\alpha_A\) is inserted into NCT3--5.  In particular,
the literal DNE3 germ is \(F_b=\mathcal N_b/E_A\), and its image
is \(\Lambda_{\mathcal N_b}\), with coefficient one.  The original
primary-coordinate maps involving \(\alpha_A\) are retained below.

\subsection{All numerator observations and all conductor columns}

Use the original lexicographic upper-root coefficient order.  For
\(\nu\in\mathbb C^{q_a}\) put
\(\mathcal N_\nu(z)=\sum_{u,w}\nu_{uw}e^{\beta_{a,u,w}z}\).
Let \(\mathscr N_{v,a}\) be the coefficient subspace on which
\(\mathcal N_\nu^{(j)}(0)=0\) for all \(0\le j<v\).
The upper Vandermonde \(\mathbb V_a=(\beta_{a,u,w}^{n})\),
\(0\le n<q_a\), is invertible, so
\(\dim\mathscr N_{v,a}=q_a-v\).

The full original primary conductor \(C_a\), with \(q'_a\) rows
and \(q_a\) columns, is fixed by the literal identity
\[
 \mathcal N_{C_a^{\mathsf T}\eta}(z)
       =E_A(z)\sum_{u,w=0}^{a-8}\eta_{uw}
                         e^{\beta_{a-8,u,w}z}.
 \tag{NCT6}
\]
This identity follows by collecting all original shifts, since
\(\beta_{a-8,u,w}+\beta_{8,r,t}=\beta_{a,u+r,w+t}\).
It retains repeated contributions to the same resulting root.
The map \(C_a^{\mathsf T}\) is injective: its zero image would make
the product in NCT6 identically zero; on a neighborhood where
\(E_A\ne0\) the lower exponential sum is zero, hence everywhere
by analyticity, and its distinct-root Vandermonde makes \(\eta=0\).
Its image lies in \(\mathscr N_{v,a}\), by the zero order of \(E_A\).

Use exactly the DNE2--3/NSO12 frame:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 W_{v,a}=\mathbb V_a^{-1}P_v,\qquad
 J_{v,a}=P_v^{\mathsf T}\mathbb V_a C_a^{\mathsf T},
 \qquad W_{v,a}J_{v,a}=C_a^{\mathsf T},\\
 Z_{\exp,a}=W_{v,a}E_{\mathcal J_a},\qquad
 F_{a,b}=\mathcal N_{Z_{\exp,a}e_b}/E_A .
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT7}
\]
Here \(P_v\) inserts zero in the first \(v\) positions;
\(\mathcal I_a\) is the first ordered set of \(q'_a\) rows
with nonzero minor of \(J_{v,a}\), and \(\mathcal J_a\) is its
complement.  Such a minor exists by the proved injectivity in NCT6.
Restricting a relation among columns of
\([J_{v,a},E_{\mathcal J_a}]\) first to \(\mathcal I_a\) and
then to its complement proves that they are independent.
Consequently \([C_a^{\mathsf T},Z_{\exp,a}]\) is the complete
frame of \(\mathscr N_{v,a}\), and \(Z_{\exp,a}\) represents its
quotient by \(\im C_a^{\mathsf T}\).  Every actual minor is unchanged.

Define the full upper observation matrix and its covariance by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathsf E_{a,N,s}[n,(u,w)]=\phi_{n,s,c_0}(\beta_{a,u,w}),
 \quad 0\le n\le N,\\
 K_{a,N,s}=\mathsf E_{a,N,s}^*\mathsf E_{a,N,s},\\
 \widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)}=Z_{\exp,a}^*
 \left[K_{a,N,s}-K_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T}
  (\overline C_a K_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
              \overline C_a K_{a,N,s}\right]Z_{\exp,a}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT8}
\]
The square covariance \(K_{a,N,s}\) is positive definite.  Its first
\(q_a\) observation rows equal an invertible triangular change of
the upper Vandermonde, because the polynomials NCT2 have nonzero
leading coefficients.  Therefore \(\mathsf E_{a,N,s}\) has full
column rank for every stated \(N\), including \(N=q_a-1\).
The middle inverse in NCT8 is positive definite by this fact and
injectivity of \(C_a^{\mathsf T}\).

The same covariance has an exact derivative-coordinate expression.
Let \(\mathsf V_{a,N}[n,(u,w)]=\beta_{a,u,w}^{n}\), and write
\(S^r=\sum_{n=0}^N\mathsf B_{s,c_0,N}[n,r]\phi_{n,s,c_0}(S)\).
The full monomial Gram is \(H_{s,c_0,N}=\mathsf B^*\mathsf B\).
Applying the complex-linear numerator functional to this identity gives
\[
 \mathsf V_{a,N}\nu=\mathsf B_{s,c_0,N}^{\mathsf T}
                          \mathsf E_{a,N,s}\nu,\qquad
 K_{a,N,s}=\mathsf V_{a,N}^*
                \overline{H_{s,c_0,N}^{-1}}\mathsf V_{a,N}.
 \tag{NCT8a}
\]
Indeed \((\mathsf B^{\mathsf T})^{-*}(\mathsf B^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
=\overline{(\mathsf B^*\mathsf B)^{-1}}\).  Thus NCT8 is the full
numerator derivative-dual covariance restricted to the vanishing-moment
space and minimized over every conductor multiple.  This proves the
coordinate morphism, including the ordinary transpose and Hermitian
conjugation, without changing the original measure or coefficient norm.



% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M03_NCT_MINIMUM
The minimum below uses the classical Schur-complement method
\cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994} on the Hermitian Gram of
$[C_a^{\mathsf T},Z_{\exp,a}]$ in $K_{a,N,s}$.
The eliminated block is invertible by the rank argument above, so
no pseudoinverse or unstated range condition is needed.
% END HUMAN CITATION M03_NCT_MINIMUM

For every original coefficient vector \(x\in\mathbb C^{m_a}\),
the exact squared norm represented by NCT8 is
\[
 x^*\widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)}x
 =\min_{\eta\in\mathbb C^{q'_a}}
  \|\mathsf E_{a,N,s}(Z_{\exp,a}x+C_a^{\mathsf T}\eta)\|_2^2.
 \tag{NCT9}
\]
Differentiate the right side with respect to its real and imaginary
coordinates, or complete its positive square.  The unique minimizer is
\[
 \eta_{\min}=-(\overline C_aK_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
                         \overline C_aK_{a,N,s}Z_{\exp,a}x.
\]
Substitution gives NCT8.  The quotient is positive definite because
\([C_a^{\mathsf T},Z_{\exp,a}]\) is independent.  The minimum
retains every conductor column, including every mixed covariance term.

For \(\nu\in\mathscr N_{v,a}\) the entries of
\(\mathsf E_{a,N,s}\nu\) below row \(v\) vanish, since those
polynomials use derivatives of order below \(v\).  This proves that
the ambient polynomial dual norm in NCT9 is exactly the quotient
dual norm in NCT4, not an extra truncation of the numerator.  All
rows \(0,\ldots,N\) remain in NCT8.

The corresponding original native metric is exactly NSO13:
\[
 x^*D_{a,D}^{(s)}x
   =\min_{\eta\in\mathbb C^{q'_a}}
      \left\|\Lambda_{\sum_bx_bF_{a,b}}
                  +\sum_{u,w}\eta_{uw}\operatorname{ev}_{\beta_{a-8,u,w}}
      \right\|_{s,c_1,D,\vee}^2.
 \tag{NCT10}
\]
NCT4 takes every representative inside this minimum to exactly the
representative in NCT9.  In particular it takes the complete lower
evaluation subspace to the complete conductor-multiple subspace, by
NCT6.  Let \(Y\) be the span of all functionals appearing in NCT10,
and \(K\) its lower evaluation subspace.  Then NCT4 induces the
isomorphism
\[
  Y/K\ \xrightarrow{\ A_{D,s}^{\vee}\ }\
  \Lambda_{\mathscr N_{v,a}}/
                    \Lambda_{\im C_a^{\mathsf T}},
  \qquad [F]\longmapsto[E_AF].
 \tag{NCT11}
\]
The two quotient Grams in the same fixed value coordinates \(x\)
are \(D_{a,D}^{(s)}\) and \(\widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)}\).
Finite-jet injectivity follows also directly: vanishing numerator
derivatives through \(N\ge q_a-1\) kill all upper coefficients by
the Vandermonde.  Thus this isomorphism holds on every retained
endpoint and on every original coefficient fibre, without a
genericity or conditioning assumption.

\subsection{Rank-dependent exterior bounds on arbitrary subquotients}

We prove the finite-dimensional statement in full.  Let
\(B:H_0\to H_1\) be an invertible linear map of complex Hilbert
spaces, and let \(K\subset Y\subset H_0\) be any subspaces.
Both quotients have their attained quotient norms.  Put
\(Y_0=Y\cap K^\perp\) and
\(Y_1=BY\cap(BK)^\perp\).  Every \(y\in Y\) has the unique
decomposition \(y=x+k\) with \(x\in Y_0\), \(k\in K\).
The squared norm of a lift of \([y]\) is
\(\|x\|^2+\|k'\|^2\), so \(x\) is its minimum lift.
Under these isometric identifications the induced quotient map is
\[
 \overline B:Y/K\longrightarrow BY/BK,
 \qquad \overline B=P_{Y_1}B|_{Y_0},\qquad
 \overline B^{-1}=P_{Y_0}B^{-1}|_{Y_1}.
 \tag{NCT12}
\]
The second formula follows by applying \(B^{-1}\) to any
representative and subtracting its \(K\) component.  It is indeed
the inverse because the subtracted components lie respectively in
\(BK\) and \(K\).  The projections are contractions on every
exterior power: in orthonormal bases their singular values are
zeros and ones.  Restrictions and inclusions of the stated Hilbert
subspaces have exterior norm at most one.  Consequently for every
\(0\le r\le\dim(Y/K)\),
\[
 \|\textstyle\bigwedge^r\overline B\|
       \le\|\textstyle\bigwedge^r B\|,
 \qquad
 \|\textstyle\bigwedge^r\overline B^{-1}\|
       \le\|\textstyle\bigwedge^r B^{-1}\|.
 \tag{NCT13}
\]
There is no factor depending on \(\dim K\).  For any ordered
independent quotient frame of length \(r\), its squared wedge norm
is its Gram determinant.  Applying the first inequality to that
wedge and the second to its image gives
\[
 -2\log\|\textstyle\bigwedge^r B^{-1}\|
 \le\log\frac{\det G_1}{\det G_0}
 \le2\log\|\textstyle\bigwedge^r B\|.
 \tag{NCT14}
\]
These statements include \(r=0\), with determinant and exterior
norm one.  A change of the common value frame by \(U\) multiplies
both determinants by \(|\det U|^2\), which cancels in their ratio.

% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M04_NCT_EXTERIOR
For the classical exterior inner product underlying NCT14, see
Russell Lyons \cite[\S4, (4.1), p.~11 of the cited arXiv version]{human:lyons2003}.
Taking both lists there to be the same quotient frame gives its
squared wedge norm as its Gram determinant. The quotient map,
inverse, and rank-dependent transport bounds are proved here.
% END HUMAN CITATION M04_NCT_EXTERIOR



Successive restrictions and quotients obey the same rank-only bound.
Indeed a subspace of \(Y/K\) has a full preimage \(Y'\) containing
\(K\), and its inherited metric is the quotient metric of \(Y'/K\).
A subsequent quotient by a subspace with full preimage \(K'\)
is \(Y'/K'\), since the union of its original affine fibres is
precisely the affine fibre modulo \(K'\).  Its minimum over those
fibres is the same minimum.  Induction reduces any finite sequence
to NCT12 with the actual full preimages; the distortion does not
accumulate a new factor at each intermediate quotient.

Apply this to \(B=A_{D,s}^{\mathsf T}\) using NCT5, and to its
orthogonal direct sum of \(b\) copies, where \(b=1\) or \(4\).
We now specify its finite constants using the original SGT symbol.
To avoid confusing the degree \(a\) with a circle radius, denote
an admissible SGT radius by \(\varrho\in(0,1)\).
Set
\[
\begin{gathered}
 w(t)=-i\arctan t,\qquad
 g(t)=t^{-v}e^{-4w(t)}E_A(w(t)),
 \qquad g(0)=(-i)^v\mu_v/v!,\\
 \rho_n=\sqrt{n!/(s/2)_n},\quad
 L_{s,D,v}=\tfrac12\max\{1,s/2-1\}\sum_{n=1}^{D+v}\frac1n.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT15}
\]
SGT10 proves the exact original matrix
\(A_{D,s}=\operatorname{diag}(\rho_0,\ldots,\rho_D)^{-1}
 T_D(g)\operatorname{diag}(\rho_v,\ldots,\rho_{D+v})\),
where \(T_D(g)_{i,j}=g_{j-i}\) for \(i\le j\), and zero otherwise.
SGT21 proves that the products of the two forward diagonal operator
norms and of the two inverse diagonal operator norms are both at
most \(e^{2L_{s,D,v}}\).

Here is the full list of original finite constants used in SGT19:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_{\rm abs}=\sum_{u,w}|a_{uw}|,\quad
 B_0=\max\{1,\max_{u,w}|\beta_{8,u,w}|\},\quad
 C_v=A_{\rm abs}B_0^v/|\mu_v|,\\
 R_0=B_0^{-1}\min\{1,(v+1)/(2eC_v)\},\quad
 a_0=\min\{1/2,\tanh(R_0/2)\},\\
 d_0=1-\frac{a_0^2}{3(1-a_0^2)},\qquad
 M_0=\frac{2v!}{|\mu_v|}d_0^{-v}e^{2R_0},\\
 S_D(r)=\sum_{n=0}^{D}r^{-n},\quad G_{0,D}=M_0S_D(a_0),\\
 \mathcal Z_\varrho=\{\zeta:|\zeta|<\varrho,\ g(\zeta)=0\},
 \quad H_\varrho=\sum_{\zeta\in\mathcal Z_\varrho}m_\zeta,\\
 A_{\zeta,h}=\frac1{(m_\zeta-h)!}
  \left.\frac{d^{m_\zeta-h}}{dt^{m_\zeta-h}}
       \frac{(t-\zeta)^{m_\zeta}}{g(t)}\right|_{t=\zeta},
 \quad 1\le h\le m_\zeta,\\
 W_\varrho=\operatorname{arctanh}\varrho,\quad
 m_{E,\varrho}=\min_{|t|=\varrho}|E_A(w(t))|,\quad
 G_\varrho=A_{\rm abs}\varrho^{-v}e^{(B_0+4)W_\varrho},\\
 M_\varrho=\frac{\varrho^ve^{4W_\varrho}}{m_{E,\varrho}}
    +\sum_{\zeta,h}\frac{|A_{\zeta,h}|}{(\varrho-|\zeta|)^h},\quad
 P_\varrho=\sum_{\zeta,h}\frac{|A_{\zeta,h}|}{(1-|\zeta|)^h},\\
 F_{\varrho,D}=G_\varrho S_D(\varrho),\quad
 V_{\varrho,D}=M_\varrho S_D(\varrho)+P_\varrho,\quad
 V_{\varrho,D}^{\rm cap}=\min\{G_{0,D},V_{\varrho,D}\}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT16}
\]
Here admissibility means that \(g\) has no zero on the indicated
circle.  The sums retain every pole and its full order.  There are
finitely many poles in each such closed disk, and there are
admissible radii arbitrarily close to one, as proved in SGT14.
SGT11--19 proves from these exact original data that
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \|T_D(g)\|\le F_{\varrho,D},\quad
 \|T_D(g)^{-1}\|\le G_{0,D},\quad\\
 T_D(g)^{-1}=C_{\varrho,D}+R_{\varrho,D},
 \quad\rank C_{\varrho,D}\le H_\varrho,
 \quad\|R_{\varrho,D}\|\le V_{\varrho,D}.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{NCT17}
\]
These are proved internal inputs, including SGT11--13's explicit
zero-free disk, SGT16--18's exact principal parts and their
lower-triangular corrections.  In particular NCT17 is a decomposition
of the entire inverse; none of its principal parts has been omitted.

For \(b\) copies the inverse exceptional rank is at most
\(bH_\varrho\); its global norm and its remainder norm are still
bounded by \(G_{0,D}\) and \(V_{\varrho,D}\), respectively.
For a rank-\(r\) subquotient put \(h_b=\min\{r,bH_\varrho\}\).
On the kernel of the exceptional matrix its inverse has norm at
most \(V_{\varrho,D}\).  The singular-value minimum principle
therefore bounds every singular value after the first \(bH_\varrho\)
by that quantity, and every singular value by \(G_{0,D}\).
Multiplying the largest \(r\) singular values, and then the two
diagonal factors, gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \|\bigwedge^r((A_{D,s}^{\mathsf T})^{\oplus b})\|
      \le e^{2rL_{s,D,v}}F_{\varrho,D}^{r},\\
 \|\bigwedge^r((A_{D,s}^{\mathsf T})^{\oplus b})^{-1}\|
      \le e^{2rL_{s,D,v}}G_{0,D}^{h_b}
                    (V_{\varrho,D}^{\rm cap})^{r-h_b}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT18}
\]
For clarity, the minimum principle here follows by diagonalizing
the positive matrix of the squared norm.  Every subspace of
codimension \(j-1\) meets the span of its first \(j\) eigenvectors,
and the complement of its first \(j-1\) eigenvectors attains the
bound.  Apply this with the kernel of the exceptional matrix.
This proves the estimate even when its rank bound exceeds the
whole matrix dimension, when \(h_b=r\).

For a fixed admissible \(\varrho\), suppress that parameter in the
notation requested for the receiving forms, and define
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal U_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}
   &=4rL_{s,D,v}+2r\log^+F_{\varrho,D},\\
 \mathcal L_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}
   &=4rL_{s,D,v}+2h_b\log^+G_{0,D}
                    +2(r-h_b)\log^+V_{\varrho,D}^{\rm cap},\\
 \mathcal B_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}
   &=\max\{\mathcal L_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]},
                         \mathcal U_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{NCT19}
\]
Here \(\log^+x=\max\{0,\log x\}\); all these constants are
finite, nonnegative and exactly zero at \(r=0\).  The index \(a\)
records the original grid and centres; the conductor constants
themselves depend only on the fixed original conductor and radius.

Let \(Q\) be any original rank-\(r\) metric obtained by a finite
sequence of restrictions and attained quotients from
\((D_{a,D}^{(s)})^{\oplus b}\), and let \(\widehat Q\) be the
identical construction, in the identical coefficient and quotient
value frames, from \((\widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)})^{\oplus b}\).
Equations NCT11--14 and NCT18 prove the finite directed statement
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 -\mathcal L_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}
   \le\log\det\widehat Q-\log\det Q
   \le\mathcal U_{a,D,s,r}^{[b]}.}
 \tag{NCT20}
\]
The full preimage of each restriction and quotient under NCT11
includes the entire conductor-multiple denominator, of dimension
\(bq'_a\).  NCT13 proves why that dimension contributes no
extra volume factor in NCT20.  No fixed coefficient map is
replaced by an orthogonal surrogate; minimum lifts are used only
to prove the bound on its original attained metric.

\subsection{The uniform limit and the exact DNE scalar conventions}

For fixed \(C_D,C_r>0\), uniformly over
\(0\le D\le C_Da^2\), \(0\le r\le C_ra\), both actual
orders, \(b=1,4\), and every original admissible subquotient,
\[
 \log\det\widehat Q-\log\det Q=o(a^3)=o(aq_a).
 \tag{NCT21}
\]
Here is the limiting proof with the order of choices explicit.
Fix an admissible \(\varrho<1\).  Since
\(S_D(\varrho)\le(D+1)\varrho^{-D}\), NCT16 gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log^+F_{\varrho,D}
     \le D\log(1/\varrho)+\log(D+1)+\log^+G_\varrho,\\
 \log^+V_{\varrho,D}^{\rm cap}
     \le D\log(1/\varrho)+\log(D+1)+\log^+(M_\varrho+P_\varrho),\\
 \log^+G_{0,D}
     \le D\log(1/a_0)+\log(D+1)+\log^+M_0.
\end{gathered}
\]
The common term \(4rL_{s,D,v}\) is \(O(a^2\log(a+1))\).
The exceptional term uses \(h_b\le bH_\varrho\), fixed before
the limit, and is \(O_\varrho(a^2+\log(a+1))\).
Consequently the limsup of the uniform absolute error divided
by \(a^3\) is at most \(2C_rC_D\log(1/\varrho)\).
Only after this limit choose admissible radii approaching one.
The bound tends to zero.  This proves NCT21 without any assertion
of a zero-free unit disk, bounded pole count near its boundary,
or a coefficient condition on the original subquotients.

The original DNE scalar and transpose square is preserved exactly.
Let \(A_{\ell,a}\) be DNE4's pairing matrix and \(Z_a\) the
original low-monomial kernel columns.  Keep
\[
\begin{gathered}
 D_{a,D}^{(s)}=A_{\ell,a}^*\overline{(H_{Y,a,N}^{(s)})^{-1}}A_{\ell,a},
 \qquad B_{K,a}=Z_a^{\mathsf T}A_{\ell,a},\\
 Q_{K^\vee}=(B_{K,a}(D_{a,D}^{(s)})^{-1}B_{K,a}^*)^{-1}
             =\overline{(Z_a^*H_{Y,a,N}^{(s)}Z_a)^{-1}},\\
 \widehat Q_{K^\vee}
       =(B_{K,a}(\widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)})^{-1}B_{K,a}^*)^{-1},
 \qquad
 \widehat H_Y=\overline{A_{\ell,a}(\widehat D_{a,D}^{(s)})^{-1}
                                     A_{\ell,a}^*},\\
 \widehat Q_{K^\vee}=\overline{(Z_a^*\widehat H_YZ_a)^{-1}}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT22}
\]
For proof, a functional with coordinate column \(u\) on a Gram
\(H\) has norm \(u^*\overline{H^{-1}}u\), since its Riesz
vector is \(H^{-1}\bar u\).  Substituting this identity and
\(B_{K,a}=Z_a^{\mathsf T}A_{\ell,a}\) proves every equality
in NCT22 by matrix multiplication.  The onto map \(B_{K,a}\)
uses its full fibre, with minimum norm
\((B_{K,a}D^{-1}B_{K,a}^*)^{-1}\).  This follows by inserting
the minimum lift \(D^{-1}B_{K,a}^*(B_{K,a}D^{-1}B_{K,a}^*)^{-1}x\)
and checking that it is \(D\)-orthogonal to \(\ker B_{K,a}\).
Thus NCT20 applies with \(r=\rank B_{K,a}\); inversion changes
the sign of the logarithmic determinant difference when it is
read on the original restricted kernel Gram.

The primary-coordinate comparison is literally the earlier
KRC/RRC map \(x\mapsto\alpha_A\mathsf N_a Z_{\exp,a}x\).
For example its four multiplication maps remain
\[
 M_i=(\alpha_A\mathsf N_jZ_{\exp,j})^{-1}
           m_{i,k}(\alpha_A\mathsf N_kZ_{\exp,k}),\qquad j=k+4.
 \tag{NCT23}
\]
Both scalars and both fixed frame maps are present before their
proved cancellation.  NCT8--11 acts in the original \(x\)
coordinates, so the matrices NCT23 are unchanged.  If one instead
writes the conductor isomorphism as \(\alpha_A A_{D,s}\), its
ordinary dual is \(\alpha_A A_{D,s}^{\mathsf T}\), and its
numerator Gram is exactly \(|\alpha_A|^2\widehat D\).
Every rank-\(r\) descendant quotient then has determinant factor
\(|\alpha_A|^{2r}\), because minimization commutes with this
positive scalar.  NCT3--22 uses the unscaled map and does not
introduce this factor into DNE10.  This describes the exact scalar
map between the two conventions, rather than silently choosing one.

\subsection{All original mixed flags and all four-endpoint signs}

For \(j=k+4\), take the original coefficient space
\(\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m_j}\) and maps of EFP18:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 B=B_{\rm cof},\quad\mathbb B=\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4),\quad
 J=\ker\mathbb B,\quad\mathscr B=\im B,\quad I=\im(BJ_0),\\
 S=\im[B,F_e],\quad W=J+\mathscr B,\quad
 K_e=S\cap W,\quad T_e=S+W,\quad
 K_i=I\cap J,\quad W_i=I+J.
\end{gathered}\tag{NCT24}
\]
All spaces and the maps NCT23 are the same coefficient objects
as before.  Replace only their ambient metric by
\(\operatorname{diag}(\widehat D_{j,N-v}^{(s_1)},4)\) to define
the numerator forms.  NCT20 applies to every one of the following
original quotients, with its actual rank:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{original form}&\text{original quotient}&\text{rank}\\\hline
 G_{h,e}&K_e/\mathscr B&h_e\\
 G_{s,e}&S/K_e&c_e\\
 G_{t,e}&T_e/W&c_e\\
 Q_{{\rm rem},e}&\mathcal U/T_e&r_o-c_e\\
 A_{\rm inv}&I/K_i&r_I\\
 H_{\rm inv}&W_i/J&r_I\\
 Q_b&W/W_i&r_b\\
 Q_o&\mathcal U/W&r_o\\
 H_{{\rm conn},e}&S/\mathscr B&r_e\\
 H_{B,j,N-v}&\mathbb C^{m_j}/\ker B_j&t_j
\end{array}\tag{NCT25}
\]
The final row uses one copy; every preceding row uses four.
For \(Q_b\), any original EFP21 fixed value-frame factor remains
on both forms.  The table's maps and ranks are the complete EFP18--21
and CPQ data; NCT12 proves their metric transfer by their full
preimages under NCT11.  It also applies to every additional
restriction of these spaces, including all eight signed-profile
spaces \(T_e,K_e,W_i,K_i,W,S,J,I\), with their exact original
frames.  Their dimensions are at most \(4m_j=O(k)\), so NCT21
applies uniformly without dropping any zero eigenvalue in a
later compressed relative operator.

For a fixed-rank original form at the four cutoffs
\(N_i=(q_j-1,q_j,2q_j-1,2q_j)\), write
\(D_i=N_i-v\) and
\(\mathcal Rf=-f(N_0)-f(N_1)+f(N_2)+f(N_3)\).
Abbreviate NCT19's constants at each endpoint by \(\mathcal L_i\)
and \(\mathcal U_i\), retaining its actual degree, order, rank
and number of copies.  The exact directed return estimate is
\[
\begin{split}
 -\mathcal U_0-\mathcal U_1-\mathcal L_2-\mathcal L_3
 &\le\mathcal R\log\det\widehat Q-\mathcal R\log\det Q\\
 &\le\mathcal L_0+\mathcal L_1+\mathcal U_2+\mathcal U_3.
\end{split}\tag{NCT26}
\]
This follows by negating the upper bounds at the two negative
endpoints and adding the lower bounds at the two positive endpoints
for the lower estimate, and doing the reverse for the upper estimate.
Every endpoint has \(D_i=O(k^2)\), and all ranks in NCT25 are
\(O(k)\).  Thus each displayed return difference is \(o(kq_j)\)
in both original source pairs \((1,1)\) and \((k,j)\).
For a source-degree \(k\) form evaluated at the same target
cutoffs the identical proof uses its original degree \(k\),
centre \(k/2\), and \(s_0=1\) or \(k\); the uniform domain
still holds since \(q_j=O(k^2)\).

Every finite CPQ determinant-line identity continues to hold for
the numerator metrics, because its proof is the same sequence
of Schur minima in the same exact coefficient flags.  In particular
the original complete complementary returns \(V_b,V_{\rm rem},V_s,V_A\)
all receive NCT26, as do the original equal-rank contraction ratios
\(\det H_{\rm inv}/\det A_{\rm inv}\) and
\(\det G_t/\det G_s\): subtract the two determinant errors before
applying the endpoint signs.  For any fixed finite signed sum the
absolute error is bounded by the sum of its original summands'
\(\mathcal B\) constants, with multiplicities.  No cancellation
of an uncomputed summand is asserted.  The CPQ affine identities
\(4S_K=\mathcal L+V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A\) and
\(P_{\rm rest}=\mathcal L+V_b\) retain their exact forms on these
newly specified numerator metrics and differ from their native
values by the proved finite errors.  The result supplies an exact
numerator observation geometry and an \(o(kq_j)\) volume transfer;
the original mixed numerator matrices and their complete returns
remain the quantities to evaluate.

\endgroup

\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}
\providecommand{\rank}{\operatorname{rank}}
