\section{The original numerator kernel and the complete mixed spectral return}
\label{sec:NPK}

This calculation receives the full conductor-dual transport proved in
NCT. It uses the original DNE numerator frame and the original CPQ flags,
with their fixed value coordinates. The source of the construction is the
accepted cumulative DNE3,6, SGT7--26, CPQ1--20 and SFB12--16. The period,
its logarithm branch, the complete nonreduced conductor and its order at
zero are fixed as the original tensor degree varies. No arithmetic
coefficient or zero packet is supplied by the following calculation.

\subsection{The actual finite numerator observations}
Fix the original simple quartet
$0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$, and the original permitted period. For
$a\equiv1\pmod4$, $a\ge9$, retain
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 q_a=(a+1)^2,\qquad q'_a=(a-7)^2,\qquad
 m_a=q_a-q'_a-v,\qquad c_a=a/2,\\
 \beta_{a,u,w}=a(1/2-\delta-i\gamma)+2u\delta+2wi\gamma,
 \quad 0\le u,w\le a,\\
 E_A(z)=\sum_{r,t=0}^8a_{rt}e^{\beta_{8,r,t}z},\quad
 v=\ord_0E_A,\quad \mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)\ne0.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK1}
\]
The proved conductor bound is $v\le32$. Let $C_a$ be the original
$q'_a\times q_a$ conductor coefficient matrix, so that its ordinary
transpose sends the lower exponential coefficient vector to the numerator
of its product with $E_A$. Write $Z_a=Z_{\exp,a}$ for DNE3's fixed
$q_a\times m_a$ matrix. Thus $N_{Z_ax}/E_A$ is the original analytic
germ for coefficient $x$, and the columns of $[C_a^{\mathsf T},Z_a]$
are a basis of the numerator subspace whose first $v$ moments vanish.
These matrices include their actual chosen minors and every original
coefficient; none is replaced by a generic frame.

For $s\in\{1,a\}$ set $b=s/2$, and use the original recurrence and norms
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 p_{0,s}(y)=1,\quad p_{1,s}(y)=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}(y)=yp_{n,s}(y)-n(n-1+b)p_{n-1,s}(y),\\
 h_{n,s}=(2\pi)^{s/2}n!(b)_n,\qquad
 y_{u,w}=(\beta_{a,u,w}-c_a)/i
       =(2w-a)\gamma-i(2u-a)\delta,\\
 \mathsf E_{a,N,s}[n,(u,w)]
   =\frac{p_{n,s}(y_{u,w})}{\sqrt{h_{n,s}}},\quad 0\le n\le N,
 \qquad \mathsf K_{a,N,s}=\mathsf E_{a,N,s}^*\mathsf E_{a,N,s}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK2}
\]
All square roots in the positive $h_{n,s}$ are positive real. These
are functional values of the original Gamma orthonormal polynomials,
whose leading coefficient in $S$ is $i^{-n}/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$.
For a numerator coefficient vector $d$, the column
$\mathsf E_{a,N,s}d$ is therefore exactly the native functional-value
column of $N_d$ at the center $a/2$. It uses the original mass
$(2\pi)^{s/2}$.

If $N\ge q_a-1$, the first $q_a$ rows are an invertible triangular
polynomial-frame change times the full original root Vandermonde.
The roots are distinct since their real and imaginary index differences
vanish separately. Hence $\mathsf K_{a,N,s}$ is positive definite.
Put
\[
 \begin{split}
 \widehat D_{a,N}^{(s)}=Z_a^*\bigl[\mathsf K_{a,N,s}
 &-\mathsf K_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T}
   (\overline C_a\mathsf K_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
                  \overline C_a\mathsf K_{a,N,s}\bigr]Z_a .
 \end{split}\tag{NPK3}
\]
Its exact value interpretation is
\[
 x^*\widehat D_{a,N}^{(s)}x
   =\min_{\eta\in\mathbb C^{q'_a}}
       \|\mathsf E_{a,N,s}(Z_ax+C_a^{\mathsf T}\eta)\|^2.
 \tag{NPK4}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M01_NPK_SCHUR
The block elimination in NPK3--4 is the classical Schur-complement
method; see Stephen Boyd, Laurent El Ghaoui, Eric Feron and
Venkataramanan Balakrishnan \cite[\S2.1, pp.~7--8, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994}.
Their real symmetric presentation is used here in its Hermitian form:
the two coordinate blocks are $Z_ax$ and $C_a^{\mathsf T}\eta$, and
the lower positive block is
$\overline C_a\mathsf K_{a,N,s}C_a^{\mathsf T}$.
Completing the square with conjugate adjoints gives exactly the two
cross factors displayed above. The conductor and its value map are
the programme's separately proved constructions.
% END HUMAN CITATION M01_NPK_SCHUR

The positive normal equation gives NPK3. Independence of
$[C_a^{\mathsf T},Z_a]$ and injectivity of $\mathsf E$ prove strict
positivity for $x\ne0$. This is the complete conductor quotient,
including both cross factors and every lower column. By the NCT
dual transport, it is the metric on the exact same quotient values
as $D_{a,N-v}^{(s)}$ after the map
$\Lambda_F\mapsto\Lambda_{E_AF}$. In these literal DNE coordinates
the numerator is $Z_ax$. The factor $\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v$ remains in
the separate original KRC/RRC primary-coordinate map; it is not
inserted into, or removed from, NPK3.

\subsection{Evaluation by two original recurrence polynomials}
This is the Christoffel--Darboux formula and its confluent form
from Koornwinder, Wong, Koekoek, Swarttouw and Reinhardt
\cite[eqs.~(18.2.12)--(18.2.13)]{human:dlmf18}, with their
$n=N$, their monic polynomials $p_n=p_{n,s}$, and their norm
$h_n=h_{n,s}=(2\pi)^{s/2}n!(s/2)_n$. In their prefactor both
leading coefficients equal one. The arguments are exactly
$z=(\mu-c_a)/i$ and $w=\overline{(\lambda-c_a)/i}$; hence the
row index is conjugated and the column index is not. NPK6 and the
degree-zero term below prove the complex polynomial identity with
this original mass, and differentiation in $z$ proves the value at
every coincident entry.

The entire observation kernel in NPK2 has the following exact formula.
For two original nodes $\lambda,\mu$ let
$z=(\mu-c_a)/i$ and $w=\overline{(\lambda-c_a)/i}$. Then
\[
 \mathsf K_{a,N,s}[\lambda,\mu]=
 \begin{cases}
 \displaystyle
 \frac{p_{N+1,s}(z)p_{N,s}(w)-p_{N,s}(z)p_{N+1,s}(w)}
      {h_{N,s}(z-w)},&z\ne w,\\[6pt]
 \displaystyle
 \frac{p'_{N+1,s}(w)p_{N,s}(w)-p'_{N,s}(w)p_{N+1,s}(w)}
      {h_{N,s}},&z=w.
 \end{cases}\tag{NPK5}
\]
The second case occurs among the actual grid entries: it includes
$\mu=\beta_{a,a-u,w_0}$ when $\lambda=\beta_{a,u,w_0}$.
It must not be omitted as a nongeneric coincidence.

Here is a direct proof with the original normalization. Write
$d_n=n(n-1+b)=h_{n,s}/h_{n-1,s}$ for $n\ge1$.
Subtract the recurrence evaluated at $z,w$ to obtain, for $n\ge1$,
\[
 \begin{split}
 (z-w)\frac{p_{n,s}(z)p_{n,s}(w)}{h_{n,s}}
 ={}&\frac{p_{n+1,s}(z)p_{n,s}(w)-p_{n,s}(z)p_{n+1,s}(w)}{h_{n,s}}\\
 &-\frac{p_{n,s}(z)p_{n-1,s}(w)-p_{n-1,s}(z)p_{n,s}(w)}{h_{n-1,s}}.
 \end{split}\tag{NPK6}
\]
At $n=0$ the separate equality is
$(z-w)/h_{0,s}=(p_{1,s}(z)p_{0,s}(w)-p_{0,s}(z)p_{1,s}(w))/h_{0,s}$;
there is no $h_{-1,s}$ term. Summing this equality and NPK6
through $N$ telescopes. The recurrence has real
coefficients, so $p_{n,s}(\overline y)=\overline{p_{n,s}(y)}$;
this identifies the sum with the Hermitian kernel NPK2. The
numerator in NPK5 vanishes when $z=w$, and its derivative in $z$
gives the displayed second line. This is a polynomial identity,
so the limiting line holds without an analytic exclusion.
At $N=0$ both lines give $1/h_{0,s}=1/(2\pi)^{s/2}$,
confirming the mass and sign.

Consequently NPK3 is specified by the two original recurrence
polynomials $p_{N,s},p_{N+1,s}$ and the original finite matrices
$C_a,Z_a$. It does not require a Taylor division by $E_A$ to
evaluate this transported form. NCT supplies the exact map back
to the divided native form and its complete finite volume error.

\subsection{The original eight flags and their numerator row update}
Now fix $j=k+4$, $k=4\ell+1\ge9$, and one of the original target
orders $s=1,j$. Put $q=q_j$, and retain the original fixed CPQ
coefficient maps on $\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m_j}$:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 I=\im(B_{\rm cof}J_0),\quad B=\im B_{\rm cof},\quad
 S=\im[B_{\rm cof},F_e],\quad J=\ker\mathbb B,\\
 K_i=I\cap J,\quad W_i=I+J,\quad W=B+J,\quad
 K_c=S\cap W,\quad T=S+J .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK7}
\]
The primitive index remains $e=0$ or $1$ with its original domain.
All spaces in NPK7 are the same coefficient subspaces used in SFB
and CPQ; the present construction changes none of their maps.
For the proof, choose independent fixed columns $X$ for each
nonzero subspace, and use determinant one for the empty space.
Set $\widehat{\mathcal D}_N=\operatorname{diag}(\widehat D_{j,N}^{(s)},4)$.

For $q\le N\le2q$, let $\xi_N$ be row $N$ of
$\mathsf E_{j,N,s}$, and evaluate all following old matrices at
$N-1$. Suppressing their $j,s$ indices only in this display, put
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 L=\overline C\mathsf K_{N-1}C^{\mathsf T},\quad
 T_0=\mathsf E_{N-1}Z,\quad V_0=\mathsf E_{N-1}C^{\mathsf T},\\
 \widehat t_N=\xi_NZ,\quad \widehat v_N=\xi_NC^{\mathsf T},\quad
 \widehat\zeta_N=\widehat v_NL^{-1}\widehat v_N^*,\\
 \widehat\rho_N=\widehat t_N-
       \widehat v_NL^{-1}V_0^*T_0,\qquad
 \widehat U_N=\operatorname{diag}
       (\widehat\rho_N/\sqrt{1+\widehat\zeta_N},4),\\
 \widehat D_N=\widehat D_{N-1}
       +\frac{\widehat\rho_N^*\widehat\rho_N}{1+\widehat\zeta_N},\qquad
 \widehat{\mathcal D}_N=\widehat{\mathcal D}_{N-1}+\widehat U_N^*\widehat U_N.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK8}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M06_NPK_ROW_UPDATE
The inverse-update identity used in this square completion is recorded
by William W.~Hager \cite[p.~221, (2)]{human:hager1989}.
In his $A-uv$ notation, set $A=L$, $u=-\widehat v_N^*$ and
$v=\widehat v_N$; the denominator is
$1+\widehat v_NL^{-1}\widehat v_N^*$.
The residual row and quotient metric in NPK8 are computed here.
% END HUMAN CITATION M06_NPK_ROW_UPDATE

These formulas retain the complete old conductor Gram $L$ and
its scalar denominator. To prove the update, write the old
minimizer as $\eta_0=-L^{-1}V_0^*T_0x$ and replace it by
$\eta_0+h$. The old squared norm is the old minimum plus
$h^*Lh$; the new row is $\widehat\rho_Nx+\widehat v_Nh$.
Its remaining minimum is
$|\widehat\rho_Nx|^2/(1+\widehat v_NL^{-1}\widehat v_N^*)$,
as follows by solving the positive normal equation, or by
substitution of
$h=-L^{-1}\widehat v_N^*\widehat\rho_Nx/(1+\widehat\zeta_N)$.
This proves every line of NPK8.

The eight actual transported compressed matrices are therefore
\[
 \widehat K_{X,N}=\widehat U_NX
 (X^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_{N-1}X)^{-1}X^*\widehat U_N^*,\qquad
 \widehat r_N=
 \frac{\widehat\rho_N\widehat D_{N-1}^{-1}\widehat\rho_N^*}
 {1+\widehat\zeta_N} .\tag{NPK9}
\]
For the empty subspace set $\widehat K=0_{4\times4}$.
The isometry by $\widehat{\mathcal D}_{N-1}^{1/2}$ writes
$\widehat K_X=Y\Pi_XY^*$, where $\Pi_X$ is the orthogonal
projector onto $\widehat{\mathcal D}_{N-1}^{1/2}\im X$ and
$YY^*=\widehat r_N I_4$. Consequently
$0\preceq\widehat K_X\preceq\widehat r_N I_4$ and inclusion
of subspaces gives matrix order. All four eigenvalues, including
zeros, are retained. The two original chains are exactly
$J\subset W_i\subset W\subset T$ and
$K_i\subset I\subset K_c\subset S$.

Let $\sigma_X=1$ for $X=T,K_c,W_i,K_i$ and $\sigma_X=-1$
for $X=W,S,J,I$. The determinant lemma applied to NPK8 gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \widehat\ell_N=\sum_X\sigma_X\log\det(I_4+\widehat K_{X,N}),\\
 \widehat{\mathcal L}_e=\sum_{N=q}^{2q}\vartheta_N\widehat\ell_N,
 \quad \vartheta_q=\vartheta_{2q}=1,\quad
 \vartheta_N=2\ (q<N<2q),\\
 \widehat{\mathcal P}_{k,e}=
 \sum_{N=q}^{2q}\vartheta_N\sum_X\sigma_X
       \sum_{a=1}^4\log\max(1,\widehat\lambda_{X,a,N}).
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK10}
\]
The native row corresponding to this $N$ is exactly $n=N-v$.
The weights sum to $2q$, and NPK10 has the unchanged four
endpoints $q-1,q,2q-1,2q$. The quantity
$\widehat{\mathcal L}_e$ is the complete CPQ common return
formed with the transported metric: the same quotient
determinant identities prove the signed eight-flag expression.
No fixed frame determinant is discarded before taking the return.

\subsection{Finite and limiting transfer of the complete signed profile}
Denote NCT's nonnegative finite volume-error bounds for $b_0$ copies,
quotient rank $r$, and an admissible original SGT radius $\varrho$
by $\mathcal U^{[b_0]}_{\varrho;a,D,s,r}$ and
$\mathcal L^{[b_0]}_{\varrho;a,D,s,r}$. They retain the actual SGT poles,
residues, zero-free radius, diagonal weights and complete conductor.
Define
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 B^{[b_0]}_{a,D,s,r}=\inf_{\substack{0<\varrho<1\\
                         \varrho\ \mathrm{admissible}}}
 \max(\mathcal L^{[b_0]}_{\varrho;a,D,s,r},
      \mathcal U^{[b_0]}_{\varrho;a,D,s,r}),\\
 \mathcal N=(q-1,q,2q-1,2q),\qquad
 E_X=\sum_{N\in\mathcal N}B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,\dim X},\qquad
 E_{\rm flags}=\sum_{X\in\{T,K_c,W_i,K_i,W,S,J,I\}}E_X.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK11}
\]
At every endpoint NCT gives
$|\log\det(X^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_NX)
 -\log\det(X^*\mathcal D_NX)|\le B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,\dim X}$.
Taking the four original signs and then the eight original signs
proves
\[
 |\widehat{\mathcal L}_e-\mathcal L_e|\le E_{\rm flags}.
 \tag{NPK12}
\]
The infimum is over the same original function $g$ and its proved
admissible radii. It is nonnegative and finite, since SGT supplies
at least one such finite bound. The endpoint inequality holds for
every admissible radius, hence also for their infimum; attainment
is unnecessary. Each $\dim X\le4m_j=O(k)$ and $N=O(k^2)$.
For each fixed radius the NCT proof bounds the limsup of the
displayed infimum divided by $kq_k$ by a constant times
$\log(1/\varrho)$, with all remaining terms tending to zero.
Only after this limit choose admissible radii tending to one.
It follows that $B^{[4]}=o(kq_k)$ and
$E_{\rm flags}=o(kq_k)$. A fixed-radius enclosure itself is
not asserted to be little-oh. Any particular finite admissible
radius remains a usable, fully specified bound in NPK11--12.

For completeness the threshold error used here has a full direct
proof. Put $h(x)=\log(1+x)-\log\max(1,x)=u(x)-v(x)$, where
$u(x)=\log(1+\min(x,1))$, and $v(x)=0$ for $x\le1$,
$v(x)=\log(2x/(1+x))$ for $x\ge1$. Both $u,v$ are increasing,
vanish at zero and are at most $\log2$. On any increasing
four-term eigenvalue chain, the alternating sum of disjoint
increments of $u$ lies between zero and $u(r)$; adding the
missing middle increment proves the upper bound. The same
argument holds for $v$. The alternating $h$ sum therefore
lies between $-v(r)$ and $u(r)$. Apply this to each of the four
ordered eigenvalues on each of the two chains in NPK9; matrix
order implies eigenvalue order by the Rayleigh min--max formula.
The difference of the two chain sums has absolute value at most
$4(u(r)+v(r))\le8\log2$ at each row. After the exact weights,
both the original and transported threshold errors are at most
$16q\log2$. Combining this with NPK12 proves the finite receiver
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 |\mathcal P_{k,e}-\widehat{\mathcal P}_{k,e}|
 \le E_{\rm flags}+32q_j\log2=o(kq_k).
 \quad}\tag{NPK13}
\]
This transfers the entire signed profile, with its correlations
and zero eigenvalues. It does not assert a rowwise approximation
of any individual eigenvalue, or a value for the transported profile.

\subsection{All complementary returns and the original arithmetic action}
\begingroup\raggedright
For each of the original CPQ forms $Q_Y$ on the specified quotient
values (in particular $G_s,Q_{\rm rem},Q_b^E,A_{\rm inv}$), form
$\widehat Q_Y$ by the same restriction and full affine minimum
using $\widehat{\mathcal D}_N$. If its rank is $r_Y$, NCT and
the original four signs give the first line below. Define the
transported target quotient and its return explicitly by
$\widehat H_{B,j,N}=(B_j(\widehat D_{j,N}^{(s)})^{-1}B_j^*)^{-1}$
and $\widehat S_{K,j}=\mathcal R\log\det\widehat H_{B,j,N}$,
in the original onto value frame of $B_j$. Its full four-copy
rank is $4t_j$. Then
\par\endgroup
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 |\widehat V_Y-V_Y|\le
 E_Y:=\sum_{N\in\mathcal N}B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,r_Y}=o(kq_k),\\
 4\widehat S_{K,j}=\widehat{\mathcal L}_e+
      \widehat V_b+\widehat V_{\rm rem}+\widehat V_s+\widehat V_A,\\
 4(\widehat S_{K,j}-S_{K,j})=
 (\widehat{\mathcal L}_e-\mathcal L_e)+
 \sum_{Y=b,{\rm rem},s,A}(\widehat V_Y-V_Y).
 \end{gathered}\tag{NPK14}
\]
The second line follows by block Schur factorization in precisely
CPQ's fixed frames, so the third is its subtraction from CPQ20.
Alternatively NCT applies directly to the rank-$4t_j$ target
quotient, yielding
$|4\widehat S_{K,j}-4S_{K,j}|\le E_{\rm target}$, where
$E_{\rm target}=\sum_{N\in\mathcal N}B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,4t_j}$.
Both enclosures hold for the same
value, so their intersection is valid. This retains every
complementary term and their exact correlation.

The independent proper-source form $Q_{\rm inv}$ uses its original
degree-$k$ frame, cutoff and center. Apply NCT there separately.
For the exact relative determinant in NFR the identity is
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\log\frac{\det\widehat A_{\rm inv}}{\det\widehat Q_{\rm inv}}
   -\log\frac{\det A_{\rm inv}}{\det Q_{\rm inv}}\\
 &\hspace{15mm}=
 (\log\det\widehat A_{\rm inv}-\log\det A_{\rm inv})
 -(\log\det\widehat Q_{\rm inv}-\log\det Q_{\rm inv}).
 \end{split}\tag{NPK15}
\]
The two finite NCT errors add to an enclosure; on the original
rank-$O(k)$ and cutoff-$O(k^2)$ domains their four-return is
$o(kq_k)$. The two distinct source orders and centers are
unchanged in this statement. The EFP same-value maps and HJR
inverse-metric identities act on the same quotient coordinates,
so NPK14--15 can be substituted into them with their full
finite error intervals. In the complete action $J=B+W-P$,
transporting only the specified volume term gives exactly
$\widehat J-J=\widehat B-B$ when the original recurrence window
$W$ and penalty $P$ are retained. This supplies an explicit
error-bearing map into that action, without assigning any
missing intrinsic coefficient or cancelling an uncomputed term.

The result is a concrete finite observation problem on the original
root grid: NPK3 and NPK5 calculate all transported forms from the
original $C_a,Z_a$ and Gamma recurrence; NPK8--10 calculate their
full mixed profile; NPK12--15 prove their quantitative relation to
the original divided forms. The surviving large-degree value of
these original numerator subquotients is the next arithmetic
calculation, and remains present in every displayed identity.
\endgroup

\label{MPBUILD:NUMERATOR_PROFILE_KERNEL:end}

\label{MTBUILD:NPK_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:end}

% EXACT INPUT SHA256 ae2aefc902a147b9613660d13997080d6b093bbd005df1400b43c983827abfcd
\clearpage
\label{MTBUILD:ORIGINAL_GRID_INTERIOR_INTERFACE_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:start}
\begingroup
\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}
\providecommand{\rank}{\operatorname{rank}}
\section{Original-grid interpolation, relation norms, and an interior mixed row}
\label{sec:IGI}

Keep the exact original simple quartet $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$,
the fixed original five-orbit period and logarithm branch, and its
complete nonreduced conductor. For $a\equiv1\pmod4$, $a\ge9$, put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 c=a/2,\quad q=(a+1)^2,\quad q'=(a-7)^2,
 \quad\beta_{u,w}=c+(2u-a)\delta+i(2w-a)\gamma,\\
 \chi(S)=\prod_{u,w=0}^a(S-\beta_{u,w}),\qquad
 E_A(z)=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}e^{\beta_{8,u,w}z},\\
 \mu_t=E_A^{(t)}(0)\ (t\ge0),\quad
 v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A,\quad \mu_v\ne0,
 \quad m=q-q'-v,\quad s\in\{1,a\},\quad N\ge q-1.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI1}
\]
Use the lexicographic order of the original grid. A single index
$\lambda$ below denotes a member of this ordered grid. The roots are
distinct because real and imaginary parts separately determine both
indices. The original bounds on $v$ and the original period domain
are unchanged. In particular $v<q$ and $m>0$.

Retain exactly
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
             |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy,\quad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+s/2)p_{n-1,s},\\
 h_{n,s}=M_sn!(s/2)_n,\quad
 \phi_n(S)=p_{n,s}((S-c)/i)/\sqrt{h_{n,s}},\\
 \langle f,g\rangle_s=\int_{\mathbb R}
                 \overline{f(c+iy)}g(c+iy)\,dm_s(y).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI2}
\]
The original SGT/NSO construction proves that these $\phi_n$ are
orthonormal. Their leading coefficient is $i^{-n}/\sqrt{h_{n,s}}$.
The inner product is conjugate-linear in its first argument; the
functional dual throughout is complex-linear.

\subsection{The full original evaluation map and its attained cardinal norm}
Define the unchanged evaluation map and its literal cardinal section:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \operatorname{ev}_N:\mathbb C[S]_{\le N}\longrightarrow\mathbb C^q,
   \quad P\longmapsto(P(\beta_\lambda))_\lambda,\\
 L_\lambda(S)=\frac{\chi(S)}{(S-\beta_\lambda)\chi'(\beta_\lambda)},
 \qquad\mathcal I x=\sum_\lambda x_\lambda L_\lambda(S).
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI3}
\]
Every displayed derivative is nonzero, since its root is simple.
Evaluation at each root proves $\operatorname{ev}_N\mathcal I=I_q$.
Polynomial division gives, with all coefficients retained,
\[
 \ker\operatorname{ev}_N=\chi\mathbb C[S]_{\le N-q},
 \qquad P=\mathcal I(\operatorname{ev}_NP)+\chi R,
                                                               \tag{IGI4}
\]
where the relation space is zero at $N=q-1$. Indeed division has
a unique remainder of degree below $q$; a remainder vanishing at
all $q$ distinct roots is zero. Thus IGI4 proves the entire kernel,
surjectivity, and the exact relation to the cardinal coordinates.

Let $G_L=(\langle L_\lambda,L_\mu\rangle_s)$. At $N\ge q$
use the complete relation basis $R_j=\chi S^j$, $0\le j\le N-q$,
and put $G_R=(\langle R_i,R_j\rangle_s)$ and
$B=(\langle R_i,L_\lambda\rangle_s)$. Then the actual value norm is
\[
\begin{gathered}
 x^*Q_Nx=\min_{\operatorname{ev}_NP=x}\|P\|_s^2,
 \qquad Q_N=G_L-B^*G_R^{-1}B,\\
 P_{N,x}=\mathcal I x-\sum_jR_j(G_R^{-1}Bx)_j .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI5}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M17_IGI_MINIMUM
The positive quadratic minimum in IGI5 is the Schur-complement
method \cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994} in the original
cardinal/relation frame: its three blocks are $G_L$, $B$ and $G_R$.
The subsequent inverse evaluation metric retains the complex-linear
transpose in the observation map.
% END HUMAN CITATION M17_IGI_MINIMUM

For $N=q-1$ put $Q_N=G_L$ and omit the empty second term.
Completing the positive square in the coefficient of $R$ proves
IGI5: every other lift has squared norm equal to this minimum plus
the positive squared norm of its difference in the relation space.
The value Gram is positive definite because a zero minimum would
be the zero polynomial with zero values. In particular no cross
Gram $B$ has been discarded.

The original numerator observation matrix is
\[
 \mathsf E_N[n,\lambda]=\phi_n(\beta_\lambda),\quad 0\le n\le N,
 \qquad K_N=\mathsf E_N^*\mathsf E_N .                    \tag{IGI6}
\]
Its first $q$ rows are an invertible triangular change of the
original Vandermonde, so $K_N>0$. If $P=\sum z_n\phi_n$, then
$\operatorname{ev}_NP=\mathsf E_N^{\mathsf T}z$, with an ordinary
transpose. The unique coefficient minimum for this constraint is
\[
 z=\overline{\mathsf E_N}
       (\mathsf E_N^{\mathsf T}\overline{\mathsf E_N})^{-1}x,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\quad Q_N=\overline{K_N^{-1}}.\quad}              \tag{IGI7}
\]
Multiplication proves the constraint. The displayed column is
orthogonal to its kernel; its squared norm is
$x^*(\mathsf E_N^{\mathsf T}\overline{\mathsf E_N})^{-1}x$.
Since $\mathsf E_N^{\mathsf T}\overline{\mathsf E_N}=\overline K_N$,
this is exactly IGI7. Equations IGI5 and IGI7 give the exact typed
map between the full cardinal Schur minimum and the original
functional covariance, including its necessary conjugation.

\subsection{All conductor columns, vanishing moments, and quotient duals}
Write $C=C_a$ and $Z=Z_{\exp,a}$ for the unchanged NCT6--7
matrices. Thus
\[
 N_{C^{\mathsf T}\eta}(z)
       =E_A(z)\sum_{u,w=0}^{a-8}\eta_{uw}e^{\beta_{a-8,u,w}z},
 \quad N_d(z)=\sum_\lambda d_\lambda e^{\beta_\lambda z}.
                                                               \tag{IGI8}
\]
The columns of $C^{\mathsf T}$ are independent by injectivity of
multiplication by the nonzero germ $E_A$ and the lower Vandermonde.
Define
\[
 G_v[d,\lambda]=\beta_\lambda^d\ (0\le d<v),\qquad
 \mathscr N_v=\ker G_v,
 \quad B_0=[C^{\mathsf T},Z],\quad
 \mathscr L_v=\im G_v^{\mathsf T}.                         \tag{IGI9}
\]
At $v=0$ both $G_v$ and $\mathscr L_v$ have zero dimension.
The original NCT7 frame proves $\im B_0=\mathscr N_v$ and its
columns independent. The rows of $G_v$ are independent by the upper
Vandermonde. Moreover $G_vC^{\mathsf T}=0$ by IGI8, so
$\mathscr L_v\subset\ker C$. Every member of $\mathscr L_v$ is
the evaluation vector of a polynomial of degree below $v$, and
every such vector occurs, by its displayed monomial columns.

The original numerator coefficient quotient has Gram
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \widehat D_N&=Z^*\{K_N-K_NC^{\mathsf T}
    (\overline C K_NC^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
                         \overline C K_N\}Z,
 \\
 x^*\widehat D_Nx&=\min_\eta
      (Zx+C^{\mathsf T}\eta)^*K_N(Zx+C^{\mathsf T}\eta).
\end{aligned}                                                      \tag{IGI10}
\]
Positive square completion gives both the formula and its unique
minimum
\[
\eta=-(\overline C K_NC^{\mathsf T})^{-1}\overline C K_NZx.
\]
Strict positivity follows from independence of $B_0$.

The exact dual value space is
\[
 \mathscr W=\ker C/\mathscr L_v,
 \qquad [z]\longmapsto Z^{\mathsf T}z\in\mathbb C^m.
                                                               \tag{IGI11}
\]
This map is well defined since $Z^{\mathsf T}G_v^{\mathsf T}=0$.
Its kernel before quotienting is
$\ker C\cap\ker Z^{\mathsf T}=\ker B_0^{\mathsf T}$.
Because $\im B_0=\ker G_v$, the ordinary bilinear annihilator
of that image is exactly $\im G_v^{\mathsf T}$: inclusion is
immediate and both dimensions are $v$. Thus the kernel is precisely
$\mathscr L_v$. The rank of the independent rows of
$[C;Z^{\mathsf T}]$ is $q-v$, so any values $(0,t)$ are attained.
This proves the isomorphism in IGI11, with its full low-degree
denominator retained.

Its attained value norm satisfies exactly
\[
\begin{gathered}
 t^*\overline{\widehat D_N^{-1}}t
     =\min_{Cz=0,\ Z^{\mathsf T}z=t} z^*Q_Nz\\
     =\min_{\substack{P\in\mathbb C[S]_{\le N}\\
               C\operatorname{ev}_NP=0,\quad
               Z^{\mathsf T}\operatorname{ev}_NP=t}}\|P\|_s^2.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI12}
\]
For a direct proof, the minimum under the onto map
$A=[C;Z^{\mathsf T}]$ is $(AQ_N^{-1}A^*)^{-1}$ in its value
coordinates, as follows from the lift
$Q_N^{-1}A^*(AQ_N^{-1}A^*)^{-1}(0,t)^{\mathsf T}$ and
orthogonality to $\ker A$. Since $Q_N^{-1}=\overline K_N$, the
Schur complement of the first block of $AQ_N^{-1}A^*$ is precisely
$\overline{\widehat D_N}$. Its bottom block inverse gives IGI12.
Taking the complete polynomial minimum IGI5 proves the second line.
Thus this is the complex-linear dual of IGI10 with its full metric,
not a coordinate dimension comparison.

The conductor condition has the exact polynomial interpretation
\[
 (C\operatorname{ev}_NP)_{u,w}
      =(\mathcal T_AP)(\beta_{a-8,u,w}),\qquad
 C\operatorname{ev}_NP=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
                   \chi_{a-8}\mid\mathcal T_AP .          \tag{IGI13}
\]
The first equality is IGI8 coefficient collection and exact root
addition. The second uses every distinct lower root and polynomial
division. It holds for every $P$ of the stated degree, retaining
all cancellations in $\mathcal T_A$. The fibre IGI12 minimizes
over all resulting low-polynomial values, rather than setting
them equal to zero.

For any original fixed coefficient subspace frame $X$ in four
copies, its restriction Gram is
$X^*\operatorname{diag}(\widehat D_N,4)X$. For a quotient
$Y/K$ with fixed independent frame $[X_K,X_Q]$, its Gram is exactly
\[
 X_Q^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_NX_Q
 -X_Q^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_NX_K
 (X_K^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_NX_K)^{-1}
 X_K^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_NX_Q,
 \quad\widehat{\mathcal D}_N=\operatorname{diag}(\widehat D_N,4).
                                                               \tag{IGI14}
\]
Square completion proves the minimum on its original affine
fibre. Applying IGI10--12 in each copy and these exact further
minima gives every original CPQ restriction and quotient, with
the same frames, full cross terms and values. No choice of
orthogonal replacement flag enters IGI14.

\subsection{The full relation Christoffel row and the original observation row}
Let $\pi_r(S)$ be the unique monic degree-$r$ polynomial minimizing
$\|\chi P\|_s^2$, and put
\[
 \nu_{r,s}=\|\chi\pi_r\|_s^2,
 \qquad\tau_r=\frac{\chi\pi_r}{\sqrt{\nu_{r,s}}},
 \qquad b_r=(\langle\tau_r,L_\lambda\rangle_s)_\lambda .    \tag{IGI15}
\]
The monic quadratic minima are orthogonal to all lower-degree
directions, by varying each complex coefficient in its real and
imaginary parts. Thus the $\tau_r$ form an orthonormal basis of
the entire relation space in IGI4. Orthogonal projection of every
cardinal column onto that complete space gives
\[
 Q_N=G_L-\sum_{j=0}^{N-q}b_j^*b_j,
 \qquad Q_N=Q_{N-1}-b_r^*b_r\quad(N=q+r).
                                                               \tag{IGI16}
\]
Every row $b_j$ is the actual cardinal/relation cross pairing.

Write $\xi_N=(\phi_N(\beta_\lambda))_\lambda$, the actual next
original observation row. The coefficient of $\phi_N$ in
$\tau_r$ is
\[
 t_N=i^N\sqrt{h_{N,s}/\nu_{r,s}},\qquad N=q+r.             \tag{IGI17}
\]
This follows from their exact leading coefficients. Its old
component $z_<=(\langle\phi_n,\tau_r\rangle_s)_{n<N}$ is
orthogonal to every old relation, since $\tau_r$ is. Its old
evaluation values are $-t_N\xi_N^{\mathsf T}$, since the full
polynomial $\tau_r$ vanishes at every original node. It is
therefore the minimum lift IGI7 at cutoff $N-1$:
\[
 z_<=-t_N\overline{\mathsf E_{N-1}}Q_{N-1}\xi_N^{\mathsf T}.
\]
Pairing it with each old cardinal column gives
$b_r=-\overline{t_N}\,\overline{\xi_N}Q_{N-1}$.
Conjugation and IGI7 yield the exact phase-sensitive row formula
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \xi_N=-i^{-N}\sqrt{\nu_{r,s}/h_{N,s}}\,\overline{b_r}K_{N-1}.
 \quad}                                                        \tag{IGI18}
\]
The missing phase cannot be supplied by a determinant identity
alone; it has been computed from the original polynomial frame.
Since $\tau_r$ has norm one, its old component has squared norm
$1-|t_N|^2$. The same minimum lift gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 b_rQ_{N-1}^{-1}b_r^*=1-\frac{h_{N,s}}{\nu_{r,s}},\qquad
 T_{r,s}^\Gamma:=\frac{\nu_{r,s}}{h_{N,s}}\ge1,\\
 \xi_NK_{N-1}^{-1}\xi_N^*=T_{r,s}^\Gamma-1,\qquad
 \frac{\det K_N}{\det K_{N-1}}=T_{r,s}^\Gamma.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI19}
\]
For the third formula, substitute IGI18 into the first, or use
the norm of $z_<$ and $|t_N|^2=h_N/\nu_r$. The determinant
formula follows by the rank-one update
$K_N=K_{N-1}+\xi_N^*\xi_N$. Positivity of $Q_N$ also proves
that the first scalar in IGI19 is strictly below one.

\subsection{The retained low-moment correction and conductor correction}
All matrices in this subsection are evaluated at cutoff $N-1$.
Suppress that cutoff only for $K=K_{N-1}$ and put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 L=\overline C KC^{\mathsf T},\quad
 Z_\perp=Z-C^{\mathsf T}L^{-1}\overline C KZ,\quad
 \widehat\zeta_N=\xi_NC^{\mathsf T}L^{-1}\overline C\xi_N^*,\\
 \widehat\rho_N=\xi_NZ_\perp,
 \qquad \widehat r_N=
       \frac{\widehat\rho_N\widehat D_{N-1}^{-1}\widehat\rho_N^*}
            {1+\widehat\zeta_N},\\
 d_N=\xi_NK^{-1}G_v^*(G_vK^{-1}G_v^*)^{-1}
                              G_vK^{-1}\xi_N^* .
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI20}
\]
At $v=0$ define $d_N=0$ and omit its inverse. At $v>0$ that inverse
exists because $G_v$ has full row rank. Every factor in $d_N$ has
its ordinary Hermitian adjoint as displayed; the defining moment
map $G_vd$ itself remains complex-linear.

The exact metric projector identity is
\[
 B_0(B_0^*KB_0)^{-1}B_0^*
 =K^{-1}-K^{-1}G_v^*(G_vK^{-1}G_v^*)^{-1}G_vK^{-1}.
                                                               \tag{IGI21}
\]
To prove it, apply the isometry $K^{1/2}$ to $\ker G_v$.
Its orthogonal complement is the range of $K^{-1/2}G_v^*$.
The orthogonal projector onto that complement is
$K^{-1/2}G_v^*(G_vK^{-1}G_v^*)^{-1}G_vK^{-1/2}$:
it is self-adjoint, idempotent, and fixes that range. The projector
onto the former space is
$K^{1/2}B_0(B_0^*KB_0)^{-1}B_0^*K^{1/2}$. Their sum is the
identity, and multiplication on both sides by $K^{-1/2}$ proves
IGI21. The empty case is the same formula with zero complement.

Multiply IGI21 by $\xi_N$ and its adjoint. The full restricted
numerator leverage is $T_{r,s}^\Gamma-1-d_N$. Completing the
old conductor/numerator square as in IGI10 decomposes that same
quantity into
$\widehat\zeta_N+\widehat\rho_N\widehat D_{N-1}^{-1}
\widehat\rho_N^*$. Consequently
\[
\boxed{\quad
 1+\widehat r_N=
       \frac{T_{r,s}^\Gamma-d_N}{1+\widehat\zeta_N},
 \qquad
 0\le d_N+\widehat\zeta_N\le T_{r,s}^\Gamma-1.
 \quad}                                                        \tag{IGI22}
\]
This identity preserves both original corrections. At $v=0$ the
complementary space is zero, so $d_N$ vanishes identically. For
$v>0$ retain the displayed exact expression, including any value
zero proved from the original data. Invertibility of the full
Vandermonde alone does not eliminate that expression.
Substitution of IGI18 into IGI20 evaluates every correction in
the same cardinal/relation cross row $b_r$, with its exact phase.

For the actual target $a=j=k+4$, let the eight fixed flags be
precisely $T_e,K_c,W_i,K_i,W,S,J,I$ of NPK7, with their two
nested chains and their original frames $X$. Then
\[
 \widehat U_N=\operatorname{diag}
       (\widehat\rho_N/\sqrt{1+\widehat\zeta_N},4),\qquad
 \widehat K_{X,N}=\widehat U_NX
 (X^*\widehat{\mathcal D}_{N-1}X)^{-1}X^*\widehat U_N^*.
                                                               \tag{IGI23}
\]
\begingroup\raggedright
These are the same eight NPK9 matrices, now with their exact
polynomial expressions IGI5,15,18,20. Set an empty compression
equal to the zero $4\times4$ matrix. The signed row keeps the
positive signs $T_e,K_c,W_i,K_i$ and negative signs $W,S,J,I$.
All four eigenvalues, including zero and subthreshold eigenvalues,
remain present. No conclusion about these eight orientations is
deduced from the scalar $T_{r,s}^\Gamma$ alone: the entire cross
row $b_r$, the original $C,Z,G_v$, and all eight $X$ are still
explicit factors of IGI23.\par\endgroup

\subsection{A finite calculation at the second original target row}
The original $Q_a(y)=i^{-q}\chi(c+iy)$ is real and even. Its root
set is invariant under conjugation and negation, by the exact
index permutations $u\mapsto a-u$ and
$(u,w)\mapsto(a-u,a-w)$, and $q$ is even. Thus
$|Q_a(y)|^2dm_s(y)$ is an even positive measure.
The original degree-one monic minimizer is therefore exactly
\[
 \pi_1(S)=S-c,\quad
 \nu_{1,s}=\int_{\mathbb R}y^2|Q_a(y)|^2dm_s(y),\quad
 T_{1,s}^\Gamma=\frac{\nu_{1,s}}{M_s(q+1)!(s/2)_{q+1}}.
                                                               \tag{IGI24}
\]
Indeed a monic linear polynomial in $y$ is $y+z$; its weighted
squared norm is the norm of $y$ plus $|z|^2$ times the positive
mass, since the mixed integral of $y$ is zero. Its minimum is
at $z=0$. The map back to $S$ is the exact monic map
$p(y)\mapsto i p((S-c)/i)$.

For a finite evaluation with no quadrature, write
$Q_a(y)=\sum_{t=0}^q c_ty^t$ using every original root coefficient.
For each integer $b\ge0$ let $J_b$ be the $(b+1)$-square Jacobi
matrix on indices $0,\ldots,b$ with diagonal zero and off-diagonal
$\sqrt{(n+1)(n+s/2)}$. The original recurrence proves
\[
 \int y^b dm_s(y)=M_s(J_b^b)_{00},\qquad
 \nu_{1,s}=M_s\sum_{t,u=0}^q
             \overline{c_t}c_u(J_{t+u+2}^{t+u+2})_{00}.
                                                               \tag{IGI25}
\]
Starting at degree zero, $b$ applications of the recurrence cannot
visit degree greater than $b$, proving the finite moment identity
and its retained mass. Expansion of the original squared polynomial
gives the second identity. These are exact finite sums.

At the non-first target row $N=q+1$, define the fully specified
nonnegative number
\[
 \mathscr B_{q+1,s}=
   \log\frac{T_{1,s}^\Gamma-d_{q+1}}
                   {1+\widehat\zeta_{q+1}}.
                                                               \tag{IGI26}
\]
Here $T_{1,s}^\Gamma$ is IGI24--25, $b_1$ is the explicit integral
of $\chi(S)(S-c)/\sqrt{\nu_{1,s}}$ against each literal cardinal
column, and IGI18,20 compute $d_{q+1},\widehat\zeta_{q+1}$
from those original finite coefficients. Its nonnegativity and
the finite estimates
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0\le\mathscr B_{q+1,s}\le
      \log T_{1,s}^\Gamma-\log(1+\widehat\zeta_{q+1})
      \le\log T_{1,s}^\Gamma,\\
 |\widehat\ell_{q+1}|\le4\mathscr B_{q+1,s},\qquad
 |\widehat p_{q+1}|\le4\mathscr B_{q+1,s},\qquad
 0\le\Delta\widehat V_X\le4\mathscr B_{q+1,s}
                    \quad(X=b,{\rm rem},s,A)
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI27}
\]
follow as follows. IGI22 makes IGI26 exactly
$\log(1+\widehat r_{q+1})$, and $d_N\ge0$ gives the first line.
For the second line, ordered projectors along each original flag
chain give ordered compressed matrices. The two disjoint increment
sums on a chain are nonnegative and at most the full increment
$4\log(1+\widehat r_N)$. Their difference is the signed eight-term
row. The same argument works with the increasing function
$\log\max(1,x)$ on each of the four ordered eigenvalues, giving
the threshold bound without omitting zeros. For the four CPQ
quotients the exact pairs are $W/W_i$, $\mathcal U/T_e$,
$S/K_c$, $I/K_i$. Full Schur elimination gives their increment
as $b_Y-b_K$, between zero and the same full four-copy budget.
Their original fixed frame factors cancel only in this increment.
The interior weight in the original return is two, so its entire
weighted contribution is bounded by $8\mathscr B_{q+1,s}$.
For the native determinant row, write the eight original frames
as $\mathscr X=\{T_e,K_c,W_i,K_i,W,S,J,I\}$ and define, using
exactly the nonnegative NPK11/NCT20 finite error constants,
\[
 E_{\rm row}(N)=\sum_{X\in\mathscr X}
 \left(B^{[4]}_{j,N-1-v,s,\dim X}
              +B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,\dim X}\right),\qquad N=q+1.
                                                               \tag{IGI27a}
\]
The two endpoint determinant errors, with their original signs,
give $|\delta\mathcal L_{e,N-v}-\widehat\ell_N|\le E_{\rm row}(N)$.
The native and transported threshold rows each differ from their
own determinant row by at most $8\log2$, by the two-chain proof
NPK13 before applying its endpoint weights. The full triangle
inequality therefore gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 |\delta\mathcal P_{e,N-v}-\widehat p_N|
       \le E_{\rm row}(N)+16\log2,\\
 |\delta\mathcal P_{e,N-v}|
       \le4\mathscr B_{q+1,s}+E_{\rm row}(N)+16\log2.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI27b}
\]
For each complementary quotient of its original rank $r_X$, the
direct determinant increment error is instead the sum
$B^{[4]}_{j,N-1-v,s,r_X}+B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,r_X}$; no threshold
error is added to a determinant increment. The actual second-row
interior weight is two, so every complete bound just displayed,
including its error terms, is multiplied by two in the full
return. These statements compare the exact scalar increments;
they assert no eigenwise approximation of the eight matrices.

\subsection{The exact interface to the older arithmetic norm results}
The scalar $\nu_{r,s}$ above is measured in $dm_s$. In GRA1 the
original arithmetic measure, at multiplicity one and degree $a$,
is $d\mu_a(y)=w_h^{*a}(y)dy$ with
$w_h(y)=|(2\xi/h)(1/2+iy)|^2/(2\pi)$ and the complete original
quartet polynomial $h$. At order one the present Gamma measure is
exactly $dm_1=d\sigma=|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2dy/(2\pi)$,
including its mass $\sqrt{2\pi}$. The identity on the unchanged
polynomial space at centre $c=a/2$ is the actual comparison map;
its density ratio is literally
\[
 \frac{d\mu_a}{dm_s}(y)=
 \frac{w_h^{*a}(y)}{
  \displaystyle\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
                   |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2}.                \tag{IGI28}
\]
The denominator is positive for real $y$. This is not a claim
that the ratio is constant.

For degree cutoff $d$ the proved all-polynomial GRA5 constants
are $a_a/D_d$ and $A_a$, with
$D_d=[1+4(d+M_h)^2]^{M_h}$ and the unchanged full GRA3
definitions of $a_a,A_a,M_h$. The proof there uses the actual
convolution upper and lower densities and the original Gamma
recurrence, and gives
$(a_a/D_d)H_1\preceq H_{\rm ar}\preceq A_aH_1$.
For $L=(s-1)/4$, Gamma recurrence gives the exact whole-source
identity and finite comparison
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s=\rho_s\prod_{u=0}^{L-1}(y^2+(2u+1/2)^2)dm_1,
 \qquad\rho_s=\frac{(2\pi)^{2L}}{(1/2)_{2L}},\\
 \mathfrak a_s=\rho_s\prod_{u=0}^{L-1}(2u+1/2)^2,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak b_{s,d}=\rho_s\prod_{u=0}^{L-1}
           \{4(d+u+1)^2+(2u+1/2)^2\},\\
 \mathfrak a_sH_1\preceq H_s\preceq\mathfrak b_{s,d}H_1,
 \qquad
 \frac{a_a}{D_d\mathfrak b_{s,d}}H_s
       \preceq H_{\rm ar}\preceq\frac{A_a}{\mathfrak a_s}H_s.
\end{gathered}                                                    \tag{IGI29}
\]
For completeness the upper Gamma comparison follows from the
orthonormal order-one recurrence: on degree at most $d$,
$\|yp\|_1\le2(d+1)\|p\|_1$. Multiplication by $iy-t_u$
at step $u$ has squared norm at most
$4(d+u+1)^2+t_u^2$ times the preceding norm, while pointwise
$|iy-t_u|^2=y^2+t_u^2\ge t_u^2$. Multiplying all factors proves
the stated comparison, with its exact scalar $\rho_s$.
Combining it with GRA5 in the displayed directions proves the
last line. Empty products at $s=1$ equal one.

Apply these inequalities to every polynomial in the same monic
fibre $\chi P$, with $d=q+r$, and take minima. This gives
\[
 \frac{a_a}{D_{q+r}\mathfrak b_{s,q+r}}\nu_{r,s}
      \le\nu_r^{\rm ar}
      \le\frac{A_a}{\mathfrak a_s}\nu_{r,s}.              \tag{IGI30}
\]
The identical inequalities apply to all fixed cardinal evaluation
fibres IGI5, or the constrained fibres IGI12, by taking the same
full minimum on both sides. Inversion and the conjugation in
IGI7 give their precise reverse dual inequalities. Thus GRA's
arithmetic norm and the numerator Gamma norm have a proved full
form map before any scalar conclusion is transported.

On the order-one Gamma relation space, the monic map
$U_rP(y)=i^{-r}P(c+iy)$ has inverse
$i^rp((S-c)/i)$ and satisfies
$U_{q+r}(\chi P)=Q_aU_rP$. Hence the original GRF whole-form
comparison applies literally to $\nu_{r,1}$ and to the full
relation Gram $G_R$ in IGI5 on precisely its proved finite
guards. The GRE reference minima then give its stated scalar
bounds, and IGI29 transports any such valid full-form inequality
to the other original source order with the displayed constants.
The degree-one evaluation IGI24--27 uses its direct proof and
does not assert the higher-degree GRF guards at $r=1$.

The cardinal cross rows $b_r$ remain the pairings IGI15 in these
maps. A bound for their single quadratic norm IGI19 determines
the complete scalar observation leverage, but does not specify
their values on the original conductor and flag columns in
IGI20,23. The exact remaining map is IGI18 followed by those
literal column maps; IGI22 exhibits its separate nonnegative
low-moment and conductor contributions. These retained expressions
are the surviving interior mixed calculation, including all
complementary CPQ returns, after the scalar relation norms have
been supplied by the proved older results.
\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:ORIGINAL_GRID_INTERIOR_INTERFACE_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:end}

\label{MTBUILD:ORIGINAL_NATIVE_INTERIOR_BOUND_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:start}
\begingroup
\section{The original native interior rows and a growing initial segment}
\label{sec:NIB}

Fix the original simple quartet, complete nonreduced conductor, period,
and logarithm branch. Put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 j=k+4,\quad k=4l+1\ge9,\quad q=(j+1)^2,\quad q'=(j-7)^2,\\
 c'=j/2-4,\quad L=q-v,\quad m=L-q',\\
 s\in\{1,j\},\quad v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A,\quad
 \mu_t=E_A^{(t)}(0)\ (t\ge0),\quad\mu_v\ne0,\\
 E_A(z)=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}e^{\beta_{8,u,w}z},\\
 \chi_j(S)=\prod_{u,w=0}^j(S-\beta_{j,u,w}),\quad
 \chi_-(S')=\chi_{j-8}(S'),\\
 \beta_{a,u,w}=a/2+(2u-a)\delta+i(2w-a)\gamma.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB1}
\]
The polynomial family, weight and norm are the classical Meixner--Pollaczek formulas, with exact parameter and measure transport proved in HPS1--4; see \cite[eqs.~(18.19.8)--(18.19.9), (18.22.8)]{human:dlmf18}.

All functions and matrices are the original NSO/CPQ objects. The
proved original bound $v\le80$ suffices here; the stronger BTK
bound is retained on its stated domain. Keep the exact source
measure, mass, recurrence, and phase
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s(y)=\frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
                  |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy,\qquad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\\
 p_{0,s}=1,\quad p_{1,s}=y,\quad
 p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+s/2)p_{n-1,s},\\
 h_{n,s}=M_sn!(s/2)_n,\qquad
 \phi_n(S')=p_{n,s}((S'-c')/i)/\sqrt{h_{n,s}},\qquad
 \|P\|_s^2=\int|P(c'+iy)|^2dm_s(y).
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB2}
\]
The polynomials are the original orthonormal basis. Inner products
are conjugate-linear in their first argument, and functional values
are complex-linear.

\subsection{The complete original annihilator at every subsequent row}
Let $F_b=N_b/E_A$, $1\le b\le m$, be the fixed original NSO12
numerator columns. The original functional frame consists of these
$m$ functionals together with all $q'$ lower evaluations. Denote
its matrix of values on $\phi_0,\ldots,\phi_d$ by
$\mathcal F_d=[V_d,T_d]$, with $L=q'+m$ columns. Explicitly,
$V_d[n,(u,w)]=\phi_n(\beta_{j-8,u,w})$ comprises every lower
evaluation column, and $T_d[n,b]=\Lambda_{F_b}(\phi_n)$ comprises
the original analytic NSO columns, for $0\le n\le d$.
At $d=L-1$
this matrix is invertible: a generating linear combination whose
first $L$ derivatives vanish has, after multiplication by $E_A$,
an upper-grid numerator whose first $L+v=q$ derivatives vanish.
The upper Vandermonde kills this numerator; the original conductor
quotient frame kills the quotient coefficients; the lower
Vandermonde kills the remaining coefficients. The triangular
polynomial-frame change has nonzero diagonal, proving the same
claim for native rows. Appending rows therefore preserves column
rank $L$ at every $d\ge L-1$.

Define the original polynomial translation and its complete relation
sequence by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 (\mathcal T_AP)(S')=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}P(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}),\\
 g_t=\mathcal T_A(\chi_j(S)S^t),\quad
 \ell_t=\mu_v\binom{q+t}{v},\quad n_t=q+t-v=L+t.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB3}
\]
Binomial expansion gives $\deg g_t=n_t$ and
$\operatorname{lc}g_t=\ell_t\ne0$. For every original numerator
column, the finite Leibniz identity gives
\[
 \Lambda_{F_b}(g_t)=\Lambda_{N_b}(\chi_j S^t)=0.
\]
Every lower evaluation also annihilates $g_t$: root addition
$\beta_{j-8,a,b}+\beta_{8,u,w}=\beta_{j,a+u,b+w}$ makes
each shifted summand zero separately. Thus $\chi_-$ divides
every $g_t$, including all of its original coefficients.

At the later native row $n=L+r$, $r\ge0$, the polynomial map of
all $L$ original functional values has rank $L$, by the preceding
old-row argument. Its kernel in degree at most $n$ has dimension
$n+1-L=r+1$. The polynomials $g_0,\ldots,g_r$ are independent
by their distinct degrees and lie in that kernel. Consequently
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \ker(\Lambda_{\rm original}|_{\mathbb C[S']_{\le L+r}})
       =\operatorname{span}(g_0,\ldots,g_r).
 \quad}                                                         \tag{NIB4}
\]
This identifies the complete annihilator, rather than selecting a
proper subspace of relations.

Let $u_t=g_t/\ell_t$ and define its full attained monic norm by
\[
 \eta_{r,s}=\min_{z_0,\ldots,z_{r-1}}
       \left\|u_r+\sum_{t<r}z_tu_t\right\|_s^2,
 \qquad
 \nu_{b,s}^-=\min_{P\text{ monic},\deg P=b}\|\chi_-P\|_s^2.
                                                               \tag{NIB5}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M18_NIB_MINIMUM
The relation minimum NIB5--6 uses classical Schur-complement
elimination \cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994}, here on
the full Gram of $u_0,\ldots,u_r$.
This credits the matrix method; the complete annihilator and
the later native Gamma bounds are proved in this section.
% END HUMAN CITATION M18_NIB_MINIMUM

Both are positive attained minima. For $r=0$ the first minimum
has no variables. For $r>0$, putting
$G_{<}=(\langle u_t,u_h\rangle_s)_{t,h<r}$ and
$z=(\langle u_t,u_r\rangle_s)_{t<r}$, its exact value is
\[
 \eta_{r,s}=\|u_r\|_s^2-z^*G_<^{-1}z,
 \qquad u_r^{\min}=u_r-\sum_{t<r}u_t(G_<^{-1}z)_t .       \tag{NIB6}
\]
Square completion proves this formula, preserving every preceding
relation and cross pairing. Scaling by $\ell_t$ is the displayed
invertible map to its monic affine fibre; every $\ell_t$ remains
in NIB3,5,6. Since $\chi_-$ divides every $g_t$, the first
fibre is contained in the second monic fibre at $b=m+r$.
It follows directly that $\eta_{r,s}\ge\nu_{m+r,s}^-$.

\subsection{The exact corrected native row at every interior position}
At cutoff $n-1=L+r-1$, retain the original $V_0,T_0$, their new
rows $v_n,t_n$, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 A_L=V_0^*V_0,\quad
 D_0=T_0^*(I-V_0A_L^{-1}V_0^*)T_0,\\
 \zeta_n=v_nA_L^{-1}v_n^*,\quad
 \rho_n=t_n-v_nA_L^{-1}V_0^*T_0,\quad
 r_n=\rho_nD_0^{-1}\rho_n^*/(1+\zeta_n),\\
 \zeta_{{\rm all},n}=[v_n,t_n]
     ([V_0,T_0]^*[V_0,T_0])^{-1}[v_n,t_n]^*.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB7}
\]
All inverses exist by the full column ranks established above.
Unlike the first row, the old full matrix need not be square.

The unit relation $\tau_r=u_r^{\min}/\sqrt{\eta_{r,s}}$
has last native coefficient
$c_n=i^n\sqrt{h_{n,s}/\eta_{r,s}}$, and annihilates the entire
original functional frame. Its old coefficient column is
orthogonal to every old annihilator by NIB4,6. If
$w_n=[v_n,t_n]$ and $F_0=[V_0,T_0]$, it is consequently the
minimum-norm solution of
$F_0^{\mathsf T}z=-c_nw_n^{\mathsf T}$, namely
\[
 z=-c_n\overline{F_0}(F_0^{\mathsf T}\overline{F_0})^{-1}
                                                  w_n^{\mathsf T}.
\]
Its squared norm is $|c_n|^2\zeta_{{\rm all},n}$ by
conjugating the positive scalar. Since the full relation has
norm one, adding $|c_n|^2$ proves the first formula below.
Applying the identical argument to the full lower-evaluation
annihilator $\chi_-\mathbb C[S']_{\le m+r}$ proves the second:
\[
 1+\zeta_{{\rm all},n}=\frac{\eta_{r,s}}{h_{n,s}},\qquad
 1+\zeta_n=\frac{\nu_{m+r,s}^-}{h_{n,s}}.                 \tag{NIB8}
\]
That lower annihilator equality follows from ordinary division by
the complete distinct-root polynomial $\chi_-$; its monic degree
$n$ fibre is exactly NIB5 with $b=m+r$.

The invertible old column operation replacing $T_0$ by
$(I-V_0A_L^{-1}V_0^*)T_0$ makes its Gram block diagonal and its
new row $[v_n,\rho_n]$. Thus
$\zeta_{{\rm all},n}=\zeta_n+\rho_nD_0^{-1}\rho_n^*$,
also for the rectangular old matrix. Together with NIB8 this gives
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 1+r_n=\frac{\eta_{r,s}}{\nu_{m+r,s}^-},\qquad
 0\le\log(1+r_n)
       \le\log\frac{\|g_r\|_s^2}
                         {|\ell_r|^2\nu_{m+r,s}^-}.
 \quad}                                                         \tag{NIB9}
\]
The last inequality uses the actual trial $u_r$ in its attained
fibre NIB5. It changes neither $g_r$ nor any earlier relation.
The measure, lower minimum, and all cross terms in the exact
identity remain the original ones.

\subsection{All finite constants for the original later row}
Define, for every $r\ge0$,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 L_s=(s-1)/4,\quad \theta=\pi/2,\quad h=1/(q+r),\quad
 \kappa=\theta-h>0,\quad \sigma=(\delta^2+\gamma^2)^{1/2},\\
 Q_-=q'+L_s,\quad Q_+=q+r+L_s,\quad R'=(j-8)\sigma,
 \quad M=\max\{2Q_-/\theta,2R'\},\\
 B_+=\max\{1,(j+8)\sigma,2L_s\},\quad
 A_{\rm abs}=\sum_{u,w}|a_{uw}|,\quad
 \beta_s=M_s/(\sqrt2\Gamma(s/2)),\\
 L_h=\pi(\sin h)^{3/2}/\Gamma(3/4)^2,\quad
 U_h=\Gamma(1/4)^2(\sin h)^{-1/2}/(2\pi),\\
 F_M=2Q_-\log M-\theta M,\quad
 \epsilon_-=(\theta-2Q_-/M)+Q_-/M^2+h(M+1)+4q'R'/M,\\
 C_b=(2b+1)\binom{2b}{b}^{2}.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB10}
\]
The classical inputs are Euler's integral, the Gamma functional equation, and Euler's reflection formula \cite[eqs.~(5.2.1), (5.5.1), (5.5.3)]{human:dlmf5}; HPS5 records the exact reflection specialization.

The Gamma recurrence and the exact Euler/reflection estimates give
\[
\begin{gathered}
 dm_s=\beta_s\prod_{u=0}^{L_s-1}(y^2+(2u+1/2)^2)d\sigma(y),\quad
 d\sigma(y)=|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2dy/(2\pi),\\
 L_he^{-(\theta+h)|y|}\le d\sigma/dy\le U_he^{-\kappa|y|}.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB11}
\]
Here is the full scalar estimate: rotate Euler's integral for
$\Gamma(b+iy/2)$ through $\operatorname{sgn}(y)(\pi/2-h)$.
The integrable bound on that ray is
$\Gamma(b)(\sin h)^{-b}e^{-(\pi/2-h)|y|/2}$.
The closing arcs vanish because $b>0$ and their cosines are at
least $\sin h>0$. Squaring this at $b=1/4$ proves the upper
bound. The exact reflection product
$|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)\Gamma(3/4-iy/2)|^2=2\pi^2/\cosh(\pi y)$,
the same upper bound at $3/4$, and $\cosh(\pi y)\le e^{\pi|y|}$
prove the lower bound. Iterating the Gamma recurrence proves the
first identity, with its factor $4^{-L_s}$ cancelled by the
displayed original density's $2^{2L_s}$. No mass is suppressed.

On the interval $[M,M+1]$ every original lower root in the
$y$ coordinate has modulus at most $R'\le M/2$. Since
$\log(1-x)\ge-2x$ for $0\le x\le1/2$,
$|\chi_-(c'+iy)|^2\ge y^{2q'}e^{-4q'R'/M}$ there.
The Gamma product contributes at least $y^{2L_s}$.
For $y=M+t$, $0\le t\le1$, the elementary inequality
$\log(1+t/M)\ge t/M-t^2/(2M^2)$ gives
\[
 2Q_-\log y-\theta y
 \ge F_M-(\theta-2Q_-/M)-Q_-/M^2.
\]
Together with $e^{-hy}\ge e^{-h(M+1)}$, this gives the lower
bound $\beta_sL_he^{F_M-\epsilon_-}$ for the complete weighted
density on this original interval.

The classical Rodrigues and norm identities used for the next
minimum are those of the Legendre family; the checked source is
Koornwinder, Wong, Koekoek, Swarttouw and Reinhardt
\cite[eq.~(18.5.5), Table~18.5.1, and the Legendre and shifted-Legendre
rows of Table~18.3.1]{human:dlmf18}.
With their degree $b$ and their argument $u=2x-1$, the polynomial
below is exactly their $P_b(2x-1)$. The change of variables contributes
$du=2\,dx$, so its norm on $[0,1]$ is $1/(2b+1)$ and its leading
coefficient in $x$ is $\binom{2b}{b}$. The square-completion argument
and the explicit $S'$-coordinate map below give the complex monic
minimum in the original fibre, with $b=m+r$.

For a complex monic degree-$b$ polynomial on an interval of
length one the exact minimum squared integral is $C_b^{-1}$.
To prove the constant, the Rodrigues polynomial
$P_b(x)=\frac1{b!}(d/dx)^b[x^b(x-1)^b]$ has leading coefficient
$\binom{2b}{b}$. Integrating by parts $b$ times proves its
orthogonality to every smaller degree; the same calculation gives
$\int_0^1P_b^2=\binom{2b}{b}\int_0^1x^b(1-x)^bdx=1/(2b+1)$.
The last beta integral is $b!^2/(2b+1)!$ by successive integration
by parts. Orthogonal square completion proves the complex monic
minimum. The map $P(S')\mapsto i^{-b}P(c'+i(M+x))$ is monic
in $x$ with inverse $i^bp((S'-c')/i-M)$ and preserves absolute
values on the interval. Taking $b=m+r$ therefore proves
\[
 \nu_{m+r,s}^-\ge
           \frac{\beta_sL_h}{C_{m+r}}e^{F_M-\epsilon_-}.   \tag{NIB12}
\]

For the upper estimate keep the original roots fixed. For every
real $y$, upper root and degree-eight shift,
$|c'+iy+\beta_{8,u,w}-\beta_{j,a,b}|\le |y|+(j+8)\sigma$.
Also
$|c'+iy+\beta_{8,u,w}|\le |y|+j/2+8\sigma
\le |y|+(j+8)\sigma$, because the original $\gamma>2$ gives
$\sigma>1/2$. Consequently the actual polynomial from NIB3 obeys
\[
 |g_r(c'+iy)|\le A_{\rm abs}(|y|+B_+)^{q+r}.
\]
Every Gamma product factor is at most $(|y|+B_+)^2$.
Integrating the two half-line bounds from NIB11, substituting
$t=|y|+B_+$ and enlarging to $t\ge0$ gives
\[
 \|g_r\|_s^2\le
   2\beta_sU_hA_{\rm abs}^2e^{\kappa B_+}
                       \frac{(2Q_+)!}{\kappa^{2Q_++1}}.    \tag{NIB13}
\]
The last integral is an exact factorial integral by integration
by parts. All cancellation in $g_r$ remains exact in NIB3--9;
this is an upper inequality for that polynomial's complete norm.

Combining the two estimates in the identical original norm gives
the finite bound, valid for every $j\ge13$ and $r\ge0$,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0\le\log(1+r_{L+r})\le E_{j,s,r},\\
 E_{j,s,r}=
 2\log\frac{A_{\rm abs}}{|\mu_v\binom{q+r}{v}|}
 +\log\frac{2U_h}{L_h}+\kappa B_++\log((2Q_+)!)
 -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa-F_M+\epsilon_-+\log C_{m+r}.
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB14}
\]
The identical $\beta_s$ cancels between the two proved bounds;
the source measure has not been divided by its mass. The exact
ratio in NIB9 is at least one, so the displayed upper constant
is necessarily nonnegative.

\subsection{Uniform initial interior segment and all complementary terms}
Fix any real $C>0$. Uniformly over integers $0\le r\le Cj$,
both original orders $s=1,j$, and the fixed original quartet and
conductor,
\[
                  E_{j,s,r}=O_{{\rm actual},C}(j\log j).   \tag{NIB15}
\]
Here is a direct verification. Eventually $M=2Q_-/\theta$,
since $Q_-\asymp j^2$ while $R'=O(j)$. Then
$F_M=f_\theta(Q_-)$, where $f_\theta(x)=2x\log(2x/\theta)-2x$,
and $\epsilon_-=O(j)$ uniformly. Also $Q_+=q+r+L_s\asymp j^2$,
$Q_+-Q_-=16j-48+r=O_C(j)$, and $B_+=O(j)$.
The factorial integral estimates
\[
 b\log b-b+1\le\log(b!)\le b\log b-b+1+\log b
 \quad(b\ge1)
\]
follow by integrating the increasing logarithm between successive
integers. Applied to $b=2Q_+$, they place
$\log((2Q_+)!) -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa$ at
$f_\theta(Q_+)+O(\log j)$, because
$\log(\theta/\kappa)\le h/\kappa$ and $Q_+h=O(1)$.
Integration of $f_\theta'(x)=2\log(2x/\theta)$ between $Q_-$
and $Q_+$ now costs $O_C(j\log j)$.
Further, $\log C_{m+r}\le\log(2m+2r+1)+4(m+r)\log2=O_C(j)$,
and $2h/\pi\le\sin h\le h$ gives
$\log(U_h/L_h)=O(\log j)$. The fixed $v$ and nonzero
$\mu_v$ imply $|\log|\mu_v\binom{q+r}{v}||=O(\log j)$
uniformly on this range. These estimates prove NIB15 directly
from its fully finite expression, with no differentiation of
an asymptotic determinant error.

At each of these rows the original four-copy metric update is
$\mathcal D_1=\mathcal D_0+U_n^*U_n$, with
$U_n=\operatorname{diag}(\rho_n/\sqrt{1+\zeta_n},4)$.
Its full relative compression is $r_nI_4$. For each original
fixed flag $X$, retain $K_{X,n}=U_nX(X^*\mathcal D_0X)^{-1}X^*U_n^*$
and all four eigenvalues. The two chains
$J\subset W_i\subset W\subset T_e$ and
$K_i\subset I\subset K_c\subset S$ give, exactly as in FMG27,
two nonnegative disjoint increment sums each at most
$4\log(1+r_n)$. Their difference is the complete original signed
row. Applying the same reasoning to the increasing threshold
function on each ordered eigenvalue proves
\[
 |\delta\mathcal L_{e,n}|\le4E_{j,s,r},\qquad
 |\delta\mathcal P_{e,n}|\le4E_{j,s,r},\qquad
 0\le\delta V_{X,n}\le4E_{j,s,r}
                       \quad(X=b,{\rm rem},s,A).          \tag{NIB16}
\]
For the last assertion, the original quotients are $W/W_i$,
$\mathcal U/T_e$, $S/K_c$, and $I/K_i$. Block Schur elimination
expresses each successive quotient determinant increment as the
difference of its two restriction increments. Nested projections
bound that difference between zero and the full update budget.
This argument retains all original fixed-frame factors until they
cancel in the successive increment, and leaves every quotient
cross term in its attained metric.

The original target row for native degree $L+r$ is $N=q+r$.
On $0\le r\le q$, its original four-endpoint weights are one
at $r=0,q$ and two otherwise. For any integer
$0\le R\le\min(q,\lfloor Cj\rfloor)$ let $\vartheta_r$
denote those unchanged weights. Summing NIB16 gives the explicit
segment bound
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R}\vartheta_r\delta\mathcal L_{e,L+r}\right|,
 \quad
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R}\vartheta_r\delta\mathcal P_{e,L+r}\right|
       \le4\sum_{r=0}^{R}\vartheta_rE_{j,s,r},\\
 0\le\sum_{r=0}^{R}\vartheta_r\delta V_{X,L+r}
       \le4\sum_{r=0}^{R}\vartheta_rE_{j,s,r}
       =O_{{\rm actual},C}(j^2\log j)=o(kq_k).
\end{gathered}                                                     \tag{NIB17}
\]
The same last upper order holds for both absolute values in the
first line. In particular $r=1$ is an actually non-first original
interior row with its complete error bound. The full initial
linear segment, together with every complementary CPQ increment,
can be removed from the leading $kq_k$ calculation by the stated
finite error. All subsequent rows remain in their original
correlated expressions, and the complete CPQ identity
$4S_K=\mathcal L_e+V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A$ is preserved.
No intrinsic coefficient for the surviving interior profile or
conclusion about RH is assigned by this segment estimate.

\subsection{Uniform bound on the full original row range and a superlinear prefix}
The same finite NIB14 constant has the stronger uniform estimate
\[
 \boxed{\quad E_{j,s,r}=O_{\rm actual}((j+r)\log j)
    \quad(0\le r\le q,\ s\in\{1,j\}).\quad}               \tag{NIB18}
\]
Here the original quartet, conductor, period and logarithm branch
are fixed before the limit. We prove the uniformity from the full
finite expression. On this range $q\le q+r\le2q$, so
$1/(2q)\le h\le1/q$. Also $Q_-=q'+L_s\asymp j^2$ and
$Q_+=q+r+L_s\asymp j^2$, with constants valid for both allowed
orders. The actual lower interval therefore has
$M=2Q_-/\theta$ eventually, independently of $r$. Thus
$F_M=f_\theta(Q_-)$ and
$\epsilon_-=\theta^2/(4Q_-)+h(M+1)+4q'R'/M=O_{\rm actual}(j)$
uniformly. In particular no new interval or root substitution
has been made in the exact finite bound.

The factorial inequalities used in NIB15 give, uniformly here,
\[
 \log((2Q_+)!) -(2Q_++1)\log\kappa
       =f_\theta(Q_+)+O(\log j).
\]
Indeed the factorial error is $O(\log Q_+)$, the remaining
$-\log\kappa$ is bounded, and changing $\kappa$ to $\theta$
costs at most $(2Q_++1)h/\kappa=O(1)$. On the entire interval
$[Q_-,Q_+]$, $|f_\theta'(x)|=|2\log(2x/\theta)|=O(\log j)$.
Its length is exactly $16j-48+r$. Integration of this derivative
therefore gives
$f_\theta(Q_+)-f_\theta(Q_-)=O((j+r)\log j)$.
The other terms are bounded directly:
$\kappa B_+=O_{\rm actual}(j)$,
$\log C_{m+r}\le\log(2m+2r+1)+4(m+r)\log2=O(j+r)$,
and $2h/\pi\le\sin h\le h$ gives
$\log(U_h/L_h)=O(\log j)$ uniformly for $q\le q+r\le2q$.
Finally, because $v$ is fixed and $\mu_v\ne0$,
the finite product for $\binom{q+r}{v}$ has logarithm
$O_{\rm actual}(\log j)$ uniformly on that same range.
Every term in NIB14 is now controlled by the claimed bound.
Its nonnegativity was already proved in NIB14, completing NIB18.

For every integer $0\le R\le q$ retain exactly the original weights
$\vartheta_0=\vartheta_q=1$ and $\vartheta_r=2$ for $0<r<q$.
Define its fully finite prefix budget
\[
 \mathcal B_{j,s}(R)=4\sum_{r=0}^R\vartheta_rE_{j,s,r}
       =O_{\rm actual}((R+1)(j+R)\log j).
                                                               \tag{NIB19}
\]
The estimate follows by summing NIB18 with
$\vartheta_r\le2$ and
$\sum_{r=0}^R(j+r)=(R+1)j+R(R+1)/2\le(R+1)(j+R)$.
For every such $R$, the two absolute prefix sums in NIB17 and
each of the four nonnegative CPQ prefix sums are bounded by
this same $\mathcal B_{j,s}(R)$: this follows by summing the
actual inequalities NIB16 with the retained positive weights.
Thus NIB19 extends the range of the proved prefix estimate;
it changes no original row or endpoint.

In particular take the explicit integer prefix
\[
 R_j=\left\lfloor\frac{j^{3/2}}{\log j}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\quad
 \mathcal B_{j,s}(R_j)
   =O_{\rm actual}\left(j^{5/2}+\frac{j^3}{\log j}\right)
   =o(kq_k).
 \quad}                                                        \tag{NIB20}
\]
For $j\ge13$, $\sqrt j\log j>1$, so
$0\le R_j<j^2<q$. The point $r=R_j$ is consequently an interior
row of the original window, and retains weight two. Also
$R_j+1\le j^{3/2}/\log j+1$ and
$j+R_j\le j+j^{3/2}/\log j$. Multiplying these inequalities
and then by $\log j$ gives an upper bound
$j^{5/2}+j^3/\log j+j\log j+j^{3/2}$, with the last two terms
absorbed in the displayed order. Since $k=j-4$ and
$q_k=(j-3)^2$, division by $kq_k$ tends to zero.
This proves NIB20 for both original source orders and both
fixed primitive indices, simultaneously in the signed loss,
the signed threshold profile, and all four complementary returns.
It is a prefix decomposition of the unchanged rows $0\le r\le q$;
the original final endpoint remains $r=q$. Every row beyond
$R_j$ remains in its full correlated tail, with no coefficient
assigned to that remaining expression.
\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:ORIGINAL_NATIVE_INTERIOR_BOUND_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:end}

% BEGIN ADDITIVE MIXED TAIL INTEGRATION INTERIOR_PREFIX_RETURN_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE.tex
% EXACT INPUT SHA256 6441d2b20dc94e1b88a7e9dbc863ae251858f414f177bab250582ab0723fe9cc
\clearpage
\label{MTBUILD:INTERIOR_PREFIX_RETURN_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:start}
\begingroup
\section{The controlled original prefix and its complete remaining return}
\label{sec:IPR}

Keep the original fixed quartet, period, logarithm branch, complete
conductor, and the two source pairs of CPQ1. Write $j=k+4$,
$q=q_j=(j+1)^2$, $L=q-v$, and $s=s_1\in\{1,j\}$.
For every original target cutoff $q-1\le N\le2q$, set
$D_N=D_{j,N-v}^{(s)}$ and $\mathcal D_N=\operatorname{diag}(D_N,4)$.
Every coefficient flag and value frame below is precisely its CPQ frame.
NIB proves, for each $0\le r\le q$, the full original update
\[
 D_{q+r}=D_{q+r-1}+u_r^*u_r,\qquad
 u_r=\frac{\rho_{L+r}}{\sqrt{1+\zeta_{L+r}}},\qquad
 a_r=u_rD_{q+r-1}^{-1}u_r^*
       =\frac{\eta_{r,s}}{\nu^-_{m+r,s}}-1\ge0 .       \tag{IPR1}
\]
Here $\eta_{r,s}$ is the full previous-relation Schur minimum, not
the norm of an arbitrarily selected relation, and $\nu^-_{m+r,s}$
is the original lower ideal minimum. The finite NIB14 constant gives
$0\le\log(1+a_r)\le E_{j,s,r}$. Define the retained weights and budget
\[
 \vartheta_0=\vartheta_q=1,\quad
 \vartheta_r=2\ (0<r<q),\qquad
 \mathfrak E_{s,R}=\sum_{r=0}^R\vartheta_r E_{j,s,r}
       \quad(0\le R\le q).                              \tag{IPR2}
\]
All sums use the existing rows of the original window.

\subsection{Exact prefix and tail maps on the original evaluation sites}
For a scalar function $f$ of the original cutoff define
$\delta_r f=f(q+r)-f(q+r-1)$ and
$\mathcal R f=-f(q-1)-f(q)+f(2q-1)+f(2q)$.
For $0\le R<q$ direct telescoping gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal R_{\le R}f
   &:=\sum_{r=0}^R\vartheta_r\delta_r f
     =2f(q+R)-f(q)-f(q-1),\\
 \mathcal R_{>R}f
   &:=\sum_{r=R+1}^q\vartheta_r\delta_r f
     =f(2q-1)+f(2q)-2f(q+R),\\
 \mathcal R f&=\mathcal R_{\le R}f+\mathcal R_{>R}f.
\end{aligned}                                                       \tag{IPR3}
\]
Indeed the first sum is $f(q)-f(q-1)$ plus twice the
successive increments from $q$ to $q+R$. The second has twice
the increments from $q+R$ to $2q-1$ and the final single increment
from $2q-1$ to $2q$. This also proves the formulas at $R=0$
and $R=q-1$. At $R=q$ define $\mathcal R_{\le q}=\mathcal R$
and $\mathcal R_{>q}=0$; the displayed three-site formulas are
not used at that last index. Each operator annihilates constants.
Thus the same fixed frame factors that cancel under $\mathcal R$
also cancel separately under both operators. No representative
depending on $N$ is substituted for a fixed frame.

For a row sequence that is not itself a determinant increment,
in particular the original threshold row $p_r$, the prefix and
tail mean exactly $\sum_{r\le R}\vartheta_r p_r$ and
$\sum_{r>R}\vartheta_r p_r$. Formula IPR3 is applied only to
an actual scalar potential $f$, such as a logarithmic determinant.

\subsection{Both original allocations and all four complementary quotients}
Let $B_j:\mathbb C^{m_j}\to\mathbb C^{t_j}$ be the original onto
value map and choose fixed independent columns $J_j$ for its kernel
and a fixed right inverse $X_j$. The square matrix $C_j=[J_j,X_j]$
is invertible: applying $B_j$ to $J_jx+X_jy=0$ gives $y=0$, and
then independence gives $x=0$. Put
\[
 H_N=(B_jD_N^{-1}B_j^*)^{-1},\quad
 H^0_N=J_j^*D_NJ_j,\qquad
 |\det C_j|^2\det D_N=\det H_N\det H^0_N .             \tag{IPR4}
\]


% BEGIN HUMAN CITATION M19_IPR_SCHUR
The block minimum in IPR4 is the classical Schur-complement
method \cite[\S2.1, (2.3)--(2.4)]{human:boyd1994} on the
unchanged frame $C_j=[J_j,X_j]$.
Its exact determinant factor $|\det C_j|^2$ is retained below.
% END HUMAN CITATION M19_IPR_SCHUR

For fixed value $y$, every lift is $X_jy+J_jx$. Completing its
positive quadratic form gives the full Schur complement of
$H^0_N$ in $C_j^*D_NC_j$. Its inverse is the bottom-right block
of $C_j^{-1}D_N^{-1}(C_j^*)^{-1}$, equal to
$B_jD_N^{-1}B_j^*$ because $B_jC_j=[0,I]$. This proves both
the literal value minimum and IPR4 with its exact frame factor.

Restriction and minimum over the same affine fibre preserve
$D_N\succeq D_{N-1}$. Hence the two logarithmic determinant
increments in IPR4 are nonnegative; their sum, by the determinant
lemma applied to IPR1, is $\log(1+a_r)$. Consequently, for
$Z=\le R$ or $>R$, define the actual partial allocations
\[
 S_K^Z=\mathcal R_Z\log\det H_N,\qquad
 S_R^Z=\mathcal R_Z\log\det H^0_N,\qquad
 \mathcal T^Z=\mathcal R_Z\log\det D_N .                \tag{IPR5}
\]
They satisfy exactly
\[
\begin{gathered}
 S_K^Z+S_R^Z=\mathcal T^Z,\qquad S_K^Z,S_R^Z\ge0,\\
 0\le S_K^{\le R},S_R^{\le R}\le\mathcal T^{\le R}
     =\sum_{r=0}^R\vartheta_r\log(1+a_r)
     \le\mathfrak E_{s,R} .                             \tag{IPR6}
\end{gathered}
\]
Their full sums are the original HJR32 allocations, with the same
value maps and native source order; no allocation fraction is introduced.

For each $e=0,1$ let $\mathcal L_e(N)$ be
$\log(\omega_{{\rm conn},e}(N)\omega_{\rm inv}(N))$.
For $X=b,{\rm rem},s,A$ let $Q_X(N)$ be respectively
$Q_b^E,Q_{{\rm rem},e},G_{s,e},A_{\rm inv}$, and put
$V_X^Z=\mathcal R_Z\log\det Q_X(N)$ and
$\mathcal L_e^Z=\mathcal R_Z\mathcal L_e(N)$.
The full fixed-frame determinant identities CPQ7--12 prove the
following, separately for the prefix and tail:
\[
 4S_K^Z=\mathcal L_e^Z+V_b^Z+V_{\rm rem}^Z+V_s^Z+V_A^Z . \tag{IPR7}
\]
To see the cancellation explicitly, CPQ8 gives
$\log\omega_{\rm conn}=\log\det G_t-\log\det G_s$.
The original invariant quotient gives
$\log\omega_{\rm inv}=\log\det H_{\rm inv}
-\log\det A_{\rm inv}$ plus its fixed frame constant.
The full target factorization gives
$4\log\det H_N=\log\det H_{\rm inv}+\log\det Q_b^E
+\log\det G_t+\log\det Q_{\rm rem}$ plus its fixed frame
constant. Subtraction proves IPR7 after applying either operator
in IPR3, since both kill those constants.

Every one of these quotients is taken in its original ambient
metric and complete kernel fibre. NIB16's same-row bound and its
two nested flag chains give
\[
\begin{gathered}
 |\mathcal L_e^{\le R}|\le4\mathfrak E_{s,R},\qquad
 |\mathcal P_e^{\le R}|\le4\mathfrak E_{s,R},\\
 0\le V_X^{\le R}\le4\mathfrak E_{s,R}
           \quad(X=b,{\rm rem},s,A).                   \tag{IPR8}
\end{gathered}
\]
Here $\mathcal P_e^{\le R}$ is the weighted original threshold
row sum, not the different quantity $\mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}$
in CPQ11. IPR7 retains the correlation between all five terms;
IPR6 supplies the stronger direct bound on their combined value
$4S_K^{\le R}$. The terms are not assigned independent extremizers.

The NIB all-row estimate gives
$\mathfrak E_{s,R}=O_{\rm actual}((R+1)(j+R)\log j)$
uniformly for $0\le R\le q$. In particular the explicitly
evaluated prefix length
\[
 R_j=\left\lfloor\frac{j^{3/2}}{\log j}\right\rfloor
 \quad\hbox{satisfies}\quad
 \mathfrak E_{s,R_j}
   =O_{\rm actual}(j^{5/2}+j^3/\log j)=o(kq_k).         \tag{IPR9}
\]
For all sufficiently large $j$ it lies below $q$. The finite
budgets IPR2 apply also before that threshold, with the prefix
restricted to the original $R\le q$ domain. Since
$kq_k=(j-4)(j-3)^2\asymp j^3$, each displayed order assertion
follows by division. This selects no new programme endpoint:
IPR3 is an identity for every original prefix, and IPR9 evaluates
one range already controlled by the proved finite inequality.

\subsection{Exact transport of the two partial returns}
Use the original NCT complex-linear map
$\Lambda_F\mapsto\Lambda_{E_AF}$, its ordinary-transpose matrix,
and the full lower quotient. For each of the eight original flag
frames $X$ and each CPQ value quotient $Y$, denote its native
and transported logarithmic determinants by $f_X,\widehat f_X$
or $f_Y,\widehat f_Y$. The original finite NCT20/NPK11 bound is
\[
 |\widehat f_Y(N)-f_Y(N)|\le B_{Y,N},\qquad
 B_{Y,N}=B^{[4]}_{j,N-v,s,\operatorname{rank}Y}\ge0 .    \tag{IPR10}
\]
All frames and values on the two sides are identical; the
conjugation in the inverse evaluation metric remains IGI7.
For $0\le R<q$ linearity of IPR3 now proves the finite bounds
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |\widehat V_Y^{\le R}-V_Y^{\le R}|
  &\le 2B_{Y,q+R}+B_{Y,q}+B_{Y,q-1}=:E_Y^{\le R},\\
 |\widehat V_Y^{>R}-V_Y^{>R}|
  &\le B_{Y,2q-1}+B_{Y,2q}+2B_{Y,q+R}=:E_Y^{>R} .
\end{aligned}                                                       \tag{IPR11}
\]
At $R=0$, repeated occurrences in the displayed upper bound
may also be combined using their actual opposite signs; the
uncombined nonnegative bound is valid. This uses only the three
evaluation sites and does not sum a determinant error separately
for every row. For the signed eight-flag profile sum these bounds
over the eight original frames to obtain $E_{\rm flags}^Z$.
For $S_K$ apply them directly to the four-copy target quotient of
rank $4t_j$, yielding $|4\widehat S_K^Z-4S_K^Z|\le E_{\rm target}^Z$.
The transported full factorization also gives the exact equality
\[
 4(\widehat S_K^Z-S_K^Z)
  =\widehat{\mathcal L}_e^Z-\mathcal L_e^Z
       +\sum_{X=b,{\rm rem},s,A}(\widehat V_X^Z-V_X^Z).
                                                               \tag{IPR12}
\]
Both enclosures concern this same value and may be intersected.
For the other allocation use the original four-copy kernel frame
$\operatorname{diag}(J_j,4)$. Its restricted determinant is exactly
$(\det H_N^0)^4$ in this direct-sum frame. A change to another
fixed original kernel frame multiplies it by a cutoff-independent
positive factor, annihilated by both operators. Thus this original
restriction flag has return $4S_R^Z$, its transported restriction
has return $4\widehat S_R^Z$, and IPR11 at its actual rank
$4\dim\ker B_j$ gives
$|4\widehat S_R^Z-4S_R^Z|\le E_{\rm kernel}^Z$.
This supplies the direct same-value transport of the second allocation.

For $0\le R<q$, each original or transported threshold row differs from its own
signed determinant row by at most $8\log2$, as proved by NPK13's
two ordered chains. The two row sequences therefore satisfy
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |\widehat{\mathcal P}_e^{\le R}-\mathcal P_e^{\le R}|
   &\le E_{\rm flags}^{\le R}+16(2R+1)\log2,\\
 |\widehat{\mathcal P}_e^{>R}-\mathcal P_e^{>R}|
   &\le E_{\rm flags}^{>R}+16\{2(q-R)-1\}\log2 .
\end{aligned}                                                       \tag{IPR13}
\]
Indeed the original prefix weights sum to $1+2R$, and the tail
weights sum to $2(q-R)-1$. These bounds compare scalar sums;
no approximation of individual eigenvalues is asserted.
NCT's fixed-radius estimate and subsequent radius limit are
uniform over the unchanged cutoff interval $q-1\le N\le2q$.
All ranks here are $O(j)$, so each finite three-site error in
IPR11 is $o(kq_k)$ by exactly that order of limits. The extra
threshold error is $O(q)$, also $o(kq_k)$. Thus IPR8--9 transport
the full controlled prefix, while IPR3 and IPR12 retain the
entire uncomputed tail and all its correlated quotients.

\subsection{Receiving the estimate in the existing source and action maps}
Every equation above holds separately for $s=1$ and $s=j$ and
for $e=0,1$. For example
\[
 \mathcal L_{e,s}^{>R}=\mathcal L_{e,s}-\mathcal L_{e,s}^{\le R},
 \quad V_{X,s}^{>R}=V_{X,s}-V_{X,s}^{\le R},\quad
 S_{K,s}^{>R}=S_{K,s}-S_{K,s}^{\le R}.                  \tag{IPR14}
\]
Consequently the exact endpoint-difference identities PRT14--20,
and the original two-order intervals CPQ19 and HJR33, propagate
by subtracting the corresponding prefix on each side. For the
positive quotient $X$, for instance, its order-difference change
is in $[-4\mathfrak E_{j,R},4\mathfrak E_{1,R}]$, because
$V_{X,j}^{\le R},V_{X,1}^{\le R}$ lie respectively in
$[0,4\mathfrak E_{j,R}]$ and $[0,4\mathfrak E_{1,R}]$.
For the signed profile its absolute change is at most
$4(\mathfrak E_{j,R}+\mathfrak E_{1,R})$. All these corrections
are $o(kq_k)$ at IPR9; none changes a previously proved leading
coefficient or supplies the unknown mixed one.

The proper source $Q_{\rm inv}$ still uses degree $k$, its own
centre and its displaced cutoffs. Its exact relation to the
target form $A_{\rm inv}$ remains NFR/EFP's logarithmic ratio
$\log\det A_{\rm inv}-\log\det Q_{\rm inv}$. Applying IPR3
to this difference gives the difference of the two partial
returns, with each form evaluated at its original prescribed
arguments. No NIB bound for the target is transferred to the
proper source without its own proved metric comparison.
Writing $V_Q$ for that unchanged proper-source return, the precise
change on removing the target prefix is
\[
 (V_A^{>R}-V_Q)-(V_A-V_Q)=-V_A^{\le R}.
\]
The removed contribution is $V_A^{\le R}$, bounded by
$4\mathfrak E_{s,R}$; the proper-source value itself remains.

Finally, in the existing complete action $J=B+W-P$, every
use of an IPR3 volume decomposition is made by literal
substitution $B=B^{\le R}+B^{>R}$ for that specified original
volume contribution, retaining its original coefficient.
Then exactly $J=(B^{>R}+W-P)+B^{\le R}$.
The recurrence window $W$ and penalty $P$ are untouched by
this algebraic identity. In particular the controlled prefix
cannot be used to cancel an unevaluated tail or penalty.
Equations IPR3--14 give the full maps and finite errors needed
for that substitution on the original mixed and CPQ terms.
The remaining calculation is their correlated interior return
at $q+R_j,2q-1,2q$, with their full Schur denominators.
\endgroup

\label{MTBUILD:INTERIOR_PREFIX_RETURN_COMPLETE_PREREQUISITE:end}
