% Complete original Gamma dual norm; every extension and quotient frame retained.
\section{The original weighted relation metric as a minimum dual extension}
\label{sec:DNE}

Retain the original simple quartet, fixed five-orbit period and
logarithm branch, all coefficients and shifts of ROG and RDS, and
the same two source orders $s\in\{1,k\}$. Put
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 q=(k+1)^2,\quad q'=(k-7)^2,\quad
 \Delta=q-q',\quad \mathfrak m=\Delta-v,\quad c'=k/2-4,\\
 N\in\{q-1,q,2q-1,2q\},\quad L=N-q,\quad
 d=N-v,\quad M=N-v-q'=L+\mathfrak m .
 \end{gathered}\tag{DNE1}
\]
Here $0\le v\le80$, $\mu_v\ne0$, and $\chi'$ is the complete
lower-grid polynomial $\chi_{k-8}$, never a derivative.
All spaces of degree at most $-1$ are zero. The source norm is
the original norm at $S'=c'+iy$, with mass $M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}$.

\subsection{A fixed original exponential quotient frame}

We specify a finite frame using only the actual original matrices.
Let $\mathbb V$ be the upper-root Vandermonde with rows
$a=0,\ldots,q-1$ and entries
$\mathbb V_{a,(i,j)}=\beta_{ij}^{a}$.
Let $P_v:\mathbb C^{q-v}\to\mathbb C^q$ insert zero in the
first $v$ positions, and let $C$ be the exact primary conductor
of CEP4, with $q'$ rows and $q$ columns. Define
\[
 W_v=\mathbb V^{-1}P_v,\qquad
 J_v=P_v^{\mathsf T}\mathbb V C^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 W_vJ_v=C^{\mathsf T}.
 \tag{DNE2}
\]
The Vandermonde is invertible by the original distinct roots.
The first $v$ rows of $\mathbb V C^{\mathsf T}$ vanish because
the corresponding exponential columns are
$E_Ae^{\beta'_{ab}z}$, whose zero order is at least $v$.
This proves the last equality. Consequently $W_v$ is an
isomorphism onto the coefficient vectors of $\mathscr N_v$,
and $J_v$ has rank $q'$, since $C^{\mathsf T}$ is injective.

Choose the first $q'$ row positions $\mathcal I$ in a fixed
ordering of all subsets for which $\det (J_v)_{\mathcal I}\ne0$,
and let $\mathcal J$ be their complement. Such a subset exists
by the proved rank. Let $E_{\mathcal J}$ insert these
$\mathfrak m$ complementary coordinates and put
\[
 Z_{\exp}=W_vE_{\mathcal J},\qquad
 \mathcal N_a(z)=
   \sum_{i,j}(Z_{\exp})_{(i,j),a}e^{\beta_{ij}z},
 \qquad F_a(z)=\frac{\mathcal N_a(z)}{E_A(z)}
       \quad(1\le a\le\mathfrak m).
 \tag{DNE3}
\]
The columns of $[J_v,E_{\mathcal J}]$ are independent:
restriction of a relation to $\mathcal I$ first kills its
$J_v$ coefficients, and then its other coefficients vanish.
Thus the $\mathcal N_a$ give a basis of the exact quotient
$\mathscr N_v/E_A\mathscr E_{k-8}$. Every root value and
actual minor defining this frame is retained. The frame is
fixed as the endpoint $N$ and source order $s$ change.

For an analytic germ $F$ at zero define the complex-linear
functional $\Lambda_F$ by
$\Lambda_F(S'^n)=F^{(n)}(0)$. Let $\ell_a$ be the
functional from RDS8. Then
\[
 \ell_a(g)=\Lambda_{F_a}(\chi'g),\qquad
 f_{\ell_a}=\chi'(D)F_a,\qquad
 A_{\ell,ba}=\ell_a(S'^b)\quad(0\le b<\mathfrak m).
 \tag{DNE4}
\]
Indeed multiplication by $\chi'$ in the functional is precisely
the constant-coefficient operator $\chi'(D)$ on its generating
series. RDS8 proves that these functionals form the entire dual
of the relation quotient. RDS2 identifies that quotient with
the increasing monomials below degree $\mathfrak m$, so
$A_\ell$ is invertible. This supplies its existence without
assuming a nonzero unevaluated minor.

\subsection{The exact original source extension problem}

On $\mathbb C[S']_{\le M}$ use the original weighted norm
$\|g\|_{\chi',s,c'}=\|\chi'g\|_{s,c'}$. Let $G_M$ be its full
monomial Gram, and let $\mathbf U_L$ be the complete ROG4
relation matrix. The quotient
\[
 Y_N=\mathbb C[S']_{\le M}/\operatorname{im}\mathcal U_L
 \simeq\mathbb C[S']_{<\mathfrak m}
\]
has Gram, in its fixed low-monomial representative frame,
\[
 H_{Y,N}^{(s)}
 =E_{<\mathfrak m}^*G_ME_{<\mathfrak m}
 -E_{<\mathfrak m}^*G_M\mathbf U_L
  (\mathbf U_L^*G_M\mathbf U_L)^{-1}
             \mathbf U_L^*G_ME_{<\mathfrak m}.
 \tag{DNE5}
\]
The second term is absent when $L=-1$. Distinct leading
degrees of the columns of $\mathcal U_L$ show that the low
representatives and all relation columns form a basis.
Positivity of $G_M$ and minimization over the full relation
columns therefore prove DNE5 and its strict positivity.

For $x\in\mathbb C^{\mathfrak m}$ write
$\mathcal N_x=\sum_a x_a\mathcal N_a$ and
$F_x=\mathcal N_x/E_A$. Its functional $\ell_x$ on $Y_N$
has the exact norm
\[
 \|\ell_x\|_{Y_N^\vee}^{\,2}
 =\min_{\eta\in\mathbb C^{q'}}
   \left\|\Lambda_{F_x}
          +\sum_{a,b}\eta_{ab}\operatorname{ev}_{\beta'_{ab}}
   \right\|_{(\mathbb C[S']_{\le d},\,\|\cdot\|_{s,c'})^\vee}^{\,2}.
 \tag{DNE6}
\]
Here the dual consists of complex-linear functionals.
To prove the equality, multiply by $\chi'$ to identify the
weighted polynomial space isometrically with
$I_N=\chi'\mathbb C[S']_{\le M}\subset\mathbb C[S']_{\le d}$.
The restriction of $\Lambda_{F_x}$ to $I_N$ is exactly
$g\mapsto\ell_x(g)$ by DNE4, and it annihilates
$\chi'\operatorname{im}\mathcal U_L$ by RDS5.

A functional annihilating a subspace has the same norm on
the Hilbert quotient: the minimum lift of each quotient
class has the same functional value, and every other lift
has larger norm. A functional on a Hilbert subspace has a
unique extension of minimum norm to the ambient space,
namely its composition with the orthogonal projection onto
that subspace. Every other extension adds a functional on
the perpendicular complement; their squared dual norms add.
These two facts reduce its quotient dual norm to the
minimum over all ambient extensions of its restriction.

The difference of two extensions annihilates $I_N$.
That annihilator has dimension
$(d+1)-(M+1)=q'$. Evaluation at each original lower root
annihilates $I_N$, and these $q'$ evaluation functionals are
independent on $\mathbb C[S']_{\le d}$, by the first $q'$
monomials and the distinct-root Vandermonde. They therefore
constitute its whole annihilator. This proves DNE6 and
retains every permitted extension.

Let
\[
 \phi_{n,s,c'}(S')
 =\frac{p_n((S'-c')/i)}{\sqrt{h_n}},\qquad
 h_n=M_sn!(s/2)_n,\qquad 0\le n\le d
\]
be the unchanged original orthonormal source frame. Form the
complete finite matrices
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 T_{N,s;na}=\Lambda_{F_a}(\phi_{n,s,c'}),\qquad
 V_{N,s;n,(i,j)}=\phi_{n,s,c'}(\beta'_{ij}),\\
 \mathcal D_N^{(s)}
 =T_{N,s}^*
  \left[I-V_{N,s}(V_{N,s}^*V_{N,s})^{-1}V_{N,s}^*\right]T_{N,s}.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{DNE7}
\]
The matrix $V_{N,s}$ has rank $q'$ by the same Vandermonde
argument, since the displayed orthonormal polynomials span
all degrees at most $d$. In an orthonormal basis the squared
norm of a complex-linear functional is the sum of the
squared moduli of its values. Thus the minimum in DNE6 is
$\min_\eta\|T_{N,s}x+V_{N,s}\eta\|_2^2$.
The minimizing vector is
$-(V_{N,s}^*V_{N,s})^{-1}V_{N,s}^*T_{N,s}x$.
Substitution proves DNE7, including both mixed factors,
and gives $\|\ell_x\|^2=x^*\mathcal D_N^{(s)}x$.
Positivity follows because the $\ell_a$ are an independent
dual frame of $Y_N$.

\subsection{Complex-linear duality and the actual fixed kernel columns}

For a positive Gram $H$ and a complex-linear functional
$y\mapsto a^{\mathsf T}y$, its squared dual norm is
$a^{\mathsf T}H^{-1}\bar a
 =a^*\overline{H^{-1}}a$.
Indeed its Riesz vector is $H^{-1}\bar a$, and evaluation
of its squared norm gives the first expression; the second
is its equal real conjugate. Therefore the precise metric
identity and determinant consequence of DNE4--7 are
\[
 \mathcal D_N^{(s)}
   =A_\ell^*\overline{(H_{Y,N}^{(s)})^{-1}}A_\ell,\qquad
 \det\mathcal D_N^{(s)}
   =\frac{|\det A_\ell|^2}{\det H_{Y,N}^{(s)}}.
 \tag{DNE8}
\]
The conjugation records the ordinary complex-linear dual
coordinates; it is not discarded by changing the source frame.
In the four-endpoint sum, the fixed factor $|\det A_\ell|^2$
cancels with signs $(1,1,-1,-1)$.

Retain the actual fixed matrix $Z_k$ of FEX27 and FGC9,
with $t=r-t_0$ independent columns in the low-monomial
frame of dimension $\mathfrak m$. Multiplying every
relation column by the same nonzero scalar
$\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v$ does not change its span or its
Schur complement. Consequently its actual native
FEX29 matrix and the exact dual quotient map are
\[
 \widehat H_{K,N}^{(s)}=Z_k^*H_{Y,N}^{(s)}Z_k,\qquad
 B_K=Z_k^{\mathsf T}A_\ell,\qquad
 Q_{K^\vee,N}^{(s)}
 =\left[B_K(\mathcal D_N^{(s)})^{-1}B_K^*\right]^{-1}
 =\overline{(\widehat H_{K,N}^{(s)})^{-1}}.
 \tag{DNE9}
\]
The map $B_K$ evaluates each relation-annihilating functional
on the actual columns $Z_k$. It is onto, because $A_\ell$
is invertible and $Z_k$ has rank $t$. The first inverse-Gram
formula is the minimum dual metric on that same evaluation
target. Substitution of DNE8 yields
\[
 B_K(\mathcal D_N^{(s)})^{-1}B_K^*
 =Z_k^{\mathsf T}\overline{H_{Y,N}^{(s)}}\bar Z_k
 =\overline{\widehat H_{K,N}^{(s)}},
\]
which proves the final equality with every transpose in
its original direction. Empty rank has determinant one.
In particular the full fixed-column return is exactly
\[
 \widehat F_k^{(s)}
 =-\sum_N\sigma_N\log\det Q_{K^\vee,N}^{(s)}.
 \tag{DNE10}
\]
Its relation to the original $F_K^{(s)}$ retains both low
quotient volumes and the full source-map error of FGC12--13
and FEX34--37; DNE10 removes none of those terms.

\subsection{A finite source bound before the high-order derivative}

Use the actual $R,R',B,C_v$ and $R_0$ of ROG6 and RDS11,
and put
\[
 \rho=\tanh(R_0/4),\qquad
 C_{F,k,A}=\frac{2R^v}{|\mu_v|}e^{RR_0}.
 \tag{DNE11}
\]
For any $\mathcal N=\sum\nu_{ij}e^{\beta_{ij}z}
\in\mathscr N_v$, RDS12 proves
$|\mathcal N/E_A|\le C_{F,k,A}\|\nu\|_1$
on the complete disk $|z|\le R_0$.
Apply the original Gamma generating identity directly to
the functional $\Lambda_{\mathcal N/E_A}$:
\[
 \sum_{n\ge0}\Lambda_{\mathcal N/E_A}
          \left(p_n((S'-c')/i)\right)\frac{z^n}{n!}
 =(1+z^2)^{-s/4}e^{ic'\arctan z}
                       \frac{\mathcal N(-i\arctan z)}
                            {E_A(-i\arctan z)}.
 \tag{DNE12}
\]
The quotient at zero is removable. On $|z|=\rho$,
$|\arctan z|\le R_0/4$.
Cauchy's coefficient estimate therefore gives the exact bound
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \|\Lambda_{\mathcal N/E_A}\|_
             {(\mathbb C[S']_{\le d},\|\cdot\|_{s,c'})^\vee}
       \le \mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}\|\nu\|_1,\\
 \mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}^{\,2}
 =\frac{C_{F,k,A}^2}{M_s}
    (1-\rho^2)^{-s/2}e^{|c'|R_0/2}
       \sum_{n=0}^d\frac{n!}{(s/2)_n}\rho^{-2n}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{DNE13}
\]
Indeed the bound for the $n$th orthonormal coefficient is
the square root of its displayed summand times the common
factor and $\|\nu\|_1$; summing squared coefficients gives
the dual norm. The full Gamma mass remains explicitly in
this formula. No application of $\chi'(D)$ and no
$q'!$ factor is needed in this exact extension norm.

For a fully specified comparison reference, use Euclidean
norm in the original upper exponential coefficient order
$\nu_{ij}$, with its actual quotient by
$\operatorname{im}C^{\mathsf T}$. Its positive Gram in the
fixed DNE3 frame is
\[
 G_{\exp}
 =Z_{\exp}^*
  \left[I-C^{\mathsf T}
     (\bar C C^{\mathsf T})^{-1}\bar C\right]Z_{\exp},
 \qquad
 0\prec\mathcal D_N^{(s)}
       \preceq q\,\mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}^{\,2}G_{\exp}.
 \tag{DNE14}
\]
The reference is explicitly specified here for comparison;
the actual weighted Gamma quotient remains DNE7.
The inverse in $G_{\exp}$ exists by the full row rank of
$C$. Its expression is the orthogonal-projection formula,
and is positive on this quotient frame by DNE3.
Every representative $\nu+C^{\mathsf T}\eta$ remains in
$\mathscr N_v$ and changes $\mathcal N/E_A$ by precisely a
lower exponential, by RDS7. Combining DNE6 and DNE13,
and then using $\|w\|_1\le\sqrt q\|w\|_2$, gives
\[
 x^*\mathcal D_N^{(s)}x
 \le\mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}^{\,2}
      \inf_\eta\|Z_{\exp}x+C^{\mathsf T}\eta\|_1^2
 \le q\,\mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}^{\,2}x^*G_{\exp}x.
\]
This proves DNE14 without a bound on an omitted frame inverse.

At fixed actual period and quartet the finite constant satisfies
\[
 \log\left(1+q\mathcal B_{d,s,k,A}^{\,2}\right)
       =O_{\rm actual}(q),\qquad s=1,k,\quad d=N-v.
 \tag{DNE15}
\]
For detail, $R_0,\rho,v,\mu_v$ are fixed, $R=O(k)$,
$|c'|=O(k)$ and $s\le k$. Thus
$\log C_{F,k,A}=O_{\rm actual}(k+\log(k+1))$.
The original Gamma ratios obey
$n!/(s/2)_n\le2\sqrt n$ for $n\ge1$ and are one at $n=0$,
as proved by the product estimate in FEX10--11. Hence the
sum in DNE13 is at most
$(d+1)\max\{1,2\sqrt d\}\rho^{-2d}$.
Since $d\le2q$, its logarithm is $O_{\rm actual}(q)$.
The remaining factors have logarithms $O_{\rm actual}(k)$,
with the original positive mass retained before estimation.
These bounds prove DNE15.

Restriction of DNE14 to any specified plane, and minimization
under any specified onto quotient map, preserve its scalar
upper bound. In particular it applies to the actual map $B_K$
with the full reference quotient
$(B_KG_{\exp}^{-1}B_K^*)^{-1}$.
This is a finite norm comparison of the original metric
with a completely retained reference form. The reference
contains the full original Vandermonde and conductor data;
neither its determinant nor the original return in DNE10
has been assigned a leading value by this upper bound.

% END CURRENT DNE

\clearpage
% BEGIN CURRENT HJR
% Literal compatible high jets, original Gamma extension and complete flags.
\section{Compatible high jets and the original positive return}
\label{sec:HJR}

Retain all fixed original quartet, amplitude, period and branch data
of LJR, DNE, PEC and OVR. In particular the arithmetic domain remains
$|u|\ge R_*$, the actual period is fixed while $k=4l+1$ grows, and
the source order is exactly $s=1$ or $s=k$. All asymptotic uses of
OFV retain its proved eventual threshold. Write
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 q=(k+1)^2,\quad q'=(k-7)^2,\quad
 n=q-v,\quad \mathfrak m=q-q'-v,\quad d_0=q-v-1,\\
 q-1\le N\le2q,\qquad D=N-v,\qquad
 e_N=N-q+1,\qquad D+1=n+e_N.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR1}
\]
The polynomial degree $D$ is the actual native source degree after
the conductor. The original centers remain $c=k/2$ and $c'=c-4$.
No change of $s$ is made between different endpoints.

\subsection{The exact compatible high-jet graph}

Let $B_{s,D}$ be the original matrix of $S'^a$ in the complete
orthonormal Gamma frame $\phi_0,\ldots,\phi_D$, as in LJR2--3,
with $h_a=M_sa!(s/2)_a$ and $M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}$. Define
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 (\mathbb V_N)_{j,(a,b)}=\beta_{ab}^{\,j}
                      \quad(0\le j\le N),\\
 (J_{E,N})_{ja}=\binom ja\mu_{j-a}
                 \quad(0\le j\le N,\ 0\le a\le D),\\
 \widetilde J_{E,N}=(J_{E,N})_{\{v,\ldots,N\},\{0,\ldots,D\}},
 \qquad P_{v,N}:\mathbb C^{D+1}\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{N+1}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR2}
\]
Here $P_{v,N}$ inserts $v$ initial zero coordinates, and an
out-of-range term in the convolution is zero. The square
$\widetilde J_{E,N}$ is lower triangular with diagonal
$\mu_v\binom{v+a}{a}$ and is therefore invertible.

On the original coefficient subspace $\mathscr N_v$ put
\[
 \mathsf F_{N,s}
  =B_{s,D}^{-\mathsf T}\widetilde J_{E,N}^{-1}
                P_{v,N}^{\mathsf T}\mathbb V_N,\qquad
 \mathsf E_{N,s}=\mathsf F_{N,s}\mathsf R_s
                  =\begin{pmatrix}I_n\\ H_{N,s}\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{HJR3}
\]
The right factor $\mathsf R_s$ is the exact low reconstruction
LJR4. Thus $\mathsf F_{N,s}$ takes original upper exponential
coefficients to their original native functional values through
degree $D$; $\mathsf E_{N,s}$ extends the first $n$ such values.
For a literal formula for the inverse convolution, if
$u_j=(\mathbb V_N\nu)_j$, then
\[
 f_a=\frac{v!}{\mu_v}a!
       \sum_{r=0}^{a}\kappa_{a-r}\frac{u_{v+r}}{(v+r)!},
 \qquad
 \mathsf F_{N,s}\nu=B_{s,D}^{-\mathsf T}(f_0,\ldots,f_D)^{\mathsf T}.
 \tag{HJR4}
\]
The coefficients $\kappa_a$ are all the actual inverse-symbol
coefficients of LJR6, with their proved finite recurrence.
The source coefficient transformation in HJR4 contains the
original phase $i^{-a}$ and every $h_a$.

To prove these statements, use the analytic germs
\[
 \mathcal N(z)=\sum\nu_{ab}e^{\beta_{ab}z},\qquad
 E_A(z)=(\mu_v/v!)z^vB_\infty(z).
\]
The order condition
$\nu\in\mathscr N_v$ makes the quotient analytic at zero.
Formal Taylor multiplication gives HJR4 and HJR2, and the
triangular $B_{s,D}^{-\mathsf T}$ changes ordinary moments to
orthonormal functional values. Its first $n$ moments and
orthonormal values depend only on the first $q$ jets $u_j$.
LJR6 therefore proves that the top block of HJR3 is $I_n$.
The bottom block $H_{N,s}$ is the specified remaining $e_N$ rows.

For a fully intrinsic description, write
$\chi(S)=S^q+\sum_{a=0}^{q-1}\chi_aS^a$. Define
$\mathcal U_{N,s}\subset\mathbb C^{D+1}$ by requiring that
$u=J_{E,N}B_{s,D}^{\mathsf T}w$ satisfy
\[
 u_{q+j}+\sum_{a=0}^{q-1}\chi_a u_{a+j}=0,
                      \qquad 0\le j<e_N .
 \tag{HJR5}
\]
Then $\mathcal U_{N,s}=\operatorname{im}\mathsf E_{N,s}$,
and its dimension is $n$. Indeed each original root is
annihilated by $\chi$, so HJR3 satisfies every recurrence.
Conversely the first $q$ entries of $u$, with its first $v$
zeros, determine a unique $\nu$ by the invertible upper
Vandermonde. The monic recurrences determine every later
$u_j$ uniquely and give $\mathbb V_N\nu$. The invertible
lower convolution block then determines $w$ uniquely.
This proves the graph equality, its dimension, and the
independence of its $e_N$ constraints. The free ambient later
functional values have not been identified with upper
exponential coefficients; the exact identification uses HJR5.

Let $V_{N,s}$ denote the $(D+1)$-by-$q'$ lower evaluation matrix
$(V_{N,s})_{a,\beta'}=\phi_a(\beta')$, and let $V=V_{q-1,s}$.
The complete lower-exponential identity gives
\[
 \mathsf E_{N,s}V=V_{N,s},\qquad
 \mathcal U_{N,s}/\operatorname{im}V_{N,s}
       \simeq\mathscr N_v/\operatorname{im}C^{\mathsf T}.
 \tag{HJR6}
\]
For its proof use $\nu=C^{\mathsf T}\eta$ in HJR4:
$\mathcal N/E_A$ is exactly the sum of the lower exponentials
with coefficients $\eta$, at all orders. Since $\mathsf E$
is injective, its induced quotient kernel is exactly
$\operatorname{im}V$. This proves the typed quotient
identification with the original denominators.

\subsection{One lower-evaluation minimum over the complete window}

Keep precisely DNE's fixed coefficient quotient frame $Z_{\exp}$.
Put $T_N=\mathsf F_{N,s}Z_{\exp}$, $T_0=T_{q-1}$, and
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 A=V^*V,\qquad K_0=A^{-1}V^*T_0,\qquad
 Z_0=T_0-VK_0,\qquad Q_0=VA^{-1/2},\\
 D_0=Z_0^*Z_0=\mathcal D_{q-1}^{(s)},\qquad
 V^*Z_0=0,\qquad Q_0^*Q_0=I_{q'}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR7}
\]
The matrices $A$ and its positive square root are the actual
lower-evaluation Gram, retaining the complete root and Gamma
conditioning. No replacement evaluation metric is made.
With $H=H_{N,s}$, define
\[
 P_N=HZ_0,\qquad Q_N=HQ_0,\qquad
 \mathcal C_N=(I_{e_N}+Q_NQ_N^*)^{-1/2}P_N .
 \tag{HJR8}
\]
At $e_N=0$ all these matrices have zero rows. The exact native
metric at every endpoint is
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \mathcal D_N^{(s)}
   =D_0+P_N^*(I_{e_N}+Q_NQ_N^*)^{-1}P_N
   =D_0+\mathcal C_N^*\mathcal C_N .
 \quad}\tag{HJR9}
\]
In particular its increment is positive and has rank
$\operatorname{rank}P_N\le\min\{\mathfrak m,e_N\}$.
The first endpoint update $N=q$ has rank at most one.
The last two endpoints have $e_{2q-1}=q$ and $e_{2q}=q+1$, respectively.

Here is a proof with the complete minimizer. Subtract the fixed
lower extension $\mathsf E VK_0$ from $T_N=\mathsf E T_0$.
Every remaining allowed extension is
$\mathsf E(Z_0x+Q_0t)$, with one vector $t\in\mathbb C^{q'}$
used for both its low and high rows. Its squared norm is
\[
 x^*D_0x+\|t\|^2+\|P_Nx+Q_Nt\|^2 .
 \tag{HJR10}
\]
The minimum occurs at
$t=-(I+Q_N^*Q_N)^{-1}Q_N^*P_Nx$.
Completing the square leaves
$x^*D_0x+x^*P_N^*[I-Q_N(I+Q_N^*Q_N)^{-1}Q_N^*]P_Nx$.
Multiplication by $I+Q_NQ_N^*$ shows that the bracket is
$(I+Q_NQ_N^*)^{-1}$, proving HJR9. DNE7 proves that these
and only these lower evaluations are the permitted native
extension differences. Thus HJR10 is exactly its full
minimum, without separate minima on different row blocks.

\subsection{The original kernel and annihilator updates}

Write $B=B_K$ and $J=J_0$ for the actual maps in PEC19,24,
so $B$ is onto, $J$ is injective, and
$\operatorname{im}J=\ker B$. Define the full initial forms
and minimum lift by
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 D_{B,0}=(BD_0^{-1}B^*)^{-1},\qquad
 D_{J,0}=J^*D_0J,\\
 L_0=D_0^{-1}B^*D_{B,0},\qquad
 \mathsf W_0=J(D_{J,0})^{-1/2}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR11}
\]
Then $BL_0=I$, $B\mathsf W_0=0$,
$\mathsf W_0^*D_0\mathsf W_0=I$, and $\mathsf W_0^*D_0L_0=0$.
These are literal original kernel and annihilator maps;
the displayed $\mathsf W_0$ is a coefficient matrix for
$\ker B$ with its full $D_0$ metric.
The induced exact updates are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 D_{J,N}=D_{J,0}+(\mathcal C_NJ)^*(\mathcal C_NJ),\\
 D_{B,N}=D_{B,0}
 +(\mathcal C_NL_0)^*
  [I+\mathcal C_N\mathsf W_0\mathsf W_0^*\mathcal C_N^*]^{-1}
                                      (\mathcal C_NL_0).
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR12}
\]
The first is restriction of HJR9. For the second, every
$B$-lift of $y$ is $L_0y+\mathsf W_0t$. Its low squared
norm is $y^*D_{B,0}y+\|t\|^2$, and its added squared norm
is $\|\mathcal C_NL_0y+\mathcal C_N\mathsf W_0t\|^2$.
The same completion as HJR10 proves the second formula.
It preserves both the original lower-evaluation denominator
in $\mathcal C_N$ and the original kernel denominator in HJR12.
Empty source, kernel or target ranks use their unique empty
matrices and determinant one.

The exact identities DNE9 and PEC25 are
\[
 D_{B,N}=\overline{(\widehat H_{K,N}^{(s)})^{-1}},
 \qquad D_{J,N}=\overline{Q_{0,N}^{-1}}.
 \tag{HJR13}
\]
Thus HJR12 is a full original native kernel and complementary
residual calculation, rather than a freely chosen coefficient
compression.

\subsection{Exact one-row updates and their allocation}

The original orthonormal polynomial sequence is independent
of its finite cutoff. At any $M$ with $q-1\le M<2q$, let
$d=M-v$, and write $T,V$ for the complete matrices through
degree $d$. Their next rows, at degree $d+1$, are $t,v_+$.
Every entry of $t$ is
$\Lambda_{\mathcal N_a/E_A}(\phi_{d+1})$, computed by
HJR4 and the original monomial matrix $B_{s,d+1}$ through
degree $d+1$; every entry of $v_+$ is
$\phi_{d+1}(\beta')$. Set
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 A_M=V^*V,\quad K_M=A_M^{-1}V^*T,\quad
 r_M=t-v_+K_M,\\
 \lambda_M=1+v_+A_M^{-1}v_+^*>0,\qquad
 h_M=\lambda_M^{-1/2}r_M .
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR14}
\]
Then the exact update is
\[
 D_{M+1}=D_M+h_M^*h_M,\qquad D_M=\mathcal D_M^{(s)}.
 \tag{HJR15}
\]
To prove this, subtract the attained old minimum from the
new extension problem. The old residual is orthogonal to
all old lower evaluations, so the remaining minimization
for coefficient correction $\eta$ is
$\eta^*A_M\eta+\|r_Mx-v_+\eta\|^2$.
Its minimizer is
$(A_M+v_+^*v_+)^{-1}v_+^*r_Mx$.
The same identity used in HJR10 gives the remaining form
$r_M^*r_M/(1+v_+A_M^{-1}v_+^*)$, proving HJR15.
Every evaluation row has participated in the old $A_M$;
the update does not reset the lower-evaluation coefficient.

At this endpoint put
$D_{B,M}=(BD_M^{-1}B^*)^{-1}$,
$L_M=D_M^{-1}B^*D_{B,M}$ and $D_{J,M}=J^*D_MJ$.
The exact inverse-metric decomposition is
\[
 D_M^{-1}
 =J(D_{J,M})^{-1}J^*
          +L_M(D_{B,M})^{-1}L_M^* .
 \tag{HJR16}
\]
Indeed $[J,L_M]$ is a full coefficient frame whose Gram in
$D_M$ is $\operatorname{diag}(D_{J,M},D_{B,M})$.
The off-diagonal blocks vanish because $BJ=0$.
Inverting that frame Gram proves HJR16.
Define the two actual nonnegative scalar row energies
\[
 x_M=h_MJ(D_{J,M})^{-1}J^*h_M^*,\qquad
 y_M=h_ML_M(D_{B,M})^{-1}L_M^*h_M^* .
 \tag{HJR17}
\]
Both retain $\lambda_M^{-1}$ from HJR14 and every original
period-dependent kernel coordinate. The determinant lemma,
proved by extending a nonzero vector to a basis or by
expanding a rank-one determinant, now gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 g_M=\log\frac{\det D_{M+1}}{\det D_M}
                  =\log(1+x_M+y_M),\\
 g_{0,M}=\log\frac{\det D_{J,M+1}}{\det D_{J,M}}
                  =\log(1+x_M),\\
 g_{K,M}=\log\frac{\det D_{B,M+1}}{\det D_{B,M}}
                  =\log\left(1+\frac{y_M}{1+x_M}\right),\qquad
 g_M=g_{0,M}+g_{K,M}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR18}
\]
The restriction update gives the second equality directly.
For the quotient use the one-row instance of HJR12: its
added form is $(h_ML_M)^*(h_ML_M)/(1+x_M)$.
Its determinant relative to $D_{B,M}$ proves the third.
This proves the allocation of each full original row
increment between the actual kernel quotient and its
annihilator, with its complete positive denominator.

\subsection{The four original signs and an exterior bound at scale \(kq\)}

For $X$ equal to the full space, $K$, or $0$, define
\[
 I_{X,N}=\log\frac{\det D_{X,N}}{\det D_{X,q-1}},
 \qquad I_N=I_{{\rm full},N}.
 \tag{HJR19}
\]
All these numbers are nonnegative and increase with $N$.
At each $N$, completing $J$ by any fixed right inverse of
$B$ factors the full Gram into its restriction and quotient.
The fixed frame determinant cancels between endpoints, so
\[
 I_N=I_{K,N}+I_{0,N},\qquad
 I_{X,N}=\sum_{M=q-1}^{N-1}g_{X,M}.
 \tag{HJR20}
\]
In the sum for the full space $g_{{\rm full},M}=g_M$.
The source dual identities HJR13 therefore give exactly
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \widehat F_k^{(s)}
       =I_{K,2q-1}+I_{K,2q}-I_{K,q},\\
 F_{0,k,s}=I_{0,2q-1}+I_{0,2q}-I_{0,q},\\
 \mathcal T_{k,s}:=\widehat F_k^{(s)}+F_{0,k,s}
       =I_{2q-1}+I_{2q}-I_q.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR21}
\]
Equivalently every line is the weighted sum of its original
row increments, with weights
\[
 w_M=
 \begin{cases}
 1,&M=q-1\text{ or }M=2q-1,\\
 2,&q\le M\le2q-2.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{HJR22}
\]
For example $I_{2q-1}$ contains all rows through $2q-2$,
$I_{2q}$ adds the last row, and $I_q$ removes one copy of
the first. This proves all endpoint signs.
Writing $a=I_q$, $b=I_{2q-1}-I_q$ and
$c=I_{2q}-I_{2q-1}$ gives
$\mathcal T=a+2b+c$ with $a,b,c\ge0$. In particular
\[
 0\le I_N\le I_{2q}\le\mathcal T_{k,s}
                         \quad(q-1\le N\le2q).
 \tag{HJR23}
\]

To give its finite original bound, retain
$\delta_{K,k,s}=\widehat F_k^{(s)}-F_K^{(s)}$ with
$|\delta_{K,k,s}|\le\mathcal E_{k,s}^{\rm FEX}$,
and precisely $\delta_{0,k,s}$ and $F_{\Xi(L)}^{(s)}$
from PEC29. The latter is the original restriction return
called $F_{W_0}^{(s)}$ there.
OAR3 and PEC29 give the exact equality
\[
 \mathcal T_{k,s}
 =\mathcal F_{\partial,k}^{(s)}
    +\mathfrak e_{k,s}+\delta_{K,k,s}
            -F_{\Xi(L)}^{(s)}-\delta_{0,k,s}.
 \tag{HJR24}
\]
The original finite signs are consequently
\[
 \begin{split}
 \max\{0,\mathcal F_{\partial,k}^{(s)}
       -e_{k,s}^--\mathcal E_{k,s}^{\rm FEX}
       -E_{k,s}^{\rm tr}-(v-t_0)\mathcal L_s\}
 &\le\mathcal T_{k,s}\\
 &\le U_{k,s}^{\rm jet}
 :=\mathcal F_{\partial,k}^{(s)}
       +e_{k,s}^++\mathcal E_{k,s}^{\rm FEX}+E_{k,s}^{\rm tr}.
 \end{split}\tag{HJR25}
\]
All errors on the right and left are the explicit accepted
FEX, OAR and PEC expressions. The inequality uses
$0\le F_{\Xi(L)}^{(s)}\le(v-t_0)\mathcal L_s$ and
$|\delta_0|\le E^{\rm tr}$ only after retaining their
actual values in HJR24. In particular
\[
 \mathcal T_{k,s}
       =\Delta q\mathfrak C_\partial+o(kq),\qquad
 U_{k,s}^{\rm jet}
       =\Delta q\mathfrak C_\partial+o(kq).
 \tag{HJR26}
\]
This follows from the fully proved OFV/OVR outer first
variation and the stated orders of the finite errors.
The actual period remains fixed; no uniformity over a
moving period is asserted.

Now take any paired restriction, quotient, or subquotient
of the common coefficient space at the two metrics
$(D_0,D_N)$. Let its rank be $t$ and its determinant
increment be $I_{t,N}$. These are attained restrictions
and quotients along the same algebraic flag, retaining
all its complete metrics. Since $D_N\ge D_0$, every
such form increases. Full flag factorization, with
nonnegative log increments on both complementary flags,
proves
\[
 0\le I_{t,N}\le I_N\le U_{k,s}^{\rm jet}=O_{\rm actual}(kq).
 \tag{HJR27}
\]
For the same reason all generalized eigenvalues for
$(D_N,D_0)$ are at least one; the sum of all their
logarithms is $I_N$. Hence every exterior degree of the
identity between these two metrics has norm at most
$\exp(U_{k,s}^{\rm jet}/2)$. The same statement holds
on every specified paired flag. This is a full exterior
bound with no additional rank multiplier and with the
complete lower-evaluation and kernel conditioning retained.

\subsection{Exact cancellation of the low leading scale}

Retain LJR's actual finite remainder $\mathscr R_{t,k,s}$,
including its full-space formula LJR28 and the two-sided
flag bounds LJR31. Keep the fixed $G_{\exp}$ quotient
or restriction associated with this same flag.
Then HJR19 gives the exact identity at every endpoint
\[
 \log\frac{\det G_t}{\det D_{t,N}}
       =2tq\log k+\mathscr R_{t,k,s}-I_{t,N}.
 \tag{HJR28}
\]
HJR27 therefore propagates the same low leading scale
$2tq\log k$ to all four endpoints, with remainder
$O_{\rm actual}(kq)$. It does so by the recurrence-compatible
graph and the full Schur minima, not by assigning the
later free jets the low dimension.

Let $p_{t,N},n_{t,N}$ be PEC's two nonnegative logarithmic
spectral parts for $(D_{t,N},G_t)$. The same identity is
\[
 n_{t,N}
   =2tq\log k+\mathscr R_{t,k,s}-I_{t,N}+p_{t,N},
 \qquad 0\le p_{t,N}\le\varepsilon_{N,s}^{\vee}=o(kq).
 \tag{HJR29}
\]
At the actual kernel quotient and annihilator restriction,
write $X=K,0$ respectively. Substituting HJR29 with the
four original signs cancels the four copies of both
$2tq\log k$ and $\mathscr R_{t,k,s}$ exactly and gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 A_{X,k,s}
       =I_{X,2q-1}+I_{X,2q}-I_{X,q}-P_{X,k,s},\\
 P_{X,k,s}=p_{X,2q-1}+p_{X,2q}-p_{X,q-1}-p_{X,q},\qquad
 0\le P_{X,k,s}\le E_{k,s}^{\vee}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR30}
\]
The signs of the $p$ terms follow by substituting their
displayed positions in HJR29. Their monotonicity and
finite bound are PEC21,26 on these same maps.
Together with HJR18 and HJR22 this supplies the explicit
original calculation to carry forward:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 A_{K,k,s}
   =\sum_{M=q-1}^{2q-1}w_M
          \log\left(1+\frac{y_M}{1+x_M}\right)-P_{K,k,s},\\
 A_{0,k,s}
   =\sum_{M=q-1}^{2q-1}w_M\log(1+x_M)-P_{0,k,s}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR31}
\]
The same original $x_M,y_M$ sum in the full row energy
$\log(1+x_M+y_M)$. Every numerator, denominator and
moment used to compute these scalars is fixed in
HJR2--4,HJR14,HJR16--17. Their weighted total has the
proved leading value HJR26; their separate allocation is
retained by the exact formulas HJR31.
Thus the next-order $kq$ problem is the specified allocation
of these original Gamma row energies, with all low
factorial and Vandermonde terms canceled only through
the exact equality HJR28.

\paragraph{The two actual row allocations in the original Gamma orders.}
Keep the original base degree $a=4l+1\ge9$, $q_a=(a+1)^2$,
the standard endpoints $N_i=q_a-1,q_a,2q_a-1,2q_a$ and
$d_i=N_i-v$. This paragraph uses the degree-$a$ standard
window, not a degree-$k$ source on a degree-$j$ target window.
Let $\Omega_a^{[a],\rm lo/hi}$ mean the full NSR2 constants
at these $d_i$. The exact ranks are
$t_a=8a-16-t_{0,a}$ and $j_{0,a}=8a-32-v+t_{0,a}$.
Then
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 S_{K,a}^{(s)}
  =\sum_{M=q_a-1}^{2q_a-1}w_M
                \log\left(1+\frac{y_M^{(s)}}{1+x_M^{(s)}}\right)
  =\widehat F_a^{(s)},\\
 S_{R,a}^{(s)}
  =\sum_{M=q_a-1}^{2q_a-1}w_M\log(1+x_M^{(s)})
  =F_{0,a,s},\qquad
 S_{K,a}^{(s)}+S_{R,a}^{(s)}=\mathcal T_{a,s}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR32}
\]
The row $h_M$ in HJR14 still contains
$(1+v_+(V^*V)^{-1}v_+^*)^{-1/2}$ and the full old
lower projection. The inverse-metric decomposition HJR16
in the full frame $[J,L_M]$ proves the two scalar energies;
HJR18 gives their determinant increments. Telescoping with
the original weights $w_M=1$ at the extremes and $2$ inside
proves HJR32, including all zero-energy rows.
Applying the full NSO same-fibre comparison to these exact
restriction and quotient values now gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 -t_a\Omega_a^{[a],\rm hi}
 \le S_{K,a}^{(a)}-S_{K,a}^{(1)}
 \le t_a\Omega_a^{[a],\rm lo},\\
 -j_{0,a}\Omega_a^{[a],\rm hi}
 \le S_{R,a}^{(a)}-S_{R,a}^{(1)}
 \le j_{0,a}\Omega_a^{[a],\rm lo}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR33}
\]
The ranks belong to the final coefficient quotient and
restriction; the full raw polynomial plane has not been
assigned these ranks. The common mass is retained before
the signed return, so these order differences are
$O_{\rm actual}(a^2\log q_a)=o(aq_a)$.

At the actual target degree $a=j=k+4$, the original
fourfold quotient flag of CPQ2--8 further gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 4S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}=V_I+V_b+V_t+V_{\rm rem},\\
 4S_{R,j}^{(s_1)}
       =4\mathcal T_{j,s_1}-V_I-V_b-V_t-V_{\rm rem},\\
 4S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}
       =\mathcal L_e+V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A .
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR34}
\]
The first line is the full target Gram factorization with
all its fixed frame determinants before the return;
the second subtracts it from four copies of HJR32.
For the third use
$\mathcal L_e=V_I+V_t-V_s-V_A$. Every positive quotient
has its literal four-row formula CPQ14--18. This transports
the same original row allocation into the connecting and
invariant calculation without assigning any individual
large-row concentration or leading coefficient.

\paragraph{The common-angle interval returned to the two original allocations.}
At the same target degree $j=k+4$, $k=4l+1\ge9$, retain the
original two source orders and every quotient of HJR34.
Put $Z_e=V_b+V_{\rm rem}+V_s+V_A$. Each summand is its
complete attained four-endpoint return: the respective spaces
are $W/W_i$, $\mathcal U/T$, $S/K_c$ and $I/K_i$.
The exact value maps and frames are CPQ4--8; in particular the

Let $L_-,L_+$ be the explicit SFC9--10 expressions of the

Define
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 K_-^{(s_1)}=\max\{0,(L_-+Z_e)/4\},\\
 K_+^{(s_1)}=\min\{\mathcal T_{j,s_1},
                  U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native},(L_++Z_e)/4\},\\
 K_-^{(s_1)}\le S_{K,j}^{(s_1)}\le K_+^{(s_1)},\qquad
 \mathcal T_{j,s_1}-K_+^{(s_1)}\le S_{R,j}^{(s_1)}
                          \le\mathcal T_{j,s_1}-K_-^{(s_1)}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{HJR35}
\]
The $U^{\rm native}$ entry is used only on the original RQB35

the bounds before intersection. Positivity of both allocations,
their exact total HJR32 and the applicable native cap prove
the stated intersection. Subtracting from that same total
reverses the bounds and proves the residual interval.
No summand of $Z_e$ is removed. HJR33 then intersects the two
order intervals with the asymmetric translations displayed
and proved in SFC19. This gives the complete finite receiving

\paragraph{The refined comparison returned to both complete allocations.}
Keep the full $Z_e^p=V_b^p+V_{\rm rem}^p+V_s^p+V_A^p$
in SFC18 and HJR35. Replace their common-angle endpoints by the
proved EFR4 endpoints and define
\[
\widehat K_-^p=\max\{0,(\widetilde L_-^p+Z_e^p)/4\},\quad
\widehat K_+^p=\min\{\mathcal T_j^p,U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native},
                     (\widetilde L_+^p+Z_e^p)/4\}.
\]
The original cap appears only on its original proved domain.
The exact affine identity
$4S_{K,j}^p=\mathcal L_e^p+Z_e^p$
proves this enclosure. The quotient
$H_{B,j,D}=(B_jD_{j,D}^{-1}B_j^*)^{-1}$ has rank $t_j$,
while the restriction $J_j^*D_{j,D}J_j$ has rank $j_{0,j}$.
Their original inverse-metric return identities are HJR32.
Applying EFP16 to both and using
$\Delta S_K+\Delta S_R=\Delta\mathcal T_j$ gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
A_K^-&=\max\{t_j\Phi_j^-,\Delta\mathcal T_j-j_{0,j}\Phi_j^+\},\\
A_K^+&=\min\{t_j\Phi_j^+,\Delta\mathcal T_j-j_{0,j}\Phi_j^-\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFR5}
\]
Thus the complete replacement for the NSR-width translation in
SFC19 is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\max\{\widehat K_-^1,\widehat K_-^0+A_K^-\}
 &\le S_{K,j}^1
 \le\min\{\widehat K_+^1,\widehat K_+^0+A_K^+\},\\
\max\{\widehat K_-^0,\widehat K_-^1-A_K^+\}
 &\le S_{K,j}^0
 \le\min\{\widehat K_+^0,\widehat K_+^1-A_K^-\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFR6}
\]
Adding the actual difference interval to pair zero and subtracting
it from pair one prove the two lines. Subtraction in reversed
endpoint order from each same-pair $\mathcal T_j^p$ gives the
residual intervals. Every low correction and all four positive
returns remain; no individual allocation is prescribed.

% Exact bounded-invariant multiplier in the original dual Gamma metrics.
\section{Native transport of the bounded original invariant multipliers}
\label{sec:MNT}

Keep the complete original fixed quartet, period, branch, conductor
and Gamma measures. Use the actual BIM1 family $\mathcal H$ on
its proved domain $k\equiv1\pmod4$, $k\ge37$.
For $h\in\mathcal H$ put $d_h=\deg h=4r_h$ and
$\ell_h=k-d_h\ge9$. The earlier finite degrees keep their
original matrices. The two source-order pairs used below are
exactly
\[
 (s_{\ell_h},s_k)=(1,1)\quad\hbox{or}\quad(\ell_h,k),\qquad
 c'_j=j/2-4,\qquad D\ge q_k-v-1,\quad
 q_j=(j+1)^2,\quad q'_j=(j-7)^2 .
 \tag{MNT1}
\]
The polynomial cutoff $D$ is the same integer in the source
and target of each map. Its relation to their different
original endpoint windows is calculated below.

\subsection{The literal finite translation sum and its two centres}

Use every actual coefficient $h_{ab}$ from BIM5 and set
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 B_h(z)=\mathcal N_h(z)
        =\sum_{a,b=0}^{d_h}h_{ab}e^{\beta_{d_h,a,b}z},\\
 \beta_{d,a,b}=d/2+(2a-d)\delta+i(2b-d)\gamma,\qquad
 \eta_{h,a,b}=\beta_{d_h,a,b}-d_h/2 .
 \end{gathered}\tag{MNT2}
\]
For $h=1$ the sole coefficient is one, $d_h=0$, and
$B_h=1$. All other arrays are the literal original
multinomial coefficients of $h(\mathsf Ht)$.
Multiplication of numerators has the exact dual map
\[
 \mathcal A_h:\mathbb C[S']_{\le D}\longrightarrow
                         \mathbb C[S']_{\le D},\qquad
 (\mathcal A_hp)(S')=\sum_{a,b}h_{ab}p(S'+\beta_{d_h,a,b}),
 \qquad
 \Lambda_{B_hF}(p)=\Lambda_F(\mathcal A_hp).
 \tag{MNT3}
\]
To prove the identity apply both sides to $S'^n$.
Leibniz' rule on $(B_hF)^{(n)}(0)$ gives precisely
$\sum_{a,b}h_{ab}\sum_{j=0}^n\binom nj
\beta_{d_h,a,b}^{\,n-j}F^{(j)}(0)$, the right side.
All germs used here are analytic at zero: the individual
source domain is the complete original low-jet subspace
$\mathscr N_{v,\ell_h}$, so $F=\mathcal N/E_A$ is analytic.
This does not assign a functional to an individual summand
outside that subspace in BIM's larger coupled domain.

For $p_k(y)=p(c'_k+iy)$ the two original centres satisfy
$c'_k-c'_{\ell_h}=d_h/2$. Thus the exact pullback is
\[
 (\mathcal A_hp)(c'_{\ell_h}+iy)
    =\sum_{a,b}h_{ab}\,
                   p_k(y+\eta_{h,a,b}/i).
 \tag{MNT4}
\]
This retains the phase $1/i=-i$ and the full center difference.
Let $H_D=\sum_{j=1}^D1/j$, with $H_0=0$, and put
\[
 K_h(D)=\sum_{a,b}|h_{ab}|
               \exp\bigl(|\eta_{h,a,b}|(H_D+2)\bigr).
 \tag{MNT5}
\]
AKJ21--23 proves
$\|\partial_y\|_{\le D,s}\le H_D+2$ for every original
source order $s\ge1$: its ratio estimate uses only $s/2\ge1/2$.
The exact finite Taylor identity
$p(y+z)=\exp(z\partial_y)p(y)$ therefore gives
$\|p(y+z)\|_s\le e^{|z|(H_D+2)}\|p\|_s$.
Apply this to each term of MNT4 and then use the triangle
inequality. For either unchanged common source order this
proves the norm bound $K_h(D)$ between the displayed centres.
In particular
$|\eta_{h,a,b}|\le d_h\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}$ and
$H_D\le1+\log D$ for $D\ge1$. Thus $K_h(D)$ is a
fully specified polynomial bound in $D+1$ at the fixed
actual data and degree $d_h\le28$.

\subsection{The complete source-order change}

For the degree-order pair put $s=\ell_h$ and $r=r_h$.
The full original measure is
\[
 dm_s(y)=
 \frac{M_s2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
       |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,\qquad M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}.
 \tag{MNT6}
\]
The Gamma recurrence, applied exactly $r$ times, proves
\[
 \frac{dm_{s+4r}}{dm_s}(y)
   =\frac{(2\pi)^{2r}}{(s/2)_{2r}}
         \prod_{j=0}^{r-1}\bigl(y^2+(s/2+2j)^2\bigr)
   \ge a_{s,r}:=(2\pi)^{2r}
                 \prod_{j=0}^{r-1}\frac{s+4j}{s+4j+2}>0 .
 \tag{MNT7}
\]
For detail the squared Gamma ratio is
$4^{-r}\prod_j(y^2+(s/2+2j)^2)$.
The factor $2^{(s+4r)/2}/2^{s/2}=4^r$ cancels it,
the mass ratio is $(2\pi)^{2r}$, and the remaining
denominator ratio is $(s/2)_{2r}$.
Pairing its consecutive factors proves the displayed
minimum. For $r=0$ every product and the constant equal one.
This computation retains the actual full source mass.

Define
\[
 \kappa_h(D)=
 \begin{cases}
 K_h(D),&(s_{\ell_h},s_k)=(1,1),\\
 K_h(D)a_{\ell_h,r_h}^{-1/2},
                         &(s_{\ell_h},s_k)=(\ell_h,k).
 \end{cases}
 \tag{MNT8}
\]
First apply MNT4--5 in order $s_{\ell_h}$. Then use
$\|p_k\|_{s_{\ell_h}}\le a_{\ell_h,r_h}^{-1/2}
\|p_k\|_{s_k}$, which follows by integrating MNT7.
This proves the complete primal bound
\[
 \|\mathcal A_hp\|_{s_{\ell_h},c'_{\ell_h}}
        \le\kappa_h(D)\|p\|_{s_k,c'_k}.
 \tag{MNT9}
\]
Since $\ell_h\ge9$ and $r_h\le7$, the positive factors
$a_{\ell_h,r_h}$ are bounded above and away from zero
by explicit constants obtained by replacing $\ell_h$
in each ratio of MNT7 by infinity or by nine.
Together with MNT5 this yields
$\log\max\{1,\kappa_h(D)\}=O_{\rm actual}(\log(D+1))$,
uniformly over the finite family and both source-order choices.

\subsection{Every original lower evaluation in the quotient map}

Let $\mathscr E_j$ denote the complete original
degree-$j$ exponential space. BIM7 gives
\[
 B_h\mathscr N_{v,\ell_h}\subset\mathscr N_{v,k},\qquad
 B_h(E_A\mathscr E_{\ell_h-8})
                       \subset E_A\mathscr E_{k-8}.
 \tag{MNT10}
\]
The first inclusion follows by multiplying an analytic
numerator with its first $v$ zero jets by the entire $B_h$.
The second is the exact root-addition identity in BIM6--7.
It follows that multiplication induces the typed map
\[
 \mathsf M_h:
   \mathscr N_{v,\ell_h}/E_A\mathscr E_{\ell_h-8}
      \longrightarrow
   \mathscr N_{v,k}/E_A\mathscr E_{k-8},\qquad
 [\mathcal N]\longmapsto[B_h\mathcal N].
 \tag{MNT11}
\]
Its kernel is exactly the source classes whose product
belongs to $E_A\mathscr E_{k-8}$. No assertion that this
kernel vanishes is needed or used.
The polynomial and low-jet multiplication kernels in BIM
and the full nonreduced component kernels in KRC remain
the same kernels after this explicit coordinate map.

At the common polynomial degree $D$, use the full original
minimum extension metric $D_{j,D}^{(s_j)}$ on the quotient
in degree $j$, defined by
\[
 \|[\mathcal N]\|_{j,D}^{\,2}
   =\min_{\eta\in\mathbb C^{q'_j}}
       \left\|\Lambda_{\mathcal N/E_A}
                    +\sum_{\beta'\in{\rm grid}(j-8)}
                          \eta_{\beta'}\operatorname{ev}_{\beta'}
       \right\|_{(\mathbb C[S']_{\le D},
                             \|\cdot\|_{s_j,c'_j})^\vee}^{\,2}.
 \tag{MNT12}
\]
The first complete jet window has $D\ge q_j-v-1$ here,
so DNE's Vandermonde and relation-annihilator argument
proves strict positivity and the exact quotient kernel.
Its proof applies at every larger finite $D$; it makes
no use of an upper cutoff.

Choose the attained source minimum in MNT12.
Precompose its functional by $\mathcal A_h$.
Its first term becomes $\Lambda_{B_h\mathcal N/E_A}$
by MNT3. Every old lower evaluation becomes the full
sum of target lower evaluations with roots
$\beta'_{\ell_h-8}+\beta_{d_h,a,b}$ by MNT3 and BIM6.
Thus this is an allowed target extension, with all its
coefficients retained. The target minimum is no larger.
The dual form of MNT9 then proves
\[
 \|\mathsf M_h[\mathcal N]\|_{k,D}
       \le\kappa_h(D)\|[\mathcal N]\|_{\ell_h,D}.
 \tag{MNT13}
\]
This proves the metric transport through the complete
original lower-evaluation denominators.
It neither resets those coefficients nor minimizes
different row blocks independently.

For exact native coordinates let
$\mathsf T_j=\alpha_A N_jZ_{\exp,j}$ be KRC5,
where $\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v$ is the same constant for
all degrees because $E_A$ is fixed.
The DNE coefficient matrix of MNT11 is
$\mathsf T_k^{-1}M_h^{\rm strip}\mathsf T_{\ell_h}$.
Equivalently multiply the original upper coefficient
array by $h_{ab}$ and reduce by the full target
$C_k^{\mathsf T}$ to the $Z_{\exp,k}$ coordinates.
The two factors $\alpha_A$ cancel only in this
conjugation of the exact multiplication map.
If one instead uses the scaled FGC dual metric,
both corresponding Grams acquire $|\alpha_A|^{-2}$,
so MNT13 remains the same inequality by that proved
common scalar map.

\subsection{The finite family reaches the native kernel image}

Restrict each source of MNT11 to its actual invariant
low-jet part $\mathscr J_{v,\ell_h}$, using precisely
the DNE matrix $J_{\ell_h}$ and native form
$J_{\ell_h}^*D_{\ell_h,D}J_{\ell_h}$.
Their direct sum has its full block diagonal native
Gram $\mathcal G_D$.
Let $\Phi_k$ be the complete coefficient matrix
of the sum of these restricted maps, and let
$\Psi_k=B_k\Phi_k$ be its composition with the actual
onto kernel-dual map. Put
\[
 \mathcal K(D)=\left(\sum_{h\in\mathcal H}\kappa_h(D)^2\right)^{1/2}.
 \tag{MNT14}
\]
Applying MNT13, the triangle inequality in the one
target Hilbert space and then Cauchy--Schwarz gives
$\|\Phi_kx\|_{k,D}\le\mathcal K(D)\sqrt{x^*\mathcal G_Dx}$.
Taking the further $B_k$ quotient can only decrease
the target norm, so the same bound holds for $\Psi_k$.
Its exact image has codimension at most the full
BIM11 defect $e_k\le(|\mathcal H|-1)v$:
the quotient of the target by that image is an image
of the full coefficient quotient by $\operatorname{im}\Phi_k$.
This is the precise native use of the individually
constrained image. No analytic germ was assigned to
the larger coupled tuple's nonanalytic summands.

To specify both metrics on this actual image, choose
an injective frame $U_k$ of $\operatorname{im}\Psi_k$
and its exact left inverse $L_{U_k}$, and set
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \widetilde\Psi_k=L_{U_k}\Psi_k,\qquad
 Q_D^{\rm source}
    =(\widetilde\Psi_k\mathcal G_D^{-1}
                              \widetilde\Psi_k^*)^{-1},\\
 H_D^{\rm image}
     =U_k^*(B_kD_{k,D}^{-1}B_k^*)^{-1}U_k,\qquad
 H_D^{\rm image}\preceq\mathcal K(D)^2Q_D^{\rm source}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{MNT15}
\]
The matrix $\widetilde\Psi_k$ is onto. Its minimum
source lift has exactly the first displayed Gram,
by the complete Hilbert quotient formula proved in
KRC19. Apply the preceding norm inequality to that
attained lift to obtain MNT15.
This specifies the exact source quotient and target
image metrics. Every exterior degree has norm at most
$\max\{1,\mathcal K(D)\}^{\operatorname{rank}U_k}$ for
this identity, since each singular value is at most
$\mathcal K(D)$. On $D=O(q_k)$ its logarithm is
$O_{\rm actual}(k\log q_k)=o(kq_k)$.
This is an upper expansion bound for the specified
operator; no inverse bound is inferred from it.

At the four original target cutoffs, the missing
kernel quotient in MNT15 has rank at most $e_k$.
Its full native determinant return is bounded by
FRE10 with that rank, so the complete kernel return
differs from the return of $H_D^{\rm image}$ by a
nonnegative $O_{\rm actual}(q_k\log k)=o(kq_k)$ term.
This uses the actual image and its remaining quotient
and does not assume evenly distributed row energies.

\subsection{The exact displaced source endpoints}

The source polynomial cutoff in every preceding map
is the literal target cutoff $D=N-v$. For each $h$ define
\[
 b_{h,k}=q_k-q_{\ell_h}
            =d_h(2k-d_h+2).
 \tag{MNT16}
\]
Thus the four source endpoint indices used in MNT12
are exactly
\[
 q_{\ell_h}-1+b_{h,k},\quad
 q_{\ell_h}+b_{h,k},\quad
 2q_{\ell_h}-1+2b_{h,k},\quad
 2q_{\ell_h}+2b_{h,k}.
 \tag{MNT17}
\]
No source cutoff has been replaced by its degree's
usual endpoint. For any fixed source flag, write
$F_h(N)=\log\det D_{X,\ell_h,N-v}$ and define the
native positive return using its original signs on
the dual metric. Its shifted return minus its usual
return is exactly
\[
 \begin{split}
 &[F_h(2q_{\ell_h}-1+2b_{h,k})-F_h(2q_{\ell_h}-1)]
  +[F_h(2q_{\ell_h}+2b_{h,k})-F_h(2q_{\ell_h})]\\
 &\quad-[F_h(q_{\ell_h}-1+b_{h,k})-F_h(q_{\ell_h}-1)]
       -[F_h(q_{\ell_h}+b_{h,k})-F_h(q_{\ell_h})].
 \end{split}\tag{MNT18}
\]
This is direct subtraction of the two four-endpoint
expressions. Each bracket is an actual nonnegative
sum of source row increments, by the same minimum
extension monotonicity used in MNT12.
The exact signed combination retains their potential
concentration. No subleading order for MNT18 is asserted.

There is likewise an exact finite relative operator
on the matched image,
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathscr R_D=(Q_D^{\rm source})^{-1/2}
                    H_D^{\rm image}(Q_D^{\rm source})^{-1/2},\\
 0\prec\mathscr R_D\preceq\mathcal K(D)^2I,\qquad
 \log\det H_D^{\rm image}
    =\log\det Q_D^{\rm source}+\log\det\mathscr R_D .
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{MNT19}
\]
Conjugating MNT15 proves the inequality and taking
determinants proves the equality. The native image's
four-return therefore contains the same four signed
relative determinants, with high endpoints positive
for the dual return and low endpoints negative.
Their negative spectral parts and the displaced
source windows MNT18 remain exact finite quantities.
The proof supplies the actual bounded-degree native
operator, its polynomial upper expansion, and the
subleading unmatched quotient. It does not assign a
value to the remaining matched relative determinant
by omitting either of these quantities.

This calculation uses the full NBG1--32 and NKC1--17 native maps,
the DFX1--35 degree-four multiplication and branch kernels, and the
KRC1--27 original annihilator coordinates. It addresses the further
target-invariant quotient in NBG30--32. Neither its kernel nor its

\section{Original spaces, maps and two positive forms}

Fix the actual simple quartet and its proved five-orbit period data.
Let $k=4l+1\ge9$, $0\le v=\operatorname{ord}_0 E_A\le80$,
$q_j=(j+1)^2$, $q'_j=(j-7)^2$, and
$D\ge q_{k+4}-v-1$. Retain precisely
\[
 (s_0,s_1)=(1,1)\ \text{or}\ (k,k+4),\qquad
 c'_j=j/2-4,
 \quad
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
       |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy.                 \tag{ITL1}
\]
The full original native dual Grams are
$D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}$ and $D_{k+4,D}^{(s_1)}$. They are the
attained minima over all original lower evaluations in MNT12;
the lower coefficients are not minimized separately across jet rows.
Use the exact KRC/DFX matrices
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 J_0=\mathbf J_k,\quad J_1=\mathbf J_{k+4},\quad
 B_1=\mathbf B_{k+4},\quad \ker B_1=\im J_1,\\
 M_i=(\alpha_A N_{k+4}Z_{\exp,k+4})^{-1}
              m_{i,k}(\alpha_A N_kZ_{\exp,k}),\quad
 \alpha_A=v!/\mu_v,\quad m_{i,k}[f]=[x_i^4f],\\
 G=J_0^*D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}J_0,\quad
 \mathcal D=\diag(D_{k+4,D}^{(s_1)},\ldots,D_{k+4,D}^{(s_1)}),\\
 F=\begin{bmatrix}M_1J_0\\M_2J_0\\M_3J_0\\M_4J_0\end{bmatrix},\quad
 \mathcal B=\diag(B_1,B_1,B_1,B_1),\quad
 W=\diag(J_1,J_1,J_1,J_1),\quad X=\mathcal B F .
                                                        \end{gathered} \tag{ITL2}
\]
All stars in metric calculations are complex adjoints. The ordinary
transposes inside the original $\mathbf B_j,\mathbf J_j$ definitions
are still those of complex-linear duality and are not changed.
The scalar $\alpha_A$ cancels only in the displayed conjugation for
$M_i$. The two source masses in ITL1 are retained in $G,\mathcal D$.

Let $V=\mathbb C^{j_{0,k}}$ be the invariant source coordinates.
The map $F$ is injective: NBG21--27 proves that the four original
target conductor minima give a strictly positive pullback on the
full conductor quotient, hence on this invariant restriction.
Equivalently DFX21 proves the full conductor tuple's injectivity
before its target-invariant quotient. Set
\[
 A=F^*\mathcal D F\succ0,\qquad
 E=\ker X=\{a\in V:Fa\in\im W\}.                 \tag{ITL3}
\]
The equality follows from $\ker\mathcal B=\im W$ in ITL2.
It is the exact NBG30 kernel, not an assumption of injectivity
after the invariant quotient.

Choose fixed full-column matrices $Z_E,Z_R$ such that
$Z_E$ is a basis of $E$ and $Z=[Z_E,Z_R]$ is an invertible
basis of $V$. Write $e=\dim E$, $r=\dim V-e$.
All choices are independent of $D$. Put
\[
 F_E=FZ_E,\quad F_R=FZ_R,\quad U=XZ_R .             \tag{ITL4}
\]
Then $U$ is an injective frame of $\im X$. For any positive form
$P$ on $V$ define its quotient form in these same lifts by
\[
 P/E=Z_R^*PZ_R-Z_R^*PZ_E(Z_E^*PZ_E)^{-1}Z_E^*PZ_R.
                                                        \tag{ITL5}
\]
When $e=0$ the subtracted block is zero. This is the original
minimum over $Z_Rb+E$: completing its square proves ITL5,
with its unique least-norm representative. Let $Q=G/E$ and
$A_E=A/E$. The matched target form is
\[
 H=U^*(\mathcal B\mathcal D^{-1}\mathcal B^*)^{-1}U .
                                                        \tag{ITL6}
\]
The inverse in ITL6 is exactly the four copies of $H_{B,D}$
from NBG30. Thus $Q,H$ are the NBG31 two image metrics, in
the particular fixed image frame $U=XZ_R$. If its earlier
frame is $U_{\mathcal X}$, write $U=U_{\mathcal X}C$ with
the unique invertible coordinate matrix $C$. Both earlier
Grams are conjugated by $C$; their determinant ratio and
generalized eigenvalues are unchanged by this proved common
coordinate map. No new metric has been selected.

\section{An explicit original coefficient determinant for the target loss}

For a full-column matrix $Y$ in the target product, let
\[
 P_Y=Y(Y^*\mathcal D Y)^{-1}Y^*\mathcal D             \tag{ITL7}
\]
be its $\mathcal D$-orthogonal projection. If $Y$ is empty,
$P_Y=0$ and its Gram determinant is one. Choose any fixed
coefficient complement $W_R$ such that
$\widehat W=[F_E,W_R]$ is a basis of $\im W$.
It exists since $F$ is injective and $F_E\subset\im W$.
This complement keeps every other original invariant direction.
Set
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 Y=(I-P_{F_E})F_R,\qquad Z_T=(I-P_{F_E})W_R,\\
 A_E=Y^*\mathcal D Y,\quad C_T=Z_T^*\mathcal D Z_T,\quad
 L=Z_T^*\mathcal D Y,\\
 H=A_E-L^*C_T^{-1}L .                              \end{gathered} \tag{ITL8}
\]
The first Gram equality follows from ITL5 with $P=A$.
For the last equality, all target lifts of $Ub$ are precisely
$F_Rb+\im W$. Indeed $\mathcal B(F_Rb+w)=Ub$ if and only
if $w\in\ker\mathcal B=\im W$. Minimize first over $F_E$
and then over $W_R$; completing the two squares gives ITL8.
Since $F_Rb\in\im W$ implies $Z_Rb\in E$, it forces $b=0$.
Consequently $H\succ0$. Also $C_T\succ0$ if its rank is
nonzero: a linear combination of $W_R$ projecting into $F_E$
would contradict the complement. These statements justify
every inverse and include their empty-rank conventions.

Define the additional target-invariant loss matrix
\[
 \Omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}
   =A_E^{-1/2}H A_E^{-1/2}
   =I-A_E^{-1/2}L^*C_T^{-1}L A_E^{-1/2},\qquad
                    0\prec\Omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}\preceq I .
                                                        \tag{ITL9}
\]
The square roots only express the identity map between its two
specified Hilbert coordinate spaces. ITL8 is its unaltered
coefficient formula. In particular
\[
 \begin{split}
 \omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}:=\det\Omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}
 &=\frac{\det\begin{pmatrix}C_T&L\\L^*&A_E\end{pmatrix}}
          {\det C_T\det A_E}\\
 &=\frac{\det([\widehat W,F_R]^*\mathcal D[\widehat W,F_R])
              \det(F_E^*\mathcal D F_E)}
          {\det(\widehat W^*\mathcal D\widehat W)
              \det([F_E,F_R]^*\mathcal D[F_E,F_R])}>0 .
                                                        \end{split} \tag{ITL10}
\]
To prove the second line, eliminate the $F_E$ block from the
three indicated complete Grams. Their Schur blocks are
respectively the block in the first line, $C_T$, and $A_E$.
The determinant of the $F_E$ block appears once on each
required side and gives precisely ITL10. No normalization
of $\widehat W$ is used. Changing its complement or the
fixed source lifts changes the adapted block determinants
by the same squared determinant factors, which cancel in
the displayed ratio, or leaves the minimum forms unchanged.

For a direct finite spectral expression put
$T_D=C_T^{-1/2}L A_E^{-1/2}$. The singular values
$\rho_i(T_D)$ lie in $[0,1)$ by ITL9, and
\[
 -\log\omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}
       =\sum_i-\log(1-\rho_i(T_D)^2).             \tag{ITL11}
\]
Zero singular values contribute zero. This is the precise
remaining invariant-target loss, evaluated after the complete
old lower gauges; it is not the earlier NBG independent-gauge
loss and it is not identified with a rank fraction.

\section{Return of both lower-gauge corrections, including the actual kernel}

Let $C_D$ be NBG27's correlated pullback on the invariant
source $V$. It is obtained by restricting the full correlated
native norm to the original $J_0$; no independent target
invariant quotient is taken in it. NBG23 and 27 prove
\[
 K_-^{-2}G\preceq C_D\preceq K_+^2G,\qquad
                       0\prec A\preceq C_D.       \tag{ITL12}
\]
Retain exactly the original constants NBG12. For the two
fixed restrictions $V$ and $E$ define
\[
 \omega_{V,D}=\frac{\det A}{\det C_D},\qquad
 \omega_{E,D}=\frac{\det(Z_E^*AZ_E)}
                        {\det(Z_E^*C_DZ_E)} .      \tag{ITL13}
\]
These are the NBG29 restriction losses $\det\Omega_{V,D}$
and $\det\Omega_{E,D}$, respectively. The zero-dimensional
restriction has determinant one.
For every positive $P$, the block determinant in the fixed
frame $Z$ gives
\[
 \det(P/E)=\frac{|\det Z|^2\det P}{\det(Z_E^*PZ_E)}.
                                                        \tag{ITL14}
\]
It follows in particular that
\[
 \frac{\det(A/E)}{\det(C_D/E)}
       =\frac{\omega_{V,D}}{\omega_{E,D}}\le1 .   \tag{ITL15}
\]
The inequality also follows by minimizing ITL12 over the
same affine fibres; it is not inferred from the separate
inequalities $\omega_V,\omega_E\le1$ alone.

Let $\mathscr R_{X,D}=Q^{-1/2}H Q^{-1/2}$, the exact
matched relative operator NBG32, and put
$e_{\mathrm{cor},D}=\log\det(C_D/E)-\log\det(G/E)$.
Combining ITL8--15 proves the exact identity
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \log\det\mathscr R_{X,D}
   =e_{\mathrm{cor},D}
      +\log\omega_{V,D}-\log\omega_{E,D}
      +\log\omega_{\mathrm{inv},D} .\quad}        \tag{ITL16}
\]
Quotienting the first pair of inequalities in ITL12 proves
the explicit finite estimate
\[
 -2r\log\max(1,K_-)\le e_{\mathrm{cor},D}
                   \le2r\log\max(1,K_+).          \tag{ITL17}
\]
This follows by bounding each positive generalized eigenvalue
and multiplying all $r$ of them. At $D=O(q_{k+4})$ its absolute
value is $O_{\mathrm{actual}}(k\log q_{k+4})$, because
$r\le j_{0,k}=8k-32-v+t_{0,k}=O(k)$ and NBG13 gives
$\log\max(1,K_+,K_-)=O_{\mathrm{actual}}(\log(D+1))$.
No lower bound for either other loss has been assumed.

\section{Exact cancellation of the common compatibility denominator}

Apply NKC6--16 to the two actual source restrictions $V$ and
$E$, using their full DNE functional images in the ambient
finite dual quotient. Their correlated isometric images
satisfy $\mathcal A_E\subset\mathcal A_V$, since $E\subset V$
and the same map $I-P_{\mathcal C}$ is used. Here the calligraphic
subscripts name subspaces, not a change in the Gram $A$.
Let $P_V,P_E$ be the corresponding native orthogonal projectors.
Retain NKC's single lower-gauge coefficient complement $Z_c$,
complete evaluation map $\mathcal V$, compatibility matrix
$\mathcal K$, next evaluation map $\mathcal V_2$, and Gram $G_c$.
In those exact coordinates let
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal H_{V,D}&=Z_c^*\mathcal K^*\mathcal V_2^*\mathcal V_2
              \mathcal KZ_c+Z_c^*\mathcal V^*
                      (P_T-P_{\mathcal C}-P_V)\mathcal VZ_c,\\
 \mathcal H_{E,D}&=\mathcal H_{V,D}
                  +Z_c^*\mathcal V^*(P_V-P_E)\mathcal VZ_c .
                                                        \end{split} \tag{ITL18}
\]
Nested orthogonal images imply $P_V-P_E$ is an orthogonal
projector of rank $r$. Thus the second difference is positive
semidefinite of rank at most $r$, and both complete Grams are
positive by NKC14. This retains the entire source complement
even when the invariant source is a proper ambient subspace.

Set $b_{k,D}=r_{\mathrm{comp},k}
\max\{-\log c_-,\log c_+\}$, with the actual NKC13 constants
and $r_{\mathrm{comp},k}=3k^2-10k-13$.
NKC15--16 gives, separately for $Y=V,E$,
\[
 \log\omega_{Y,D}
  =\log\det\mathcal H_{Y,D}-\log\det G_c+\eta_{Y,D},
                   \qquad |\eta_{Y,D}|\le b_{k,D}. \tag{ITL19}
\]
Subtract the two exact equalities before estimating their errors:
the common $\log\det G_c$ cancels. Therefore
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \log\det\mathscr R_{X,D}
   =\log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D}}
      +\log\omega_{\mathrm{inv},D}+\epsilon_D,
 \quad}
 \tag{ITL20}
\]
where the finite bound, with the original rank $r$, is
\[
 |\epsilon_D|\le 2r\max\{\log\max(1,K_-),\log\max(1,K_+)\}
                                  +2b_{k,D}.       \tag{ITL21}
\]
On the original endpoint range this is
$O_{\mathrm{actual}}(q_k\log q_k)=o(kq_k)$.
Both displayed determinant ratios in ITL20 are at most one.
Their four-endpoint signed sums are not thereby nonpositive.
If $P_V-P_E=YY^*$ is an isometric factor, a smaller exact
formula for the first ratio is
\[
 \frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D}}
   =\det\!\left(I_r+
       Y^*\mathcal VZ_c\mathcal H_{V,D}^{-1}
                     Z_c^*\mathcal V^*Y\right)^{-1}.
                                                        \tag{ITL22}
\]
The determinant lemma proves this directly from ITL18.
All coefficients in its $r$ by $r$ matrix come from the
specified original source-complement projector and full Gram;
no bound on the inverse is asserted without calculating it.

\section{Branch defect and all four original endpoint signs}

The source kernel $E$ is not replaced by zero in any preceding
formula. DFX24--28 supplies the exact inclusion
\[
 E\subset\mathscr E_{v,k}
     =\mathscr J_{v,k}\cap\ker\mathsf j_k,
 \quad
 \mathscr J_{v,k}/E\twoheadrightarrow
 \mathscr J_{v,k}/\mathscr E_{v,k}
                 \simeq\im\mathcal B_{\{1,2,3,4\},k},
                                                        \tag{ITL23}
\]
with kernel $\mathscr E_{v,k}/E$. All source coordinates are
transported by the original $\mathsf T_kJ_0$ in this display.
For the actual full biforms $B_a=\psi_2(Q_a)$, let
$\mathcal L=\operatorname{lcm}(B_1,B_2,B_3,B_4)$ have
bidegree $(b_z,b_w)$. DFX26--27 gives
\[
 0\le e\le\dim\mathscr E_{v,k}
 \le(k-b_z+1)(k-b_w+1)-(k-7)^2 .                   \tag{ITL24}
\]
These bounds keep the maximum factor multiplicities in the
lcm and all shared components. They do not identify the
actual kernel with the full shared-component space; its
exact finite coefficient kernel remains DFX31--35.

At $N=q_{k+4}-1,q_{k+4},2q_{k+4}-1,2q_{k+4}$ use
$D=N-v$ and signs $\varepsilon_N=-1,-1,+1,+1$.
Summing ITL20 gives the original further-quotient return
\[
 \begin{split}
 \sum_N\varepsilon_N\log\det\mathscr R_{X,N-v}
 ={}&\sum_N\varepsilon_N
             \log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,N-v}}
                          {\det\mathcal H_{E,N-v}}\\
 &+\sum_N\varepsilon_N\log\omega_{\mathrm{inv},N-v}
                  +\sum_N\varepsilon_N\epsilon_{N-v}.
                                                        \end{split} \tag{ITL25}
\]
The last term has the explicit sum of four ITL21 bounds,
and is $o(kq_k)$. The other two full native determinants are
ITL10 and ITL18/22. This is a finite quantitative reduction
of the additional invariant quotient, not a value assigned
to its remaining leading coefficient. The accepted NW1--13
displacement estimate applies to fixed original source flags
when subsequently returning their cutoffs to their own degree;
it does not itself estimate either long signed sum in ITL25.

When $r=0$, both matched Grams are empty and every determinant
in the matched ratio is one. Then $E=V$, the two compatibility
Grams coincide, and ITL16 and ITL20 give exactly zero with
zero actual error; a nonzero upper error bound is not used
as a replacement for this identity. No RH consequence,
native invariant isometry, or new Lean execution is claimed.

\section{The identical native update sharpens the error to the image rank}

The preceding separate use of NKC15 is valid, but the two native
comparisons share more data than that estimate uses. Retain the
same endpoint, the same lower-gauge isometry $W_c$ of NKC6,
and the same nested source projectors $P_E\preceq P_V$ of ITL18.
Choose an isometric frame $Y$ for their difference, so that
$YY^*=P_V-P_E$ and $Y$ has exactly $r$ columns. This is a
coordinate expression of the original difference subspace;
it does not replace either native metric. Define
\[
 \mathcal J_D=Y^*W_c:\mathbb C^{r_{\mathrm{comp},k}}
                         \longrightarrow\mathbb C^r .
\]
For $Z=V,E$ retain the two exact NKC10 and NKC13 matrices
\[
 \Xi_{Z,D}=I-W_c^*P_ZW_c,\qquad
 \Gamma_{Z,D}=W_c^*
    (\mathcal R^*\mathcal R+P_T-P_{\mathcal C}-P_Z)W_c.
\]
Their differences are literally the same Gram:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Xi_{E,D}=\Xi_{V,D}+\mathcal J_D^*\mathcal J_D,\qquad
 \Gamma_{E,D}=\Gamma_{V,D}+\mathcal J_D^*\mathcal J_D,\\
 0\prec c_-\Xi_{V,D}\preceq\Gamma_{V,D}
                         \preceq c_+\Xi_{V,D},\qquad
 0<c_-\le1\le c_+.
 \end{gathered}                                      \tag{ITL26}
\]
The first two identities follow by subtracting the displayed
definitions, using $P_V-P_E=YY^*$. The last two facts are
NKC13--14 with their original constants
$c_-=\min(1,L_-^{-2})$, $c_+=\max(1,L_+^2)$.
All inverses below are therefore positive-matrix inverses
already justified by the exact native intersection theorem.

In the actual $r$-dimensional image-difference coordinates put
\[
 T_\Xi=\mathcal J_D\Xi_{V,D}^{-1}\mathcal J_D^*,\qquad
 T_\Gamma=\mathcal J_D\Gamma_{V,D}^{-1}\mathcal J_D^*.
\]
The determinant lemma applied twice to ITL26 gives
\[
 \begin{split}
 \log\omega_{V,D}-\log\omega_{E,D}
     &=-\log\det(I_r+T_\Xi),\\
 \log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D}}
     &=-\log\det(I_r+T_\Gamma).
 \end{split}                                        \tag{ITL27}
\]
For the first line use $\det\Omega_{Z,D}=\det\Xi_{Z,D}$
from NKC10. For the second use the exact common conjugation
$\Gamma_{Z,D}=G_c^{-1/2}\mathcal H_{Z,D}G_c^{-1/2}$
from NKC16, so its denominator cancels before the determinant
lemma is applied. Thus neither line estimates or omits a
kernel determinant. In particular the second line is precisely
ITL22, expressed through the complete original gauge isometry.

Inversion reverses the positive form comparison ITL26.
Congruence by $\mathcal J_D^*$ consequently gives
\[
 \frac1{c_+}T_\Xi\preceq T_\Gamma
              \preceq\frac1{c_-}T_\Xi,\qquad
 \frac1{c_+}(I_r+T_\Xi)\preceq I_r+T_\Gamma
              \preceq\frac1{c_-}(I_r+T_\Xi).        \tag{ITL28}
\]
For the second pair, the first pair supplies the $T_\Xi$
term, while $c_+^{-1}I_r\preceq I_r\preceq c_-^{-1}I_r$
supplies the identity term. This argument does not require
$T_\Xi,T_\Gamma$ to commute. Congruence by
$(I_r+T_\Xi)^{-1/2}$ puts each of the $r$ positive
generalized eigenvalues between $c_+^{-1}$ and $c_-^{-1}$.
Multiplication and logarithms prove the finite asymmetric bound
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \vartheta_D:=(\log\omega_{V,D}-\log\omega_{E,D})
       -\log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D}}
       =\log\det(I_r+T_\Gamma)-\log\det(I_r+T_\Xi),\\
           -r\log c_+\le\vartheta_D\le-r\log c_- .
 \end{gathered}                                      \tag{ITL29}
\]
The source-complement matrices and their inverses remain in
ITL27; their possibly small eigenvalues are not replaced by
an absolute conditioning bound. ITL29 measures only the
difference between the two specified evaluations of the same
rank-at-most-$r$ update, not the size of either negative logarithm.

Combine ITL29 with the exact ITL16 and the finite ITL17.
The very same $\epsilon_D$ in ITL20 now has the sharper bounds
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \epsilon_D=e_{\mathrm{cor},D}+\vartheta_D,\qquad
             -\alpha_D\le\epsilon_D\le\beta_D,\\
 \alpha_D=2r\log\max(1,K_-)+r\log c_+,\qquad
 \beta_D=2r\log\max(1,K_+)-r\log c_- .
 \end{gathered}                                      \tag{ITL30}
\]
Both constants are nonnegative and retain exactly the two
original source-order families. Since $r=O(k)$ and all the
displayed logarithmic constants are
$O_{\mathrm{actual}}(\log(D+1))$ by NBG13/NKC3--4,
this gives $\epsilon_D=O_{\mathrm{actual}}(k\log q_k)$
at the four original endpoints. The previous larger bound
ITL21 remains true; ITL30 is the bound to use for the same
error in ITL20 and ITL25.

Write $D_i=N_i-v$ for the four ordered target endpoints
of ITL25. With all their signs retained,
\[
 -\beta_{D_1}-\beta_{D_2}-\alpha_{D_3}-\alpha_{D_4}
 \le\sum_{i=1}^4\varepsilon_i\epsilon_{D_i}
 \le\alpha_{D_1}+\alpha_{D_2}+\beta_{D_3}+\beta_{D_4}.
                                                        \tag{ITL31}
\]
Thus the remainder of that exact four-return is now
$O_{\mathrm{actual}}(k\log q_k)=o(kq_k)$.
No estimate for either leading long determinant sum in ITL25
is asserted by this sharpening. The original source kernel
$E$ and its nonreduced enclosing defect in ITL23--24 are
unaltered. If $r=0$, $\mathcal J_D$ has zero rows, both
determinants in ITL27 are one, and ITL29--31 are exactly zero.

\paragraph{Current arithmetic rows and simultaneous spectrum.}
The original invariant-target term in ITL25 now has the
full arithmetic representation
\[
 \sum_i\varepsilon_i\log\omega_{D_i}

with no image-rank multiplier. NFR1--5 proves the literal
map to ITL20/25 and retains the asymmetric ITL30--31 error.

specified quantities; no asymptotic value is assigned.

\section{The native target loss in the earlier matched quotient}
\label{sec:NFR}
Keep the actual simple quartet, period, branch and nonreduced conductor,
$k=4l+1\ge9$, $j=k+4$, and $D\ge q_j-v-1$. The exact original
source-order pairs are $(s_0,s_1)=(1,1)$ or $(k,j)$.
All original DNE, KRC, DFX and NBG maps are those in ITL1--6:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathsf T_a=\alpha_A N_aZ_{\exp,a},\quad\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v,\quad
 M_i=\mathsf T_j^{-1}m_{i,k}\mathsf T_k,\quad
 F=\operatorname{stack}_i(M_iJ_0),\\
 \mathcal B=\operatorname{diag}(B_1,4),\quad
 \ker\mathcal B=\operatorname{im}\operatorname{diag}(J_1,4),
 \quad E=\ker(\mathcal BF),\\
 G=J_0^*D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}J_0,\quad
 \mathcal D_D=\operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D}^{(s_1)},4),\quad
 A=F^*\mathcal D_DF,\quad Q=G/E .
\end{gathered}\tag{NFR1}
\]
Here $\operatorname{diag}(P,4)$ means four copies, and every quotient
$P/E$ is its attained minimum in the fixed lifts $[Z_E,Z_R]$ of ITL4.
The actual image frame is $U=\mathcal BFZ_R$.
Its native target form is
$H=U^*(\mathcal B\mathcal D_D^{-1}\mathcal B^*)^{-1}U$ and its
source pullback form before the last target quotient is $A_E=A/E$.
A lift of $Ub$ is exactly $FZ_Rb+\ker\mathcal B$.
Its complete minimum over that kernel gives ITL8's $H$.
The source minimum permits just $FZ_E$. This proves the typed
identification of ITL9's $\omega_D=\det H/\det A_E$ with the
last target loss in NBG31--32, retaining the full source kernel.

Let $C_D$ be the original correlated pullback on the invariant source.
Schur factorization in $[Z_E,Z_R]$ gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log\det(Q^{-1/2}HQ^{-1/2})
  =e_{{\rm cor},D}
      +\log\omega_{V,D}-\log\omega_{E,D}+\log\omega_D\\
  =\log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D}}
                  +\log\omega_D+\epsilon_D,\qquad
 -a_D\le\epsilon_D\le b_D,\\
 a_D=2r\log\max(1,K_-)+r\log c_+,\quad
 b_D=2r\log\max(1,K_+)-r\log c_-,
 \quad r=\dim V-\dim E .
\end{gathered}\tag{NFR2}
\]
In the first line
$e_{\rm cor}=\log\det(C_D/E)-\log\det(G/E)$ and the two restriction
losses are exactly ITL13. Their determinant ratio equals
$\det(A/E)/\det(C_D/E)$ because the same full frame factor cancels.
The complete compatibility forms in the second line are ITL18.
Their common denominator $G_c$ cancels before estimating.
Their identical positive update has rank at most $r$; compression
of NKC's two-sided comparison bounds its logdet difference between
$-r\log c_+$ and $-r\log c_-$. Adding the correlated quotient
interval ITL17 proves exactly NFR2. This is ITL26--30 applied to
the original matched quotient, not a replacement of its target metric.

\section{Every original row and both signs in the long return}
Put $F_E=FZ_E$, $F_R=FZ_R$, and let
$\widehat W=[F_E,W_R]$ span $\ker\mathcal B$.
The four fixed flags are the images of
$Y_K=F_E$, $Y_S=[F_E,F_R]$,
$Y_W=\widehat W$, $Y_T=[\widehat W,F_R]$.
They obey $S\cap W=K$, because $Fa\in\ker\mathcal B$ is exactly
$a\in E$. Their raw frames are
$Z_{Y,D}=[\mathcal V_D,\mathcal T_DY]$, using all original
lower rows and all numerator rows IRR7--11.
Analytic numerator division is the literal recursion
\[
 f_{a,n}=
 \frac{\nu_{a,n+v}-\sum_{d=v+1}^{n+v}
                 \binom{n+v}{d}\mu_df_{a,n+v-d}}
      {\binom{n+v}{v}\mu_v},
\]
and the polynomial coefficients and complete row formulas remain
IRR8--9, with $i^{-n}$, $h_{n,s_1}=(2\pi)^{s_1/2}n!(s_1/2)_n$
and center $j/2-4$. Thus there is no unspecified substitute evaluator.

Write $G_{Y,D}=Z_{Y,D}^*Z_{Y,D}$ and
$L_{Y,n}=z_{Y,n}G_{Y,n-1}^{-1}z_{Y,n}^*$ for the original new
four-row block. Eliminating the entire lower block and then the
actual source or target kernel gives
$\omega_D=\det G_{T,D}\det G_{K,D}/(\det G_{W,D}\det G_{S,D})$.
The determinant lemma for each raw Gram therefore proves
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log\omega_n-\log\omega_{n-1}
       =\gamma_{H,n}-\gamma_{A,n},\\
 \gamma_{H,n}=\log\det(I_4+L_{T,n})-\log\det(I_4+L_{W,n})\ge0,\\
 \gamma_{A,n}=\log\det(I_4+L_{S,n})-\log\det(I_4+L_{K,n})\ge0,\\
 \mathscr L_{\rm inv}=\sum_{n=d+1}^{d+q_j+1}
       \vartheta_n(\gamma_{H,n}-\gamma_{A,n}),\quad d=q_j-v-1,\\
 \vartheta_{d+1}=\vartheta_{d+q_j+1}=1,\qquad
 \vartheta_n=2\quad(d+2\le n\le d+q_j).
\end{gathered}\tag{NFR3}
\]
For positivity, transform each pair $(K,S)$ or $(W,T)$ to its old
minimum-section columns. The larger raw covariance is
$L_Y=L_X+R_XQ_X^{-1}R_X^*$; factoring its positive determinant
gives IRR21's nonnegative increment. The common raw lower minimum
is never taken separately for different rows. Finally telescope
over $[d,d+q_j]$ and $[d+1,d+q_j+1]$. Their overlap gives the
exact displayed weights.

\section{The simultaneous native budget and the exact signed receiver}
For the same original ordered generalized eigenvalues
$\lambda_i(D)$ of $(H_D,A_{E,D})$ put
$u_i(D)=\operatorname{arcosh}(\lambda_i(D)^{-1/2})$.
On the positive path joining two actual adjacent metrics, the original
minimum lifts give orthonormal paired vectors $p_i,q_i$ with
$p_i^*q_m=\sqrt{\lambda_m}\delta_{im}$.
Consequently $J_i=q_iq_i^*-p_ip_i^*$ has eigenvalues
$\pm\sqrt{1-\lambda_i}$ on mutually orthogonal planes.
The full repeated-eigenvalue and zero-angle arguments IF5--6 and
FAB6--9 retain the ordered spectrum through crossings and give,
almost everywhere,
\[
 (\log\lambda_i)'=\operatorname{Tr}(BJ_i),\qquad
 \sum_i|(\log\lambda_i)'|\le\operatorname{Tr}B,\qquad
 \sum_i|u_i'|\le\tfrac12\operatorname{Tr}B .
\]
Indeed sign maximization of the trace takes place on those disjoint
planes, so its operator norm is at most one regardless of their count.
Here $B=\mathcal D^{-1/2}\Delta\mathcal D^{-1/2}\succeq0$.
Its integral is the exact ambient logdet increment. The complete lower
row minimization gives, with all rows through $n-1$,
$C_L=V^*V$, $D_0=T^*(I-VC_L^{-1}V^*)T$,
$\zeta_n=v_nC_L^{-1}v_n^*$ and
$\rho_n=t_n-v_nC_L^{-1}V^*T$.
Completing its square proves the one-target update
$\rho_n^*\rho_n/(1+\zeta_n)$ and hence
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \beta_n=4\log\left(1+
          \frac{\rho_nD_0^{-1}\rho_n^*}{1+\zeta_n}\right),\\
 \sum_i|\log\lambda_i(n)-\log\lambda_i(n-1)|\le\beta_n,\quad
 \sum_i|u_i(n)-u_i(n-1)|\le\beta_n/2,\quad
 |\mathscr L_{\rm inv}|\le\sum_n\vartheta_n\beta_n .
\end{gathered}\tag{NFR4}
\]
No image-rank factor multiplies this budget.

At the four ordered endpoints $D_i=d,d+1,d+q_j,d+q_j+1$,
$\varepsilon=(-1,-1,+1,+1)$, substituting NFR3 into NFR2 gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \sum_i\varepsilon_i\log\det\mathscr R_{X,D_i}
  =\sum_i\varepsilon_i
       \log\frac{\det\mathcal H_{V,D_i}}{\det\mathcal H_{E,D_i}}
      +\sum_n\vartheta_n(\gamma_{H,n}-\gamma_{A,n})
      +\mathcal E_{\rm fin},\\
 -b_{D_1}-b_{D_2}-a_{D_3}-a_{D_4}
 \le\mathcal E_{\rm fin}
 \le a_{D_1}+a_{D_2}+b_{D_3}+b_{D_4}.
\end{gathered}\tag{NFR5}
\]
The signs follow by negating the two low endpoint errors.
Only this finite comparison error is $O_{\rm actual}(k\log q_k)$.
The two positive row sums and the full compatibility ratio are
retained in their exact source metrics. The $q_j+1$ row degrees

signed difference is assigned an arithmetic leading coefficient.
When $r=0$, both quotient forms are empty and all actual loss terms
vanish exactly. The common target-source mass cancels from a ratio
only through the equal quotient ranks; it remains in every row and
absolute native Gram in NFR1--4.
