% Complete original residual boundary quotient, branch kernel and native rows.
\section{The surviving boundary quotient and its original arithmetic kernel}
\label{sec:RQB}

Retain the actual simple quartet, its proved five-orbit period and logarithm
branch, every coefficient of the original nonreduced conductor, and the
complete CIT, CNC, DFX, KRC, DNE, NBG and PCJ maps. Put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 k=4l+1\ge9,\quad j=k+4,\quad q_a=(a+1)^2,\quad q'_a=(a-7)^2,
 \quad m_a=16a-48-v,\\
 0\le v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A\le80,\quad
 \mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)\ne0,\quad \alpha_A=v!/\mu_v,\\
 (s_0,s_1)=(1,1)\ \hbox{or}\ (k,j),\qquad
 c'_a=a/2-4,\qquad D\ge q_j-v-1 .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB1}
\]
All coefficient spaces and dual functionals are complex-linear. No star
replaces an ordinary transpose in an original algebraic map. Metric stars
are complex adjoints. The degree progression and the common cutoff $D$
are literal, including the two distinct source measures. Write
$s_k=s_0$ and $s_j=s_1$ when source orders have degree subscripts.

\subsection{The exact coefficient kernel, rank and original kernel quotient}

Let $V=\mathbb C^{m_k}$, $U=\mathbb C^{4m_j}$ and retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 B_0=\mathbf B_k,\quad B_1=\mathbf B_j,\quad
 J_0=\mathbf J_k,\quad J_1=\mathbf J_j,\quad
 \ker B_a=\operatorname{im}J_a\quad(a=0,1),\\
 M_i=(\alpha_A N_jZ_{\exp,j})^{-1}
          m_{i,k}(\alpha_A N_kZ_{\exp,k}),\qquad m_{i,k}[f]=[x_i^4f],\\
 B_{\rm cof}=\operatorname{stack}_{i=1}^4M_i,\quad
 \mathbb B=\operatorname{diag}(B_1,4),\quad
 X_{\mathbb J}=\operatorname{diag}(J_1,4),\quad
 \mathbb J=\operatorname{im}X_{\mathbb J},\\
 \Phi=\mathbb B B_{\rm cof},\quad J=\operatorname{im}J_0,\quad
 \mathscr L=\ker\Phi,\quad Y=J+\mathscr L,\quad
 E=\ker(\Phi J_0).
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB2}
\]
Here $\operatorname{diag}(P,4)$ means four copies. The original KRC
bijections specify the inverses onto their full images; both occurrences
of $\alpha_A$ are retained before their proved cancellation in $M_i$.
CNC6 or DFX21 proves $B_{\rm cof}$ injective on the entire $V$.
All $\mathbf B_a$ are onto their actual kernel-dual coordinate spaces.
Write $t_{K,a}$ for their ranks and $j_{0,a}$ for the column ranks of
$\mathbf J_a$; the original exact values are
$t_{K,a}=8a-16-t_{0,a}$ and $j_{0,a}=8a-32-v+t_{0,a}$.

Set exactly as in CIT24
\[
 W=\mathbb J+\operatorname{im}B_{\rm cof},\qquad
 W_{\rm inv}=\mathbb J+\operatorname{im}(B_{\rm cof}J_0).
\]
The literal map and its full kernel are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \pi_b:V\longrightarrow W/W_{\rm inv},\qquad
                 x\longmapsto[B_{\rm cof}x],\qquad \ker\pi_b=Y,\\
 V/Y\xrightarrow{\sim}W/W_{\rm inv}
 \xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{im}\Phi/\operatorname{im}(\Phi J_0),
 \qquad [x]\longmapsto[B_{\rm cof}x]\longmapsto[\Phi x].
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB3}
\]
Indeed $B_{\rm cof}x\in W_{\rm inv}$ precisely when
$B_{\rm cof}(x-J_0a)\in\mathbb J$ for some $a$, equivalently
$x-J_0a\in\ker\Phi$. This proves the first kernel and both
surjections. The second arrow has kernel exactly $\mathbb J$
before the displayed quotient, because $\ker\mathbb B=\mathbb J$.
Thus it is injective on the stated quotient.

Put $\ell=\dim\mathscr L$, $\epsilon=\dim E$ and $r_b=\dim(W/W_{\rm inv})$.
Since $J_0$ is injective, it identifies $E$ with $J\cap\mathscr L$.
Consequently
\[
\begin{gathered}
 r_b=m_k-j_{0,k}-\ell+\epsilon
    =t_{K,k}-\ell+\epsilon
    =\operatorname{rank}\Phi-\operatorname{rank}(\Phi J_0),\\
 0\longrightarrow B_0\mathscr L
   \longrightarrow\mathbb C^{t_{K,k}}
   \xrightarrow{\ [B_0x]\mapsto[B_{\rm cof}x]\ }W/W_{\rm inv}
   \longrightarrow0,\qquad
 \dim B_0\mathscr L=\ell-\epsilon .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB4}
\]
The middle arrow is well defined because changing $x$ by $\ker B_0=J$
changes its image by $W_{\rm inv}$. Its kernel is $B_0Y=B_0\mathscr L$.
Under KRC4--5 this is the exact quotient of
$(K_k/(K_k\cap L_k))^\vee$, where $L_k=\mathbb C[S]_{<v}$ is
the original polynomial low space. Thus the original low overlap
$t_{0,k}$ remains in both the domain rank and its actual quotient.

Here is a finite calculation of $\mathscr L$ using the input polynomials.
Use the exact Segre coefficient map
$\Psi_a:S_{x,a}\to T_{a,a}$ of DFX13, the selector $U_a^{\rm inv}$
of degree-$a$ monomials with $\sum i\nu_i\equiv0\pmod5$, and the
complete original primary conductor convolution $C_a^{\mathsf T}$.
Let $\operatorname{quo}(G)$ be the following exact row-pivot quotient:
take the first independent columns $F$ of $G$, the first row set
$\mathcal R$ with $F_{\mathcal R}$ invertible, and send
$x$ to $(x-FF_{\mathcal R}^{-1}x_{\mathcal R})_{\mathcal R^c}$.
It is onto and has kernel $\operatorname{im}G$, since the subtracted
vector agrees with $x$ on $\mathcal R$ and a zero remainder means
$x$ is that same column combination. Empty and full ranks give the
identity and zero map respectively. Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
 P_j^{\rm inv}=\operatorname{quo}
       [C_j^{\mathsf T},\Psi_j U_j^{\rm inv}],\\
 (H_{i,k}\nu)_{ab}=\sum_{r,t=0}^4b_{i,r,t}\nu_{a-r,b-t},\qquad
 b_{i,r,t}=[Z^rW^t]L_i(Z,W)^4,\\
 \mathcal A_k=\operatorname{stack}_{i=1}^4
                 (P_j^{\rm inv}H_{i,k}Z_{\exp,k}),\qquad
 \mathscr L=\ker\mathcal A_k,\quad E=\ker(\mathcal A_kJ_0).
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB5}
\]
Every off-rectangle coefficient in the convolution is zero. Membership
of $H_{i,k}Z_{\exp,k}x$ in the displayed column space means its full
conductor class has an invariant representative. Its first $v$ jets
already vanish by Leibniz multiplication; the conductor columns have
zero such jets, so the invariant representative has zero jets as well.
It is therefore exactly membership in $\mathscr J_{v,j}$, which
KRC5 identifies with $\operatorname{im}J_1$. This proves RQB5 and
shows that the two computed kernels are those in RQB2, including all
exceptional coefficient minors. In particular the first line of RQB4
is an exact finite rank formula in the original coefficients, without
a chosen generic rank.

\subsection{A branch constraint for arbitrary source numerators}

Work first in $S_x=\mathbb C[x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4]$, with the original
$\mathsf H$, $Q=Q_0(\mathsf H^{-1}x)$, $Q_a=g^aQ$ and
$\mathcal A=\prod_{a=1}^4Q_a$, where
\[
 g f(x)=f(\operatorname{diag}(\zeta^{-1},
               \zeta^{-2},\zeta^{-3},\zeta^{-4})x).
\]
The primary order in the Segre map is unchanged. In the polynomial
UFD $T=\mathbb C[z_0,z_1,w_0,w_1]$ put
\[
 \psi f=f(\mathsf H(z_0w_0,z_0w_1,z_1w_0,z_1w_1)^{\mathsf T}),
 \quad \ell_i=\psi x_i,\quad B_a=\psi Q_a,\quad
 A^{\rm hom}=\prod_{a=1}^4B_a .
 \tag{RQB6}
\]
The restriction map is onto $T_{d,d}$ with kernel $QS_{x,d-2}$:
each biform monomial has the lift in KRC13, and two lifts with
the same exponents differ by the relation $t_1t_4-t_2t_3$.
Grouping terms with the same image proves the full kernel, as in
DFX13. Hence the complete branch quotient is
$T_{d,d}/B_aT_{d-2,d-2}$; no radical is taken.

The four $\ell_i$ are linearly independent $(1,1)$ biforms,
because their coefficient matrix is the actual invertible $\mathsf H$.
Any irreducible common factor of three has bidegree $(1,0)$,
$(0,1)$ or $(1,1)$: factors of a bihomogeneous polynomial are
bihomogeneous, as follows by comparing least and greatest degree
in each grading in a domain. Multiples of a fixed factor of these
types inside $T_{1,1}$ have dimensions two, two or one.
Three independent rows cannot be contained there. It follows that
any irreducible factor divides at most two $\ell_i$, and therefore
\[
       \gcd\{\ell_i^4\ell_t^4:1\le i<t\le4\}=1
                         \quad\hbox{in }T .
 \tag{RQB7}
\]
For each irreducible factor, choose two indices outside the at most
two rows divisible by it. Their pair product is not divisible by it.
This proves RQB7 in the biform polynomial UFD, without transferring
factorization to the homogeneous quadric quotient ring.

Let $[f]\in\mathscr C_{v,k}$ be the image of a vector of $\mathscr L$.
For each $i$ there is an invariant $p_i$ such that
$x_i^4f-p_i\in(Q,\mathcal A)$.
Fix $a\in\{1,2,3,4\}$ and reduce modulo $(Q,Q_a)$.
The ideal $(Q,\mathcal A)$ maps to zero there; applying $g^a$
to it also maps to zero, because $g^aQ=Q_a$ and $g^a\mathcal A$
contains $Q$ as the factor with original index $5-a$.
Since $g^ap_i=p_i$, the two reductions give
\[
 x_i^4f=\lambda_{i,a}x_i^4g^af,\qquad
 \lambda_{i,a}=\zeta^{-4ia}\quad\bmod(Q,Q_a).
 \tag{RQB8}
\]
For fixed nonzero $a$ the four $\lambda_{i,a}$ are nonzero and
pairwise distinct. Multiply the equation with index $i$ by
$x_t^4$ and that with index $t$ by $x_i^4$, then subtract.
It follows first that $x_i^4x_t^4g^af=0$, and then that
$x_i^4x_t^4f=0$ in this same full branch quotient.
On applying $\psi$, every irreducible factor $P$ of $B_a$,
with its actual exponent $e_P$, therefore satisfies
\[
 e_P\le\operatorname{ord}_P(\ell_i^4\ell_t^4\psi f)
                  \quad\hbox{for every }i<t .
\]
RQB7 supplies a pair whose multiplier has exponent zero.
Thus $\operatorname{ord}_P\psi f\ge e_P$ for every $P$,
so $B_a$ divides $\psi f$ with all its multiplicities.
Repeating for all four $a$ proves the new arbitrary-source constraint
\[
 \mathsf T_k\mathscr L\subset
 \mathscr H_{v,k}:=\mathscr C_{v,k}\cap\ker\mathsf j_k,\qquad
 \mathsf T_k=\alpha_A N_kZ_{\exp,k}.
 \tag{RQB9}
\]
DFX24 proves its invariant-source specialization. The proof above
retains the arbitrary $f$ and $g^af$ as two different branch values
until the pair equations eliminate them.

The constraint is computable on the complete nonreduced factor scheme.
Let
\[
 \mathcal L_{\rm br}=\operatorname{lcm}(B_1,B_2,B_3,B_4),
 \quad (b_z,b_w)=\operatorname{bideg}\mathcal L_{\rm br},\quad
 G_{\rm br}=A^{\rm hom}/\mathcal L_{\rm br}.
\]
Retain the actual scalar in $A^{\rm hom}$ and compensating factor
units in this equation. Divisibility gives the exact kernel
\[
 \ker\mathsf j_k\simeq
 T_{k-b_z,k-b_w}/G_{\rm br}T_{k-8,k-8},
 \quad [u]\mapsto[\mathcal L_{\rm br}u],\qquad
 h_k=(k-b_z+1)(k-b_w+1)-(k-7)^2 .
 \tag{RQB10}
\]
Indeed all four branch classes vanish exactly when their biform
representative is divisible by this lcm. Such representatives
differ by $A^{\rm hom}$ exactly by the stated denominator.
Nonzero biform multiplication is injective, proving the dimension.

Choose a quotient monomial basis in RQB10 by the specified row-pivot
rule, multiply by $\mathcal L_{\rm br}$, take its affine coefficient
array and apply the original jet rows
$\beta_{k,a,b}^{\,n}$, $0\le n<v$. Call this full matrix
$\mathcal J_{\rm br,v}$. The denominator is killed since it is a
multiple of $E_A$ after the original exponential substitution.
Thus, with $h_{v,k}=\dim\mathscr H_{v,k}$,
\[
 h_{v,k}=h_k-\operatorname{rank}\mathcal J_{\rm br,v},\qquad
 d_{v,k}=\dim(\mathscr H_{v,k}\cap\mathscr J_{v,k}),\qquad
 0\le\ell-\epsilon\le h_{v,k}-d_{v,k}.
 \tag{RQB11}
\]
For example $d_{v,k}$ is exactly the nullity of the four branch
quotient matrices on $\mathsf T_kJ_0$, computed with DFX30/KRC13.
The last inequality follows from the injective map
$\mathscr L/(\mathscr L\cap J)\to
\mathscr H_{v,k}/(\mathscr H_{v,k}\cap\mathscr J_{v,k})$
induced by the original $\mathsf T_k$. Its kernel is precisely
$\mathscr L\cap J$, so no dimension of an overlapping low space
is dropped. The resulting finite rank control is
\[
 \max\{0,t_{K,k}-h_{v,k}+d_{v,k}\}
       \le r_b\le t_{K,k},\qquad
 r_b=t_{K,k}-\operatorname{rank}(B_0Z_{\mathscr L}),
 \tag{RQB12}
\]
where $Z_{\mathscr L}$ is the exact kernel frame from RQB5.
This preserves every shared-component defect. It gives full rank
exactly on the coefficient strata where $B_0Z_{\mathscr L}=0$;
that criterion is a computed matrix equality, not a replacement
of a nonreduced conductor by its reduced support.

\subsection{Full native and raw Gamma realizations}

Use the actual fixed ITL4 complement $Z_R$ of $E$, so
$I_R=B_{\rm cof}J_0Z_R$. Choose a fixed complement $E_b$ of $Y$
in $V$ by exact coefficient elimination, and set
\[
 R_b=B_{\rm cof}E_b,\qquad
 Y_{\rm inv}=[X_{\mathbb J},I_R],\qquad
 Y_W=[X_{\mathbb J},I_R,R_b].
 \tag{RQB13}
\]
These are full frames of $W_{\rm inv}$ and $W$: applying
$\mathbb B$ kills $X_{\mathbb J}$, makes $\Phi J_0Z_R$ independent,
and RQB3 makes the last quotient columns independent.
In particular $R_b$ represents exactly $r_b$ quotient values.
For the full target DNE form set
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal D_D=\operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D}^{(s_1)},4),\quad
 Q_b^E(D)=R_b^*\mathcal D_D R_b
 -R_b^*\mathcal D_DY_{\rm inv}
       (Y_{\rm inv}^*\mathcal D_DY_{\rm inv})^{-1}
                          Y_{\rm inv}^*\mathcal D_D R_b,\\
 \mathcal H_D=\operatorname{diag}(H_{B,j,D},4),\quad
 H_{B,a,D}=(\mathbf B_a(D_{a,D}^{(s_a)})^{-1}\mathbf B_a^*)^{-1}
                   =\overline{\widehat H_{K,a,D}^{-1}},\\
 U_R=\Phi J_0Z_R,\quad U_b=\Phi E_b,\qquad
 Q_b^E(D)=U_b^*\mathcal H_DU_b
 -U_b^*\mathcal H_DU_R(U_R^*\mathcal H_DU_R)^{-1}
                                      U_R^*\mathcal H_DU_b .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB14}
\]
Both formulas are attained minima: the first minimizes over the
complete $W_{\rm inv}$; the second first minimizes over
$\ker\mathbb B=\mathbb J$, then over its remaining image $U_R$.
The fibres are identical by RQB3, so the formulas agree.
Every inverse is justified by the full frames and DNE positivity.
Empty image ranks omit their subtracted blocks.

If $R_b^{\rm CIT}$ is the particular earlier CIT25 lift, there is
a unique invertible $C_b$ on quotient values and some $A_b$ with
$R_b^{\rm CIT}=R_b C_b+Y_{\rm inv}A_b$.
The exact minimum gives
\[
 Q_b^{\rm CIT}=C_b^*Q_b^E C_b,\qquad
 \det Q_b^{\rm CIT}=|\det C_b|^2\det Q_b^E .
 \tag{RQB15}
\]
The coefficients are obtained by solving the full frame $Y_W$.
They are fixed as $D$ or the source-order pair changes. Thus this
new calculation transports to the original CIT25 factor with its
actual squared frame determinant; it does not silently choose a
different quotient volume.

For complete arithmetic rows at target degree $j$ keep every original
moment $\mu_d=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{u,w}\beta_{8,u,w}^{\,d}$, and put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \beta_{j,a,b}=j(1/2-\delta-i\gamma)+2a\delta+2bi\gamma,\qquad
 \nu_{a,n}=\sum_{u,w}(Z_{\exp,j})_{uw,a}\beta_{j,u,w}^{\,n},\\
 f_{a,n}=
 \frac{\nu_{a,n+v}-\sum_{d=v+1}^{n+v}\binom{n+v}{d}
                       \mu_d f_{a,n+v-d}}
      {\binom{n+v}{v}\mu_v},\\
 b_{-1,t}=0,\quad b_{0,t}=\mathbf1_{t=0},\qquad
 b_{n+1,t}=i^{-1}(b_{n,t-1}-c'_j b_{n,t})
                  -n(n-1+s_1/2)b_{n-1,t},\\
 t_n=\left(\frac{\sum_t b_{n,t}f_{a,t}}
                 {\sqrt{(2\pi)^{s_1/2}n!(s_1/2)_n}}\right)_a,
 \qquad
 v_n=\left(\frac{\sum_t b_{n,t}\beta_{j-8,u,w}^{\,t}}
                 {\sqrt{(2\pi)^{s_1/2}n!(s_1/2)_n}}\right)_{u,w}.
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB16}
\]
The first recursion follows by differentiating $E_AF_a=N_a$
$n+v$ times and isolating the term with $\mu_v$.
The second is the original monic Gamma recurrence after
$y=(S'-c'_j)/i$, with $1/i=-i$ retained. Thus $t_n,v_n$ are the
complex-linear evaluations in the original orthonormal frame.
The measure is exactly
\[
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
                |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy ,
 \qquad s=s_1\in\{1,j\}.
\]
No $\alpha_A$ is inserted in the division rows: it belongs to
the KRC coefficient transport already specified in RQB2.

Stack these rows through $D$ as $T_D,V_D$, and put
$\mathcal T_D=\operatorname{diag}(T_D,4)$,
$\mathcal V_D=\operatorname{diag}(V_D,4)$. Define the literal
full raw frames
\[
 Z_{{\rm inv},D}=[\mathcal V_D,\mathcal T_DY_{\rm inv}],
 \qquad
 Z_{W,D}=[Z_{{\rm inv},D},\mathcal T_DR_b],
 \qquad G_{X,D}=Z_{X,D}^*Z_{X,D}.
 \tag{RQB17}
\]
Every lower evaluation occurs. Both frames are independent:
a zero raw value modulo the lower evaluations gives a zero
coefficient class by the original upper Vandermonde at
$D+v\ge q_j-1$, and then the coefficient frames in RQB13
are independent. Full elimination, with the complete old
lower and invariant coefficients, therefore proves
\[
 Q_b^E(D)=(\mathcal T_DR_b)^*
                 (I-P_{Z_{{\rm inv},D}})\mathcal T_DR_b,
 \qquad
 \det Q_b^E(D)=\frac{\det G_{W,D}}{\det G_{{\rm inv},D}},
 \quad P_Z=Z(Z^*Z)^{-1}Z^* .
 \tag{RQB18}
\]
This is the full raw Gamma realization of the original residual
factor. It has no independent row-by-row gauge minimization.

\subsection{The exact original source denominator and compatibility transport}

Let $Z_Y$ be a fixed full basis of $Y$ and retain the complement
$E_b$ from RQB13. On the source put $G_0=D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}$.
Its quotient form in these fixed residual lifts is
\[
 Q_0(D)=E_b^*G_0E_b
 -E_b^*G_0Z_Y(Z_Y^*G_0Z_Y)^{-1}Z_Y^*G_0E_b .
 \tag{RQB19}
\]
Choose the first independent columns $Z_\Lambda$ of
$B_0Z_{\mathscr L}$ and write
$C_0=[Z_\Lambda,B_0E_b]$, an invertible full frame of
$\mathbb C^{t_{K,k}}$. The exact same fibre in RQB4 gives
\[
 \det Q_0(D)=
 \frac{|\det C_0|^2\det H_{B,k,D}}
      {\det(Z_\Lambda^*H_{B,k,D}Z_\Lambda)} .
 \tag{RQB20}
\]
Indeed the source quotient first minimizes over $J$ to obtain
the original $H_{B,k,D}$ and then over $B_0\mathscr L$.
The adapted full Gram has determinant
$|\det C_0|^2\det H_{B,k,D}$; eliminating its first block proves
RQB20. In full matrix form $Q_0$ is the Schur complement in the
lifts $B_0E_b$. This preserves both the source low overlap
$t_{0,k}$ and its further hidden kernel of rank $\ell-\epsilon$.

Let $A_D=B_{\rm cof}^*\mathcal D_D B_{\rm cof}$ and let $C_D$
be the full correlated pullback from NBG27 on $V$.
The notation $A_D/Y$, $C_D/Y$ means the attained quotient in
the fixed lifts $E_b$. By RQB3 the further target minimum
satisfies
\[
 0\prec Q_b^E\preceq A_D/Y\preceq C_D/Y,\qquad
 K_-^{-2}Q_0\preceq C_D/Y\preceq K_+^2Q_0 .
 \tag{RQB21}
\]
To prove the first comparison, all representatives in
$B_{\rm cof}E_bb+B_{\rm cof}Y$ are allowed in
$B_{\rm cof}E_bb+W_{\rm inv}$, while
$W_{\rm inv}\cap\operatorname{im}B_{\rm cof}=B_{\rm cof}Y$.
The native form on the former fibre is $A_D/Y$.
The remaining comparisons are NBG27 minimized on the same
source affine fibre; this establishes their exact typed maps.

Define
\[
 \omega_b(D)=\frac{\det Q_b^E(D)}{\det(A_D/Y)},\qquad
 e_b^{\rm cor}(D)=\log\frac{\det(C_D/Y)}{\det Q_0(D)},\qquad
 \omega_Z(D)=\frac{\det A_D|_Z}{\det C_D|_Z}\quad(Z=V,Y).
\]
The restriction determinants in the last ratio use identical fixed
frames. The adapted determinant identity cancels those frames and gives
\[
 \log\frac{\det Q_b^E}{\det Q_0}
 =e_b^{\rm cor}+\log\omega_V-\log\omega_Y+\log\omega_b ,
 \qquad 0<\omega_b\le1 .
 \tag{RQB22}
\]
Apply the complete PCJ13--16 shared-update proof to the actual
nested original source images $Y\subset V$; these are their
DNE functional classes modulo the original source lower evaluations.
Its source difference rank is exactly $r_b$ by RQB4.
The full compatibility-plus-source-complement forms are the unchanged
PCJ1 matrices; call their nonnegative logarithms $\chi_Y,\chi_V$.
Consequently
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \log\frac{\det Q_b^E(D)}{\det Q_0(D)}
       =-(\chi_Y(D)-\chi_V(D))+\log\omega_b(D)+\varepsilon_b(D),\\
 -2r_b\log\max(1,K_-)-r_b\log c_+
       \le\varepsilon_b(D)
       \le2r_b\log\max(1,K_+)-r_b\log c_- .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB23}
\]
Here $c_-=\min(1,L_-^{-2})$, $c_+=\max(1,L_+^2)$ are the
actual NKC3--4/13 constants at this same $k,D$ and source pair.
For the signs, PCJ16 says
$-\log(\omega_V/\omega_Y)=\chi_Y-\chi_V+\nu$ with
$r_b\log c_-\le\nu\le r_b\log c_+$.
The correlated quotient error is in
$[-2r_b\log\max(1,K_-),2r_b\log\max(1,K_+)]$ by NBG28.
Subtracting $\nu$ proves exactly RQB23.
Since $r_b\le t_{K,k}=O(k)$, the explicit error is
$O_{\rm actual}(k\log q_j)=o(kq_k)$ at the original four
target endpoints. The two full logarithmic losses remain.
In particular the proved one-sided native bound is
$Q_b^E\preceq K_+^2Q_0$, retaining RQB20's original source
denominator; an inverse estimate has not been assumed.

\subsection{Four original rows and the complete retained target budget}

For a new row degree $n\ge q_j-v$, use degree-major row order and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 z_{{\rm inv},n}=[\operatorname{diag}(v_n,4),
                       \operatorname{diag}(t_n,4)Y_{\rm inv}],
 \quad z_{W,n}=[z_{{\rm inv},n},\operatorname{diag}(t_n,4)R_b],\\
 L_{X,n}=z_{X,n}G_{X,n-1}^{-1}z_{X,n}^*,\\
 R_n=\operatorname{diag}(t_n,4)R_b
 -z_{{\rm inv},n}G_{{\rm inv},n-1}^{-1}
                   Z_{{\rm inv},n-1}^*\mathcal T_{n-1}R_b .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB24}
\]
The row permutation from copy-major order is fixed and unitary.
Eliminate the old $Z_{\rm inv}$ block in the full raw frame:
its Gram becomes $\operatorname{diag}(G_{\rm inv},Q_b^E)$
and its new row becomes $[z_{\rm inv},R_n]$. Thus
\[
\begin{gathered}
 L_{W,n}=L_{{\rm inv},n}
                    +R_nQ_b^E(n-1)^{-1}R_n^*,\\
 Q_b^E(n)=Q_b^E(n-1)
           +R_n^*(I_4+L_{{\rm inv},n})^{-1}R_n,\\
 \gamma_{b,n}:=\log\frac{\det Q_b^E(n)}{\det Q_b^E(n-1)}
       =\log\det(I_4+L_{W,n})-\log\det(I_4+L_{{\rm inv},n})
       \ge0 .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB25}
\]
For the middle equality, minimize the added squared row norm over
the old eliminated variables. Completing that square gives the
displayed inverse $I_4+L_{\rm inv}$, as can also be checked by
multiplication. The first identity and the rectangular determinant
lemma give the last one. All zero-energy rows and rank-zero
quotients are included; in the latter case $\gamma_{b,n}=0$.

Put $d=q_j-v-1$ and retain the original endpoint signs. The exact return is
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal V_b=-\log\det Q_b^E(d)-\log\det Q_b^E(d+1)
              +\log\det Q_b^E(d+q_j)+\log\det Q_b^E(d+q_j+1)\\
 \hfill=\sum_{n=d+1}^{d+q_j+1}w_n\gamma_{b,n}\ge0,\qquad
 w_{d+1}=w_{d+q_j+1}=1,\quad w_n=2\ \hbox{otherwise}.
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB26}
\]
Telescoping both low-to-high intervals proves every weight and sign.
The same return for $Q_b^{\rm CIT}$ is equal by RQB15: its
constant $2\log|\det C_b|$ cancels only under these four signs.

Complete $[U_R,U_b]$ by any fixed columns $U_o$ to a full frame
$C_T=[U_R,U_b,U_o]$ of the fourfold original kernel-dual space
$\mathbb C^{4t_{K,j}}$. Define $Q_o(D)$ as its full quotient metric
modulo $\operatorname{im}\Phi$ in the lifts $U_o$.
The first restriction in this frame is the exact ITL6 Gram
$H_{\rm inv}(D)=U_R^*\mathcal H_DU_R$. Sequential attained minima give
\[
 |\det C_T|^2(\det H_{B,j,D})^4
       =\det H_{\rm inv}(D)\det Q_b^E(D)\det Q_o(D).
 \tag{RQB27}
\]
For proof, first eliminate the $U_R$ block in
$C_T^*\mathcal H_DC_T$; its next diagonal Schur block is RQB14.
Eliminate that block; the remaining minimum allows precisely
$\operatorname{im}[U_R,U_b]=\operatorname{im}\Phi$ and is $Q_o$.
Thus every fixed-frame determinant is displayed. The missing rank is
$4t_{K,j}-\operatorname{rank}\Phi$, with no image-completeness assumption.

Each restriction and quotient in RQB27 increases as $D$ increases:
the original DNE metric increases by retaining additional orthonormal
rows, and taking minima on the same affine fibres preserves that
inequality. Therefore all three original weighted returns are
nonnegative. The same is true row by row, and HJR13,18,21 give
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}+\mathcal V_o
               =4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)},\\
 \mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
       =\mathcal R\log\det H_{\rm inv},\quad
 \mathcal V_o=\mathcal R\log\det Q_o,\qquad
 0\le\mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
                         \le4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)} .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB28}
\]
Here $\mathcal R$ has exactly the signs and cutoffs of RQB26.
Its $\widehat F_j$ is the original fixed-column kernel return,
since $H_{B,j,D}=\overline{\widehat H_{K,j,D}^{-1}}$.
The inverse changes the primal determinant sign exactly;
no equality with the earlier $F_K$ has been imposed.
The frame constant in RQB27 disappears only because the four
signs sum to zero. The inequality also holds on every single
row, with the right side $4g_{K,N}$ for that target row.
Thus the full aggregate budget retains zero-energy rows and
does not require a hypothesis about concentration.

For an explicit finite cap already proved for these same measures,
retain HJR24--25 at base degree $j$:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \widehat F_j^{(s_1)}+F_{0,j,s_1}
 =\mathcal F_{\partial,j}^{(s_1)}+\mathfrak e_{j,s_1}
       +\delta_{K,j,s_1}-F_{\Xi(L),j}^{(s_1)}-\delta_{0,j,s_1},\\
 |\delta_{K,j,s_1}|\le\mathcal E_{j,s_1}^{\rm FEX},\quad
 |\delta_{0,j,s_1}|\le E_{j,s_1}^{\rm tr},\quad
 -e_{j,s_1}^-\le\mathfrak e_{j,s_1}\le e_{j,s_1}^+,\quad
 F_{\Xi(L),j}^{(s_1)}\ge0,\\
 \mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
  \le4U_{j,s_1}^{\rm jet},\qquad
 U_{j,s_1}^{\rm jet}
 =\mathcal F_{\partial,j}^{(s_1)}
       +e_{j,s_1}^++\mathcal E_{j,s_1}^{\rm FEX}+E_{j,s_1}^{\rm tr}.
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB29}
\]
Every symbol here is the unchanged finite original OAR/FEX/PEC
quantity in the complete HJR proof. The exact equality is retained
before applying bounds. Both $F_{0,j,s_1}$ and the omitted positive
restriction are nonnegative by the original native row calculation.
Together with RQB28 this proves RQB29. HJR26's already proved
outer first variation gives
\[
 \mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
   \le4\Delta_jq_j\mathfrak C_\partial+o(kq_k)
                =O_{\rm actual}(kq_k),\qquad \Delta_j=16j-48 .
 \tag{RQB30}
\]
The actual period is fixed, and $j q_j/(kq_k)\to1$.
This is an aggregate upper bound on two specified positive
native volumes, with their full missing quotient retained in RQB28.
It does not assign an allocation or a leading value to either one.

Finally transport the calculation directly into the accepted CIT25--26.
In its original lifts use RQB15 and choose the fixed full frame $X_W$
there, so $[X_{\mathbb J},I_R,R_b^{\rm CIT}]=X_WC_W$.
Then the earlier complete equality remains literally
\[
 |\det C_W|^2g_W
    =g_{\mathbb J}\det H_{\rm inv}
                         |\det C_b|^2\det Q_b^E .
 \tag{RQB31}
\]
Substitution into CIT26 replaces its one retained factor
$\log\det Q_b^{\rm CIT}$ by
$\log\det Q_b^E+2\log|\det C_b|$. The raw calculation is RQB16--18,
its exact original source quotient is RQB19--20, and its two
independent native losses are RQB23. In the signed four-return
the frame constants cancel and the residual contribution obeys
the directed interval
\[
 -4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}
       +\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
             \le-\mathcal V_b\le0 .
 \tag{RQB32}
\]
Indeed RQB28 says
$\mathcal V_b\le4\widehat F_j-\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}$.
This is precisely the sign of the residual factor in CIT26.
Its kernel and source rank are computed by RQB4--12, and all
lower-gauge, source-complement, invariant-overlap and fixed-frame
terms remain in the exact transport. The common long connecting
loss is not replaced by this bound.

\subsection{The directed original kernel cap and its source correction}

There is a sharper finite version of RQB29 which uses the full original
positive observation return before bounding it. All quantities in this
paragraph have base degree $j$, source $s=s_1\in\{1,j\}$ and
$N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3=q_j-1,q_j,2q_j-1,2q_j$.
Retain the primal sign vector $\sigma=(1,1,-1,-1)$ in FEX.
With $t=t_{K,j}$ and $L_j=\mathbb C[S]_{<v}$, the exact FEX31/34
source correction is
\[
 \delta_{K,j,s}:=\widehat F_j^{(s)}-F_K^{(s)}
       =\sum_{i=0}^3\sigma_i e_{N_i,s}
                    -F_{L_j}^{(s)}+F_{(K_j+L_j)/K_j}^{(s)} .
 \tag{RQB33}
\]
The endpoint scalar $e_{N,s}$ is the actual logarithmic Jacobian
between the full original $(K_j+L_j)/L_j$ source and its conductor
image in FEX34. It is not a deleted low direction. For full numerical
coefficient expressions retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 M_N^A=\max\left\{1,\frac{v!}{|\mu_v|}
       \sum_{a,b=0}^8|a_{ab}|
         e^{|\beta_{8,a,b}-4|(H_N+2)}\right\},\quad m_N^A=\log M_N^A,\\
 w_{d,s}=d!/(s/2)_d,\quad
 \zeta_{N,s}=\sum_{d=v}^N\log(w_{d,s}/w_{d-v,s}),\\
 e^-_{N,s}=\zeta_{N,s}-2(N+1-v-t)m_N^A,\qquad
 e^+_{N,s}=2t m_N^A,\\
 D_s^+=e^+_{q_j-1,s}+e^+_{q_j,s}
                    -e^-_{2q_j-1,s}-e^-_{2q_j,s},\\
 \mathcal E_{j,s}^{\rm FEX}
       =v\mathcal L_s+2\max_i|\zeta_{N_i,s}|
                           +2(4q_j+1)m_{2q_j}^A .
\end{gathered}\tag{RQB34}
\]
Here $H_N$ is the harmonic sum and $\mathcal L_s$ is the original
AKS two-window constant at degree $j$. The explicit alternative
FEX33 is available with its unchanged target degree and measure.
FEX34 proves $e^-_{N,s}\le e_{N,s}\le e^+_{N,s}$ using the
whole Gamma Jacobian and the actual conductor norm.
The original two low quotient metrics give
$0\le F_{L_j}\le v\mathcal L_s$ and
$0\le F_{(K_j+L_j)/K_j}\le(v-t_{0,j})\mathcal L_s$.
Thus RQB33 proves
$\delta_{K,j,s}\le D_s^++(v-t_{0,j})\mathcal L_s$.
FEX36--37 independently proves
$|\delta_{K,j,s}|\le\mathcal E_{j,s}^{\rm FEX}$.
These two bounds retain the original source mass in their proved
Gamma norm ratios and keep $t_{0,j}$ before estimating.

Let $\Phi_{j,s}$ be exactly OAR4's full positive sum
$\log(1+\eta_{q_j-1})+
2\sum_{N=q_j}^{2q_j-2}\log(1+\eta_N)+\log(1+\eta_{2q_j-1})$.
All $\eta_N$ are the original ROQ constrained rows with their complete
conductor-observation covariance. If $U_{j,s}^{\rm old}$ denotes the
retained AKS/KAF kernel cap, OAR3/7 and RQB33--34 give the finite number
\[
\begin{split}
 U_{j,s}^{\rm native}
   :=\min\bigl\{U_{j,s}^{\rm jet},\
       &\min\{U_{j,s}^{\rm old},
              \mathcal F_{\partial,j}^{(s)}+e_{j,s}^+-\Phi_{j,s}\}\\
       &+\min\{D_s^++(v-t_{0,j})\mathcal L_s,
                                     \mathcal E_{j,s}^{\rm FEX}\}\bigr\},\\
 0\le\mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}
             &\le4\widehat F_j^{(s)}
                       \le4U_{j,s}^{\rm native}.
\end{split}\tag{RQB35}
\]
Indeed the inner first minimum bounds the original $F_K$ from above,
using its full residual subtraction. The second minimum bounds the
exact difference $\widehat F_j-F_K$. Their sum bounds $\widehat F_j$;
HJR25 supplies the other cap. This proves the outer minimum without
identifying the two different kernel metrics or assuming a new
conditioning estimate. Replacing the full $\Phi$ by only its first
term recovers the earlier OAR6 partial cap, whereas RQB35 retains
all computed positive terms.

Finally FEX37 transfers the proved OVR9 limit superior to this exact
fixed-column kernel return. Therefore, with the original
$\kappa_*=32\log(25\log3/(4\pi))$ and the same
$\mathfrak C_\partial$ as in RQB30,
\[
 0\le\limsup_{k\to\infty}
       \frac{\mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_{\rm inv}^{\rm target}}{kq_k}
       \le4\min\{\kappa_*,16\mathfrak C_\partial\}.
 \tag{RQB36}
\]
For proof divide RQB28 by $kq_k$, insert
$j q_j/(kq_k)\to1$ and
$(\widehat F_j-F_K)/(j q_j)\to0$, then apply OVR9 separately
in each unchanged source order. This proves only the displayed
upper bound on the sum of two nonnegative exact volumes.
Both remain present in the identity RQB28, with its full
missing quotient; no individual limit or allocation is presumed.

\paragraph{The complete missing target quotient and its connecting restriction.}
Keep every block of the original $C_T=[U_R,U_b,U_o]$ in RQB27.
At both original source-order pairs use
$\pi_o=[0\ 0\ I_{r_o}]C_T^{-1}\mathbb B$,
$E_e=\pi_oF_e$, $c_e=\operatorname{rank}E_e$.
The kernel of $\pi_o$ is exactly $W$ by the equation
$\mathbb Bz=\Phi x$, and its restriction to $\mathscr S_e$
has kernel $K_e=\mathscr S_e\cap W$.
Choose the original fixed complement $Z_c$ of $\ker E_e$
and $C_o=[E_eZ_c,R_e^o]$. The complete two target minima are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 G_{t,e}=(E_eZ_c)^*Q_o(E_eZ_c),\qquad
 Q_{{\rm rem},e}=\operatorname{Gram}(\mathcal U/T_e)
       \ \hbox{in the lifts }R_e^o,\quad T_e=\mathscr S_e+W,\\
 |\det C_T|^2|\det C_o|^2(\det H_{B,j,D})^4
       =\det H_{\rm inv}\det Q_b^E
                         \det G_{t,e}\det Q_{{\rm rem},e},\\
 \operatorname{rank}G_{t,e}=c_e,\qquad
 \operatorname{rank}Q_{{\rm rem},e}=r_o-c_e .
 \end{gathered}\tag{RQB37}
\]
The quotient form on $\mathcal U/W$ first minimizes over every
lower evaluation and $\mathbb J$, then over $\operatorname{im}\Phi$,
and is exactly $Q_o$. On the connecting values its restriction
is therefore the first line. The further minimum over the whole
connecting image gives the stated quotient on $\mathcal U/T_e$.
Eliminating the first block of $C_o^*Q_oC_o$ proves
$|\det C_o|^2\det Q_o=\det G_t\det Q_{\rm rem}$.
Multiplication by the original RQB27 identity proves every
absolute factor in RQB37.

Set $V_t=\mathcal R\log\det G_{t,e}$ and
$V_{\rm rem}=\mathcal R\log\det Q_{{\rm rem},e}$.
The entire original budget now reads
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 V_o=V_t+V_{\rm rem},\qquad V_t,V_{\rm rem}\ge0,\\
 V_I+V_b+V_t+V_{\rm rem}=4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)},\\
 -4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}+V_I+V_t
                   \le -V_b\le0,\\
 -(r_o-c_e)\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le\Delta V_{\rm rem}
                   \le(r_o-c_e)\Omega_j^{\rm lo},\qquad
 -c_e\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le\Delta V_t\le c_e\Omega_j^{\rm lo}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{RQB38}
\]
The original increasing row metric preserves order under both
complete minima, proving positivity. Taking the four signs of
RQB37 cancels only its fixed frame determinants. The third line
uses $V_{\rm rem}\ge0$ after retaining its exact value in the
second. The last line uses NSO's same-fibre comparison at the
two actual ranks, with $D=q_j-v-1,q_j-v,2q_j-v-1,2q_j-v$ and
$s_1=1,j$. It retains the original low overlaps in $r_o$.

For a literal calculation of the new quotient choose full
coefficient columns $X_T$ spanning $T_e$ and fixed lifts $X_Q$
under $\mathbb B$ of $U_oR_e^o$. Through the old degree $D$ put
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 Z=[\mathcal V_D,\mathcal T_DX_T],\quad G=Z^*Z,\quad
 Q=(\mathcal T_DX_Q)^*(I-ZG^{-1}Z^*)\mathcal T_DX_Q,\\
 a=[\operatorname{diag}(v_{D+1},4),
             \operatorname{diag}(t_{D+1},4)X_T],\quad
 b=\operatorname{diag}(t_{D+1},4)X_Q,\\
 R=b-aG^{-1}Z^*\mathcal T_DX_Q,\quad L=aG^{-1}a^*,\\
 Q^+=Q+R^*(I_4+L)^{-1}R,\qquad
 \log\frac{\det Q^+}{\det Q}
   =\log\frac{\det(I_4+L+RQ^{-1}R^*)}{\det(I_4+L)}\ge0 .
 \end{gathered}\tag{RQB39}
\]
The original RQB16 rows retain all division moments, the phase
$1/i$, the centres and $h_{n,s_1}=(2\pi)^{s_1/2}n!(s_1/2)_n$.
DNE positivity and the full coefficient frames prove that $Z$
and $[Z,\mathcal T_DX_Q]$ are independent even at the first
cutoff. Their minimum is exactly $Q_{\rm rem}$.
Subtracting the old attained section leaves
$\eta^*G\eta+\|Rx-a\eta\|^2$; its minimum proves the last line,
including the original old lower Gram. The return uses weights
one at the first and last rows and two inside. Thus no new
row model or independent rowwise gauge minimum was substituted.

% Full finite receiving of accepted NSO in the original native returns.
\section{Source-order intervals in the original connecting and invariant returns}
\label{sec:NSR}
This section receives the accepted NSO1--31 proof into the exact
CIT28, NCR20, LCC36, CIT30/RQB and NFR6 formulas. It preserves
their full source and target quotients. The scalar polynomial
estimate remains the already proved AMT23,29--30 estimate, with
the exact native dual square NSO7--15; it is not a new analytic
bound. Keep the actual simple quartet, the proved five-orbit
domain $|u|\ge R_*$, fixed logarithm branch, nonreduced conductor,
all original coefficients and
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 k=4l+1\ge9,\quad j=k+4,\quad q_a=(a+1)^2,\quad q'_a=(a-7)^2,
 \quad c'_a=a/2-4,\\
 0\le v\le80,\quad \mu_v\ne0,\quad
 m_a=16a-48-v,\quad
 t_a=8a-16-t_{0,a},\quad j_{0,a}=8a-32-v+t_{0,a},\\
 p=0:(s_0,s_1)=(1,1),\qquad p=1:(s_0,s_1)=(k,j),\\
 D_0=q_j-v-1,\quad D_1=q_j-v,\quad
 D_2=2q_j-v-1,\quad D_3=2q_j-v,\\
 \mathcal Rf=-f(D_0)-f(D_1)+f(D_2)+f(D_3).
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR1}
\]
Here $t_{0,a}$ is the actual dimension of the original kernel's
intersection with the low polynomial space. A superscript $p$
always means this complete original pair, and
$\Delta Z=Z^1-Z^0$. The $D_i$ are polynomial cutoffs.
They remain the same for source degree $k$ and target degree $j$.

\subsection{The finite native intervals and their exact value spaces}
For $s=4\ell_s+1$ retain the full NSO constants
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \rho_s=\frac{(2\pi)^{2\ell_s}}{(1/2)_{2\ell_s}},\quad
 \Pi_s=\prod_{h<\ell_s}(2h+\tfrac12)^2,\quad
 U_{s,D}=\prod_{h<\ell_s}\{4(D+h+1)^2+(2h+\tfrac12)^2\},\\
 \mathfrak a_s=\rho_s\Pi_s,\quad
 \mathfrak b_{s,D}=\rho_sU_{s,D},\quad
 \omega_{s,D}=\log(\mathfrak b_{s,D}/\mathfrak a_s),\\
 \Omega_s^{\rm lo}=\omega_{s,D_0}+\omega_{s,D_1},\qquad
 \Omega_s^{\rm hi}=\omega_{s,D_2}+\omega_{s,D_3},\qquad
 W_s=\Omega_s^{\rm lo}+\Omega_s^{\rm hi}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR2}
\]
All $s=1$ products equal one and all its widths are zero.
The underlying measures remain
$dm_s=(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}|\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2
dy/(4\pi\Gamma(s/2))$.

For a specified rank-$r$ native dual restriction or quotient
$G_X^{(s)}(D)$ at one fixed degree and center, put
\[
 L_X(D)=\log\frac{\det G_X^{(s)}(D)}{\det G_X^{(1)}(D)}.
 \quad
 -r\log\mathfrak b_{s,D}\le L_X(D)\le-r\log\mathfrak a_s,
 \quad
 -r\Omega_s^{\rm hi}\le\mathcal RL_X\le r\Omega_s^{\rm lo}.
 \tag{NSR3}
\]
These are exactly NSO16 and NSO19, at the explicitly specified
$D_i$. To recall their precise map, let
$S'^n=\sum_h(\mathsf B_{s,c,D})_{hn}\phi_{h,s,c}$ with
$\phi_{h,s,c}=p_{h,s}((S'-c)/i)/\sqrt{(2\pi)^{s/2}h!(s/2)_h}$.
The derivative values of a complex-linear functional are
$\mathsf B_s^{\mathsf T}w_s$, and its Taylor values are
$U_sw_s$, $U_s=\operatorname{diag}(1/n!)\mathsf B_s^{\mathsf T}$.
Consequently
\[
 w_s=(\mathsf B_s^{\mathsf T})^{-1}\mathsf B_1^{\mathsf T}w_1,
 \qquad
 G^\vee_s=\overline{H_s^{-1}},\qquad
 \mathfrak b_{s,D}^{-1}G^\vee_1
       \preceq G^\vee_s\preceq\mathfrak a_s^{-1}G^\vee_1 .
 \tag{NSR4}
\]
The same map carries every numerator and every lower evaluation.
On a fixed fibre $Xx+Zz$ the full minimum is
$X^*GX-X^*GZ(Z^*GZ)^{-1}Z^*GX$ in the value $x$.
Applying NSR4 to all members of that fibre preserves its two
constants. Every restriction, subsequent quotient and fixed
direct sum therefore has the same inequality at its final rank.
Relative eigenvalues prove the first interval in NSR3.
Writing $L_X=-r\log\mathfrak a_s-u_D$ with
$0\le u_D\le r\omega_{s,D}$ gives
$\mathcal RL_X=u_{D_0}+u_{D_1}-u_{D_2}-u_{D_3}$ and proves
the second interval with its directed signs. This recalls
the full established transport, without deleting any raw gauge.

All scalar and frame factors in NSR3 remain until this same
value space is identified. In particular a fixed change $R$
of quotient lifts changes the Gram by $R^*GR$, giving
$|\det R|^2$ in its determinant; the factor cancels only in
the proved same-frame order ratio or in $\mathcal R$.
NSO6 gives, for $s=k,j$ and every $D_i$ here,
$\log\mathfrak b_{s,D_i}=O(k\log(q_j+1))$.
Thus all ensuing rank-$O(k)$ intervals are $o(kq_k)$.
This statement does not give that conclusion on an
$O(q_k)$-rank raw polynomial plane.

\subsection{The two low source and image determinants at their actual ranks}
For $e=0,1$ put $D_e=q_j-v-1+e$ and
$r_e=8k+24-3e$. Retain the actual CIT primitive Taylor
complement $P_e^{\rm Tay}$. In the two source orders
$P_e^{(s_0)}=U_{s_0,c'_k,D_e}^{-1}P_e^{\rm Tay}$.
The source norm minimizes over all columns $[V_0,T_0]$.
The target coefficient $F_e$ is uniquely determined by
\[
 \operatorname{stack}_i\operatorname{Mult}(B_i)P_e^{\rm Tay}
  =\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde T_j,4)F_e
       +\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde V_j,4)E_e^{\rm low}.
 \tag{NSR5}
\]
The matrix $\operatorname{Mult}(B_i)[n,h]$ is
$B_i^{(n-h)}(0)/(n-h)!$ for $n\ge h$, and zero otherwise.
The two Taylor columns on the right are the same actual
analytic germs and lower exponentials in both orders.
If two representations differ, multiplication by $E_A$
makes the numerator vanish through $D_e+v\ge q_j-1$;
the full original upper Vandermonde kills its coefficient
class, and the lower Vandermonde then kills the lower
difference. This proves uniqueness of $F_e$.
Existence is the original primitive condition: all jets
are allowed at $e=0$, while at $e=1$ the four rows
$\Lambda(\mathcal T_{B_i}\mathcal T_A\chi_j)$ impose exactly
the missing target coordinate in each copy.
Their rank four gives
$(D_e+1-4e)-(q_k-v)=r_e$.
The positive connecting image has this same rank by
CIT9's full kernel
$\operatorname{im}\mathsf T\cap\mathcal L_1
=\mathsf T\mathcal L_0$.

Write $L_{{\rm src},e}=\Delta\log\det H_{{\rm src},e}$
and $L_{{\rm conn},e}=\Delta\log\det H_{{\rm conn},e}$
at this defining cutoff. Define the literal four endpoints
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 s_e^-=-r_e\log\mathfrak b_{k,D_e},\qquad
 s_e^+=-r_e\log\mathfrak a_k,\qquad
 t_e^-=-r_e\log\mathfrak b_{j,D_e},\qquad
 t_e^+=-r_e\log\mathfrak a_j,\\
 s_e^-\le L_{{\rm src},e}\le s_e^+,\qquad
 t_e^-\le L_{{\rm conn},e}\le t_e^+ .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR6}
\]
The source fibre is $P_ex+T_0\xi+V_0\eta$; the image fibre
first minimizes every target lower evaluation, then
$F_ex+B_{\rm cof}\xi$. Equation NSR4 proves both intervals
on these complete fibres. In particular the number
multiplying the logarithm is $r_e$, not the number of raw
columns used to implement the minimum.

Retain the complete original CIT17 errors, with no new
constant substituted:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal X_e^p=\chi_{X_k,D_e}^p-\chi_{Y_e,D_e}^p,\quad
 A_e^p=\log\det H_{{\rm conn},e}^p-\log\det H_{{\rm src},e}^p
        =-\mathcal X_e^p+\epsilon_e^p,\\
 \epsilon_e^{p,-}
  =-2r_e\log\max(1,K_-^p)-r_e\log c_+^p,\qquad
 \epsilon_e^{p,+}
  =2r_e\log\max(1,K_+^p)-r_e\log c_-^p,\\
 c_-^p=\min(1,(L_-^p)^{-2}),\quad
 c_+^p=\max(1,(L_+^p)^2),\qquad
 b_e^-=\epsilon_e^{1,-}-\epsilon_e^{0,+},\quad
 b_e^+=\epsilon_e^{1,+}-\epsilon_e^{0,-}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR7}
\]
The $K_\pm,L_\pm$ are the original NBG/NKC constants,
including their degree-$k+8$ comparison, all coefficients,
centers and full measures. They give precisely the
CIT17/PCJ16 interval for $\epsilon_e^p$.

CIT28, NCR20 and the second line of LCC36 now have the
following full finite receiving:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 L_{{\rm conn},e}=-\Delta\mathcal X_e+\zeta_e,\qquad
 \zeta_e=L_{{\rm src},e}+\epsilon_e^1-\epsilon_e^0,\\
 s_e^-+b_e^-\le\zeta_e\le s_e^++b_e^+,\\
 s_e^-+b_e^- -t_e^+
      \le\Delta\mathcal X_e
      \le s_e^++b_e^+ -t_e^- .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR8}
\]
Subtracting the two exact equalities in NSR7 proves the
first line. Add the source and error intervals to get
the second. Solve the first line for $\Delta\mathcal X_e$
and use both actual target endpoints in NSR6 to obtain
the third. Thus both low determinant ratios and this
complete compatibility difference are $o(kq_k)$ with
the displayed finite bounds. At $e=0$ only,
$\chi_{Y_0}=0$ because its source complement is zero.
The four constraints and $\chi_{Y_1}$ remain at $e=1$.

The earlier PSR26 finite source interval also remains
valid on its own domain
\[
 k\ge\max(9,v-1,\lceil(\pi/2)\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}\rceil+1)
\]
and its original admissible SGT14 radius $0<a<1$.
On that domain replace $[s_e^-,s_e^+]$ in NSR8 by its
intersection with PSR26's full centered interval, retaining
$D_e^{\rm ord}$ and both PSR errors. Both intervals contain
the same source ratio; their intersection does too.
No PSR domain is imposed on NSR6 itself.
The factors in the source determinant are unchanged since
\[
 (C_e^{(s)})^{\mathsf T}P_e^{(s)}
   =(R_e^{\rm pol})^{\mathsf T}\operatorname{diag}(n!)P_e^{\rm Tay}.
 \tag{NSR9}
\]
This follows by canceling the exact $\mathsf B_s^{\mathsf T}$
with its inverse from $U_s^{-1}$. It proves equality of
the full $\Theta_e$ and the actual complement/frame factors
in PSR23 before any order comparison.

\subsection{Exact connecting and full-cohomology contraction return}
Set $\mathcal V=\mathbb C^{m_k}$,
$\mathcal U=\mathbb C^{4m_j}$, and keep
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 B=B_{\rm cof},\quad \mathbb B=\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4),
 \quad \mathbb J=\ker\mathbb B,\quad \Phi=\mathbb B B,\\
 \mathscr B=\operatorname{im}B,\quad
 \mathscr S_e=\operatorname{im}[B,F_e],\quad
 W=\mathbb J+\mathscr B,\quad
 K_e=\mathscr S_e\cap W,\quad T_e=\mathscr S_e+W,\\
 c_e=\operatorname{rank}[\Phi,\mathbb BF_e]
                      -\operatorname{rank}\Phi,\qquad
 h_e=r_e-c_e .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR10}
\]
Here $B$ is injective on the whole original conductor
coefficient source, as proved by CNC/DFX, so
$\dim\mathscr B=m_k$ and $\dim\mathscr S_e=m_k+r_e$.
The map
\[
 \mathscr S_e\longrightarrow
       \operatorname{im}[\Phi,\mathbb BF_e]/\operatorname{im}\Phi,
 \qquad z\longmapsto[\mathbb Bz]
 \tag{NSR11}
\]
is onto and its kernel is exactly $K_e$: its value is zero
precisely when $z-Bx\in\ker\mathbb B=\mathbb J$ for some
$x$. Thus $\dim(\mathscr S_e/K_e)=c_e$ and
$\dim(K_e/\mathscr B)=h_e$. The identical representative
map identifies $\mathscr S_e/K_e$ with $T_e/W$.
Both are the exact quotient-value spaces in CIT21.
In particular $0\le c_e\le r_e$.

At every later cutoff the common ambient form is
$\mathcal D_D^{(s_1)}=\operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D}^{(s_1)},4)$.
Let $G_{e,S}^p,G_{e,T}^p$ be its two full quotient metrics
on $\mathscr S_e/K_e$ and $T_e/W$, in the same ordered
values under NSR11. Then
\[
 \omega_{{\rm conn},e}^p(D)=
            \frac{\det G_{e,T}^p(D)}{\det G_{e,S}^p(D)},\qquad
 \left|\Delta\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}(D)\right|
                       \le c_e\omega_{j,D},\qquad
 \left|\Delta\mathcal R\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}\right|
                       \le c_eW_j .
 \tag{NSR12}
\]
To prove the middle inequality, each quotient in NSR11
has rank $c_e$ and the constants in NSR3. Subtract its
two endpoint log intervals. The common term
$-c_e\log\mathfrak a_j$ cancels, leaving
$[-c_e\omega_{j,D},c_e\omega_{j,D}]$.
For the last inequality apply the original signs
$(-,-,+,+)$ to these four symmetric intervals.
The entire lower gauges and the two different larger
fibres $K_e,W$ are retained in this argument.
There is no supposition that their minimum representatives
or angles coincide. This is an explicit finite bound
for the common-return order difference in CIT21/NCR14.

The full low cohomology has the fixed cycle space
$\mathscr Z_e\supset\mathscr S_e$, with
$\dim(\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr S_e)=\kappa_e\le3\max(v-e,0)$.
Its boundary remains $\mathscr B$. On the same native
form, the source-order log ratio of its full cohomology
Gram has rank $r_e+\kappa_e$, and that of its remaining
quotient by the connecting image has rank $\kappa_e$:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 -(r_e+\kappa_e)\log\mathfrak b_{j,D}
 \le\Delta\log\det\operatorname{Gram}(\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr B)
 \le-(r_e+\kappa_e)\log\mathfrak a_j,\\
 -\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le
 \Delta\mathcal R\log\det\operatorname{Gram}
                          (\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr S_e)
 \le\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm lo}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR13}
\]
The exact connecting inclusion identifies its cohomology
quotient with $\ker U_e$ by LCC13; these are the attained
metrics of that same flag. Completing a fixed frame of
$\mathscr S_e/\mathscr B$ gives the full determinant
as its restriction determinant times this last quotient
determinant and the fixed adapted-frame factor. Thus
NSR13 retains the whole quotient in the last paragraph
of LCC36, rather than erasing it.

There is also a rank-exact comparison of the contractions.
Put $K_e^{\rm full}=\mathscr Z_e\cap W$ and
$T_e^{\rm full}=\mathscr Z_e+W$. The representative map gives
\[
 0\longrightarrow K_e^{\rm full}/K_e
 \longrightarrow\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr S_e
 \longrightarrow T_e^{\rm full}/T_e\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad d_K+d_T=\kappa_e .
 \tag{NSR14}
\]
The kernel proof is $z\in\mathscr Z_e$ maps into $T_e$
exactly when $z-s\in W$ for some $s\in\mathscr S_e$;
hence its kernel modulo $\mathscr S_e$ is
$(\mathscr S_e+K_e^{\rm full})/\mathscr S_e
\simeq K_e^{\rm full}/K_e$.
The adapted determinant identities for the two cross
ratios then express $\log\omega_e-\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}$
as the sum of the $T_e^{\rm full}/T_e$ and
$K_e^{\rm full}/K_e$ log determinants, minus that of
$\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr S_e$, with their fixed frame constant.
Since $d_T+d_K=\kappa_e$, NSR3 gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \left|\Delta(\log\omega_e-\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e})(D)\right|
                      \le\kappa_e\omega_{j,D},\\
 \left|\Delta\mathcal R(\log\omega_e-\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e})\right|
                      \le\kappa_eW_j,\qquad
 \left|\Delta\mathcal R\log\omega_e\right|
                      \le(c_e+d_T)W_j\le(c_e+\kappa_e)W_j .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR15}
\]
For the first bound subtract the three intervals in
NSR3: their common scalar has coefficient
$d_T+d_K-\kappa_e=0$, and both remaining one-sided
widths are $\kappa_e\omega_{j,D}$. Taking the four
signs proves the second. The full quotient map
$\mathscr Z_e/(\mathscr Z_e\cap W)\to(\mathscr Z_e+W)/W$
has rank $c_e+d_T$ by the exact sum inclusion in NSR14.
Applying the same two-minimum proof as NSR12 at that
actual rank gives the third inequality; $d_T\le\kappa_e$
gives its last bound. These new order intervals coexist with NCR19's
$|\mathcal R\log\omega_e^p-
\mathcal R\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}^p|
\le2\kappa_e\log A_{j,s_1}^{\rm end}$ for each original
pair. In particular the middle upper bound in NSR15
can be replaced by the minimum of its displayed value
and $2\kappa_e(\log A_{j,j}^{\rm end}+\log A_{j,1}^{\rm end})$.
Both concern that same actual scalar difference.

\paragraph{The sharper tail ranks in the same source-order returns.}
Retain all NSR1--15 objects on their original five-orbit domain,
including both complete native pairs, their fixed coefficient
frames and the displaced polynomial cutoffs $D_0,D_1,D_2,D_3$.
The widths are exactly those of NSR2:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\omega_{j,D}=\log(\mathfrak b_{j,D}/\mathfrak a_j),\qquad
\Omega_j^{\rm lo}=\omega_{j,D_0}+\omega_{j,D_1},\\
\Omega_j^{\rm hi}=\omega_{j,D_2}+\omega_{j,D_3},\qquad
W_j=\sum_{a=0}^3\omega_{j,D_a}
   =\Omega_j^{\rm lo}+\Omega_j^{\rm hi}.
\end{gathered}
\]
The complete BTK25--26 calculation gives
$v\le32$ and $\kappa_e\le\overline\kappa_e:=3\max(v-e-2,0)$.
In NSR14, its first quotient also has the exact presentation
\[
\frac{\mathscr Z_e\cap\mathbb J}{\mathscr S_e\cap\mathbb J}
 \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_e^{\rm full}/K_e,
 \qquad[j]\mapsto[j].
\]
An element of $K_e^{\rm full}$ is $b+j$ with
$b\in\mathscr B\subset\mathscr S_e$ and
$j\in\mathscr Z_e\cap\mathbb J$; this proves surjectivity.
Its kernel is exactly $\mathscr S_e\cap\mathbb J$,
proving the isomorphism. The following arrow of NSR14 is
the identity $[z]\mapsto[z]$ onto
$(\mathscr Z_e+W)/(\mathscr S_e+W)$.
It is onto since the terms of $W$ disappear in the target;
its kernel is precisely the first quotient, because
$z=s+b+j$ implies $[z]=[j]$.
Consequently $d_K+d_T=\kappa_e$. The full quotient-value map is
\[
\Psi_e:\mathscr Z_e/(\mathscr Z_e\cap W)
 \longrightarrow\mathbb B\mathscr Z_e/\operatorname{im}\Phi,
 \qquad[z]\longmapsto[\mathbb Bz].
\]
It is onto by the displayed target, and its kernel is zero:
$\mathbb Bz=\Phi x$ means $z-Bx\in\mathbb J$, hence
$z\in\mathscr Z_e\cap W$. Thus its rank is
$\dim((\mathscr Z_e+W)/W)=c_e+d_T$, by the sum-quotient
sequence with subspace $(\mathscr S_e+W)/W$ of dimension $c_e$.
With the unchanged $\Delta Z=Z^1-Z^0$ and
$\mathcal Rf=-f(D_0)-f(D_1)+f(D_2)+f(D_3)$, the sharpened
finite intervals are
\[
\begin{gathered}
0\le d_T\le\kappa_e\le\overline\kappa_e,\\
\left|\Delta\mathcal R\log\omega_e\right|
 \le(c_e+d_T)W_j\le(c_e+\overline\kappa_e)W_j,\\
\left|\Delta\mathcal R
 (\log\omega_e-\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e})\right|
 \le\kappa_e W_j\le\overline\kappa_e W_j,\\
-\overline\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm hi}
 \le\Delta\mathcal R\log\det\operatorname{Gram}
             (\mathscr Z_e/\mathscr S_e)
 \le\overline\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm lo}.
\end{gathered}\tag{RKR3}
\]
To verify the constants and signs, for any one of the fixed
quotients $X$ of rank $r$ put
$L_X(D)=\Delta\log\det\operatorname{Gram}(X)$.
The full native form inequality NSR4 and its same affine
minima give
$L_X(D)=-r\log\mathfrak a_j-u_X(D)$ with
$0\le u_X(D)\le r\omega_{j,D}$.
The complete contraction of rank $c_e+d_T$ has two such
quotient forms in the same value coordinates; subtracting
their intervals cancels the common scalar and gives the
pointwise width $(c_e+d_T)\omega_{j,D}$.
The adapted determinant identity for the difference between
the full and connecting contractions uses the quotients
of ranks $d_K,d_T,\kappa_e$ with signs $(+,+,-)$.
Its same-frame order difference is
$-u_K-u_T+u_{\mathscr Z/\mathscr S}$, since
$d_K+d_T-\kappa_e=0$.
It lies in $[-\kappa_e\omega_{j,D},
\kappa_e\omega_{j,D}]$. Applying the four original signs
proves the two symmetric intervals in RKR3.
For the single quotient of rank $\kappa_e$, those signs give
$u(D_0)+u(D_1)-u(D_2)-u(D_3)$, hence the directed interval
$[-\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm hi},\kappa_e\Omega_j^{\rm lo}]$.
Every width is nonnegative. Substituting BTK25 therefore
proves the last inequalities without changing their directions.

The middle interval may also be intersected with the original
two-family bound from CIT23. Its absolute value is at most
\[
\min\!\left\{\overline\kappa_e W_j,
 2\overline\kappa_e
  (\log A_{j,j}^{\rm end}+\log A_{j,1}^{\rm end})\right\}.
\]
Both entries bound the same scalar in the same frames, by the
triangle inequality applied to the two original family returns.
For $v-e\le2$, the actual spaces satisfy
$\mathscr Z_e=\mathscr S_e$, so $d_K=d_T=0$ and
$\omega_e^p(D)=\omega_{{\rm conn},e}^p(D)$ at every retained
cutoff, $p=0,1$. The tail terms in RKR3 are then zero.
The first NSR13 interval retains its exact rank
$r_e+\kappa_e$; the present calculation inserts no value
for the remaining connecting rank $c_e$ or its absolute loss.

\subsection{The complete boundary ranks, metrics and positive returns}
Write $J=\operatorname{im}J_0=\ker B_k$ and retain the
full original coefficient kernels
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathscr L=\ker\Phi,\quad \ell=\dim\mathscr L,\quad
 E=\ker(\Phi J_0),\quad\epsilon=\dim E,\quad Y=J+\mathscr L,\\
 r_I=j_{0,k}-\epsilon,\qquad
 r_b=t_k-\ell+\epsilon,\qquad
 r_o=4t_j-m_k+\ell,\qquad r_I+r_b+r_o=4t_j .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR16}
\]
No symbol $\ell$ here replaces the source-order index
$\ell_s$ in NSR2. Since $J_0$ is injective,
$J\cap\mathscr L=J_0E$ and $\dim Y=j_{0,k}+\ell-\epsilon$.
Hence $\operatorname{rank}\Phi=m_k-\ell$,
$\operatorname{rank}\Phi J_0=r_I$, and its image quotient
has dimension $r_b$. Completing its full image in
$\mathbb C^{4t_j}$ gives $r_o$ and the last equality.
All three ranks are nonnegative actual ranks, with both
original low intersections and every hidden coefficient
kernel present.

Keep the original full lift frames $Z_R$ of the invariant
source modulo $E$, $E_b$ of $\mathcal V/Y$, and $U_o$
completing the image frame
\[
 U_R=\Phi J_0Z_R,\quad U_b=\Phi E_b,\quad
 C_T=[U_R,U_b,U_o]\quad\hbox{invertible}.
 \tag{NSR17}
\]
The target form is four copies of the original
$H_{B,j,D}^{(s_1)}
=[B_j(D_{j,D}^{(s_1)})^{-1}B_j^*]^{-1}$.
Its restriction on $U_R$ is $H_{\rm inv}$, its next
quotient in lifts $U_b$ is $Q_b^E$, and its quotient
modulo $\operatorname{im}\Phi$ in lifts $U_o$ is $Q_o$.
Their ranks are $r_I,r_b,r_o$. Before any return,
\[
 |\det C_T|^2(\det H_{B,j,D}^{(s_1)})^4
       =\det H_{\rm inv}^p(D)\det Q_b^{E,p}(D)\det Q_o^p(D).
 \tag{NSR18}
\]
Successively minimize the first and then the second
coordinate blocks in this fixed frame to prove the identity.
In original conductor coordinates the same fibres are
$\mathbb J+\operatorname{im}(BJ_0)$ and
$\mathbb J+\operatorname{im}B$, because
$\ker\mathbb B=\mathbb J$. Thus all lower and invariant
gauges are included. The map $\mathcal V/Y\to W/W_{\rm inv}$
is $[x]\mapsto[Bx]$; its full zero fibre is
$x-J_0z\in\mathscr L$. This also proves the original
RQB quotient identification used in CIT29.

Let
$\mathcal V_I^p=\mathcal R\log\det H_{\rm inv}^p$,
$\mathcal V_b^p=\mathcal R\log\det Q_b^{E,p}$ and
$\mathcal V_o^p=\mathcal R\log\det Q_o^p$.
Then the complete original budget and its order receiving are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal V_I^p+\mathcal V_b^p+\mathcal V_o^p
                      =4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)},\qquad
 \mathcal V_I^p,\mathcal V_b^p,\mathcal V_o^p\ge0,\\
 -r_X\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le\Delta\mathcal V_X
                    \le r_X\Omega_j^{\rm lo}\quad(X=I,b,o),\\
 -4t_j\Omega_j^{\rm hi}\le
 4(\widehat F_j^{(j)}-\widehat F_j^{(1)})
                    \le4t_j\Omega_j^{\rm lo},\\
 \Delta\mathcal V_I+\Delta\mathcal V_b+\Delta\mathcal V_o
                =4(\widehat F_j^{(j)}-\widehat F_j^{(1)}).
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR19}
\]
Here $(r_X)=(r_I,r_b,r_o)$. The budget is RQB27--28:
the conjugate inverse
$H_{B,j,D}=\overline{\widehat H_{K,j,D}^{-1}}$
changes the primal sign exactly, and the fixed frame
factor in NSR18 cancels under $\mathcal R$.
Increasing original Gamma row cutoffs increases each
native form and preserves that order under its complete
fibre minimum; this proves positivity of all returns.
NSR3 now proves each order interval at its actual rank.
The last equality retains their exact correlation.

For the earlier CIT lifts,
$R_b^{\rm CIT}=R_b^EC_b+Y_{\rm inv}A_b$ with $C_b$
invertible and fixed. Hence
$Q_b^{\rm CIT}=C_b^*Q_b^EC_b$ by the same minimum;
all finite determinant identities retain $|\det C_b|^2$.
If $[X_{\mathbb J},I_R,R_b^{\rm CIT}]=X_WC_W$, then
\[
 |\det C_W|^2g_W
   =g_{\mathbb J}\det H_{\rm inv}\,
                   |\det C_b|^2\det Q_b^E.
 \tag{NSR20}
\]
This is the original CIT25/RQB31 equality. Its two fixed
coordinate determinants cancel in the order ratios and
in $\mathcal R$, because NSR5 and NSR17 retain the
same ordered value coordinates.

The residual source denominator remains the full
quotient $Q_0=D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}/Y$ in lifts $E_b$,
of rank $r_b$. Equivalently it is the original
kernel-dual quotient by $B_k\mathscr L$, whose rank is
$\ell-\epsilon$. With the fixed original frame
$C_0=[Z_\Lambda,B_kE_b]$ it satisfies
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \det Q_0(D)
  =\frac{|\det C_0|^2\det H_{B,k,D}}
             {\det(Z_\Lambda^*H_{B,k,D}Z_\Lambda)},\\
 -r_b\Omega_k^{\rm hi}\le
             \Delta\mathcal R\log\det Q_0
                    \le r_b\Omega_k^{\rm lo},\\
 -r_b(\Omega_j^{\rm hi}+\Omega_k^{\rm lo})
   \le\Delta\mathcal R\log\frac{\det Q_b^E}{\det Q_0}
   \le r_b(\Omega_j^{\rm lo}+\Omega_k^{\rm hi}).
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR21}
\]
To prove the first equality, minimize first over
$J=\ker B_k$ and then over $B_k\mathscr L$; the complete
fibre is exactly $Y$. The independent frame $C_0$
gives its Schur determinant. NSR3 proves the second,
and subtraction of the two directed rank-$r_b$ return
intervals proves the third. No inverse bound for the
residual multiplier has entered this calculation.

\subsection{The common contraction in CIT30 with all boundary factors}
The original ITL loss is
$\omega_{\rm inv}^p(D)=\det H_{\rm inv}^p(D)/
\det A_{\rm inv}^p(D)$, where
$A_{\rm inv}=F^*\mathcal D_DF/E$,
$F=BJ_0$ and the quotient values are $Z_R$.
Its two attained target-fibre forms have the same
rank $r_I$. Therefore, by the proof of NSR12,
\[
 \left|\Delta\log\omega_{\rm inv}(D)\right|
        \le r_I\omega_{j,D},\qquad
 \left|\Delta\mathcal R\log\omega_{\rm inv}\right|\le r_IW_j .
 \tag{NSR22}
\]
The source fibre here is $FZ_Rx+F E$ and the target fibre
is $FZ_Rx+\mathbb J$; $FE=\operatorname{im}F\cap\mathbb J$
by its complete coefficient kernel. Thus this compares
the same original two minima, not the independent
source-order metric $Q_{\rm inv}$ used below.

Put exactly as in CIT30
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal P_e^p
  =\mathcal R(\log g_{T_e}+\log g_{K_e}-\log g_{\mathscr S_e}
                   -\log g_{\mathbb J}-\log\det A_{\rm inv})^p,\\
 \mathcal L_e^p
   =\mathcal R(\log\omega_{{\rm conn},e}
                         +\log\omega_{\rm inv})^p,\qquad
 C_e^{\rm ord}=(c_e+r_I)W_j .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR23}
\]
Substitution of NSR20 in the full CIT26 cross ratio proves
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal L_e^p=\mathcal P_e^p-\mathcal V_b^p,\qquad
 \mathcal P_e^p-4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}+\mathcal V_I^p
           \le\mathcal L_e^p\le\mathcal P_e^p,\\
 -C_e^{\rm ord}\le\Delta\mathcal L_e\le C_e^{\rm ord},\\
 -C_e^{\rm ord}-r_b\Omega_j^{\rm hi}
       \le\Delta\mathcal P_e
       \le C_e^{\rm ord}+r_b\Omega_j^{\rm lo},\\
 \max\{\mathcal P_e^1-4\widehat F_j^{(j)}+\mathcal V_I^1,\,
          \mathcal P_e^0-\mathcal V_b^0-C_e^{\rm ord}\}
 \le\mathcal L_e^1\\

The first directed bound is the unchanged RQB32 inequality
for the negative residual factor: use
$0\le\mathcal V_b^p\le4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-\mathcal V_I^p$.
The second line adds NSR12 and NSR22, with exact ranks
$c_e,r_I$. Since $\Delta\mathcal P_e=
\Delta\mathcal L_e+\Delta\mathcal V_b$, NSR19 proves the
third line with its asymmetric widths. The last two lines
intersect the original pair-one bound with
$\mathcal L_e^0+[-C_e^{\rm ord},C_e^{\rm ord}]$ and substitute
the exact pair-zero residual identity. Both intervals
contain the same $\mathcal L_e^1$, so their intersection

$\mathcal V_b+\mathcal V_I+\mathcal V_o=4\widehat F_j$

while neither common value in the first line is evaluated.

\subsection{Both source-order intervals in the matched invariant return}
Keep NFR1's full original source quotient
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 Q_{\rm inv}^p(D)
       =(J_0^*D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}J_0)/E
          \quad\hbox{in the same lifts }Z_R,\qquad
 \operatorname{rank}Q_{\rm inv}=r_I,\\
 \mathscr R_X^p(D)
    =(Q_{\rm inv}^p(D))^{-1/2}H_{\rm inv}^p(D)
                         (Q_{\rm inv}^p(D))^{-1/2},\\
 V_Q^p=\mathcal R\log\det Q_{\rm inv}^p,\qquad

 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR25}
\]
The kernel $E=\ker(\Phi J_0)$ and complement $Z_R$ are
independent of the measures. Hence these are the actual
NFR/ITL matched quotient-value spaces. At every endpoint
$\det H_{\rm inv}=\det Q_{\rm inv}\det\mathscr R_X$,
even though its inverse square roots depend on the source
metric. This proves the exact matrix-to-determinant map.

The complete NFR6 return and the new finite order interval are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal M^p
  =4\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}-\mathcal V_b^p-\mathcal V_o^p-V_Q^p,
 \qquad \mathcal M^p+V_Q^p=\mathcal V_I^p\ge0,\\
 -r_I\Omega_k^{\rm hi}\le\Delta V_Q
                  \le r_I\Omega_k^{\rm lo},\\
 \Delta\mathcal M
  =4(\widehat F_j^{(j)}-\widehat F_j^{(1)})
                   -\Delta\mathcal V_b-\Delta\mathcal V_o-\Delta V_Q,\\
 \Lambda^-:=-r_I(\Omega_j^{\rm hi}+\Omega_k^{\rm lo})
       \le\Delta\mathcal M
       \le r_I(\Omega_j^{\rm lo}+\Omega_k^{\rm hi})=:\Lambda^+ .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR26}
\]
The first equality substitutes NSR18--19 in the exact
matched determinant. The second follows from NSR3 on
the rank-$r_I$ source quotient. For the last interval
use $\Delta\mathcal M=\Delta\mathcal V_I-\Delta V_Q$,
then subtract the directed intervals for these same two
rank-$r_I$ values. The preceding line preserves its full
target-budget realization with all three target factors.
Using the actual invariant rank before estimating avoids
assigning the much larger compatibility-plane rank to
this matched quotient.

In NSR26, the source degree is still $k$, with center
$c'_k$, at the displayed $D_i$ based on $q_j$.
Its displacement from its degree-$k$ standard endpoints is
$b=q_j-q_k=8k+24$ at both lows and $2b$ at both highs.
The constants $\omega_{k,D_i}$ in NSR2 use exactly
those displaced cutoffs. No window displacement is
silently absorbed into $\Lambda^\pm$.

Let $U_{j,1}^{\rm native},U_{j,j}^{\rm native}$ be the
complete RQB35 caps, with their original positive
observation sum, full FEX source correction and all
finite-domain restrictions of those providers. Define
\[
 U_{\mathcal M}^p
    =4U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native}-\mathcal V_b^p-\mathcal V_o^p-V_Q^p .
 \tag{NSR27}
\]
Both original source-order intervals are retained explicitly:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 -V_Q^0\le\mathcal M^0\le U_{\mathcal M}^0,\qquad
 -V_Q^1\le\mathcal M^1\le U_{\mathcal M}^1,\\
 \max\{-V_Q^1,\,-V_Q^0+\Lambda^-\}
       \le\mathcal M^1
       \le\min\{U_{\mathcal M}^1,\,U_{\mathcal M}^0+\Lambda^+\},\\
 \max\{-V_Q^0,\,-V_Q^1-\Lambda^+\}
       \le\mathcal M^0
       \le\min\{U_{\mathcal M}^0,\,U_{\mathcal M}^1-\Lambda^-\}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR28}
\]
For each pair, nonnegativity of $\mathcal V_I$ gives its
lower bound, and $\widehat F_j^{(s_1)}\le U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native}$
in the first line of NSR26 gives the upper bound with the
full missing return retained. Adding $\Lambda^-,\Lambda^+$
to the pair-zero interval and intersecting with the pair-one
interval proves the second line. Reversing the difference
and intersecting in the other direction proves the third.
In particular this refines the displayed NFR6 upper bound
by keeping its exact nonnegative $\mathcal V_o$, while
preserving $Q_{\rm inv}$ and $\mathcal V_b$ on both sides.

The preexisting NFR5 error also has an exact receiving.
Let $\mathcal C^p=\mathcal R\log
(\det\mathcal H_V^p/\det\mathcal H_E^p)$, with the full
original compatibility and source-complement forms, and
let $\mathcal A^p=\mathcal R\log\omega_{\rm inv}^p$.
At each cutoff NFR2's constants, denoted here by
$a_i^p,b_i^p$, retain their actual rank $r_I$:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 a_i^p=2r_I\log\max(1,K_-^p(D_i))+r_I\log c_+^p(D_i),\\
 b_i^p=2r_I\log\max(1,K_+^p(D_i))-r_I\log c_-^p(D_i),\\
 E_p^-=-b_0^p-b_1^p-a_2^p-a_3^p,\qquad
 E_p^+=a_0^p+a_1^p+b_2^p+b_3^p,\\
 \mathcal M^p=\mathcal C^p+\mathcal A^p+\mathcal E^p,\qquad
 E_p^-\le\mathcal E^p\le E_p^+,\\
 \Lambda^- -r_IW_j-E_1^++E_0^-
    \le\Delta\mathcal C
    \le\Lambda^+ +r_IW_j-E_1^-+E_0^+ .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR29}
\]
The first four lines are NFR2/5 with the two low errors
negated and the two high errors unnegated.
The exact difference is
$\Delta\mathcal C=\Delta\mathcal M-\Delta\mathcal A-
\Delta\mathcal E$.
Insert NSR22, NSR26 and these two error intervals to prove
the last line. Thus the full shared compatibility ratio
also receives a finite $o(kq_k)$ order-difference bound,
without replacing either individual large compatibility
determinant or either original source-complement projection.

\subsection{The existing arithmetic allocation comparison and its exact map}
For the original degree-$a$ standard window only, denote
its cutoffs by $d_i^{[a]}=q_a-v-1,q_a-v,2q_a-v-1,2q_a-v$;
write $\Omega_a^{[a],{\rm lo/hi}}$ for NSR2 evaluated
there. Keep HJR16--22's original inverse-metric
decomposition with $D=D_{a,d}^{(s)}$, $J=J_a$ and
$B=B_a$:
\[
 D^{-1}=J(J^*DJ)^{-1}J^*
              +L(BD^{-1}B^*)L^*,\qquad
 L=D^{-1}B^*(BD^{-1}B^*)^{-1}.
 \tag{NSR30}
\]
Indeed $[J,L]$ is a full coefficient frame with
$BJ=0$, $BL=I$, and block Gram
\[
 \operatorname{diag}(J^*DJ,(BD^{-1}B^*)^{-1}).
\]
Inverting that exact frame Gram proves NSR30.
The complete original new row $h$ gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 x=hJ(J^*DJ)^{-1}J^*h^*,\\
 y=hL(BD^{-1}B^*)L^*h^*.
 \end{gathered}
\]
The original old lower Schur denominator remains inside $h$.
The row returns are
$\log(1+x)$ and $\log(1+y/(1+x))$, with the original
endpoint weights one at the two extremes and two inside.
Their exact sums are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 S_K^{(s)}=\mathcal R_a\log\det H_{B,a,d}^{(s)}
                 =\widehat F_a^{(s)},\qquad
 S_R^{(s)}=\mathcal R_a\log\det(J_a^*D_{a,d}^{(s)}J_a)
                 =F_{0,a,s},\\
 S_K^{(s)}+S_R^{(s)}=\mathcal T_{a,s},\\
 -t_a\Omega_a^{[a],{\rm hi}}\le S_K^{(a)}-S_K^{(1)}
                          \le t_a\Omega_a^{[a],{\rm lo}},\\
 -j_{0,a}\Omega_a^{[a],{\rm hi}}\le S_R^{(a)}-S_R^{(1)}
                          \le j_{0,a}\Omega_a^{[a],{\rm lo}} .
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR31}
\]
This is the same native restriction/quotient transport
NSO15--19 applied to the exact preexisting HJR maps.
All zero rows remain in their aggregate. It gives no
rowwise or averaged allocation value.

To connect these actual sums to the earlier AMT
arithmetic objects, retain the original FEX/RQB33 identity
and its complete low correction:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 S_K^{(s)}=F_K^{(s)}+\delta_{K,a,s},\qquad
 \delta_{K,a,s}=\sum_{i=0}^3\sigma_i e_{N_i,s}
                     -F_{L_a}^{(s)}+F_{(K_a+L_a)/K_a}^{(s)},
 \quad \sigma=(1,1,-1,-1),\\
 S_R^{(s)}=\mathcal T_{a,s}-F_K^{(s)}-\delta_{K,a,s},\\
 S_K^{(a)}-S_K^{(1)}
  =-\Delta_K^{\Gamma,[a]}+\delta_{K,a,a}-\delta_{K,a,1},\\
 S_R^{(a)}-S_R^{(1)}
  =\mathcal T_{a,a}-\mathcal T_{a,1}
        +\Delta_K^{\Gamma,[a]}-\delta_{K,a,a}+\delta_{K,a,1}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{NSR32}
\]
Here $N_i=d_i^{[a]}+v$ and the original low space is
$L_a=\mathbb C[S]_{<v}$. The scalar $e_{N_i,s}$ is the
full original conductor source Jacobian; its exact
finite bounds remain RQB34/FEX34.
The existing AMT44 convention is
$\Delta_K^{\Gamma,[a]}=F_K^{(1)}-F_K^{(a)}$.
Its proved interval is
\[
 -(8a-16)\Omega_{\Gamma,a}^{\rm low}
 \le\Delta_K^{\Gamma,[a]}
 \le(8a-16)\Omega_{\Gamma,a}^{\rm high},\qquad
 \Delta_K^\Gamma+\Delta_B^\Gamma=\mathcal H_a ,
 \tag{NSR33}
\]
where $\Omega_{\Gamma,a}^{\rm low}
=\omega_{a,q_a-1}+\omega_{a,q_a}$ and
$\Omega_{\Gamma,a}^{\rm high}
=\omega_{a,2q_a-1}+\omega_{a,2q_a}$ use the original
primal, unshifted cutoffs of AMT43.
For the exact underlying map, AMT7--9 minimizes the
unchanged original polynomial remainder map, restricts
to $K_a$ and takes the original observation quotient;
AMT42 compares their two full original source Grams
in those same values. Their adapted determinant gives
the retained sum with $\mathcal H_a$.
Subtraction of these already proved equalities is
precisely NSR32. Thus the original primal rank $8a-16$,
the native rank $t_a=8a-16-t_{0,a}$, the residual rank
$j_{0,a}$, and every low correction stay in the typed
comparison. No new analytic bound or new equality of
those distinct source presentations is claimed.

All receiving formulas above apply their finite intervals
before limits. The surviving order differences are
$o(kq_k)$ on the fixed actual programme, while CIT30,
RQB28 and NFR6 continue to retain the common long
connecting/ITL loss, the positive residual and missing
returns, and the original $S_K,S_R$ allocation.

e^{\lambda^-_{a,D}}D_{a,D}^{(1)}

\]
The polynomial degree in the QOT/EOR entries is exactly $N-v-q'_a$.
Taking the maximum of two scalar lower bounds and minimum of their

\section{Native Gamma-order transport and its equilibrium-support refinement}
\label{sec:QOT_EOR}
\paragraph{Source and presentation.}
This chapter retains the complete refined QOT and EOR source, with every
proof, original constant, coordinate map, finite domain and bibliography.
Explicit equation tags preserve the source equation numbers. Local symbols,
theorem environments, labels and bibliography keys are scoped or namespaced
for the cumulative edition. A precision sentence at the EOR flags records
that the coefficient space is fixed while its Gamma metric depends on source
order. The delivered source and every reversible presentation change remain
preserved in the source closure.

\paragraph{Scope of the complete delivered calculation.}
For the original fixed simple-quartet family, a full quadratic-form comparison controls the change from Gamma order one to the original degree-dependent order. It acts on every original coefficient direction before either relation or observation minimization. The difference of the two native kernel allocations, and separately of the two residual allocations, is $O(q_k)$, with limiting absolute allowance $8\log(2+\sqrt3)$ after division by $q_k$. The common connecting/invariant long loss has source-order difference $O(q_k)$ with allowance $32\log(2+\sqrt3)$. At the two defining primitive cutoffs, the paired source-to-connecting-image order correction is $o(q_k)$. Its scalar shift is evaluated exactly, and the original compatibility difference is retained and compared in the same coordinates. The individual $kq_k$-scale allocation coefficients and the absolute common long loss are not evaluated here. No offcritical packet is excluded by these comparisons alone.
