\section{The complete original action relative to its arithmetic total}
\label{sec:complete-action-integration}

This is a local reconstruction of the complete delivered response following
the 16 September 2026, 15:02:50 prompt in
\texttt{ChatGPT-Residual Lower Bound Continuation-20260916-2150.md},
lines 7043--7730. The response reported that its build tools timed out:
no original standalone archive, compiled PDF, successful fresh Lean run or
actual-period numerical certificate is inferred from it. This section
supplies the additive proof in full. Its input source is the unchanged
\texttt{14\_CURRENT\_KERNEL\_AND\_MULTIPLICITY\_PROOFS.tex}; the original
AMT, MCE and OMR formulas below are rederived at the points where used.
The fixed arithmetic density-envelope constants are precisely AMT4--6's
proved constants. Their defining bounds and domains are displayed, rather
than replaced by newly selected measures or a new positivity premise.

\subsection{Domain, unchanged arithmetic objects and exact minimum metrics}

Use the original stipulated full zero-quartet domain: fixed
$0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$, and complete common order $m\ge1$ at the four
distinct centres. The statement below concerns these original objects; it
does not assert that such an off-line zero quartet exists. Write
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \rho=\tfrac12+\delta+i\gamma,\quad
 \mathcal R=\{\rho,\bar\rho,1-\rho,1-\bar\rho\},\quad
 h(s)=\prod_{\zeta\in\mathcal R}(s-\zeta)^m,\quad g=2\xi,\quad v_h=g/h,\\
 w_h(y)=|v_h(1/2+iy)|^2/(2\pi),\quad m_{h,b}=w_h^{*b},\quad
 \mu_h=\int_{\mathbb R}w_h(y)\,dy,\\
 k=4\ell+1\ge9,\quad c=k/2,\quad e_k=1+k(m-1),\quad
 q=e_k(k+1)^2,\\
 \beta_{ab}=c+(2a-k)\delta+i(2b-k)\gamma,\quad
 \chi(S)=\prod_{a,b=0}^{k}(S-\beta_{ab})^{e_k},\quad
 E=\mathbb C[S]/(\chi),\quad A=M_S.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI1}
\]
The arithmetic source is $L^2(\mathbb R,m_{h,k}(y)\,dy)$ with its
mass $\mu_h^k$, evaluated in the literal coordinate $S=c+iy$.
Its inner product is conjugate-linear in the first argument. For each
$N\ge0$ let $\mathcal P_N=\mathbb C[S]_{\le N}$ and let
$p_n(S)=i^nQ_n((S-c)/i)$ be the monic orthogonal polynomials; $Q_n$ is
monic in the real variable $y$. Their full squared norms are
$\omega_{n,k}^{\rm ar}>0$. The substitution has inverse
$P(S)=f((S-c)/i)$ and is never a change of the original source mass.
In formulas below $\omega_n$ abbreviates this same arithmetic norm.

The exact remainder map $J_N:\mathcal P_N\to E$ is onto for
$N\ge q-1$, with kernel $\chi\mathcal P_{N-q}$, where
$\mathcal P_{-1}=0$. Put $b_n=[p_n]_\chi$ in the frame
$1,S,\ldots,S^{q-1}$. Then
\[
 R_N=\sum_{n=0}^N b_nb_n^*/\omega_n,\qquad
 G_N=R_N^{-1},\qquad V_N=\det G_N.\tag{CAI2}
\]
The first $q$ columns form a unit-triangular monic remainder matrix, so
$R_N>0$. In any source coefficient frame with Gram $H_N$, the actual
minimum section is $H_N^{-1}J_N^*G_N$. Its composition with $J_N$ is the
identity. A vector in $\ker J_N$ pairs to zero with this section;
therefore the squared norm of another lift is its minimum squared norm
plus the squared norm of that relation. This proves the interpretation
of $G_N$ without changing the admitted relation subspace.

The original period observation is the AMT3 map
$\Lambda:E\twoheadrightarrow B$, with actual kernel inclusion
$I:K\hookrightarrow E$. Explicitly, if $E_h=\mathbb C[s]/(h)$,
$U=M_{j_hv_h}$, $\iota[P]=[P(s_1+\cdots+s_k)]$, $\Pi$ is the original
period matrix and $P_k$ its original tensor invariant projector, then
$\jmath\Lambda=P_k\Pi^{\otimes k}U^{\otimes k}\iota$, where
$\jmath:B\hookrightarrow(\mathbb C^{4m})^{\otimes k}$ is the image
inclusion. No period branch or kernel frame is changed. The action
calculation below is independent of that choice because it uses the
complete quotient $E$; the exact relation to the original observation
is the following splitting. In the fixed kernel and image frames set
\[
 H_{K,N}=I^*G_NI,\quad
 Q_{B,N}=(\Lambda G_N^{-1}\Lambda^*)^{-1},\quad
 S_N=G_N^{-1}\Lambda^*Q_{B,N}.\tag{CAI3}
\]
Direct multiplication proves $\Lambda S_N=1$ and $I^*G_NS_N=0$.
For any fixed section $S_*$ of $\Lambda$, the frame $[I,S_*]$ is
invertible and $S_N-S_*$ has image in $K$. Thus changing $[I,S_*]$ to
$[I,S_N]$ has determinant one. Orthogonality proves the exact identity
\[
 \det H_{K,N}\det Q_{B,N}=|\det[I,S_*]|^2V_N.\tag{CAI4}
\]
Empty factors have determinant one, so this also covers zero-dimensional
kernel or image. In particular no kernel invariance under $A$ is needed.

For any one of the original sources $a={\rm ar},1,k$, define its full
two-window return and the kernel and boundary returns by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal B_k^a=\log\frac{V_{q-1}^aV_q^a}{V_{2q-1}^aV_{2q}^a},\\
 F_K^a=\log\frac{\det H_{K,q-1}^a\det H_{K,q}^a}
                       {\det H_{K,2q-1}^a\det H_{K,2q}^a},\quad
 F_B^a=\log\frac{\det Q_{B,q-1}^a\det Q_{B,q}^a}
                       {\det Q_{B,2q-1}^a\det Q_{B,2q}^a},\\
 F_K^a+F_B^a=\mathcal B_k^a,\qquad
 \delta_k^\sigma=\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}-\mathcal B_k^\sigma,\quad
 \mathcal H_k=\mathcal B_k^\sigma-\mathcal B_k^{(k)}.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI5}
\]
Here $a=1$ denotes the original fixed Gamma source $\sigma$.
The equality follows by multiplying CAI4 at the two lower degrees and
dividing by its two upper-degree instances. Its four identical frame
constants cancel exactly, rather than being set equal to one.

\subsection{The actual rank-two control, reflection and exterior benchmark}

The quotient $v_h$ is entire at the selected centres because their
complete zero orders have been divided out. The exact equalities
$\xi(1-s)=\xi(s)$ and $h(1-s)=h(s)$ show that $w_h$ and every $m_{h,b}$
are even. Monic orthogonality consequently gives $Q_n(-y)=(-1)^nQ_n(y)$.
Self-adjoint multiplication by $y$, orthogonality to degrees below
$n-1$, and pairing with $Q_{n-1}$ prove the real monic recurrence.
In the original $S$ coordinate it is
\[
 Sp_n=p_{n+1}+cp_n-(\omega_n/\omega_{n-1})p_{n-1},\tag{CAI6}
\]
with the lower term absent at $n=0$. Inserting its quotient columns into
CAI2 cancels every interior adjacent pair and gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
 AR_N+R_NA^*-kR_N=(b_{N+1}b_N^*+b_Nb_{N+1}^*)/\omega_N,\\
 D_N^{\rm ctrl}=A^*G_N+G_NA-kG_N,\quad
 G_N^{-1/2}D_N^{\rm ctrl}G_N^{-1/2}=(yx^*+xy^*)/\omega_N,\\
 x=G_N^{1/2}b_N,\qquad y=G_N^{1/2}b_{N+1}.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI7}
\]
The symbol $D_N^{\rm ctrl}$ is distinct from the scalar source constant
$D_N$ below. Reflection $P(S)\mapsto P(k-S)$ preserves $\chi$ because
it permutes $(a,b)$ with $(k-a,k-b)$ and $q$ is even. It therefore induces
an involution $\Sigma$ on $E$, with $\Sigma A\Sigma=kI-A$.
Changing variables $y\mapsto-y$ is an isometry of each entire remainder
fibre. Uniqueness of the minimum proves $\Sigma^*G_N\Sigma=G_N$.
Parity gives $\Sigma b_n=(-1)^nb_n$, and hence
$b_N^*G_Nb_{N+1}=-b_N^*G_Nb_{N+1}=0$. Thus the only possibly nonzero
eigenvalues of the relative Hermitian control in CAI7 are
$\epsilon_N,-\epsilon_N$, where
\[
 \epsilon_N^2=\frac{\|x\|^2\|y\|^2}{\omega_N^2},\qquad
 \phi_N=0.\tag{CAI8}
\]
For comparison with the original tilted formulas, before specialization
the exact expression is
$(\|x\|^2\|y\|^2-|x^*y|^2)/\omega_N^2$ and
$x^*y=i\omega_N\phi_N$. At the present untilted source only the
\emph{total} pairing vanishes. In the orthogonal kernel--boundary split
write $x=x_K\oplus x_B$, $y=y_K\oplus y_B$, and
$z_K=x_K^*y_K$, $z_B=x_B^*y_B$. Then $z_B=-z_K$, not generally zero.
In particular the mixed exterior summand remains
\[
 \|x_K\otimes y_B-y_K\otimes x_B\|^2
 =\|x_K\|^2\|y_B\|^2+\|x_B\|^2\|y_K\|^2+2|z_K|^2.\tag{CAI9}
\]

Here is the original source-to-class map and its lower allowance. The
complete Taylor unit $\upsilon_h=j_hv_h$ is invertible in $E_h$, and
\[
 \eta:E\longrightarrow E_h^{\otimes k},\qquad
 \eta[P]=\upsilon_h^{\otimes k}P(A_\Sigma)1,\qquad
 A_\Sigma=\sum_iM_{s_i},\qquad \eta A=A_\Sigma\eta.\tag{CAI10}
\]
In each local tensor factor the nilpotent has order $m$. The sum of the
$k$ nilpotents has exact order $e_k$: the power $e_k-1=k(m-1)$ has
nonzero top coefficient $[k(m-1)]!/[(m-1)!]^k$, and every term of its
next power has one exponent at least $m$. Every centre $\beta_{ab}$
occurs, and distinct pairs have distinct centres. The annihilator of
$A_\Sigma$ on its cyclic unit is therefore exactly $\chi$; this proves
injectivity of CAI10, including all jets. Reflection fixes
$\upsilon_h$ and intertwines this inclusion with the original tensor
reflection $s_i\mapsto1-s_i$.

Let $E_+$ be the direct sum of all complete primary blocks with $a>k/2$.
Its dimension is $t_+=q/2$. A primary idempotent is explicitly
$\chi_\beta\sum_{j=0}^{e_k-1}(1/\chi_\beta)^{(j)}(\beta)
(S-\beta)^j/j!\pmod\chi$, where $\chi_\beta=\chi/(S-\beta)^{e_k}$.
Its product with the powers $(S-\beta)^d$, $0\le d<e_k$, gives a
complete primary basis. The top wedge $\xi_+$ of these bases is nonzero,
and remains nonzero under $\bigwedge^{t_+}\eta$. Its additive action
eigenvalue is $\operatorname{Tr}(A|_{E_+})$; nilpotents contribute zero
to that trace. The relative Hermitian additive action on any exterior
degree $1\le t\le q-1$ has largest eigenvalue $\epsilon_N$, since its
eigenvalues are sums of distinct members of
$\{\epsilon_N,-\epsilon_N,0,\ldots,0\}$. Its expectation on $\xi_+$
is $2\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}(A|_{E_+})-kt_+$. Summing the
positive odd numbers $2a-k$ gives $(k+1)^2/4$, so
\[
 0<L_{h,k}:=2\delta e_k(k+1)\frac{(k+1)^2}{4}
          =\frac{\delta q(k+1)}2\le\epsilon_N
          \qquad(N\ge q-1).\tag{CAI11}
\]
This is a calculation on the same stipulated arithmetic class, not a
claim of its existence and not an assumed desired upper estimate.

\subsection{Exact cancellation before any estimates}

Set $d_n=V_n/V_{n-1}$ for $n\ge q$, and
$b_n^{\rm rec}=\omega_n/\omega_{n-1}$; neither is the centre displacement
$\delta$ or the quotient column $b_n$. From the rank-one covariance
update and the determinant lemma,
\[
 \frac{b_N^*G_Nb_N}{\omega_N}=1-d_N\quad(N\ge q),\qquad
 \frac{b_{N+1}^*G_Nb_{N+1}}{\omega_{N+1}}=d_{N+1}^{-1}-1.
\]
The first equality follows by removing the last covariance column;
the second follows by adding the next one. At $N=q-1$ the first $q$
columns are the full quotient basis and
$b_{q-1}^*G_{q-1}b_{q-1}=\omega_{q-1}$, without using any inverse at
$q-2$. Thus CAI8 gives the original MCE23/OMR18 formulas
\[
 \epsilon_{q-1}^2=b_q^{\rm rec}(d_q^{-1}-1),\qquad
 \epsilon_N^2=b_{N+1}^{\rm rec}(1-d_N)(d_{N+1}^{-1}-1) (N\ge q).
 \tag{CAI12}
\]
Positive covariances give $0<d_n\le1$, and CAI11--12 force $d_n<1$
at every degree appearing here. All following logarithms therefore
exist. Put $w_{q-1}=w_{2q-1}=1$ and $w_N=2$ for $q\le N\le2q-2$;
their sum is $2q$. Retain exactly
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathcal W_k^{\rm ar}=\log\frac{\omega_{2q-1}\omega_{2q}}
                                      {\omega_{q-1}\omega_q},\\
 \mathcal P_-=-\log(1-d_{2q-1})-2\sum_{n=q}^{2q-2}\log(1-d_n),\\
 \mathcal P_+=-\log(1-d_q)-\log(1-d_{2q})
                              -2\sum_{n=q+1}^{2q-1}\log(1-d_n),\\
 J_k^{\rm action}=2\sum_{N=q-1}^{2q-1}w_N\log\epsilon_N,\quad
 \Psi_k=F_K^{\rm ar}+F_B^{\rm ar}-F_B^{(k)},\quad
 C_k^{\rm budget}=F_B^{(k)}+\mathcal W_k^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_--\mathcal P_+.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI13}
\]
Multiply CAI12 over $N=q-1,\ldots,2q-2$ and over
$N=q,\ldots,2q-1$ separately. The two exact products are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \prod_{N=q-1}^{2q-2}\epsilon_N^2
 &=\frac{\omega_{2q-1}V_{q-1}}{\omega_{q-1}V_{2q-1}}
            (1-d_{2q-1})\prod_{n=q}^{2q-2}(1-d_n)^2,\\
 \prod_{N=q}^{2q-1}\epsilon_N^2
 &=\frac{\omega_{2q}V_q}{\omega_qV_{2q}}
            (1-d_q)(1-d_{2q})\prod_{n=q+1}^{2q-1}(1-d_n)^2.
\end{aligned}\tag{CAI14}
\]
Indeed $d_{N+1}^{-1}-1=(1-d_{N+1})/d_{N+1}$; norm ratios telescope,
reciprocal $d$ factors give the displayed volume ratios, and the
endpoint and interior contraction exponents are precisely those written.
Taking logarithms and using CAI5 proves
\[
 \boxed{J_k^{\rm action}=\Psi_k+C_k^{\rm budget}
    =\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}+\mathcal W_k^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_--\mathcal P_+.}
 \tag{CAI15}
\]
Equivalently $\Psi_k=F_K^{(k)}+\mathcal H_k+\delta_k^\sigma$ and
$C_k^{\rm budget}=\mathcal B_k^{(k)}-F_K^{(k)}+
\mathcal W_k^{\rm ar}-\mathcal P_--\mathcal P_+$.
The cancellation uses the exact joint identities, not independent
estimates for the kernel, residual, low metrics or transfer errors.

\subsection{All-degree multiplication bounds on the actual arithmetic blocks}

The AMT1 domain is every convolution order $b\ge3$, not just the later
tensor subsequence. Its constants, without alteration, are
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \alpha=\pi/2,\quad p=8m-\tfrac92,\quad B_h=42+8m,\quad M=21+4m,\\
 \vartheta_h=\int_{-1}^1w_h(y)\,dy>0,\quad
 M_\alpha=\int_{\mathbb R}e^{\alpha y}w_h(y)\,dy\in(0,\infty),\\
 c_\Gamma=\sqrt2e^{-7/6},\quad C_\Gamma=\sqrt{2\sqrt5}e^{2/3},\\
 a_b=\frac{c_h\vartheta_h^{b-3}e^{-\alpha(b-3)}(b-2)^{-B_h}}
                   {C_\Gamma2^{B_h/2}},\quad
 A_b=\frac{C_h^{\rm tilt}}{c_\Gamma}b^{p+1}M_\alpha^{b-1},\quad
 D_N=[1+4(N+M)^2]^M.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI16}
\]
Here $c_h$ is the original TW7 lower-envelope constant and
$C_h^{\rm tilt}$ the original BT12 compact/tail maximum, as recorded
in AMT4. Their already proved defining inequalities, for every $b\ge3$,
are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 m_{h,b}(y)&\ge c_h\vartheta_h^{b-3}
             e^{-\alpha(|y|+b-3)}(b-2+|y|)^{-B_h},\\
 m_{h,b}(y)&\le bC_h^{\rm tilt}M_\alpha^{b-1}
             e^{-\alpha|y|}(1+|y|/b)^{-p},\\
 c_\Gamma e^{-\alpha|y|}(1+|y|)^{-1/2}
 &\le \frac{d\sigma}{dy}\le
 C_\Gamma e^{-\alpha|y|}(1+|y|)^{-1/2},\qquad
 d\sigma=|\Gamma(1/4+iy/2)|^2dy/(2\pi).
\end{aligned}\tag{CAI17}
\]
The factors $b-2+|y|\le(b-2)(1+|y|)$,
$(1+|y|)^{B_h}\le2^{B_h/2}(1+y^2)^M$, and
$1+|y|/b\ge(1+|y|)/b$, with $p>1/2$, give
$a_b(1+y^2)^{-M}d\sigma/dy\le m_{h,b}\le A_b d\sigma/dy$.
The orthonormal Gamma recurrence is
$ye_j=\sqrt{(j+1)(j+1/2)}e_{j+1}+\sqrt{j(j-1/2)}e_{j-1}$.
It gives $\|yP\|_\sigma\le2(N+1)\|P\|_\sigma$ on degree $N$.
Iterate at the successive degrees and expand $(1+y^2)^M$ to get
$\int|P|^2(1+y^2)^M d\sigma\le D_N\|P\|_\sigma^2$.
Cauchy--Schwarz applied with the two weights $(1+y^2)^{\pm M/2}$
therefore proves, for every complex polynomial of degree at most $N$,
\[
 \frac{a_b}{D_N}\|P\|_\sigma^2\le\|P\|_{m_{h,b}}^2
                         \le A_b\|P\|_\sigma^2.\tag{CAI18}
\]
These are precisely the full-polynomial AMT6 inequalities; in particular
they apply at $b=3,4,5$ with no new source-order restriction.

The fixed Gamma monic polynomials have full norms
$\gamma_n=\sqrt{2\pi}\,n!(1/2)_n$. Their recurrence gives the
generating series $F(y,z)=\sum_{n\ge0}p_n^\sigma(y)z^n/n!$:
\[
 (1+z^2)\partial_zF=(y-z/2)F,\quad F(y,0)=1,\qquad
 F=(1+z^2)^{-1/4}e^{y\arctan z}.\tag{CAI19}
\]
One may verify the differential equation coefficient by coefficient,
and integration at zero uniquely gives the displayed analytic function
on $|z|<1$. On $|z|=1/4$, its prefactor has modulus at most
$(16/15)^{1/4}$ and
$|\arctan z|\le\sum_{j\ge0}|z|^{2j+1}/(2j+1)=\operatorname{arctanh}(1/4)$.
Consequently Cauchy's coefficient bound and
$n!/(1/2)_n=4^n/\binom{2n}{n}\le2n+1$ imply
\[
 |P(y)|^2\le C_\sigma(N+1)(2N+1)16^N e^{\nu_0|y|}\|P\|_\sigma^2,
 \quad C_\sigma=\frac4{\sqrt{15}\sqrt{2\pi}},\quad
 \nu_0=\log(5/3).\tag{CAI20}
\]
For the binomial inequality the central coefficient is the maximum of
the $2n+1$ positive coefficients summing to $4^n$. For the polynomial
inequality expand in the complete degree-$N$ orthonormal basis and
apply Cauchy--Schwarz to the coefficient vector; then bound each of
the $N+1$ summands by $(2N+1)16^N$. This covers complex coefficients.

Put $d_0=\alpha-\nu_0>0$. Positivity and convolution give
$\int e^{\alpha y}m_{h,b}(y)\,dy=M_\alpha^b$ by Tonelli, and evenness
gives $\int e^{\alpha|y|}m_{h,b}(y)\,dy\le2M_\alpha^b$.
For $x>R\ge0$,
$x^2e^{-d_0x}\le(16/d_0^2)e^{-d_0R/2}$: the maximum of
$x^2e^{-d_0x/2}$ is $16/(e^2d_0^2)\le16/d_0^2$.
Moreover $N+M\le M(N+1)$ since $M\ge1$, so
$D_N\le(1+4M^2)^M(N+1)^{2M}$. Combining these facts with CAI18--20,
and $2N+1\le2(N+1)$, proves for $b=3,4,5$
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \int_{|y|>R}y^2|P(y)|^2m_{h,b}(y)\,dy
 \le H_h(N+1)^{2M+2}16^Ne^{-d_0R/2}\|P\|_{m_{h,b}}^2,
 \\ H_h=\frac{64C_\sigma(1+4M^2)^M}{d_0^2}
                          \max_{b=3,4,5}\frac{M_\alpha^b}{a_b}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{CAI21}
\]
All constants are fixed-$h$ data, including the unscaled source masses.
Choose
\[
 T_h=\frac2{d_0}\{\log16+2M+3+\log^+H_h\},\qquad
 C_{\rm mult}(h)=\sqrt{T_h^2+1}.\tag{CAI22}
\]
Here $\log^+x=\max(0,\log x)$. At $R=T_h(N+1)$ the logarithm of the
factor in CAI21 is at most
$\log H_h+(2M+2)N+N\log16-
(\log16+2M+3+\log^+H_h)(N+1)\le0$, using
$\log(N+1)\le N$. The inner interval has $y^2\le T_h^2(N+1)^2$.
Adding its integral and the tail, and using $(N+1)^2\ge1$, proves
\[
 \|yP\|_{m_{h,b}}\le C_{\rm mult}(h)(N+1)\|P\|_{m_{h,b}},
                         \qquad b=3,4,5.\tag{CAI23}
\]

\subsection{Total-degree tensor compression, including the output face}

For every integer $k\ge3$, write $r_k=\lfloor k/3\rfloor$ and
partition $k$ into $r_k$ blocks of sizes $3,4,5$. If $k=3r_k$ take
all threes; if $k=3r_k+1$ or $3r_k+2$, replace one three by four or
five. This also covers $k=3,4,5$. Convolution proves the exact isometry
\[
 L^2(m_{h,k})\longrightarrow\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r_k}L^2(m_{h,b_i}),
 \quad f(y)\longmapsto f(y_1+\cdots+y_{r_k}),\quad
 \sum_i(b_i/2+iy_i)=k/2+i\sum_iy_i.\tag{CAI24}
\]
The norm identity follows first by Tonelli for $|f|^2$ and then by
the definition of convolution. Its image is the sum-dependent
subspace, not the whole product space; total masses multiply to
$\mu_h^k$. In each block let $\varphi_n^{(b)}$ be the real orthonormal
polynomial with positive leading coefficient. Its exact recurrence is
\[
 y\varphi_n^{(b)}=a_{n+1}^{(b)}\varphi_{n+1}^{(b)}
                         +a_n^{(b)}\varphi_{n-1}^{(b)},\qquad
 a_0^{(b)}=0,\quad 0<a_n^{(b)}\le C_{\rm mult}(h)n\ (n\ge1).
\]
There is no diagonal term by evenness. The bound follows by taking
the coefficient of $\varphi_n$ in $y\varphi_{n-1}$ and applying CAI23.

Use all product basis vectors of total degree at most $N+1$, and let
$M_{N+1}$ be the compression of multiplication by $\sum_i y_i$ to
their span. It is a real symmetric matrix. At a vertex
$\nu=(\nu_1,\ldots,\nu_{r_k})$ of total degree at most $N$, its full
absolute row sum is at most
$C_{\rm mult}\sum_i(\nu_i+(\nu_i+1))\le C_{\rm mult}(2N+r_k)$.
At total degree $N+1$ only downward entries remain in the compression,
so that row sum is at most $C_{\rm mult}(N+1)\le C_{\rm mult}(2N+r_k)$.
Symmetry gives the same bound for column sums and hence the operator
norm: directly, for any vector $z$, pair the equal off-diagonal entries
and apply $2|z_\nu z_\mu|\le|z_\nu|^2+|z_\mu|^2$ to its quadratic
form. Its absolute Rayleigh quotient is bounded by the maximal row sum.

The image of a univariate polynomial of degree at most $N$ under CAI24
has total degree at most $N$. Multiplication by $\sum_i y_i$ has total
degree at most $N+1$, so no output is lost by the compression. Therefore
\[
 \|yP\|_{m_{h,k}}\le C_{\rm mult}(h)(2N+r_k)\|P\|_{m_{h,k}},\qquad
 \boxed{\frac{\omega_{N+1,k}^{\rm ar}}{\omega_{N,k}^{\rm ar}}
         \le C_{\rm mult}(h)^2(2N+r_k)^2}\quad(k\ge3,N\ge0).
 \tag{CAI25}
\]
For the last step $yQ_N$ is an actual degree-$N+1$ monic competitor;
its orthogonal monic minimum has no greater norm. The $S$-monic phases
have modulus one, so precisely the original norms occur. This proof
uses the full total-degree space, not independent degree-$N$ cutoffs
in each factor, and yields $N+k$ rather than $kN$.

\subsection{Both original contraction penalties on their complete window}

Return now to the stipulated packet in CAI1. By CAI11--12 and
$1-d_N\le1$,
\[
 d_n\le\frac{b_n^{\rm rec}}{b_n^{\rm rec}+L_{h,k}^2}
                         \quad(q\le n\le2q).
\]
For $n=q$ this is the endpoint formula; for $n\ge q+1$ it follows
from the formula at $N=n-1$. CAI25 bounds all these recurrence ratios by
\[
 B_{h,k}=C_{\rm mult}(h)^2(4q+r_k-2)^2,\qquad
 z_{h,k}=\frac{B_{h,k}}{L_{h,k}^2}
   =\frac{4C_{\rm mult}(h)^2(4+(r_k-2)/q)^2}{\delta^2(k+1)^2}.
 \tag{CAI26}
\]
The map $b\mapsto b/(b+L^2)$ is increasing for $b>0$, whence
\[
 0<d_n\le\frac{z_{h,k}}{1+z_{h,k}},\qquad
 \frac1{1+z_{h,k}}\le1-d_n<1,\qquad q\le n\le2q.\tag{CAI27}
\]
It is the complementary factors $1-d_n$, not $d_n$ themselves, that
approach one. In CAI13 the coefficient sum in $\mathcal P_-$ is
$1+2(q-1)=2q-1$, and that in $\mathcal P_+$ is $2+2(q-1)=2q$.
Thus the exact finite bound is
\[
 \boxed{0\le\mathcal P_-+\mathcal P_+
      \le\Pi_{h,k}:=(4q-1)\log(1+z_{h,k}).}\tag{CAI28}
\]
Since $q=e_k(k+1)^2$ and $r_k\le k/3$, $z_{h,k}=O_h(k^{-2})$ and
$\log(1+z)\le z$ show that $\Pi_{h,k}=O_h(q/k^2)=O_h(e_k)$.
This is $O_h(1)$ for $m=1$, and $O_h(k)=o(q)$ for any fixed $m>1$.
No early-$k$ smallness assumption was used in CAI27--28.
At an interior degree $N\ge q$, writing $\tau_N=-\log d_{N+1}$ gives
the exact pointwise comparison
$\epsilon_N^2/(b_{N+1}^{\rm rec}e^{\tau_N})=(1-d_N)(1-d_{N+1})$,
whose logarithm lies in $[-2\log(1+z_{h,k}),0]$ on the window.
At $N=q-1$ the one remaining factor is $1-d_q$.

\subsection{The four-norm factorial window and its finite remainder}

Minimize CAI18 over all monic polynomials, retaining the literal
degree-$n$ coefficient one. It gives
$(a_k/D_n)\gamma_n\le\omega_{n,k}^{\rm ar}\le A_k\gamma_n$.
Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \Lambda_{k,n}=\log(A_kD_n/a_k),\quad
 E_{\rm lo}=\Lambda_{k,q-1}+\Lambda_{k,q},\quad
 E_{\rm hi}=\Lambda_{k,2q-1}+\Lambda_{k,2q},\\
 \mathcal W_q^\sigma=\log\frac{\gamma_{2q-1}\gamma_{2q}}
                                     {\gamma_{q-1}\gamma_q},\qquad
 -E_{\rm hi}\le\mathcal W_k^{\rm ar}-\mathcal W_q^\sigma\le E_{\rm lo}.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI29}
\]
For the lower bound put both numerator norms at their lower bounds
and both denominator norms at their upper bounds; for the upper bound
reverse those choices. The common $a_k,A_k$ terms are thereby counted
exactly twice. This comparison occurs \emph{after} the window has
telescoped, not once at each of its $q$ steps. More explicitly, the
original AMT22 constants give
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathfrak s_h=\alpha+\log(M_\alpha/\vartheta_h),\quad
 \mathfrak c_h=-\log M_\alpha+3\log\vartheta_h-3\alpha+
       \log\frac{C_h^{\rm tilt}C_\Gamma2^{B_h/2}}{c_hc_\Gamma},\\
 \log(A_k/a_k)=k\mathfrak s_h+(p+1)\log k+B_h\log(k-2)+\mathfrak c_h.
\end{gathered}\tag{CAI30}
\]
Since $h$ is fixed and $\log D_n=O_h(1+\log(n+1))$, the two errors
in CAI29 are $O_h(k+\log q)$.

The identity $(1/2)_n=(2n)!/(4^nn!)$ gives
$\gamma_n=\sqrt{2\pi}(2n)!/4^n$. Cancelling these four literal norms,
including their masses, gives
\[
 \mathcal W_q^\sigma
 =2\log\frac{(4q)!}{(2q)!}-4q\log2
                       +\log\frac{2q-1}{2(4q-1)}.\tag{CAI31}
\]
Here the endpoint factor results from
$(4q-2)!=(4q)!/[(4q)(4q-1)]$ and
$(2q-2)!=(2q)!/[(2q)(2q-1)]$; it is not discarded.

For a fully finite enclosure put
$C(q)=4q\log q+(8\log2-4)q$ and
$\eta_q=1/(16q)+1/(4q-2)$. For $f(x)=\log x$, integration by parts
twice on each unit interval $[j,j+1]$ gives
\[
 \frac{f(j)+f(j+1)}2-\int_j^{j+1}f(x)\,dx
 =\frac12\int_j^{j+1}(x-j)(j+1-x)f''(x)\,dx.
\]
Since $f''(x)=-x^{-2}$ and $(x-j)(j+1-x)/2\le1/8$, summing from
$j=2q$ to $4q-1$ bounds the composite trapezoidal error by
$-\frac18\int_{2q}^{4q}x^{-2}dx=-1/(32q)\le\xi_q\le0$.
The factorial sum is the trapezoidal sum plus
$[f(4q)-f(2q)]/2$. Substitution in CAI31 and exact integration give
\[
 \mathcal W_q^\sigma=C(q)-\log2+2\xi_q+
                                  \log(1-1/(4q-1)).
\]
Finally $-x/(1-x)\le\log(1-x)\le0$ for $0<x<1$, obtained by
integrating $-1/(1-x)$, proves for every integer $q\ge1$
\[
 C(q)-\log2-\eta_q\le\mathcal W_q^\sigma\le C(q)-\log2.
 \tag{CAI32}
\]

\subsection{Complete finite interval, action mean and exact receiving scope}

Combining CAI15, CAI28 and CAI29 proves the entirely specified interval
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}+\mathcal W_q^\sigma-E_{\rm hi}-\Pi_{h,k}
       \le J_k^{\rm action}
       \le\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}+\mathcal W_q^\sigma+E_{\rm lo}.}
 \tag{CAI33}
\]
In particular CAI32 gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}+C(q)-\log2-\eta_q-E_{\rm hi}-\Pi_{h,k}
     &\le J_k^{\rm action},\\
 J_k^{\rm action}&\le\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}+C(q)-\log2+E_{\rm lo}.
\end{aligned}\tag{CAI34}
\]
For fixed $h$ and its complete multiplicity, $e_k=O_h(k)$ and
$q=e_k(k+1)^2$. All noncentral terms in CAI34 are
$O_h(k+\log q)=o(q)$. Therefore
\[
 \boxed{J_k^{\rm action}=\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}
     +4q\log q+(8\log2-4)q+O_h(k+\log q).}\tag{CAI35}
\]
The finer finite centre in CAI34 retains $-\log2$ and every error term.
The coefficient $8\log2-4$ is a coefficient of
$J_k^{\rm action}-\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}$, not a coefficient of
$J_k^{\rm action}$ after replacing $\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}$ by only its
leading asymptotic. The exact signed correction
$\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}=\mathcal B_k^\sigma+\delta_k^\sigma$ remains
unevaluated at scale $q$. Nothing in CAI35 assigns either individual
kernel/residual allocation, or the absolute order-one long loss.

The original weighted arithmetic geometric mean is exactly
\[
 \mathcal G_k=\prod_{N=q-1}^{2q-1}\epsilon_N^{w_N/(2q)}
             =\exp(J_k^{\rm action}/(4q)).
\]
Its finite bounds are the exponentials of CAI34 divided by $4q$.
Consequently, since $(k+\log q)/q=O_h(1/k)$ for fixed $m$,
\[
 \boxed{\frac{\mathcal G_k}{q\exp(\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}/(4q))}
          =\frac4{\exp(1)}(1+O_h(k^{-1}))\longrightarrow\frac4{\exp(1)}.}
 \tag{CAI36}
\]
This uses $e^x=1+O(x)$ as $x\to0$, which follows directly by integrating
the exponential derivative on a bounded interval. It also proves
$(J_k^{\rm action}-\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar})/(kq)\to0$ since
$\log q=O_h(\log k)$.

The exact exterior receiver is unchanged:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 4q\log L_{h,k}\le J_k^{\rm action},\\
 L_{h,k}\le\min_{q-1\le N\le2q-1}\epsilon_N\le\mathcal G_k,
 \\
 \frac{\mathcal G_k}{L_{h,k}}=
 \frac8{\exp(1)\delta(k+1)}\exp(\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}/(4q))
                              (1+O_h(k^{-1})).\end{gathered}\tag{CAI37}
\]
The first two inequalities follow term by term from CAI11 and the
weights' sum $2q$; a minimum is bounded by, not identified with, the
weighted mean. In the original simple-quartet baseline domain alone,
AMT40 and the complete BSL/HBL source proof give
$\mathcal B_k^{\rm ar}/q^2\to C_B>769/17010>0$. Substitution into
CAI35 proves $J_k^{\rm action}/q^2\to C_B$, and CAI37 then tends to
infinity exponentially in $q$. This last consequence uses that already
proved baseline only on its original $m=1$ domain; the new interval,
penalty bound and relative expansion retain every complete multiplicity.

For the existing original simple-quartet outer receiver, retain its
literal finite identity
$\Psi_k=\mathcal F_{\partial,k}^{(k)}-\Phi_{k,k}
+\mathcal H_k+\delta_k^\sigma+\mathfrak e_{k,k}$, with the original
$-e_{k,k}^-\le\mathfrak e_{k,k}\le e_{k,k}^+$. Substituting CAI15 gives
\[
 \Phi_{k,k}\le\mathcal F_{\partial,k}^{(k)}+\mathcal H_k
       +\delta_k^\sigma+e_{k,k}^++C_k^{\rm budget}-4q\log L_{h,k}.
 \tag{CAI38}
\]
This is exactly OMR26's arithmetic backward receiver. Every signed low
metric and error remains where its original identity puts it. CAI33--34
can be substituted for the \emph{complete} action; they do not justify
independently improving one summand while holding its correlated budget
fixed. No failure of the broader programme or exclusion at other
admitted degrees follows from the size of this particular mean.

\paragraph{Continuation to the derived growing-window endpoints (RGI8--10).}
The complete action in this section has the following exact continuation to
the original allowance rows $q-1,\ldots,q+r-1$, for each integer $r\ge1$.
Use weight one at the two ends and two in the interior; equivalently write
these weights as $w_j$ for the row $q+j-1$.  Their sum is $2r$.  The original
adjacent relations are
$d_{q+j}=V_{q+j}/V_{q+j-1}=1/T_j^{\rm ar}$ and
$\beta_{q+j}=\omega_{q+j}/\omega_{q+j-1}$.  Taking logarithms of the exact
reflection identity RGC24 gives
\begin{equation}
 \log\epsilon_{q+j-1}^2
 =\log\beta_{q+j}-\log d_{q+j}-a_{q+j}
       -\boldsymbol1_{j\ge1}a_{q+j-1},\qquad
 a_d=-\log(1-d_d).
 \tag{RGI8}\label{RGI:rowaction}
\end{equation}
On the positive-allowance domain supplied by RGC25--26 all these logarithms
are defined.  For any positive sequence $z_n$, direct cancellation of each
interior exponent gives
\[
 \sum_{j=0}^{r}w_j(\log z_{q+j}-\log z_{q+j-1})
 =\log z_{q+r-1}+\log z_{q+r}-\log z_{q-1}-\log z_q.
\]
For $r=1$ the occurrences of $z_q$ cancel on the right as well.  Apply this
identity separately to the original $\omega$ and $V$ in RGI8.  Thus
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal J_{q,r}=\mathcal B_{q,r}+\mathcal W_{q,r}-\mathcal P_{q,r},
 \qquad
 \mathcal B_{q,r}=\log\frac{V_{q-1}V_q}{V_{q+r-1}V_{q+r}},\quad
 \mathcal W_{q,r}=\log\frac{\omega_{q+r-1}\omega_{q+r}}
                              {\omega_{q-1}\omega_q}.
 \tag{RGI9}\label{RGI:action}
\end{equation}
Here $\mathcal J_{q,r}=\sum_jw_j\log\epsilon_{q+j-1}^2$ and the exact penalty
is RGC29.  Its coefficients are $2,1$ for $r=1$ and
$3,4,\ldots,4,3,1$ for $r\ge2$, with total $4r-1$.
Inserting the proved intersection bound RGI7 and the four original AMT
endpoint errors RGC32 yields
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal B_{q,r}+\mathcal W^\sigma_{q,r}
 -\Lambda_{k,q+r-1}-\Lambda_{k,q+r}-P_{q,r}^{\rm cap}
 &\le\mathcal J_{q,r},\\
 \mathcal J_{q,r}
 &\le\mathcal B_{q,r}+\mathcal W^\sigma_{q,r}
           +\Lambda_{k,q-1}+\Lambda_{k,q}.
 \end{split}
 \tag{RGI10}\label{RGI:actionbounds}
\end{equation}
This follows by adding the lower, respectively upper, endpoint inequalities
to RGI9 and using $0\le\mathcal P_{q,r}\le P_{q,r}^{\rm cap}$.
Only the four endpoint errors are charged.  At $r=q$ the endpoints and
weights are exactly those of the earlier complete action.  Its correlated
allocation cancellation remains the same identity.  The intrinsic signed
mixed profile and the CPQ returns retain their original receiving maps; no
value for them is assigned by these canonical allowance bounds.

\label{GRBUILD:GRB_SHIFTED_ACTION:start}
\paragraph{The moving-degree result in the complete action (GRB9--10).}
The new growing-ratio proof also gives an exact receiver at shifted rows.
Keep $q,\chi,E$, all three source forms, and every kernel/boundary map
fixed as in this section.  Choose an integer $s\ge1$, put $a=q+s-1$,
$b=a+q$, and give rows $N=a+i$ the weights $w_0=w_q=1$ and $w_i=2$
otherwise.  Their relation index is exactly $r=s+i$ in RGI8.  Every row
now has the preceding contraction, because $a\ge q$.  Expanding the
weighted differences for any positive sequence $z$ gives
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^q w_i(\log z_{a+i+1}-\log z_{a+i})
 =\log z_b+\log z_{b+1}-\log z_a-\log z_{a+1}.
\]
Applying this separately to the original $V$ and $\omega$ in the exact
adjacent identity proves
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}
 &=\log\frac{V_aV_{a+1}}{V_bV_{b+1}}
  +\log\frac{\omega_b\omega_{b+1}}{\omega_a\omega_{a+1}}
  -\sum_{j=0}^{q+1}c_j[-\log(1-T_{s-1+j}^{-1})],\\
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}&=2\sum_{i=0}^qw_i\log\epsilon_{a+i},\qquad
 (c_0,\ldots,c_{q+1})=(1,3,\underbrace{4,\ldots,4}_{q-2},3,1).
\end{split}\tag{GRB9}\label{GRB:shifted-action}
\end{equation}
Indeed $c_j=w_j+w_{j-1}$ with absent weights zero, so their total is
$2\sum_iw_i=4q$.  These four endpoints and every coefficient are derived
from the original maps.  The full finite scalar enclosures, four AMT
endpoint errors and their proof are GRA27--32; GRB1--4 intersects them
with all eligible earlier bounds.  For $t=s/q\to\infty$ and
$t\log t=o(q/k)$ the complete proof GRA19--35 consequently gives
\begin{equation}
 \log\min_{a\le N\le b}\epsilon_N\sim\frac{q}{2t\log t},\qquad
 \log\mathcal P_{k,s}\sim-\frac{q}{t\log t},\qquad
 J_{k,s}^{\rm action}\sim\frac{2q^2}{t\log t}.
\tag{GRB10}\label{GRB:shifted-rates}
\end{equation}
The proof controls the full adjacent index interval $s-1,\ldots,s+q$;
its error is uniform on that interval, as shown there before summing.

The exact transfer to the mixed spaces retains $I:K\hookrightarrow E$
and $\Lambda:E\twoheadrightarrow B$.  For each unchanged positive metric
$G_N^v$ its minimum section is
$S_N^v=(G_N^v)^{-1}\Lambda^*(\Lambda(G_N^v)^{-1}\Lambda^*)^{-1}$.
It satisfies $\Lambda S_N^v=1$ and $I^*G_N^vS_N^v=0$ by direct
multiplication.  Relative to any fixed section $S_*$, the frame change
from $[I,S_*]$ to $[I,S_N^v]$ has identity diagonal because
$\Lambda(S_N^v-S_*)=0$.  Its determinant is one.  Block orthogonality
therefore gives
\[
 \det(I^*G_N^vI)\,
 \det(\Lambda(G_N^v)^{-1}\Lambda^*)^{-1}
 =|\det[I,S_*]|^2\det G_N^v.
\]
The four-endpoint return cancels that same frame constant and proves
$F_K^v+F_B^v=\mathcal B^v$.  Thus the complete allocation and its matching
budget in GRA36 still sum to GRB9: the occurrence of $F_K^{(k)}$ in one

\providecommand{\im}{\operatorname{im}}

\section{Original objects and the finite comparison data}
This calculation composes the full-form comparisons proved in NSO1--31,
QOT and EOR with the actual coefficient maps of NSR, CPQ and SFC.
The analytic localization proof remains the complete QOT/EOR provider.
Here every subsequent map, determinant operation, endpoint sign and
interval intersection is proved explicitly. The comparison constants below
are their finite constants, not their asymptotic limits.

Fix the original actual hypothetical simple quartet, its conductor,
one original five-orbit period and its branch. Keep
$0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$, the complete conductor coefficients
$a_{uv}$, $v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A\le80$,
$\mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)\ne0$, and $\alpha_A=v!/\mu_v$.
Keep the original rank domain, including $|u|\ge R_*$.
For an actual degree $a=4l+1\ge9$, set
\[
\begin{gathered}
q_a=(a+1)^2,\quad q'_a=(a-7)^2,\quad c'_a=a/2-4,\quad
m_a=16a-48-v,\\
t_a=8a-16-t_{0,a},\quad
j_{0,a}=8a-32-v+t_{0,a},\quad m_a=t_a+j_{0,a},\\
D=N-v,\qquad A=D-q'_a,\qquad R_a=a\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP1}\label{efp:data}
\]
The symbol $\Delta$ on a single-degree form means its order-$a$ value
minus its order-one value. On a paired object it means the value in
$p=1:(k,k+4)$ minus its value in $p=0:(1,1)$, at the same cutoffs
and in the same coefficient or quotient-value frames.
The capital $A$ in this section is the polynomial degree bound, not a
coefficient matrix or an assigned allocation. Each cutoff and each source
order retains its original centre. The full measures are
\[
dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
 |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy,\qquad
h_{n,s}=(2\pi)^{s/2}n!(s/2)_n,\quad s=1,a.
\tag{EFP2}\label{efp:measure}
\]
The native metric is
$D_{a,D}^{(s)}=A_{\ell,a}^*\overline{(H_{Y,a,N}^{(s)})^{-1}}A_{\ell,a}$.
The invertible $A_{\ell,a}$ is the same actual coefficient map in both
orders. $H_Y$ is the complete quotient of the original polynomial
norm by its relation columns. In particular its polynomial degree is
$A=N-v-q'_a$. Complex conjugation in this duality formula is retained.

Write $\ell=(a-1)/4$ and $\tau_r=2r+1/2$. NSO's finite full-monomial
comparison, transferred by its exact ordinary-transpose Taylor equation
and its whole lower-evaluation minimum, gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
\rho_a=\frac{(2\pi)^{2\ell}}{(1/2)_{2\ell}},\qquad
\mathfrak a_a=\rho_a\prod_{r=0}^{\ell-1}\tau_r^2,\\
\mathfrak b_{a,D}=\rho_a
 \prod_{r=0}^{\ell-1}\{4(D+r+1)^2+\tau_r^2\},\\
\mathfrak b_{a,D}^{-1}D_{a,D}^{(1)}
 \preceq D_{a,D}^{(a)}
 \preceq\mathfrak a_a^{-1}D_{a,D}^{(1)}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP3}\label{efp:nso}
\]
This applies throughout the original NSO domain, irrespective of the
additional QOT/EOR tests below.

Here are the finite QOT inputs with the actual degree $a$ and polynomial
bound $A$ written separately. They are used when
\[
a\ge29,\quad R_a\le2^{-33}q_a,\quad 0\le A\le2q_a.
\tag{EFP4}\label{efp:qguard}
\]
The applications below also verify the provider's stronger
$A+\ell\le2q_a$ range. In the standard degree-$a$ window,
$A_{\min}=16a-49-v\ge335$ for $a\ge29$ and $v\le80$,
while $A_{\max}=2q_a-v-q'_a$ and
$2q_a-(A_{\max}+\ell)=q'_a+v-\ell\ge477+v$.
The last expression increases for $a\ge29$.
Put $q=q_a$, $q'=q'_a$,
$\epsilon_0=2^{-32}$, $\vartheta=\pi/2$, $h=1/q$, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
\ell_s=(s-1)/4,\quad Q_s=q'+\ell_s,\quad
\beta_s=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}}{\sqrt2\Gamma(s/2)},\quad \beta_1=1,\\
C_L=\pi/\Gamma(3/4)^2,\qquad
K_q=\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}}{C_L}\frac{(2q+1)^2}{q}e^{-2q},\\
\mathcal E_a=\frac{qR_a^2}{\epsilon_0^2q^2-R_a^2}+\log(1+K_q),\\
r_{\ell_s}=\frac{\sum_{r=0}^{\ell_s-1}(2r+1/2)^2}{\epsilon_0^2q^2}
 +\log(1+K_q)\quad(\ell_s>0),\qquad r_0=0,\\
L_h=C_L(\sin h)^{3/2},\quad
U_h=\frac{\Gamma(1/4)^2}{2\pi}(\sin h)^{-1/2},\\
K_{A,Q}=2Q+1+2A+2\sqrt{A(A+2Q)},\\
b^-_{a,A,s}=-\mathcal E_a+\log L_h-K_{A,Q_s}\log(1+h/\vartheta),\\
b^+_{a,A,s}=\mathcal E_a+r_{\ell_s}+\log U_h+
                         K_{A,Q_s}\log\frac1{1-h/\vartheta},\\
d^-_{a,A}=4\sum_{r=0}^{\ell-1}\arsinh\sqrt{(A+r+1/2)/(2q')},\quad
d^+_{a,A}=4\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\arsinh\sqrt{(A+r+1/2)/(2q')},\\
L^Q_{a,A}=b^-_{a,A,a}-b^+_{a,A,1}-d^-_{a,A},\quad
U^Q_{a,A}=b^+_{a,A,a}-b^-_{a,A,1}+d^+_{a,A},\\
\kappa_a=\beta_a(4q'/\pi)^{2\ell}
       =2^{10\ell}\frac{(2\ell)!}{(4\ell)!}(a-7)^{4\ell}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP5}\label{efp:qconstants}
\]
In particular $\kappa_a$ is a displayed source mass and scale, retained
without a change of measure or a change of coefficient vectors.

We specify EOR's finite tests and endpoints as well. Put
$m_0=\lfloor A/2\rfloor$, $m_1=\lfloor(A-1)/2\rfloor$. In parity block
$\varepsilon=0,1$ require $m_\varepsilon\ge1$ and
\[
\alpha_\varepsilon=(q'+\varepsilon-1/2)/m_\varepsilon,\quad
\beta_\varepsilon=\vartheta q/m_\varepsilon,\quad
(\alpha_\varepsilon,\beta_\varepsilon)\in
 [3/2,5/2]\times[\pi/2,3\pi/2].
\tag{EFP6}\label{efp:parity}
\]
For this pair let $[A_\varepsilon,B_\varepsilon]$ be the EIQ equilibrium
support for $\beta_\varepsilon\sqrt{x}-\alpha_\varepsilon\log x$.
It is specified by the unique positive endpoints
$A_\varepsilon=u_\varepsilon^2$, $B_\varepsilon=w_\varepsilon^2$ satisfying
\[
u_\varepsilon K(\zeta_\varepsilon)=
 \alpha_\varepsilon\pi/\beta_\varepsilon,\quad
w_\varepsilon E(\zeta_\varepsilon)=
 (\alpha_\varepsilon+2)\pi/\beta_\varepsilon,\quad
\zeta_\varepsilon^2=1-u_\varepsilon^2/w_\varepsilon^2.
\]
$K(z)=\int_0^{\pi/2}(1-z^2\sin^2t)^{-1/2}dt$ and
$E(z)=\int_0^{\pi/2}(1-z^2\sin^2t)^{1/2}dt$.
For each block separately, suppress its index and set
\[
\begin{gathered}
g=B-A,\quad h_*=\frac{\beta}{2\pi(B+1)(B+2)(B+3)},\quad
c_*=\tfrac23h_*\sqrt g,\\
\kappa_*=\min\{Ah_*\sqrt g,\ \beta\sqrt B/[6\pi(3B+2)]\},
\quad\eta_*=\min\{A/2,1\},\\
L_*=\max_{A\le x\le B}|\beta/(2\sqrt x)-\alpha/x|,
\quad C_*^2=32e^{3L_*/2},\\
\eta_m=\left\{\frac{\log(C_*^2(B+2))+10\log(m+1)}
                         {c_*m}\right\}^{2/3},
\quad \varepsilon_m=(m+1)^{-6},\\
m\ge\max\{1,2/g\},\qquad \eta_m\le\eta_*,
\qquad m\kappa_*\sqrt{\eta_m}\ge\ell+2 .
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP7}\label{efp:eguards}
\]
The maximum defining $L_*$ is evaluated at $A,B$ and, when inside this
interval, $(4\alpha/\beta)^2$; differentiating the expression proves
this finite candidate list. Retain both parity blocks, their actual
$\eta_\varepsilon,\varepsilon_\varepsilon$, and define
\[
\begin{aligned}
F^-_{a,A}&=\min_{\varepsilon=0,1}
 [\ell\log(A_\varepsilon-\eta_\varepsilon)
                    +\log(1-\varepsilon_\varepsilon)],\\
F^+_{a,A}&=\max_{\varepsilon=0,1}
 [\ell\log(B_\varepsilon+\eta_\varepsilon)
                    +\log(1+\varepsilon_\varepsilon)],\\
L^E_{a,A}&=b^-_{a,A,a}-b^+_{a,A,1}
                 +2\ell\log(q\vartheta/(2q'))+F^-_{a,A},\\
U^E_{a,A}&=b^+_{a,A,a}-b^-_{a,A,1}
                 +2\ell\log(q\vartheta/(2q'))+F^+_{a,A}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP8}\label{efp:econstants}
\]
At a cutoff satisfying \eqref{efp:qguard}, use
$\underline b_{a,A}=L^Q_{a,A}$,
$\overline b_{a,A}=U^Q_{a,A}$, intersecting with the EOR endpoints
only when \eqref{efp:parity}--\eqref{efp:eguards} hold in both blocks:
\[
\underline b_{a,A}=\max\{L^Q_{a,A},L^E_{a,A}\},\qquad
\overline b_{a,A}=\min\{U^Q_{a,A},U^E_{a,A}\}.
\tag{EFP9}\label{efp:qor}
\]
The proved QOT/EOR full-form inequalities give, on the same original
dual coefficient metric,
\[
\kappa_a^{-1}e^{-\overline b_{a,A}}D_{a,D}^{(1)}
 \preceq D_{a,D}^{(a)}
 \preceq\kappa_a^{-1}e^{-\underline b_{a,A}}D_{a,D}^{(1)}.
\tag{EFP10}\label{efp:analyticdual}
\]
These are the two accepted analytic inputs used below. Their full proofs
are QOT's full original source-order theorem and EOR's full high-endpoint
theorem, including its exact two-sheet square map. No analytically
unproved bound is used in the propagation.

\section{Exact intersection before any restriction or quotient}
Define $\lambda^-_{a,D}=-\log\mathfrak b_{a,D}$ and
$\lambda^+_{a,D}=-\log\mathfrak a_a$ on the whole native domain.
On every native endpoint define $w_{a,D}=\lambda^+_{a,D}-\lambda^-_{a,D}$,
using the final endpoints prescribed here.
At an endpoint where \eqref{efp:qguard} holds replace those endpoints by
\[
\begin{aligned}
\lambda^-_{a,D}
 &=\max\{-\log\mathfrak b_{a,D},
                    -\log\kappa_a-\overline b_{a,D-q'_a}\},\\
\lambda^+_{a,D}
 &=\min\{-\log\mathfrak a_a,
                    -\log\kappa_a-\underline b_{a,D-q'_a}\},\\
w_{a,D}&=\lambda^+_{a,D}-\lambda^-_{a,D}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP11}\label{efp:lambda}
\]
Then
$e^{\lambda^-_{a,D}}D^{(1)}_{a,D}
 \preceq D^{(a)}_{a,D}\preceq
 e^{\lambda^+_{a,D}}D^{(1)}_{a,D}$.
For any vector, both original lower inequalities hold on its same
quadratic form, so their larger scalar is still a lower bound; the
upper proof uses the smaller scalar. Applying to a nonzero vector
shows $\lambda^-\le\lambda^+$, hence $w\ge0$.
In the guarded domain one can record the unchanged source scalar by
writing
\[
\begin{gathered}
\underline b^{\,*}_{a,D}
 =\max\{\log\mathfrak a_a-\log\kappa_a,\underline b_{a,D-q'_a}\},
\quad
\overline b^{\,*}_{a,D}
 =\min\{\log\mathfrak b_{a,D}-\log\kappa_a,\overline b_{a,D-q'_a}\},\\
\lambda^-=-\log\kappa_a-\overline b^{\,*},\qquad
\lambda^+=-\log\kappa_a-\underline b^{\,*}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP12}\label{efp:center}
\]
These equalities change the written scalar endpoints only. They do
not multiply any original matrix by $\kappa_a$.

Here is the full typed restriction and quotient argument. Let $Y$ be a
fixed coefficient subspace, $K\subset Y$ a fixed subspace, $X_K$ an
independent frame of $K$ and $X_Q$ fixed lifts of a basis of $Y/K$.
For each of the two metrics $G^{(s)}$ define
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q^{(s)}&=X_Q^*G^{(s)}X_Q-X_Q^*G^{(s)}X_K
 (X_K^*G^{(s)}X_K)^{-1}X_K^*G^{(s)}X_Q,\\
x^*Q^{(s)}x&=\min_z (X_Qx+X_Kz)^*G^{(s)}(X_Qx+X_Kz).
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP13}\label{efp:quotient}
\]
The positive Hessian makes the minimum attained and unique in $z$.
Its normal equation gives the displayed formula. For every $z$ the
two scalar metric inequalities hold on the same vector $X_Qx+X_Kz$.
Taking their minima therefore gives
$e^{\lambda^-}Q^{(1)}\preceq Q^{(a)}
 \preceq e^{\lambda^+}Q^{(1)}$.
Restriction is the case $K=0$. An iterated quotient has the same
property by successive applications to the original affine fibres.
A block diagonal sum of four copies has the same constants by addition
of the four quadratic inequalities. In particular the coefficient
dimensions do not introduce an extra factor of four in these constants.

For a rank-$r$ such form, conjugating by the inverse positive square root
$(Q^{(1)})^{-1/2}$ for the proof of the determinant inequality bounds each of
its $r$ positive relative eigenvalues between $e^{\lambda^-}$ and
$e^{\lambda^+}$. It follows that
\[
r\lambda^-_{a,D}\le
 \log\det Q^{(a)}-\log\det Q^{(1)}
 \le r\lambda^+_{a,D}.
\tag{EFP14}\label{efp:det}
\]
All determinant ratios here use the same value frame in both orders.
Replacing that common frame by $C$ multiplies both determinants by
$|\det C|^2$ and hence leaves the displayed difference exactly unchanged.
This proof does not change the original forms.

\section{Four endpoints and equal-rank contractions}
For any of the actual four cutoffs $N_i$ let $D_i=N_i-v$.
Use exactly
$\mathcal Rf=-f(N_0)-f(N_1)+f(N_2)+f(N_3)$ and put
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Phi_a^-&=-\lambda^+_{a,D_0}-\lambda^+_{a,D_1}
                      +\lambda^-_{a,D_2}+\lambda^-_{a,D_3},\\
\Phi_a^+&=-\lambda^-_{a,D_0}-\lambda^-_{a,D_1}
                      +\lambda^+_{a,D_2}+\lambda^+_{a,D_3},\\
W_a&=\sum_{i=0}^3w_{a,D_i}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP15}\label{efp:returnconstants}
\]
For any fixed rank-$r$ subquotient over this window,
\[
r\Phi_a^-\le
 \Delta\mathcal R\log\det Q\le r\Phi_a^+,\qquad
\Phi_a^+-\Phi_a^-=W_a.
\tag{EFP16}\label{efp:return}
\]
Indeed negate the upper endpoint at $i=0,1$ for the lower bound,
and add the lower endpoints at $i=2,3$; the upper proof reverses these
choices. This verifies every sign. Where \eqref{efp:center} applies
at all four endpoints, the four copies of $-r\log\kappa_a$ cancel
because $-1-1+1+1=0$. The lower and upper expressions become,
respectively,
$r(\underline b^*_0+\underline b^*_1-\overline b^*_2-\overline b^*_3)$
and
$r(\overline b^*_0+\overline b^*_1-\underline b^*_2-\underline b^*_3)$.
The same cancellation is valid if the NSO-only endpoints are
written algebraically with this same scalar. It requires no new
analytic comparison at an unguarded endpoint.

For two same-rank source and target quotients in matched value
coordinates let $\omega=\det Q_t/\det Q_s$. Both receive
\eqref{efp:det}. Subtracting the two directed intervals yields
\[
-rw_{a,D}\le\Delta\log\omega(N)\le rw_{a,D},\qquad
|\Delta\mathcal R\log\omega|\le rW_a .
\tag{EFP17}\label{efp:contraction}
\]
The common scalar cancels already in this same-endpoint ratio.
For two contractions of ranks $r_1,r_2$ in the same ambient metric
the product consequently has return difference bounded by
$(r_1+r_2)W_a$. No lower singular-value estimate for a separate
observation matrix has been used.

\section{The five original CPQ quotients and their full frames}
Take $j=k+4$, fix $e\in\{0,1\}$, and use the actual pairs
$p=0:(1,1)$ and $p=1:(k,j)$.
All target cutoffs here are
$N=(q_j-1,q_j,2q_j-1,2q_j)$.
The fixed coefficient maps are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\mathcal U=\C^{4m_j},\quad B=B_{\rm cof}:\C^{m_k}\hookrightarrow\mathcal U,
\quad\mathbb B=\operatorname{diag}(B_j,4):
 \mathcal U\twoheadrightarrow\C^{4t_j},\\
J=\ker\mathbb B,\quad \Phi=\mathbb BB,\quad
\mathscr B=\im B,\quad S=\im[B,F_e],\\
W=J+\mathscr B,\quad K_e=S\cap W,\quad T_e=S+W .
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP18}\label{efp:flags}
\]
The original Taylor cochain equation of NSR5 makes $F_e$ the same
fixed coefficient map in both source presentations, and identifies
$\C^{r_e}$ with $S/\mathscr B$, $r_e=8k+24-3e$.
Keep $C_T=[U_R,U_b,U_o]$ and
$\pi_o=[0\ 0\ I]C_T^{-1}\mathbb B$.
For $z\in\mathcal U$, $\pi_oz=0$ is equivalent to
$\mathbb Bz\in\im(\mathbb BB)$, hence to $z\in W$.
Surjectivity follows from that of $\mathbb B$.
Thus $\pi_o$ induces the exact isomorphism
$\mathcal U/W\to\C^{r_o}$.
Writing $E_e=\pi_oF_e$, its restriction gives
$S/K_e\to\im E_e$ and $T_e/W\to\im E_e$.
The identity on representatives is the exact isomorphism
$S/K_e\to T_e/W$. Its target fibre is larger, so its attained
target norm is at most its attained source norm.

Choose the original fixed $Z_h$ spanning $\ker E_e$, complement $Z_c$,
$C_P=[Z_h,Z_c]$, $A_e=E_eZ_c$ and $C_o=[A_e,R_e^o]$.
Keep $F=BJ_0$, $E=\ker(\Phi J_0)$ and the original lifts $Z_R$.
The five quotient maps, with their full original coefficient fibres,
are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\text{form}&Y/K&\text{value lifts }X_Q&\text{rank}\\ \hline
G_{h,e}&K_e/\mathscr B&F_eZ_h&h_e\\
G_{s,e}&S/K_e&F_eZ_c&c_e\\
G_{t,e}&T_e/W&F_eZ_c&c_e\\
Q_{{\rm rem},e}&\mathcal U/T_e&
   \text{fixed lifts of }U_oR_e^o\text{ under }\mathbb B&r_o-c_e\\
A_{\rm inv}&\im F/FE&FZ_R&r_I
\end{array}\tag{EFP19}\label{efp:five}
\]
Here $c_e=\rank E_e$, $h_e=r_e-c_e$,
$r_I=j_{0,k}-\dim\ker(\Phi J_0)$,
$r_o=4t_j-m_k+\dim\ker\Phi$, and
$r_b=t_k-\dim\ker\Phi+\dim\ker(\Phi J_0)$.
For the fourth row $\pi_o$ followed by the quotient by $\im A_e$
is the value map. For the fifth row injectivity of $B$ identifies
$\im F/FE$ with the original coefficient quotient in $Z_R$.
Every row is therefore precisely \eqref{efp:quotient}, evaluated in
$\mathcal D_N=\operatorname{diag}(D_{j,N-v}^{(s_1)},4)$.
In particular the fourth rank is not replaced by the whole $r_o$.

With $V_X^p=\mathcal R\log\det X^p$, \eqref{efp:return} gives
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
h_e\Phi_j^-\le\Delta V_h\le h_e\Phi_j^+,&
c_e\Phi_j^-\le\Delta V_s\le c_e\Phi_j^+,\\
c_e\Phi_j^-\le\Delta V_t\le c_e\Phi_j^+,&
(r_o-c_e)\Phi_j^-\le\Delta V_{\rm rem}\le(r_o-c_e)\Phi_j^+,\\
r_I\Phi_j^-\le\Delta V_A\le r_I\Phi_j^+.&
\end{array}\tag{EFP20}\label{efp:cpq}
\]
The identical proof also applies to $V_I,V_b,V_o,V_{\rm conn}$
with ranks $r_I,r_b,r_o,r_e$. These inequalities retain the exact
correlations
$V_{\rm conn}=V_h+V_s$ and $V_o=V_t+V_{\rm rem}$.
They refine CPQ19 without replacing any summand.

For clarity the absolute frame identities that precede every return
are still
\[
\begin{gathered}
|\det C_P|^2\det H_{\rm conn}=\det G_h\det G_s,\qquad
|\det C_o|^2\det Q_o=\det G_t\det Q_{\rm rem},\\
|\det C_T|^2(\det H_{B,j,D})^4
 =\det H_{\rm inv}\det Q_b^E\det Q_o,\\
P(N)=4\log\det H_{B,j,D}-\log\det Q_{\rm rem}
 -\log\det G_s-\log\det A_{\rm inv}+\log\mathfrak c_e,\\
\mathfrak c_e=
 \frac{|\det C_T|^2|\det C_o|^2|\det C_b|^2}
 {c_{{\rm conn},e}|\det C_W|^2}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP21}\label{efp:frames}
\]
These follow by the Schur eliminations in \eqref{efp:quotient} in
the displayed full frames and the original
$Q_b^{\rm CIT}=C_b^*Q_b^EC_b$. Thus each constant has its original
place before $\mathcal R1=0$ cancels it. The propagation uses the
same minimum over the original lower-evaluation columns. For example,
for the raw old kernel Gram $G_K^{\rm raw}$, appended kernel rows $a_n$,
quotient rows $b_n$ and preceding projection coefficients $C_n$,
the unchanged increment is
\[
Q^+=Q+R_n^*(I+a_n(G_K^{\rm raw})^{-1}a_n^*)^{-1}R_n,
\qquad R_n=b_n-a_nC_n .
\tag{EFP22}\label{efp:row}
\]
It follows by minimizing
$\eta^*G_K^{\rm raw}\eta+\|R_nx-a_n\eta\|^2$.
Thus neither the old lower Gram nor the mixed kernel denominator
is removed by the finite scalar comparisons.

\section{The common return and the complete allocation intervals}
Put $I=\im(BJ_0)$, $K_i=I\cap J$, $W_i=I+J$.
The identity maps $I/K_i\to W_i/J$ and $S/K_e\to T_e/W$ are
bijections: each kernel is the stated intersection and each image
is the stated sum modulo its denominator.
Their Gram ratios in the actual matched frames are
$\omega_{\rm inv}=\det H_{\rm inv}/\det A_{\rm inv}$ and
$\omega_{{\rm conn},e}=\det G_t/\det G_s$.
Consequently for the actual common return
$\mathcal L_e=\mathcal R\log(\omega_{\rm inv}\omega_{{\rm conn},e})$,
\[
|\Delta\mathcal L_e|\le B_e,\qquad
B_e=(r_I+c_e)W_j,\qquad
\mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}=\mathcal L_e+V_b .
\tag{EFP23}\label{efp:common}
\]
This is \eqref{efp:contraction} applied to both exact quotient
bijections. In particular the common projection cross block in
SFC5 is retained; a product determinant does not require that
cross block to vanish.

Let $L_-^p,L_+^p$ be the actual SFC9--10 finite expressions in pair
$p$. They are determined from
$\omega(N_0)=\det G_t\det H_{\rm inv}/(\det G_s\det A_{\rm inv})$,
$x=\operatorname{arcosh}(\omega(N_0)^{-1/2})$, and
$\eta=\tfrac12\log(\det\mathcal D_{N_1}/\det\mathcal D_{N_0})$,
$b=\tfrac12\log(\det\mathcal D_{N_2}/\det\mathcal D_{N_1})$,
$c=\tfrac12\log(\det\mathcal D_{N_3}/\det\mathcal D_{N_2})$,
all in that same pair. To make their finite meaning explicit, put
$f(t)=-2\log\cosh t$, $A_c=e^{2c}$, $B_b=e^{2b}$.
Let $z_*\ge1$ be the unique root
\[
A_c^2B_bz^3-\{A_cB_b(A_c+1)-3A_c\}z^2
 -(3A_cB_b-A_c-1)z-1=0.
\]
Then $t_*=b+c+\tfrac12\log z_*$ and
$\widehat t=\min\{x+\eta,\max(\max(0,x-\eta),t_*)\}$ give
\[
L_-=f(x+\eta+b)+f(x+\eta+b+c)-f(x)-f(x+\eta),\qquad
L_+=f((\widehat t-b)_+)+f((\widehat t-b-c)_+)-f(\widehat t)-f(x).
\]
Their complete proof is SFC9--16; they are actual scalar evaluations,
not independently assigned angle bounds.

Use the following exact two-pair intersections:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde L_-^1&=\max\{L_-^1,L_-^0-B_e\},&
\widetilde L_+^1&=\min\{L_+^1,L_+^0+B_e\},\\
\widetilde L_-^0&=\max\{L_-^0,L_-^1-B_e\},&
\widetilde L_+^0&=\min\{L_+^0,L_+^1+B_e\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP24}\label{efp:angleintersection}
\]
Each contains its actual $\mathcal L_e^p$: combine CIT35 in pair
$p$ with \eqref{efp:common} and CIT35 in the other pair.
Consequently the earlier signed remainder lies in
$[V_b^p+\widetilde L_-^p,V_b^p+\widetilde L_+^p]$.
This interval may also be intersected with the already proved
CIT33 bounds. In source-order differences the exact identity gives
$\Delta\mathcal P_e^{\rm rest}\in
[r_b\Phi_j^--B_e,r_b\Phi_j^++B_e]$.

Write $\mathcal T_j^p=\mathcal T_{j,s_1}$ in its actual pair.
Keep the complete $Z_e^p=V_b^p+V_{\rm rem}^p+V_s^p+V_A^p$ and
the actual native cap $U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native}$ only on its proved
domain. Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
\widehat K_-^p=\max\{0,(\widetilde L_-^p+Z_e^p)/4\},\\
\widehat K_+^p=\min\{\mathcal T_j^p,U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native},
                      (\widetilde L_+^p+Z_e^p)/4\}.
\end{gathered}\tag{EFP25}\label{efp:K}
\]
Omit the cap entry outside its original domain.
The exact CPQ20 affine identity $4S_{K,j}^p=\mathcal L_e^p+Z_e^p$
and $S_{R,j}^p=\mathcal T_j^p-S_{K,j}^p$ prove
$\widehat K_-^p\le S_{K,j}^p\le\widehat K_+^p$ and the reversed
residual interval. All four summands in $Z_e^p$ remain.

The native allocation maps are
$S_{K,j}=\mathcal R\log\det H_{B,j,D}$
and $S_{R,j}=\mathcal R\log\det(J_j^*D_{j,D}J_j)$,
of ranks $t_j$ and $j_{0,j}$, with $J_j$ the full fixed frame
of $\ker B_j$. The first is the attained quotient by that kernel;
the second is its restriction. Equation \eqref{efp:return} therefore
controls both, with the original inverse-metric signs.
Writing the actual $\Delta\mathcal T_j=\mathcal T_j^1-\mathcal T_j^0$,
put
\[
\begin{aligned}
A_K^-&=\max\{t_j\Phi_j^-,\ \Delta\mathcal T_j-j_{0,j}\Phi_j^+\},\\
A_K^+&=\min\{t_j\Phi_j^+,\ \Delta\mathcal T_j-j_{0,j}\Phi_j^-\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP26}\label{efp:allocationdifference}
\]
The first pair of entries is the direct kernel interval.
The second follows by subtracting the residual interval from the
exact equality $\Delta S_K+\Delta S_R=\Delta\mathcal T_j$.
They contain the actual $\Delta S_K$ and so are ordered.
The fully propagated SFC19/HJR35 receiver is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\max\{\widehat K_-^1,\widehat K_-^0+A_K^-\}
 &\le S_{K,j}^{1}
 \le\min\{\widehat K_+^1,\widehat K_+^0+A_K^+\},\\
\max\{\widehat K_-^0,\widehat K_-^1-A_K^+\}
 &\le S_{K,j}^{0}
 \le\min\{\widehat K_+^0,\widehat K_+^1-A_K^-\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP27}\label{efp:allocation}
\]
To prove the first line add the actual difference interval to the
pair-zero interval and intersect with pair one. For the second line
subtract that difference from pair one and intersect with pair zero.
Subtract these endpoints in reversed order from each same-pair
$\mathcal T_j^p$ to obtain the complete residual allocation bounds.
No allocation value is selected by this interval composition.

\section{The degree-k source at the displaced NFR cutoffs}
The proper source quotient is
$Q_{\rm inv}^{p}=(J_0^*D_{k,D}^{(s_0)}J_0)/E$ in the original
$Z_R$ values, while the invariant target form is $H_{\rm inv}^p$.
They have the same rank $r_I$ and retain different centres $c'_k,c'_j$.
Let $V_Q^p=\mathcal R\log\det Q_{\rm inv}^p$ and
$\mathcal M^p=V_I^p-V_Q^p$.
For the source constants evaluate \eqref{efp:lambda} at degree $k$
and at the actual $D_i=N_i-v$ used by the target.
Relative to the standard degree-$k$ window these cutoffs have
displacement $b=q_j-q_k=8k+24$ at the two lows and $2b$ at the two highs.

For these source cutoffs the largest polynomial degree is
\[
A_{k,\max}=2q_j-v-q'_k=k^2+34k+1-v.
\tag{EFP28}\label{efp:source-degree}
\]
For $k\ge37$ one has
$2q_k-A_{k,\max}=k^2-30k+1+v\ge260+v$,
and
$2q_k-(A_{k,\max}+(k-1)/4)
 =k^2-(121/4)k+5/4+v\ge251+v$.
Both right sides increase for $k\ge37$ by differentiation, or by
taking their exact positive finite differences at successive $4l+1$.
The smallest degree is
$q_j-v-1-q'_k=24k-25-v\ge0$.
Thus the stated $k\ge37$ guard, together with the actual QOT root
test at $k$, validates the polynomial degree range at all four
displaced source endpoints. It is a sufficient finite guard; no
minimality is claimed. EOR is added at an endpoint only when its
own two-block tests hold with this degree-$k$ value of $A$.

Let $\Phi_k^\pm$ and $W_k$ now mean precisely these displaced
degree-$k$ values, while $\Phi_j^\pm,W_j$ use the target degree.
Then
\[
\Lambda_{\rm new}^-=r_I(\Phi_j^--\Phi_k^+)
 \le\Delta\mathcal M
 \le r_I(\Phi_j^+-\Phi_k^-)=\Lambda_{\rm new}^+ .
\tag{EFP29}\label{efp:nfr}
\]
This follows by subtracting the full rank-$r_I$ source-return
interval from its target-return interval. At a single cutoff their
log-determinant difference retains the centre
$-r_I\log(\kappa_j/\kappa_k)$ wherever both comparisons are guarded.
Only after the four endpoint sum do the four copies cancel.
Replacing the degree-$k$ endpoints by degree-$j$ endpoints would
change this exact source map and is not used.

For each pair define the exact translation
$D_e^p=-V_t^p+V_s^p+V_A^p-V_Q^p$.
By NFR7, $\mathcal M^p=\mathcal L_e^p+D_e^p$.
Thus \eqref{efp:angleintersection} gives the interval
$[\widetilde L_-^p+D_e^p,\widetilde L_+^p+D_e^p]$.
Intersect it with $-V_Q^p\le\mathcal M^p$ and, on the cap domain, with
\[
\mathcal M^p\le
 4U_{j,s_1}^{\rm native}-V_b^p-V_t^p-V_{\rm rem}^p-V_Q^p .
\]
Call these resulting endpoints $\widehat M_-^p,\widehat M_+^p$.
The earlier NFR12 interval becomes
\[
\begin{aligned}
\max\{\widehat M_-^1,\widehat M_-^0+\Lambda_{\rm new}^-\}
 &\le\mathcal M^1
 \le\min\{\widehat M_+^1,\widehat M_+^0+\Lambda_{\rm new}^+\},\\
\max\{\widehat M_-^0,\widehat M_-^1-\Lambda_{\rm new}^+\}
 &\le\mathcal M^0
 \le\min\{\widehat M_+^0,\widehat M_+^1-\Lambda_{\rm new}^-\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP30}\label{efp:nfrinterval}
\]
Its proof is the same addition and subtraction of exact directed
intervals used in \eqref{efp:allocation}, now with both actual sources.

Finally retain the full proper-source compatibility error.
Use the original endpoint constants
\[
\begin{gathered}
a_i^p=2r_I\log\max(1,K_-^p(D_i))+r_I\log c_+^p(D_i),\\
b_i^p=2r_I\log\max(1,K_+^p(D_i))-r_I\log c_-^p(D_i),\\
E_p^-=-b_0^p-b_1^p-a_2^p-a_3^p,\qquad
E_p^+=a_0^p+a_1^p+b_2^p+b_3^p.
\end{gathered}
\]
For $\mathcal C^p=\mathcal R\log(\det\mathcal H_V^p/
\det\mathcal H_E^p)$, NFR5 is the exact identity
$\mathcal M^p=\mathcal C^p+
\mathcal R\log\omega_{\rm inv}^p+\mathcal E^p$ with
$E_p^-\le\mathcal E^p\le E_p^+$.
Equations \eqref{efp:contraction} and \eqref{efp:nfr} therefore give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Lambda_{\rm new}^- -r_IW_j-E_1^++E_0^-
 \le\Delta\mathcal C
 \le\Lambda_{\rm new}^+ +r_IW_j-E_1^-+E_0^+ .
\end{aligned}\tag{EFP31}\label{efp:nfrerror}
\]
Subtract the target invariant-loss difference and the error
difference from $\Delta\mathcal M$ to verify each of these signs.
The original source-complement projections, constants, and both
old low cutoffs remain in this formula.
% Bounded primitive-only receiving derivation, 16 September 2026.
% Analytic inputs: complete QOT+EOR provider; native maps: NSO and NSR.
% This fragment adds no analytic comparison theorem.
\section{The defining primitive source: exact finite intersection}
\label{sec:epr:primitive}
Keep the original fixed hypothetical simple quartet, the complete nonreduced
conductor, the full arithmetic unit, the fixed five-orbit period and logarithm
branch, and the original period domain $|u|\ge R_*$ (or its specified exact
period tests). Write
\[
 k=4\ell+1\ge9,\qquad j=k+4,\qquad
 q_a=(a+1)^2,\quad q'_a=(a-7)^2,\quad c'_a=a/2-4,
\]
\[
 D_e=q_j-v-1+e,\quad d_e=8k+24+e,\quad
 r_e=d_e-4e=8k+24-3e,\qquad e\in\{0,1\}.
\]
Here $v=\operatorname{ord}_0E_A$ is the actual conductor order,
$0\le v\le80$, and $\mu_v=E_A^{(v)}(0)\ne0$.
Every statement below fixes $e$; subtraction is between the source-order
pairs $p=0:(1,1)$ and $p=1:(k,j)$, never between $e=0$ and $e=1$.
The source centre is $c'_k$ and the target centre is $c'_j$.

The inherited finite constants are exactly NSO3--4/NSR2 and QOT11--14:
\[
 \ell_a=(a-1)/4,\qquad
 \rho_a=\frac{(2\pi)^{2\ell_a}}{(1/2)_{2\ell_a}},\qquad
 \mathfrak a_a=\rho_a\prod_{h=0}^{\ell_a-1}(2h+\tfrac12)^2,
\]
\[
 \mathfrak b_{a,D}=\rho_a\prod_{h=0}^{\ell_a-1}
       \{4(D+h+1)^2+(2h+\tfrac12)^2\},
\]
\[
 \kappa_a
 =2^{10\ell_a}\frac{(2\ell_a)!}{(4\ell_a)!}(a-7)^{4\ell_a},
 \qquad
 l_{a,b}=b^-_{a,b,a}-b^+_{a,b,1}
       -4\sum_{h=0}^{\ell_a-1}
          \operatorname{arsinh}\sqrt{\frac{b+h+1/2}{2q'_a}},
\]
\[
 u_{a,b}=b^+_{a,b,a}-b^-_{a,b,1}
       +4\sum_{h=1}^{\ell_a}
          \operatorname{arsinh}\sqrt{\frac{b+h+1/2}{2q'_a}}.
\]
The $b^\pm$ in the last two formulas are the complete GPA constants
QOT11--12, equivalently EOR6; the original inside-region error, source
mass, root radius, and trigonometric factors are retained.
No new analytic estimate replaces those constants.
In particular $\kappa_a$ is the original full-mass scalar of QOT20.

\paragraph{The exact source map.}
Let
\[
 E_A(z)=\sum_{b,c=0}^8a_{bc}e^{\beta_{8,b,c}z},\qquad
 \mathcal T_Ap(S')=\sum_{b,c=0}^8a_{bc}p(S'+\beta_{8,b,c}),
\]
and keep the unscaled original relation columns
\[
 r_h=\mathcal T_A(\chi_kS^h),\qquad 0\le h<d_e,
 \qquad \psi_k=\chi_{k-8}.
\]
At every lower root $\beta_{k-8,u,w}$ and every summand,
\[
 \beta_{k-8,u,w}+\beta_{8,b,c}=\beta_{k,u+b,w+c}.
\]
Thus $\chi_k$ vanishes term by term. The lower roots are distinct, since
$0<\delta<1/2$ and $\gamma>2$, and their complete product $\psi_k$
divides every $r_h$. For a polynomial of degree $n$,
\[
 \mathcal T_Ap
   =\sum_{t=0}^n\frac{\mu_t}{t!}p^{(t)}.
\]
The first nonzero summand has $t=v$, so the leading coefficient of $r_h$
is $\binom{q_k+h}{v}\mu_v\ne0$ and
$\deg r_h=q_k+h-v$. Consequently the full relation columns are independent,
their maximum degree is $D_e$, and the polynomial degree to which the
weighted QOT theorem is applied is exactly
\begin{equation}
 a_{{\rm src},e}
 =\max_{h<d_e}\deg(r_h/\psi_k)
 =q_k-v+d_e-1-q'_k
 =24k-25-v+e.
 \tag{EFP32}
\label{epr:eq:source-degree}
\end{equation}
No leading-term replacement was used to obtain the restriction map:
the entire polynomial $r_h/\psi_k$ is retained in that map.

Write $R_e^{\rm pol}$ for the columns of the unscaled original $r_h$
(not the divided polynomials $r_h/\psi_k$) in
$1,S',\ldots,S'^{D_e}$, and use the exact native monomial matrix
$\mathsf B_{s,c'_k,D_e}$ of NSO7. Put
\[
 C_e^{(s)}=\mathsf B_{s,c'_k,D_e}R_e^{\rm pol},\qquad
 H_e^{(s)}=(C_e^{(s)})^*C_e^{(s)},\qquad
 U_s=\operatorname{diag}(1/n!)\mathsf B_{s,c'_k,D_e}^{\mathsf T}.
\]
The Gram $H_e^{(s)}$ is precisely the integral Gram of all $r_h(c'_k+iy)$
for the original measure
\[
 dm_s(y)=\frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
       |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy .
\]
The underlying derivative values are complex-linear; hence
the transpose in $U_s$ is ordinary transpose.

For completeness, the relation annihilator is exactly the analytic plane
used in the original primitive quotient. If a native analytic column has
generating germ $G$, then $E_AG$ is an upper-grid exponential numerator.
For the lower columns $G=e^{\beta_{k-8,u,w}z}$ the same assertion follows
from the displayed root addition. Therefore
\[
 \Lambda_G(r_h)
   =\left[(\chi_k(\partial)\partial^h)(E_AG)\right]_{z=0}=0 .
\]
The full native columns $[V_0,T_0]$ have rank $q_k-v$:
if their Taylor combination vanishes through $D_e$, its product with $E_A$
is an upper-grid numerator vanishing through $D_e+v\ge q_k-1$;
the upper Vandermonde makes its coefficient vector zero, the fixed
numerator quotient of NSO12 makes its analytic class zero, and the lower
Vandermonde makes its lower coefficients zero. These are exactly the
same complete columns as NSO12--13 and NSO21. Since
$D_e+1-d_e=q_k-v$ and $C_e^{(s)}$ has rank $d_e$, this proves
\[
 \ker (C_e^{(s)})^{\mathsf T}=\operatorname{im}[V_0,T_0].
\]

The original Taylor primitive plane is the whole jet plane for $e=0$;
for $e=1$ it is the kernel of all four independent original rows
$\Lambda(\mathcal T_{B_i}\mathcal T_A\chi_j)$.
Its analytic subspace is the preceding complete plane.
Thus the original Taylor complement $P_e^{\rm Tay}$ and its native lifts
$P_e^{(s)}=U_s^{-1}P_e^{\rm Tay}$ give
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_e=(C_e^{(s)})^{\mathsf T}P_e^{(s)}
   =(R_e^{\rm pol})^{\mathsf T}
       \operatorname{diag}(n!)P_e^{\rm Tay}.
 \tag{EFP33}
\label{epr:eq:theta}
\end{equation}
This matrix is literally independent of $s$, including every factorial,
affine phase, mass and original coefficient. It has rank $r_e$;
it is square invertible for $e=0$, and has the original rank-$r_e$
constraint image for $e=1$.

For a value $x$ in this fixed rank-$r_e$ frame, the complete affine fibre is
\[
 P_e^{(s)}x+\operatorname{im}[V_0,T_0]
     =\{w:(C_e^{(s)})^{\mathsf T}w=\Theta_ex\}.
\]
Every member remains in the primitive plane, because the analytic plane
is contained in it. The Euclidean least norm on an onto fibre $Aw=b$ is
$b^*(AA^*)^{-1}b$: the minimizing vector is
$A^*(AA^*)^{-1}b$, and its inner product with every vector of $\ker A$
is zero. Here
\[
 AA^*=(C_e^{(s)})^{\mathsf T}\overline{C_e^{(s)}}
       =\overline{H_e^{(s)}}.
\]
We have consequently proved the exact original source identity
\begin{equation}
 H_{{\rm src},e}^{(s)}
   =\Theta_e^*\overline{(H_e^{(s)})^{-1}}\Theta_e .
 \tag{EFP34}
\label{epr:eq:source-map}
\end{equation}
This is the QOT26--27 map, with the identical NSO21,26/NSR9 value frame.
In particular the primitive source has not been identified with $H_Y$.
Although $D_e-q'_k=a_{{\rm src},e}$ numerically at this defining cutoff,
that equality alone proves no map of forms and no extension to later
primitive source cutoffs.

\paragraph{All finite guards.}
NSO25 and NSR6 hold on their original domain $k\ge9$ without a GPA radius
guard. Add the QOT source interval only where
\[
 k\ge29,\qquad R_k=k\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}\le2^{-33}q_k;
\]
add its defining target interval only where the corresponding conditions
hold for $j$. Retain those two conditions explicitly in paired use.
The actual polynomial degrees are
\[
 a_{{\rm src},e}=24k-25-v+e,\qquad
 a_{{\rm tgt},e}=D_e-q'_j=16k+15-v+e=16j-49-v+e .
\]
The requirements $0\le a_{{\rm src},e}$,
$a_{{\rm src},e}+\ell_k\le2q_k$ and their target analogues hold for
$k,j\ge29$: positivity follows using $v\le80$, while the upper expressions
are at most $25k$ and $17j$, respectively, and these are below
$2(k+1)^2$ and $2(j+1)^2$ on that domain. The exact degrees, not these
bounds, are used in all constants.

The EOR21 endpoint rule additionally requires, for both parity blocks,
\[
 m_\epsilon=\lfloor(a-\epsilon)/2\rfloor\ge1,\quad
 \alpha_\epsilon=(q'_b+\epsilon-\tfrac12)/m_\epsilon
       \in[3/2,5/2],\quad
 \beta_\epsilon=\frac{\pi q_b}{2m_\epsilon}\in[\pi/2,3\pi/2],
\]
as well as all EOR12 localization guards with the EOR9 constants.
Here $(b,a)$ would be $(k,a_{{\rm src},e})$ or
$(j,a_{{\rm tgt},e})$. Neither application is permitted on the present
GPA domain. Indeed $\gamma>2$ gives
\[
 2k<R_k\le2^{-33}q_k,\qquad q_k>2^{34}k .
\]
At the source the upper rectangle guard would imply
\[
 q_k\le3m_\epsilon\le\tfrac32a_{{\rm src},e}
       \le36k-36<2^{34}k,
\]
a contradiction. At the target it would imply
\[
 q_j\le3m_\epsilon\le\tfrac32a_{{\rm tgt},e}
       \le24k+24<2^{34}k<q_j,
\]
again a contradiction. The middle strict inequality holds for $k\ge29$.
Therefore EOR21's actual fallback here is exactly QOT's low interval.
No high-endpoint equilibrium constants are inserted into this primitive
comparison. Its high target-flag applications remain separate.

\paragraph{The componentwise intersection and its proof.}
In the joint GPA domain define
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 \lambda_{{\rm src},e}
  &=\max\{l_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}},\,\log\mathfrak a_k-\log\kappa_k\},&
 \upsilon_{{\rm src},e}
  &=\min\{u_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}},\,\log\mathfrak b_{k,D_e}-\log\kappa_k\},\\
 \lambda_{{\rm tgt},e}
  &=\max\{l_{j,a_{{\rm tgt},e}},\,\log\mathfrak a_j-\log\kappa_j\},&
 \upsilon_{{\rm tgt},e}
  &=\min\{u_{j,a_{{\rm tgt},e}},\,\log\mathfrak b_{j,D_e}-\log\kappa_j\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{EFP35}
\label{epr:eq:centered-intersection}
\end{equation}
Let $L_{{\rm src},e}$ and $L_{{\rm conn},e}$ denote the logarithmic
source-order determinant ratios of NSR6. The exact finite result is
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 S_e^-=-r_e(\log\kappa_k+\upsilon_{{\rm src},e})
       \le L_{{\rm src},e}
       \le-r_e(\log\kappa_k+\lambda_{{\rm src},e})=S_e^+,\\
 T_e^-=-r_e(\log\kappa_j+\upsilon_{{\rm tgt},e})
       \le L_{{\rm conn},e}
       \le-r_e(\log\kappa_j+\lambda_{{\rm tgt},e})=T_e^+ .
\end{gathered}
\tag{EFP36}
\label{epr:eq:component-intervals}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, without moving the scalar centres,
\[
\begin{split}
 S_e^-&=\max\{-r_e\log\mathfrak b_{k,D_e},
              -r_e(\log\kappa_k+u_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}})\},\\
 S_e^+&=\min\{-r_e\log\mathfrak a_k,
              -r_e(\log\kappa_k+l_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}})\},\\
 T_e^-&=\max\{-r_e\log\mathfrak b_{j,D_e},
              -r_e(\log\kappa_j+u_{j,a_{{\rm tgt},e}})\},\\
 T_e^+&=\min\{-r_e\log\mathfrak a_j,
              -r_e(\log\kappa_j+l_{j,a_{{\rm tgt},e}})\}.
\end{split}
\]
Where one GPA guard fails, omit that component's QOT candidate and use
its original NSR6 interval. This is an unconditional finite rule on the
original NSO domain, with explicitly guarded additional proved bounds.

To prove the source statement, apply QOT15 to the fixed complete
columns $r_h/\psi_k$ of degree at most $a_{{\rm src},e}$:
\[
 \kappa_ke^{l_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}}}H_e^{(1)}
 \preceq H_e^{(k)}
 \preceq \kappa_ke^{u_{k,a_{{\rm src},e}}}H_e^{(1)}.
\]
Inversion reverses this inequality, coefficient conjugation preserves it,
and the same full-rank $\Theta_e$ in \eqref{epr:eq:source-map} gives
the rank-$r_e$ dual determinant interval QOT27.
The whole-source minimum NSO21 independently gives NSO25/NSR6 on the
identical affine fibre. Each lower endpoint bounds the same scalar
from below, and each upper endpoint bounds it from above. Their
maximum and minimum give $S_e^\pm$. Their interval is nonempty
because it contains that actual scalar.

For the target, the ordinary Taylor multiplier equation NSO23/NSR5 is
\[
 \operatorname{stack}_i\operatorname{Mult}(B_i)P_e^{\rm Tay}
 =\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde T_j,4)F_e
       +\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde V_j,4)E_e^{\rm low}.
\]
All its columns represent the same actual germs. If two representations
differ, multiplying the zero Taylor difference by $E_A$ produces an
upper-grid numerator zero through $D_e+v\ge q_j-1$; the upper Vandermonde
and the fixed numerator quotient kill the analytic class, and the lower
Vandermonde kills the remaining lower class. Thus $F_e$ is literally
the same coefficient matrix in the two pairs. The full multiplier kernel
of NSO24 gives its quotient by the complete boundary image rank $r_e$.
Its metric is the attained minimum
\[
 x^*H_{{\rm conn},e}^{(s_1)}x
 =\min_\xi(F_ex+B_{\rm cof}\xi)^*
          \operatorname{diag}(D_{j,D_e}^{(s_1)},4)
                         (F_ex+B_{\rm cof}\xi).
\]
This includes all four lower minima inside $D_{j,D_e}$ and the full
boundary minimum over $\xi$. QOT16 on that target degree
$a_{{\rm tgt},e}$ therefore gives QOT30; NSO24--25 independently gives
the target NSR6 interval. Taking their intersection proves $T_e^\pm$.
No source-centre identification with $c'_j$ occurs in this argument.

\paragraph{The strongest propagation from these component intervals.}
Retain the exact NSR7 identity, with all original constants,
\[
 A_e^p
  =\log\det H_{{\rm conn},e}^p-\log\det H_{{\rm src},e}^p
  =-\mathcal X_e^p+\epsilon_e^p,\qquad
 \mathcal X_e^p=\chi_{X_k,D_e}^p-\chi_{Y_e,D_e}^p ,
\]
\[
 \epsilon_e^{p,-}
 =-2r_e\log\max(1,K_-^p)-r_e\log c_+^p,\qquad
 \epsilon_e^{p,+}
 = 2r_e\log\max(1,K_+^p)-r_e\log c_-^p,
\]
\[
 c_-^p=\min(1,(L_-^p)^{-2}),\qquad
 c_+^p=\max(1,(L_+^p)^2),\qquad
 b_e^-=\epsilon_e^{1,-}-\epsilon_e^{0,+},\quad
 b_e^+=\epsilon_e^{1,+}-\epsilon_e^{0,-}.
\]
These $K_\pm^p,L_\pm^p$ retain the original NBG/NKC domains, the degree
$k+8$ comparison, both centres, the shift
$(\beta_{4,u,w}-2)/i$, every coefficient and both full Gamma masses.
They are not replaced by the scalar order-comparison constants.

Set $\mathcal J_e^{\rm ord}=A_e^1-A_e^0$ and
$\Delta\mathcal X_e=\mathcal X_e^1-\mathcal X_e^0$. Then
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 T_e^- -S_e^+\le\mathcal J_e^{\rm ord}\le T_e^+-S_e^-,\\
 \zeta_e=L_{{\rm src},e}+\epsilon_e^1-\epsilon_e^0,\qquad
 S_e^-+b_e^-\le\zeta_e\le S_e^++b_e^+,\\
 L_{{\rm conn},e}=-\Delta\mathcal X_e+\zeta_e,\qquad
 S_e^- -T_e^++b_e^-\le\Delta\mathcal X_e
                         \le S_e^+-T_e^-+b_e^+ .
\end{gathered}
\tag{EFP37}
\label{epr:eq:propagation}
\end{equation}
In the joint GPA domain the scalar-bearing forms of the two principal
intervals are exactly
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 r_e(\lambda_{{\rm src},e}-\upsilon_{{\rm tgt},e})
 \le \mathcal J_e^{\rm ord}
          +r_e\log(\kappa_j/\kappa_k)
 \le r_e(\upsilon_{{\rm src},e}-\lambda_{{\rm tgt},e}),\\
 r_e(\lambda_{{\rm tgt},e}-\upsilon_{{\rm src},e})+b_e^-
 \le \Delta\mathcal X_e-r_e\log(\kappa_j/\kappa_k)
 \le r_e(\upsilon_{{\rm tgt},e}-\lambda_{{\rm src},e})+b_e^+ .
\end{gathered}
\tag{EFP38}
\label{epr:eq:centered-propagation}
\end{equation}
Here the exact inherited centre is
\[
 \frac{\kappa_j}{\kappa_k}
   =\frac{256(k-3)^{k+3}}{k(k+2)(k-7)^{k-1}} .
\]
This is QOT31, including the full source masses; it is not absorbed
into an error interval.

For proof, subtract the two component determinant ratios to get
$\mathcal J_e^{\rm ord}=L_{{\rm conn},e}-L_{{\rm src},e}$.
The smallest value on their interval rectangle is $T_e^--S_e^+$,
and the largest is $T_e^+-S_e^-$. Subtracting the two exact NSR7
identities gives
$\Delta\mathcal X_e=L_{{\rm src},e}-L_{{\rm conn},e}
+\epsilon_e^1-\epsilon_e^0$, which proves the remaining bounds by
the same endpoint calculation. Substitution of the scalar-bearing
expressions \eqref{epr:eq:component-intervals} proves
\eqref{epr:eq:centered-propagation} with both displayed signs.

These are the smallest enclosures obtained from the component intervals
and these exact linear identities: a linear function attains each bound
at the indicated rectangle vertex. This is a statement about the
information in those intervals, not about realizability or optimality
for the actual correlated matrices. In particular this componentwise
procedure retains mixed NSO/QOT endpoint choices that can be lost by
merely intersecting the two separately propagated paired intervals.
The common variables in
$\zeta_e=L_{{\rm src},e}+\epsilon_e^1-\epsilon_e^0$ and
$\Delta\mathcal X_e=\zeta_e-L_{{\rm conn},e}$ remain shared.
Their marginal intervals must not subsequently be treated as independent
when recovering $L_{{\rm conn},e}=\zeta_e-\Delta\mathcal X_e$.

The earlier PSR26 full centered interval is also retained exactly as in
NSR8--9: denote its unchanged endpoints by $P_e^-,P_e^+$, and, only
on its original domain
\[
 k\ge\max\{9,v-1,\lceil(\pi/2)\sqrt{\delta^2+\gamma^2}\rceil+1\}
\]
with the same original admissible SGT14 radius $0<a<1$, replace
$S_e^-$ by $\max\{S_e^-,P_e^-\}$ and $S_e^+$ by
$\min\{S_e^+,P_e^+\}$ in \eqref{epr:eq:propagation}.
Here $P_e^\pm$ are the literal preexisting PSR26 endpoints, including
its $D_e^{\rm ord}$ and both errors, not newly defined analytic constants.
No PSR guard is imposed on NSO or QOT. The proof is again intersection
of bounds on the same source determinant ratio; the original
$\Theta_e$ and complement/frame factors remain as in
\eqref{epr:eq:theta}.

At $e=0$ the established equality $\chi_{Y_0,D_0}=0$ may be used.
At $e=1$ the complete four-row primitive constraint and
$\chi_{Y_1,D_1}$ stay in every formula.
Since these intersections are subsets of QOT27,30,32,34, they preserve
the already proved $O_{\rm actual}(k^{3/2}+k\log q_k)$ paired error and
the conclusions $\mathcal J_e^{\rm ord}=o(q_k)$ and
$\Delta\mathcal X_e=o(q_k)$ at fixed actual data. Indeed the original
low $l,u$ are $O_{\rm actual}(\sqrt{k}+\log q_k)$, the rank is $O(k)$,
$\log(\kappa_j/\kappa_k)=O(\log k)$, and NSO27 gives
$\epsilon_e^{p,\pm}=O_{\rm actual}(k\log q_k)$.
These inherited order-difference conclusions assign no absolute
compatibility value and no later primitive source extension.

\section{Consequences and exact scope}
Every new interval is an intersection of finite inequalities on the
same original coefficient directions or matched quotient values.
Consequently it is contained in the corresponding NSO interval
and, where available, in the QOT/EOR interval. Empty coefficient
blocks have rank zero, determinant one and zero return, so all
formulas retain those cases.

The EOR limiting allowances already proved in the complete provider
therefore remain valid for the direct allocation differences and
the common return after these further finite intersections:
the two allocation endpoints are
$-8\log(\pi v_*/4)$ and $8\log(4/(\pi u_*))$ after division by
$q_a$, and the common difference allowance after division by
$q_k$ is $16\log(v_*/u_*)$, on their same fixed-actual-data eventual
domains. This follows by containment of intervals, not by a new
limit exchange. Here $u_*K(\zeta)=2$, $v_*E(\zeta)=4$ and
$\zeta^2=1-u_*^2/v_*^2$ as in EOR.

The finite expressions retain the actual initial common angle,
all four returns in $Z_e$, the original independent source quotient,
and every proper-source error. No numerical individual allocation,
no uniform estimate in a moving period, and no exclusion of an
off-line quartet follows solely from these identities. The
mathematical output is the proved finite propagation through the
displayed exact maps, now available at every stated receiving site.
